  **The real mechanism, confirmed live by forcing styles via `el.style.setProperty(..., 'important')` and watching what actually changed the rendered width — not reasoned from the class list, which gave a WRONG answer on the first pass**: `<button>` elements get shrink-to-fit sizing for `width: auto` from the browser's own form-control rendering rules, regardless of `display: block` — so `block w-full lg:w-auto` on a `<button>` works exactly as intended (confirmed on `School`'s own "Log out" and `ChildDetail`'s "Revoke access", both correctly content-width). `<Link>`/`<a>` elements get NO such exception — an ordinary block-level anchor with `width: auto` fills its containing block, full stop, which is the standard CSS2.1 block-box algorithm. `lg:w-auto` alone is silently inert on a `<Link>`; the fix is `lg:inline-block` (or another shrink-to-fit display value) alongside it. Two real instances fixed this way: `principal/passports/enrol/page.tsx`'s "Cancel" and `ReviewSection.tsx`'s locked-FBA "Save as PDF" link — both `<Link>`, both genuinely stretched (992px / full width) despite already carrying `lg:w-auto`.

  **The first version of this exact entry was itself wrong**, and is worth recording as the reason to keep verifying rather than stopping at a plausible-sounding explanation: the first theory (from one live measurement of a broken `<Link>` and a lone code-read of a working `<button>`) was "`width: auto` is inert on any `display: block` element, only inline-block/flex items shrink." That's a coherent-sounding CSS claim, and it was accepted and nearly written down before a second live measurement (forcing `width: auto` directly via inline `!important` on a "broken" `<button>` — no effect, it was already content-width) disproved it. The tag (form control vs. anchor) was the actual variable, not `display`. Don't let a theory that merely explains the one example you already found stand in for actually testing it against a second one.

- **`min-width: auto` IS THE DEFAULT ON A FLEX ITEM, AND IT DOESN'T ANNOUNCE ITSELF UNTIL SOME CHILD IS WIDE ENOUGH TO HIT IT.** A flex item refuses to shrink below its own content's intrinsic width unless given `min-w-0` explicitly — this has already bitten `principal/layout.tsx` and `clinician/layout.tsx` (their own content divs, fixed pre-emptively) and, found in this same pass, `ChildDetail.tsx`'s and `ClinicalFileDetail.tsx`'s own tab-content pane (`<div className="flex-1 px-4 py-4">` / `<main className="flex flex-1 flex-col gap-4 px-4 py-4">`, both missing `min-w-0`, both copied from the same original vertical-tab-rail pattern). Symptom on `ChildDetail`'s Messages tab: a message row's own un-wrapping badge+timestamp content (built correctly, with `min-w-0 flex-1 truncate` on the one span that should shrink) had nothing above it in the ancestor chain willing to shrink FIRST, so the whole row overflowed 103px past the 1280px viewport — masked from view only because a grandparent card has `overflow-hidden`, which hides the overflow but still hard-clips the text mid-word instead of letting the intended `truncate` ellipsis do its job. Confirmed the shared `PassportMessagesTab`/`MessageCard` components are NOT the cause — the identical component renders cleanly on the teacher's own passport page, which has no equivalent flex-row tab-rail wrapper to trigger it. When a `flex-1` (or any flex-item) pane wraps genuinely dynamic/unpredictable-width content — anything with a `truncate` span inside it — give the pane itself `min-w-0`, not just the innermost span.

# Verification environment

- **A DEPLOYMENT BEING "Ready" IS NOT THE SAME AS IT BEING LIVE — THE PRODUCTION BADGE IS A SEPARATE FACT, AND A MANUAL REDEPLOY IS UNTRACEABLE BY SHA. COST AN HOUR, WILL HAPPEN AGAIN. 19 Sept 2026.** Found during the Silo 2 placeholders build: client code was definitely pushed (confirmed on `origin/main`), typecheck/lint/build were all clean, and the deployed app definitely did not serve it — every one of 24 live JS chunks, fetched fresh with `cache: 'no-store'` directly from an authenticated session, searched for the new code and found none of it. That diagnosis was correct and thorough. The conclusion it pointed at — "the build must be broken" — was one level too shallow.

  **What had actually happened**: a Vercel Builds & Deploy incident the night before backed up eight deployments behind one stuck `Initializing`. Daniel cancelled the head; the rest correctly resolved to "queued stale," since they're sequential commits on `main` and the newest contains them all. He then manually redeployed the latest — it built successfully, "Ready in 1m 10s" — **and was never promoted to Production**. A manual redeploy does not take the Production badge automatically. Production stayed on the commit before the Silo 2 work the entire time. Every chunk fetched during verification was correct for what was genuinely being served; the served deployment was simply the wrong one.

  **THE RULE THIS EARNS: when verification finds code that is definitely pushed and definitely absent from the served bundle, the question is not "is the build broken" — it's "which deployment currently holds the Production badge."** A "Ready" status proves the build succeeded; it proves nothing about whether it's what a real user (or a browser-verification pass) is actually being served. A manual redeploy's own dashboard entry reads "Redeploy of `<id>`," not a commit SHA — which is exactly what makes it untraceable against `git log` from this side. If a from-scratch investigation (fresh tab, service worker unregistered, Cache Storage cleared, CDN cache-age checked and confirmed `age: 0`/`PRERENDER`) still shows old code being served, stop debugging the browser and ask directly which deployment is live in the dashboard — that question resolves it in one step where hours of client-side cache theories won't.

  **THE RECURRING CAVEAT WAS THE REAL FINDING, PER DANIEL'S OWN INSTRUCTION TO RECORD IT AS SUCH**: every verification report in this file ends with some version of "no Vercel credential, cannot confirm the deployed SHA" — usually a footnote nobody acts on because it's never once been the actual cause of anything, until this. **A read-only Vercel API token would let a future session confirm a deployment against a commit SHA directly, independent of dashboard access, removing this exact hour-long blind investigation the next time a genuinely-pushed change doesn't appear live.** Recorded as a real, standing tooling gap — not a footnote to keep repeating.

- **Browser verification runs against the DEPLOYED app** — `.claude/launch.json`'s `deployed` entry (`https://behaviour-hive-app.vercel.app`), not `local-dev`. A local dev-server check exercises real RLS against the real Supabase project, which is genuinely useful, but it does not prove the deployed build behaves the same — different bundle, different environment. **State which environment was actually used in every report, every time** — the same way the deployed commit SHA gets stated, not left implied. "Verified live" with no environment named is not a real claim; it's the gap that let browser verification run against `local-dev` for two straight days (Aug 25–27) while every report during that window said "verified live" and meant local. Caught only when asked directly, not by anything failing.

- **WHEN A BROWSER-TOOL CLICK ON A CTA "TIMES OUT" BUT INSTEAD HIGHLIGHTS THE BUTTON'S LABEL TEXT, IT'S TOOLING, NOT THE APP — AND IT HAS A FIX.** Hit twice in the same session before being named properly (Stage 3's own browser-verification pass) and wrongly written off both times as "the documented input-synthesis outage" that "resolves on its own" — it doesn't resolve on its own, and treating it as an unfixable environment flake meant one interaction (the ABC logger's reactive lapsed-access message) went unverified in the first version of a report that claimed the rest of the stage was checked live. What's actually happening: the click resolves to the text node inside the button rather than the button element itself (visibly, the label gets selected/highlighted, like a text drag). The fix, in order: (1) dispatch a real click on the button ELEMENT via `javascript_exec` — find it (`document.querySelectorAll('button')`, matched by text or a stable selector) and call `el.dispatchEvent(new MouseEvent('click', {bubbles: true, cancelable: true, view: window}))` — this reliably opens sheets/triggers handlers the `computer` tool's click could not; (2) if that's not available, aim the coordinate click at the button's edge padding, not the centre of its label, so no text node sits under the pointer. Try (1) first — it worked every time it was tried. Don't spend another session's budget re-diagnosing this as a fresh outage.

# Deferred work

- **PRD 11 STAGE 5 — DURING A STAY. MIGRATION 0302 RUN, VERIFIED LIVE AT BOTH THE RPC LAYER (24/24) AND THROUGH THE REAL BROWSER FLOW. CLOSED. 25 Sept 2026.** Section 6, all four pieces — recon reported and confirmed by Daniel in full before any code was written; this entry is the build against those confirmed answers, followed by a real client screen and a live verification pass, unlike every backend-only stage before it in this PRD.

  **1. Handover — activation-scoped for BOTH roles, deliberately unlike every one of Stage 4's six gated targets.** A new `Handover` message category (`applies_to: 'child'`, `centre_manager`/`care_staff`), a new sender-role branch in `send_message()`, a matching branch in `can_view_message()`, and — found only by reading `send_message()`'s live body before writing anything — a new candidate branch in `get_message_recipient_candidates()`, since every child-scoped recipient's own role is resolved by looking them up in that exact function; a respite recipient with no matching branch there would have inserted a null `recipient_role` and been refused as "not authorized," not silently admitted. `acknowledge_message()` untouched — it already does exactly what "the record of who knew what" needs. `send_message()`'s two new branches are deliberately written as SEPARATE `elsif` clauses, never a combined lookup with `limit 1`, so this can never become a fifth instance of the "unordered SELECT INTO" trap this schema has already been burned by four times.

  **2. The on-call indicator — institution-scoped, never stay- or activation-scoped, matching `respite_activations`' own metadata posture exactly.** `respite_on_call_designations`, append-only; "current" is simply the latest row by `set_at`, read directly, no derived status column and no RPC needed for reading. `on_call_until` is a real `timestamptz`, rendered so a stale value reads as obviously stale — golden-brown, not the reserved Support-Button red, correcting a first-draft misuse of that token before it shipped.

  **3. Check-ins — a genuinely new table, not an extension of `morning_checkins`.** `respite_stay_checkins`, `stay_id`-keyed rather than "today"-keyed — a multi-day stay's own day 2 morning check-in is just the second `morning`-typed row for that `stay_id`, no special multi-day logic needed. Activation-scoped write for both roles; read matches Stage 4's own split (activation for care_staff, placement for centre_manager).

  **4. "Since last time" — no new schema at all.** `respite_stays`' own SELECT policy (0297) was already institution-wide to any current-standing staff, role-agnostic, confirmed by reading it directly before assuming a new RPC was needed. The client diffs each section's own `updated_at` against the prior stay's `ends_at`, both already readable. Option (a) — surfacing the prior stay's own finalised report, which `respite_post_stay_reports` currently restricts to centre_manager only — recorded as a real, deliberate enhancement for later, not built here.

  **The screen itself, `RespiteChildRecord.tsx`, shared by `/care/passport/[id]` and `/centre/passport/[id]` — the first time this PRD has shipped a real client surface rather than backend-only infrastructure.** A pinned "Crisis Plan" button, outside the scroll, closing PRD 7's own long-recorded "how does a cover worker find it on a phone in three seconds" question. Below it, in the order Daniel set: who this child is (name, age, primary communication method — thirty seconds of orientation before any safety content) — check-ins — triggers — calm cards — communication — calming — current strategies — medical & intimate care — since last time — handover. The FBA's own full narrative is deliberately excluded from this screen (reachable elsewhere on the activated record) — not "read standing up" material. Reused the existing messaging component suite wholesale for handover (`useMessageThread`, `ComposeMessageSheet`, `MessageList`) — no new UI mechanism, only a new category and two new server-side branches. Both `/care/dashboard` and `/centre/dashboard` replaced their old placeholder copy with a real on-call card and a real list of active children, via a new role-branched `get_my_centre_active_children()` (care_staff sees only what's activated for them; centre_manager sees the whole placement roster, activated or not).

  **A real, live bug, found only by the browser pass itself, not by the RPC-level suite — worth naming because it's exactly the kind of thing this session's own verification discipline exists to catch.** The check-in history list rendered `checked_in_at` (the literal database write timestamp) instead of `check_in_date` (the day the entry is actually FOR). Since the fixture's two backdated "yesterday" check-ins were inserted via service role only seconds before the live check, their `checked_in_at` was "now" — today — even though their `check_in_date` was correctly yesterday. The RPC-level suite never caught this because it asserted on the correct underlying data (`check_in_date`), never on what the screen actually displayed; the live render showed all three check-ins looking like they'd happened on the same day, silently defeating the entire point of a multi-day-aware check-in list. Fixed and re-confirmed live: the list now reads "Morning -- Fri 25 Sept" / "End of day -- Thu 24 Sept" / "Morning -- Thu 24 Sept," genuinely spanning two days.

  **VERIFIED, real fixture (`ZZ0302CLINIC`/`ZZ0302CENTRE`/`ZZ0302OTHER`), real signed-in sessions throughout, 24/24 at the RPC layer, then a full live browser pass on the actual deployed-shape screens (local dev).** Care_staff A sent a real handover naming care_staff B; an outsider care_staff at a wholly unrelated centre could neither send nor read it; care_staff B read and acknowledged it for real, `message_recipients.acknowledged_at` set and `messages.status` transitioning to `'acknowledged'`. The centre manager set a real on-call contact through the real RPC (a non-manager refused); a second designation correctly became "current" by `set_at`; an outsider read none of it. Check-ins: the two backdated rows read correctly, a real morning check-in recorded live, a same-day duplicate refused, the manager reading all three spanning two distinct days, the outsider reading none. `get_respite_child_summary()` and `get_my_centre_active_children()` both proven for the real child and refused for the outsider. **Then, live in the browser**: signed in fresh as care_staff, consented through the real screen (whose own copy already correctly named handover and acknowledgement — PRD 11 Stage 2's own consent-screen work, unmodified here), landed on `/care/dashboard` showing the real on-call card and the real active-children list, opened the child's own record and watched every section assemble in the correct order with real data (triggers, the published calm card, communication, medical fields all correctly "None recorded," the acknowledged handover correctly appearing under Archived once the recipient had actioned it), tapped the pinned Crisis Plan button from a scrolled position and confirmed the real modal with the real plan body. Then signed in as centre_manager, confirmed the placement-scoped view of the identical screen, and confirmed the on-call "Update on-call" form as a genuine write round trip through the real UI. Fixture torn down, zero orphans (the same benign transient read-after-write artifact on `institutions`, cleared on immediate retry, seen repeatedly this session). Checked the adversarial suite for `ZZ0302` collisions first, per standing rule — none found. Typecheck/lint/build all clean.

  **The pre-stay summary (PRD 11 section 7a — two written sections, one from the parent, one from the clinical director, neither waiting on the other, both visible to both) is NOT built here, per Daniel's own explicit instruction — its own deferred item, below.**

  **THE FBA IS NOT TOUCHED BY ANY PART OF THIS MIGRATION.**

PRD 11 section 7a. 25 Sept 2026.** Two written sections — one from the parent, one from the clinical director — neither waiting on the other, both visible to both. Confirmed as a genuinely new object; nothing in this schema builds it today. Daniel's own framing: this is the thing the first-five-minutes screen (`RespiteChildRecord.tsx`, built in Stage 5) will eventually OPEN WITH, once built — the natural first section above "who this child is," not a replacement for it. Not scoped or started.

- **PRD 11 STAGE 4 — THE ACCESS MODEL. MIGRATION 0301 RUN, VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE (47/47). CLOSED. 24 Sept 2026.** Section 4, the centrepiece — recon reported and confirmed by Daniel in full before any code was written, then built the same pass. The full four-point recon (what activation gates, how finalisation closes it, the never-finalised backstop, where ABC read sits) is recorded in the conversation that opened this stage; this entry is the build against Daniel's own four confirmed answers.

  **What activation gates, confirmed, not guessed at**: the behavioural profile (passport sections B/C/D/E), the FBA and the BSP (named explicitly — `bsp` and, via the shared `_bsp_is_readable_by_caller()`, `bsp_strategies` for free, the same lever 0245's cross-org-grant branch already used on that function), calm cards (published only, on a completed FBA, matching the parent's own existing gate), and `clinical_plans` — **body only, never attachments**. Explicitly NOT gated, on purpose: `session_notes`, `assessments` (raw Silo 1 material), and every attachment on every table — a `clinical_lead` at the very clinic that WROTE a session note cannot read it (0228/0233's own deliberate asymmetry), and respite gets nothing more permissive than that. `afls_assessments` is deliberately excluded too, even though it's "part of the FBA" — the same reasoning the clinical export screen already established: AFLS is raw scored data, not the FBA's own conclusions, and extending it to a new reader is a decision nobody asked for.

Standing.

  **The mechanism, per Daniel's own precedent naming**: a deliberate, write-triggered flip — `respite_activations`, one open (`closed_at is null`) row at most per stay, a partial unique index enforcing that structurally — never a passive clock comparison (`temporary_access`'s own confirmed-wrong precedent from Stage 1 recon, confirmed again here). **Activation is stay-scoped; the manager's own reach is not** — the one thing that couldn't be inferred from the write side alone, and the same split 0299/0300 already established for ABC logs, extended to six more tables: care_staff reads through an OPEN ACTIVATION, centre_manager reads through the PLACEMENT ITSELF (`episodes_of_care`, `ended_at is null`), completely unaffected by whether any activation is open, closed, or never existed. **Why ABC read stays outside this gate entirely, recorded in the migration's own header, not just in 0299/0300's**: if it were inside, finalising the report — the very act that closes activation — would close read access to the entries the report is assembling from, at the exact moment they're needed.

  **Four ways an activation closes, not two — Daniel's own correction of the original brief.** (1) `report_finalised` — the poka-yoke: `finalize_respite_stay_report()` closes the stay's own open activation in the same transaction as finalising the report, the same atomic shape `sign_bsp()` already uses for its own supersede. (2) `manual` — a centre_manager closes it by hand, any time. (3) `expired` — a timed backstop, `expire_stale_respite_activations()` (service-role only, no grant to `authenticated`, matching `purge_stale_app_events()`/`fail_stale_pending_bookings()`'s established shape exactly), closing any activation whose own stay ended more than 7 days ago and was never otherwise closed — 7 is a conservative, arbitrary starting number, named as such in its own comment, matching the stagnation queue's own evidence-floor precedent; the real number comes from real use. A placement runs for years, so discharge alone was not enough — a stay from March with no report would otherwise leave staff access open until the child left the centre entirely, a real data-protection problem, not a process one. (4) `discharged` — added while building this, not asked for directly but the same shape as the other three: `end_clinic_episode()`'s own respite branch now also closes any still-open activation for that exact child at that exact centre as a coarser, later backstop than the 7-day expiry, a no-op for a clinic discharge.

  **This does not block the report.** A centre_manager's own read of every gated table stays placement-scoped, completely independent of activation state — an overdue report can still be written today, next week, or next year, for as long as the placement itself stays open. Staff access is stay-scoped and expires; a manager's reach is placement-scoped and does not — the whole reason the two branches are built separately rather than as one shared predicate.

  **Activation can be set up in advance, structurally, not by convention**: `activate_respite_stay()` has no time-window check against the stay's own `starts_at`/`ends_at` at all — the gate a caller passes is "does an open activation exist for this child at my centre," never "is `now()` inside this stay's own window."

  **Outstanding work, data only, no screen yet** — `get_respite_stays_awaiting_report()`, matching PRD 5 Stage 7's own "build the data, not the screen" posture exactly: a centre's own stays that have ended with no finalised report against them, infrastructure for whichever future pass builds the centre dashboard's own outstanding-work bucket.

  **`clinical_plans`' own gate is deliberately independent of `school_visibility_override`/`_clinical_plan_is_school_visible()`** — that flag governs SCHOOL visibility specifically; respite gets its own dedicated gate, the same way FBA/BSP each have their own rather than reusing a column built for a different audience.

- **THE PENDING-APPROVAL MISDIAGNOSIS, CLOSED AS A CLASS — FOUR DASHBOARDS COLLAPSED ONTO ONE HOOK, A REAL PRE-EXISTING ACCESS-CONTROL BUG FOUND AND FIXED AS A DIRECT RESULT. 24 Sept 2026.** PRD 11 Stage 2's own report closed with the pending-approval misdiagnosis as its third instance (clinic_admin, clinical_lead, centre_manager) — asked directly whether it could be closed as a class rather than fixed a fourth time.

  **Yes, and it was feasible without any dashboard needing to opt in beyond calling one hook.** `useInstitutionMembership(userId, role)` (`src/hooks/useInstitutionMembership.ts`, new) is now the ONLY place any dashboard resolves "do I have a current, approved `institution_staff` row for this role" — the one correct two-query shape (approved-only first, a separate pending-only query only when that finds nothing) that three of the four original dashboards already had right, now written exactly once. `MembershipMissingState` (`src/components/clinic/`, new, sibling to `PendingApprovalState`) is the shared render for the "missing" outcome (deactivated, rejected, or genuinely absent), replacing three different ad hoc error treatments (an inline `<p>` beside a still-visible header, a full-screen centred block, and — on clinical_lead — no missing-state UI at all). All four dashboards (`clinic-admin`, `clinical-lead`, `centre`, `care`) are rebuilt on both.

  **A FOURTH, more serious bug found retrofitting `clinical_lead`'s own dashboard onto the hook, never previously documented.** That page's hand-rolled check used a SINGLE query with no `approved_at is not null` filter, then branched client-side on the fetched row's own `approved_at`/`rejected_at` columns — a shape none of the other three dashboards shared. The branch condition (`approved_at === null && rejected_at === null`, true only while genuinely pending) meant: a REJECTED lead (`rejected_at` set, `approved_at` still null) failed that condition and fell straight through to the fully functioning dashboard render — their real scoped client list, their real approval queue, not an error and not a pending state. A genuinely MISSING row (no `institution_staff` row at all, or one filtered out by deactivation) fell through the opposite way — the early-return branch fired, `isPendingApproval` was set to `Boolean(data)` = false, and the function returned with `institutionId` permanently null and no error message at all; the dashboard rendered a header with nothing underneath, silently. **The rejected case is a real access-control bug, not a cosmetic one** — a rejected lead reaching their real screens, not merely a bad error message.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, local-dev, all three non-approved states, real fixture** (`ZZMEMCLASS...`, a clinic, a real approving principal, and three `clinical_lead` accounts — rejected, genuinely missing, and genuinely pending). The rejected account, signed in fresh, now correctly sees `MembershipMissingState` ("Could not find your clinic") — confirmed via a real signed-in session, not inferred from the code. The genuinely-missing account (no `institution_staff` row at all) sees the identical, correct state — previously silent. The genuinely-pending account still sees the correct `PendingApprovalState` ("Your request is with your clinical director") — the regression check, since this page's logic changed the most. Fixture torn down (institution, all users, consents), zero orphans confirmed, including a stray institution + 2 orphaned users left by the fixture script's own first, failed setup attempt (missing `rejected_by`/`rejection_reason` on the paired-check constraint) — swept up by prefix, not missed. Checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own codes first, per standing rule — none found. Typecheck/lint/build all clean throughout.

  **THE THREE LIVE-ONLY BUGS AS A SECOND CLASS — runtime allow-lists/role branches that typecheck clean and fail only in a browser.** Asked whether a build scanner (matching `clinic-copy-scan.mjs`/`clinician-role-target-scan.mjs`'s own established shape) could catch an allow-list or switch that enumerates roles without covering all of them.

  **Answer: a heuristic scanner is the WRONG tool for this specific shape — TypeScript's own compile-time exhaustiveness checking is, and it already exists as a proven idiom in this codebase** (`ROLE_LABEL_DEFAULT`, `STAYING_ROLE_OPTIONS`, `ROLE_TILES_BY_TYPE` all already use `Record<SomeUnion, X>` literals that fail to compile when a union grows and the literal doesn't grow with it). A scanner would need either a canonical list to diff against (which doesn't exist as a runtime artifact — this schema has no generated types, a fact CLAUDE.md already names) or a heuristic inferring the canonical set from the union of everything it finds — noisy, and exactly the kind of "verified-before-added, or it's a standing claim nobody checked" risk this file's own allowlist-scanner entries already warn against. A `Record<Role, X>` literal has neither problem: it fails the BUILD, not a separate scan step, the moment the union it's built against grows and the literal doesn't, with zero false positives (only sites that opt in are checked) and zero maintenance (nothing to keep in sync by hand).

  **Built for `/api/set-role`'s `SELF_SERVICE_ROLES`** — the exact shape that missed a role twice (`clinician`, then `centre_manager`/`care_staff`). Rewritten as `SELF_SERVICE_ROLE: Record<Role | "parent", boolean>` (`Role` imported directly from `vocabulary.ts`, the schema's own canonical role vocabulary) — every entry a real decision, not a placeholder: `institution_admin: false`, since that role's own self-service onboarding is deliberately parked (this file's own "INSTITUTION CREATION BEING MANUAL IS DELIBERATE" entry) and no join screen could ever POST it; every other role `true`, since a real, working role-tile picker needs it admitted. **Proven to actually fire, not just written**: a key was removed, `tsc --noEmit` refused to compile with the exact expected error (`Property 'care_staff' is missing in type '...' but required in type 'Record<Role | "parent", boolean>'`), then restored and reconfirmed clean — the same "verify the check actually flags the thing without the entry" discipline this file's own allowlist-scanner entries already require, applied here to a compile-time check instead of a runtime scanner.

  **NOT built for `/more`'s role-routing, and reported as such rather than forced through.** `/more`'s branching is not a flat lookup table — it's an imperative, async control-flow chain (RPC calls to resolve `isVerifiedClinicalPractitioner`, `router.replace()` early-returns interleaved with role checks, several further, narrower role checks scattered through the render for feature-gating rather than routing) across a large file whose own specific historical bug (both missing-role fallthroughs) is already fixed. Retrofitting the WHOLE routing decision onto compile-time exhaustiveness would mean restructuring live async control flow into something like a `Record<Role, () => Promise<...>>` dispatch table — a genuine, sizeable rewrite carrying real risk of introducing a new bug into a large, currently-correct file, not a small, safe addition like the two built above. Recorded as real follow-up work, not done opportunistically inside this pass — the same posture this file already takes for other sizeable, deliberately-deferred sweeps (see the "QUEUED: A FULL SWEEP FOR `role = 'clinician'`..." entry).

- **PRD 11 STAGE 2 — RESPITE CENTRES GET THEIR OWN ROLES AND ROUTE TREE. MIGRATION 0296 RUN, VERIFIED LIVE THROUGH THE REAL SIGNUP FLOW FOR BOTH ROLES, FAIL-CLOSED SWEEP PROVEN. CLOSED. 24 Sept 2026.** Stage 1 was recon only. Two architectural decisions from Daniel opened this stage: (1) respite gets its own roles and its own route tree — `institution_staff_one_principal_per_institution` structurally forces exactly one principal, incompatible with PRD 11's "several managers," so `centre_manager` is a genuinely new role value, never a relabelled principal, and `/principal` is not widened; `/centre` (managers) and `/care` (care staff) are standalone minimal trees, matching `/clinic-admin`/`/clinical-lead`'s own precedent. (2) every two-way `institutionType === "clinic" ? X : Y` ternary that performs a real action or shows institution-specific copy gets an explicit third outcome — refuse, redirect, or an honest "not available here" — never a silent fall into the school branch.

  **Eight build items, all delivered**: `institutions.type` widened to add `'respite_centre'`; `institution_staff.role` widened to add `'centre_manager'`/`'care_staff'`, and every sibling CHECK constraint that duplicates the role vocabulary widened alongside it (`institution_staff_approval_source_check`, `messages_sender_role_check`, `message_recipients_recipient_role_check`, `consents_role_check`, plus the self-link INSERT policy's own role list); the self-link INSERT policy gained a real respite arm (`institutions.type = 'respite_centre' and role in ('centre_manager', 'care_staff')`); the role picker (`SchoolStaffRoleSelectContent.tsx`) gained respite tiles via a compile-enforced `Record<InstitutionType, RoleTile[]>` lookup, closing the exact two-way-ternary trap Decision 2 named; two new consent screens (`CentreManagerAgreementScreen.tsx`, `CareStaffAgreementScreen.tsx`), each in the institution's own words, wired into `consent/page.tsx`'s switch with a new `default:` case (an honest fallback, never a silent drop into school copy); `getRoleLabel`/`vocabulary.ts` widened, compile-enforced the same way; `/centre` built as a minimal real route (a manager signs in and lands on a genuine, if empty, dashboard — its one real, working mechanism is approving the second manager or first care worker who joins after them, via the same `ReviewStaffJoinSheet`/`get_institution_staff_roster()` the principal dashboard already uses); and the fail-closed sweep itself.

  **Two explicit scope answers, both held to**: "centre manager countersigns" means finalising the post-stay report (Stage 3+), not the school incident RPCs — `can_own_incident()`, `create_incident_stamp()`, `raise_support_alert()` all confirmed untouched, incidents stay a placeholder for respite. `useGuardSchoolOnlyRoute` excluded respite too, belt and braces, even though every one of its five call sites lives under `/principal/*`, itself `useRequireRole("principal")`-gated — structurally unreachable by centre_manager/care_staff regardless, same posture as `HandOverPrincipalSheet`'s own `respite_centre: []` entry (this sheet is `hand_over_principal()`-backed, principal-only, and respite has no principal at all).

  **`approve_staff_join()`'s own `approval_source` — a real "column name is a claim" fix caught while reading the function, not asked for separately.** The original body hardcoded `approval_source = 'principal'` on every approval; a centre manager approving a colleague would have written a false claim. Rewritten to resolve the caller's actual role (`v_caller_role`) and set `approval_source` accordingly — `'centre_manager'` is now a real, valid value, not just `'principal'`/`'behaviour_hive'`.

  **`send_message()`'s own trap, hit and self-caught before running anything**: a first draft reconstructed the function's body from memory/fragments and got the parameter list wrong. Caught before running, re-read the true live body verbatim from migration 0261, and rewrote the migration section with only the one role-check line changed — this schema's own standing "`CREATE OR REPLACE` doesn't collapse a longer parameter list onto a shorter one" and "read the live definition, not the first one you find" gotchas, both avoided here by re-checking before shipping rather than after.

  **THREE REAL BUGS, ALL FOUND LIVE DURING THE VERIFICATION PASS ITSELF, NOT BY REVIEW — the build's own compile/lint/build gates caught none of them, since all three are runtime allow-list/branch gaps, not type errors.**
  1. **`/api/set-role`'s `SELF_SERVICE_ROLES` allow-list never got the two new roles** — the role-tile picker correctly rendered respite tiles (proving the fail-closed fix worked), but selecting one failed at the API layer with a bare "Invalid role." The exact same mistake this file already documents happening for clinic roles in this exact file, made a second time for a fourth institution type. Fixed, and the file's own comment now says so plainly rather than only fixing it silently.
  2. **`/more` has no role gate and no branch for the two new roles at all** — found by a proactive sweep after bug 1, not by hitting it live: a centre_manager/care_staff session would have silently fallen through to the generic parent-shaped bottom nav, the exact failure class this file's own pre-existing comment in that page already documents happening once before for SNA. Fixed with an early redirect branch matching the `clinic_admin` pattern.
  3. **"Could not find your centre." shown to a genuinely-pending second manager** — the third instance of a failure class this file already documents twice (`clinic_admin`, `clinical_lead`'s dashboards): an ordinary, expected "joined, not yet approved" state rendered as a scary generic error. Fixed by reusing `PendingApprovalState` (given an optional `waitingFor` prop, defaulting to its original wording so both existing callers need zero changes) and replicating `clinic-admin/dashboard/page.tsx`'s own two-query pattern — a first query requiring `approved_at is not null`, and if empty, a second scoped to `approved_at is null and rejected_at is null` to distinguish pending from genuinely missing/rejected — in both `/centre/dashboard` and `/care/dashboard` (which had no data-fetching at all before this, just a static "You're all set" that would have been actively false for care_staff, who never bootstraps and always starts pending). A smaller, adjacent bug fixed in the same pass: the dashboard's error state and its empty state were not mutually exclusive — both could render together ("Could not find your centre." directly above "Nothing needs your attention right now.") — fixed by gating both on `!error`.

  **VERIFIED THROUGH THE REAL SIGNUP FLOW, local-dev, genuinely fresh accounts, per Daniel's own explicit instruction that this is the only way a new institution type is ever proven** (echoing the clinic-track onboarding-placeholder lesson elsewhere in this file: "a new institution type is only proven by signing up through the real screens as each of its roles"). A fresh respite centre (`ZZRESPITE...`) and three roleless accounts: **manager1** (institution code → role tiles, correctly showing "What's your role at the centre?" with Centre Manager/Care Staff → the correct `CentreManagerAgreementScreen` copy → real ticks via dispatched MouseEvents → bootstrap-auto-approved → landed on the real `/centre/dashboard`, institution name and an honest empty state). **manager2** (identical flow → landed on the genuine `PendingApprovalState`, "Your request is with your centre manager" — the case a `principal` index would have refused outright, now correctly a second, independently-approvable manager) → **approved by manager1 through the real `ReviewStaffJoinSheet` UI** (correct copy: "Requesting to join as Centre Manager", "your centre") → confirmed via service-role read that `approval_source = 'centre_manager'`, not a false `'principal'` claim → signed back in as manager2, confirmed landing on the real dashboard, no longer pending. **care_staff** (fork → institution code → role tiles → the correct `CareStaffAgreementScreen` copy, "speak to your centre manager" footer → consent → the genuine `PendingApprovalState`, since care_staff never bootstraps) → approved by manager1 via the same real UI ("Requesting to join as Care Staff") → signed back in, confirmed landing on the real `/care/dashboard`'s genuine "You're all set" state.

  **THE FAIL-CLOSED SWEEP, proven two ways.** The role-picker ternary: proven live — a respite institution code correctly resolved to the respite tiles and copy, never silently falling into the school branch (the exact trap Decision 2 named). The `/principal`-gated surfaces: a care_staff session navigating directly to `/principal/dashboard` was redirected away by `useRequireRole("principal")` before ever reaching `useGuardSchoolOnlyRoute` — confirmed live — proving respite is structurally refused at the outer gate for every one of `useGuardSchoolOnlyRoute`'s five call sites, exactly as Decision 2 anticipated ("belt and braces, even though respite should never reach them").

  Fixture torn down (institution, all `institution_staff` rows, all 3 auth users), zero orphans confirmed via direct query; checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution code first, per standing rule — none found. Typecheck/lint/build all clean, including the prebuild allowlist scanners (`clinic-copy-scan.mjs`'s `KNOWN_SCHOOL_ONLY_EXCEPTIONS` gained both new consent screens, flagged only for containing "countersign" in a respite-specific sense; `clinic-copy-allowlist.json` had two pre-existing entries' line numbers corrected for the shift).

  **Not in this stage, per Daniel's own scoping**: placement, stays, the access model, handover, on-call, the forms. Stage 3 onward.

- **ONBOARDING — A PARENT/STAFF DIFFERENTIATOR SCREEN IN FRONT OF THE INSTITUTION CODE. DONE, VERIFIED LIVE ON LOCAL-DEV WITH FOUR FRESH ACCOUNTS, 24 Sept 2026.** Reported directly: a parent landing on "Enter your organisation's code" (the top of the onboarding funnel) doesn't have one and doesn't know what it means — the only escape was a small link ("I don't have a code, or I'm a parent") buried under a text field meant for staff.

  **New `/role-select` — a plain fork, two choices, no code asked for yet.** "I work at a school, clinic or respite centre" → `/role-select/institution` (the exact former `/role-select` content, moved verbatim — same lookup, same destinations, same "I don't have a code" escape hatch, a Back control added since there was nowhere "before it" to go back to until now). "I am a parent or guardian" → `/role-select/parent` (new). "Respite centre" is copy only, on this one line — no new institution type, no branching, no schema change; the institution code itself still decides what an organisation is, exactly as before.

  **`/role-select/parent` asks for the CLAIM code directly, not the institution code — the actual source of the original confusion.** `redeem_passport_claim_code()`'s live body (0116) is identity-gated only (`auth.uid()` present, nothing else) — confirmed by reading it before building this, not assumed — so it's safe to call here, before role is set and before consent, the same way the institution branch resolves a real institution before any role exists. `consent/page.tsx`'s own institutionType resolution for a parent already reads `passport_guardians` rows and explicitly anticipates a guardian row existing before consent (its own comment: "a brand-new parent who hasn't claimed anything yet... can be genuinely empty") — claiming here first means the very next screen shows the right school/clinic copy immediately, proven live (a school-linked claim produced the correct school-branded consent screen on the first render, no "genuinely empty" fallback needed). Role is set FIRST via `/api/set-role`, same order the institution branch's own role-tile picker uses (role, then the joining action) — `/api/set-role` is a plain idempotent overwrite, safe to repeat on retry, and an "already have access" redemption error (a retry after an earlier success) is treated as success rather than shown as a scary failure.

  **No code yet: not a dead end, and not a route back to self-created-passport creation (retired, see the `sync_passport_guardian_from_user_id()` entry above).** Explains plainly, inline on the same screen, that the child's school or clinic creates the record and issues the code — then continues exactly like a parent who claimed successfully would, straight to `/consent`, landing on a genuine (if empty) dashboard with the same already-working claim prompt (`/passport/welcome` → `/passport/claim`) they'll reach naturally once they do have one.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, local-dev, four genuinely fresh, roleless accounts through the real screens** (a fifth used only to re-confirm the role-tile picker's own Back button): a teacher (fork → institution code → school role tiles → correct school consent screen); a clinic practitioner (fork → institution code → clinic role tiles → correct clinic consent screen, confirming the clinic role picker, live and reachable since PRD 5's own placeholder fix, is unaffected by this reroute); a parent with a real claim code (fork → parent path → claim redeemed via the real RPC → landed on consent with the correct school-branded copy, confirmed via a service-role read that `app_metadata.role = "parent"` and a real `passport_guardians` row existed); a parent with no code (fork → parent path → "I don't have a code" → the explanation → "Continue" → landed on consent with the safe default copy, confirmed via a service-role read that role was set and — correctly — no guardian row exists). Back navigation proven from every branch: institution screen → fork, parent path → fork, and the role-tile picker → institution screen (not skipping past it to the fork). Fixture torn down, zero orphans; checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own codes first, per standing rule — none found. Typecheck/lint/build clean throughout.

- **A SCHOOL CANNOT LINK ITSELF TO A CHILD WHO ALREADY HAS A CLINIC-CREATED PASSPORT — NOTHING IN THE SCHEMA ATTACHES AN EXISTING PASSPORT TO A NEW INSTITUTION. Found in recon, 23 Sept 2026, closing the Share sheet's own dead code — real, structural, NOT YET BUILT.** Every insert into `passport_institution_links`, across the whole schema, happens inside a passport-*creation* function (`create_school_passport()`, `onboard_clinic_client()`) — confirmed by grepping every migration for the insert, not assumed. There is no RPC anywhere that takes an *existing* `passport_id` and attaches a new institution to it. `cross_organisation_grants` does not fill this gap — it shares an FBA or a BSP specifically, and its own direction trigger refuses a receiving institution the child isn't already linked to. So today: a clinic creates a passport, the parent claims it, and the parent wants their child's school to have it — and there is no path. A school's only entry point, `principal/passports/enrol`, unconditionally calls `create_school_passport()`, confirmed by reading the real submit handler — enrolling a child who already has a clinic passport creates a genuine SECOND, duplicate passport, silently, breaking the one-passport-per-child rule the whole model rests on.

  **THE SHAPE IT MUST TAKE, so nobody builds the wrong one: PARENT-INITIATED, not school-side detection.** The parent gives the school a code; the school enters it and links. A school typing a child's name and being told "this child already has a passport" would disclose that the family sees a clinic — theirs to tell, not this app's. Two halves needed: an RPC that links an existing passport to a new institution, and a school-side screen to enter the code. Plus a real decision, not yet made, on what the school gets on linking — a school-side relationship and the passport sections, never clinical content, which is what `cross_organisation_grants` is for.

  **Does not block next week's clinic family (no school involved) or the school trial (no clinic passports involved).** Blocks telling any school and any clinic that the two organisations work together — the first real family that needs both will hit this directly.

  **The Share sheet itself was rebuilt around this finding, the same day.** `ShareBottomSheet.tsx` (and `src/lib/generatePassportCode.ts`, its only other caller) is deleted outright — both halves of what it did were dead: the clinician-code field (every real clinician now joins via an organisation, `clinician_code` is null for all of them, confirmed against production — the RPCs it called, `lookup_clinician_by_code`/`connect_clinician`, are NOT dead themselves, they're also used by `GrantClinicianAccessSheet.tsx` on the school side, just pointless for a parent with nobody to enter a code for) and the passport-code-for-a-teacher field (`lookup_passport_by_code()` has zero client callers anywhere — its only consumer, `AddChildSheet.tsx`, was deleted in PRD 3 Stage 2, and nobody connected that deletion back to the parent-facing screen still generating and promoting a code that goes nowhere; confirmed live against production — a real, freshly-claimed passport, `SAM-9954`, `passport_code_active: true`, with no school link and no screen anywhere that could ever consume it). Two other "Link your clinician" dead-ends fed by the same removed field (`ClinicalSupportSection.tsx`'s no-clinician FBA card, `CalmUnlockSheet.tsx`'s no-clinician state) now show information only, no broken action. The Passport ID (`passport_reference`, migration 0289) — a reference, not a key; identifies a child when a parent contacts the clinic, grants nothing — moved to `/more`, plainly labelled, not under "Share," no access language, per Daniel's own instruction that it must never be confusable with an actual credential.

- **QUEUED: A FULL SWEEP FOR `role = 'clinician'` LITERAL CHECKS THAT REFUSE A PRACTISING DIRECTOR OR LEAD — CONFIRMED NEEDED, NOT YET RUN. 21 Sept 2026.** PRD 10 section 4a made every clinical role a genuine practitioner — a director or `clinical_lead` can now author their own FBAs/BSPs/session notes, same as a `clinician`, with authority layered on top rather than instead of it. But their `institution_staff.role` stays `'principal'`/`'clinical_lead'`, never `'clinician'` — any helper or policy written BEFORE this PRD that checks the caller's own role for the literal string `'clinician'` silently refuses a director or lead doing exactly the practitioner-level thing PRD 10 now says they may do. Five separate functions have been found with this exact shape, this build alone, none by deliberately auditing for it — each found live, while building something else:

  1–3. **Three found during PRD 10 Stage 1 itself**, closing the clinic UI/authorization gaps that stage's own build surfaced — `is_verified_clinic_director_or_lead()` (`0266`, the clinician-track gate's own widening) and the two authorization helpers fixed in `0267` (`_is_director_of_institution()`, `_is_verified_clinician_at_institution()`) all needed correcting for a director/lead to reach surfaces a plain `role = 'clinician'` check would have kept refusing them from.
  4. **`set_clinician_workspace_email()`'s own caller check** (PRD 9 Stage 1) — a director/lead now practising for real needs their own workspace email set the same way a practitioner does, and the original check refused them.
  5. **`_clinician_authored_at_institution()`** (`0246`, PRD 8) — found live building PRD 10 Stage 6's own propose screen: a director's own completed FBA and signed BSP were invisible to `get_fba_reports_for_director()`/`get_bsp_for_director()`, both of which gate through this one helper. It backs **eight director-read call sites** across `0246`/`0249`/`0250`/`0251` (session notes, FBA, BSP, clinical plans, assessments, published clinical content, attachments) — fixed in `0275`, widened to `role in ('clinician', 'clinical_lead', 'principal')`, run and verified live (the director's own FBA/BSP correctly returned afterward, the propose screen correctly offered them, a real school confirmed reading both — see the Stage 6 entry below for the full proof).

  **The sweep itself has not been run.** Five instances found by accident, none by deliberately grepping for the pattern, is exactly the shape this file's own "AN UNORDERED `SELECT ... INTO`..." entry (above, Supabase/Postgres gotchas) already warns is how this kind of bug survives — found one at a time, each only once something new happened to exercise it. **The mechanical check, when this is picked up**: grep every migration for `role = 'clinician'` and `role in (` clauses touching `institution_staff`/`clinicians`, read each one's live (highest-numbered) definition, and ask whether a director or `clinical_lead` acting as a practitioner in their own right should pass it. Not urgent enough to have stopped Stage 6 for — recorded here so the next migration that touches clinician-track authorization checks this list first, rather than finding a sixth instance by accident too.

- **THE CLINIC TRACK WAS FULLY BUILT, FULLY ADVERSARIALLY VERIFIED ACROSS SEVEN STAGES, AND COMPLETELY UNJOINABLE BY A HUMAN — FOUND BY DANIEL'S OWN FIRST CLICK, NOT BY REVIEW, NOT BY ANY CHECK IN A SUITE OF OVER A THOUSAND. FIXED, VERIFIED THROUGH THE REAL SIGNUP SCREENS ON THE DEPLOYED APP FOR ALL FOUR ROLES. CLOSED. 21 Sept 2026. THE MOST IMPORTANT FINDING OF PRD 5, PER DANIEL'S OWN INSTRUCTION TO RECORD IT AS SUCH.** Daniel created a real clinic institution and tried to join it through the real signup flow. He got: *"Not set up yet. Accounts for this kind of organisation aren't available here yet."* — `SchoolStaffRoleSelectContent.tsx`'s own deliberate, commented, greppable clinic placeholder from the onboarding restructure, still live because PRD 5 Stage 1 made `getInstitutionType()` read the real `institutions.type` column, which means a genuine clinic now reaches a screen nothing had ever replaced.

  **Nothing in PRD 5's own seven stages ever touched this screen.** Every one of them — the self-link RLS (Stage 2), the four consent screens (Stage 2), episodes/tags/scope (Stages 3–4), change requests (Stage 5), caseload verification (Stage 6), the director's dashboard (Stage 7) — was built and adversarially verified through fixtures (`institution_staff` inserted directly) or by calling RPCs as an already-signed-up test account. Not one verification pass, across any stage, ever created a fresh account and walked it through the real screens as a clinic user, because doing so would have hit this exact placeholder immediately. **Every surface downstream of signup was correct. The signup itself led nowhere.** This is the sharpest instance yet of a shape this file already names repeatedly for individual roles (the clinician-track specialty gate, the `get_passport_team()` finding, the Stage 7 dashboard-fallthrough bug) — here it wasn't one gate on one surface, it was the entire front door to a seven-stage feature.

  **THE FIX, four pieces, all in `SchoolStaffRoleSelectContent.tsx` and what it touches:**
  1. **The placeholder replaced with a real clinic role picker** — Clinical Director / Practitioner / Clinical Lead / Admin, titles resolved through `getRoleLabel(role, "clinic")` (`src/lib/vocabulary.ts`, PRD 5's own already-built translation layer), never hardcoded — confirmed live: the picker renders exactly these four labels, matching the vocabulary function's own output.
  2. **`/api/set-role`'s `SELF_SERVICE_ROLES` allow-list widened** to admit `clinician`/`clinical_lead`/`clinic_admin`. The route's own comment (the "no independent clinician" model correction) was right in its REASONING — every clinician belongs to an organisation, joins by its code, same as `class_teacher`/`sna`/`principal` — but the allow-list was never actually widened for the code-gated path that reasoning was describing, because that path's own UI didn't exist yet to hit the gap. The INDEPENDENT no-code route (`role-select/no-code/page.tsx`) still never sends `"clinician"` — unchanged.
  3. **`getPostAuthRedirect()` had no case for `clinical_lead`/`clinic_admin`** — both fell through to the default `/role-select`, sending a genuinely joined, consented user back to code entry, indistinguishable from someone who'd never signed up. Neither role has a real dashboard yet (a separate, deliberate, already-recorded product decision — see the `clinical_lead` entry elsewhere in this file — unchanged by this fix). Both now land on a small, honest holding page (`/clinical-lead/dashboard`, `/clinic-admin/dashboard`) that says plainly the account is set up and the dashboard isn't built yet, rather than looping.
  4. **A second, related bug found building the proof, not anticipated going in**: a clinic practitioner who joins by code has no `clinicians` row until their director approves them (`approve_staff_join()`'s own clinic branch, `0222`, only creates one on approval). Every caller of `ClinicianAccessGate` (all eight), plus `/clinician/dashboard`'s own separate, un-migrated copy of the identical logic, read "no `clinicians` row" as "hasn't picked a specialty yet" — the INDEPENDENT path's own next step, wrong and actively misleading for someone who joined a clinic by code and is waiting on their director. The dashboard's own version was worse: it hard-redirected to `/clinician/specialty` immediately, with no message at all. Fixed by extending `useClinicianReviewState` to also check for a pending `institution_staff` row with `role = 'clinician'` (never a legal school role — `institution_staff_role_check`'s school branch doesn't include it — so a pending row with this role is unambiguously a clinic join, no institution-type check needed) and exposing `institutionJoinPending`; the gate and the dashboard's own duplicate both show "Your request is with your clinical director" instead.

  **CHECKED AND CONFIRMED ALREADY CORRECT, NO FIX NEEDED — Daniel's own checklist, answered by reading the live code, not assumed:**
  - Does the first clinical director auto-approve, as a school's first principal does? **Yes** — `derive_staff_join_approval()` (live definition, `0101`) special-cases `role = 'principal'` with no institution-type branch at all; confirmed live, the Director account auto-approved (`approval_source: 'bootstrap'`) on first join.
  - Does each role reach its own consent screen? **Yes, all four** — `/consent/page.tsx`'s own switch was already wired for `clinical_lead`/`clinic_admin` (built in PRD 5 Stage 2, simply unreachable until now); confirmed live, all four clinic-branded screens rendered correctly (`PrincipalAgreementScreen`'s clinic variant, `ClinicianAgreementScreen`'s clinic variant, `ClinicalLeadAgreementScreen`, `ClinicAdminAgreementScreen`).
  - Does a practitioner's approval create their `clinicians` row, as Stage 6 built? **Yes** — confirmed live via the real director dashboard's own "Review → Approve" flow: the practitioner's `clinicians` row was created at the moment of approval, `verification_status: 'verified'`, `verification_route: 'organisation'`, a real `clinician_code` generated, `specialty: 'unspecified'` exactly as `0222` designs it.
  - Does each land on the right dashboard afterwards? **Two yes, two fixed** — Clinical Director → real `ClinicDirectorDashboard`; Practitioner → correctly the honest pending message, then the REAL `/clinician/dashboard` once approved (all stats, all action tiles, live); Clinical Lead/Admin → previously a redirect loop, now the honest holding page (see fix #3 above).

  **THE SWEEP FOR OTHER PLACEHOLDERS, per Daniel's own instruction.** The onboarding restructure's own comments name exactly two: this one, and the institution-employed-clinician tile (a fourth "Clinician" tile that used to sit on the SCHOOL role picker, for a school's own in-house clinician). Checked directly: that tile was **removed** on 19 Sept, not fixed — it simply isn't offered any more, a deliberate, already-recorded parked decision (`CLAUDE.md`'s own "INSTITUTION-EMPLOYED CLINICIAN VERIFICATION — PARKED, POST-TRIAL" entry), not a live dead-end. Grepped broadly beyond the two named placeholders for every "not set up yet"/"coming soon"/locked-gate screen in the app: the independent clinician path's own "Coming Soon" specialty gate (`ClinicianAccessGate.tsx`, `/clinician/dashboard`'s own duplicate) already correctly skips for `verificationRoute === 'organisation'` (this file's own earlier "CLINICIAN-TRACK SPECIALTY GATE" fix, confirmed still in place, not regressed); the `resources` pages' own "Coming Soon" stubs (teacher, clinician) are deliberate, known, unrelated feature-completeness placeholders, not onboarding dead-ends. No third live onboarding placeholder found.

  **VERIFIED THROUGH THE REAL SIGNUP SCREENS, deployed app, all four roles, fresh accounts — not fixtures, not RPC calls, per Daniel's own explicit instruction that this is the entire lesson.** A fresh disposable clinic (`ZZURGENT21`) and four fresh accounts, each signed in through the real `/login` form and driven through the real screens: institution code entry → the real clinic role picker → the real clinic-branded consent screen → landing. Clinical Director: auto-approved, landed on the real `ClinicDirectorDashboard`, "ZZ Urgent Verify Clinic" rendered correctly. Practitioner: pending, correct "Your request is with your clinical director" message (not the specialty prompt, not a redirect). Clinical Lead and Admin: pending, correct consent screens, landed on their own honest holding pages, no loop. Then, as the Director, the real dashboard's own "Outstanding Work" queue correctly listed all three pending joins with correct role labels ("Requesting to join as Practitioner"/"...Clinical Lead"/"...Admin") — approved all three through the real `ReviewStaffJoinSheet` UI. Confirmed directly against the database: all three `approved_at`/`approval_source: 'principal'` set; the Practitioner's `clinicians` row created exactly as `0222` designs it. Signed back in as the Practitioner: the full, real `/clinician/dashboard` rendered completely — stats, Recent Activity, every action tile (Resources, Messages, View Passports, Add Log, FBAs, Strategy Insights, Strategy Bank). Fixture torn down, zero orphans confirmed (institution, all four users).

  **ONE DEPLOYMENT-LAG INSTANCE, worth naming since it nearly read as a code bug**: the practitioner's own pending-message fix (piece 4 above) was pushed as a second, separate commit partway through this verification pass, and the first live check against it showed the OLD behaviour (redirect to `/clinician/specialty`) despite the database state being exactly right. Diagnosed correctly, per this file's own standing method for this exact symptom: fetched the actual served JS chunks directly and searched for a string unique to the new code (`institutionJoinPending`) — genuinely absent from the first check, present after the deploy caught up. Not a bug; the fix landed and verified clean once the deploy had actually finished.

- **PRD 8 STAGE 3 — THE STAGNATION QUEUE. MIGRATION RUN (0260), CLIENT BUILT, VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE (22/22) AND A REAL BROWSER PASS ON BOTH THE EMPTY STATE AND A GENUINELY FIRING ROW. CLOSED. 20 Sept 2026.** Section 6 — Daniel's own framing: "the single most valuable thing in PRD 8 and the only feature in the product that could not exist on any other platform... a failing intervention, surfaced automatically." Clients with active clinic involvement AND rising incident/restraint rates at school. Recon-first, per his own explicit instruction — do not build — across four questions; two came back as structural findings that change the shape of the build before any decision was even needed.

  **STRUCTURAL FINDING 1, THE MORE IMPORTANT ONE — Stage 1's cross-organisation grant mechanism does NOT make incidents reachable, and Daniel's own premise going in was wrong about this, corrected directly rather than built around.** `cross_organisation_grants`/`has_cross_org_grant_access()` (0245) is clinic→school by trigger (`_validate_cross_organisation_grant_direction()`) and scoped to `scope_items <@ array['fba_report', 'bsp']` by a positive CHECK — incidents were never a candidate value, and none of the function's three real call sites (SELECT policies on `fba_reports`, `bsp`, `bsp_strategies`) touch `incidents`. The mechanism is built for a clinic OFFERING its own material to a school — the reverse of what this feature needs. What actually reaches incidents, and always has, independent of anything Stage 1 built: `clinician_access`, via `get_clinician_incidents()` (0245's own withholding/countersign gates added on top of the pre-existing PRD 8 Stage 1 "incident-flow check" finding that a clinic-engaged clinician already sees a school's incidents for their client, zero grant table involved).

  **STRUCTURAL FINDING 2 — the read path exists, but not for the caller this feature actually needs.** `get_clinician_incidents()` authorizes a SPECIFIC clinician reading their OWN caseload (`is_verified_clinician(auth.uid())` + their own active `clinician_access` row) — not a director surveying the whole clinic's roster the way every other `ClinicDirectorDashboard.tsx` bucket does. And `clinician_access` itself has no institution scoping at all — a `'parent'`-engaged clinician (no clinic tie whatsoever) gets identical incident visibility to an `'institution'`-engaged one. **Confirmed scope for the eventual build, Daniel's own instruction: a new director-facing incident-read RPC, same POSTURE as the five PRD 8 Stage 2 director-read RPCs (director-only, clinic-only, `institution_staff_has_current_standing`) but a genuinely different SHAPE — not "was this clinician active at my clinic when they authored this clinic-owned document" (the existing five), but "is this child currently one of my clinic's engaged clients, and if so, read their SCHOOL-authored incidents the way an individual engaged clinician already can." Anchored on `episodes_of_care` (institution-scoped `started_at`/`ended_at`, the established "our clients" table across every other clinic-dashboard bucket), not `clinician_access` directly — confirmed as the right anchor specifically because `clinician_access` has no institution scoping of its own to lean on.** Real scope, not a detail — named here so it isn't rediscovered as a surprise when this gets built.

  **DECISION 1 — WITHHELD INCIDENTS: OPTION A, compute on what the clinic can see.** Not Option B (compute on what actually happened, including withheld incidents, without exposing content) — reversing withholding's own meaning is a separate decision about what withholding is FOR, and Daniel built it deliberately as a real gate, not a redaction, accepting a real latency cost on the reasoning that it is only meaningful if nothing has crossed first (PRD 8 Stage 1's own entry, above). Overriding that on an inference here — however good the inference — would have been exactly the wrong move; asking rather than assuming was correct. **To record plainly wherever this feature's own documentation lives once built, not left implicit**: this queue computes on visible incidents only; a withheld incident is invisible to it, by design; a school minimising a problem makes the queue quieter, which is the one case it most exists to catch. **Flagged separately, explicitly NOT built as part of this**: whether a clinician should know THAT a withhold happened on a given child, without seeing what or why — Daniel's own framing, "a clinician seeing 'three things you have not been told' is clinically relevant on its own" — a real, different question, deserving its own decision, not folded into this queue.

  **DECISION 2 — THE EVIDENCE FLOOR: a single named constant, a conservative starting value, documented in its own comment as arbitrary and trivial to change — never tuned to make the queue fire.** Daniel's own reasoning, worth keeping verbatim rather than paraphrased: *"A queue that fires at n=3 teaches people to ignore it, and then it is worthless in the case it was built for."* **The queue being often empty is CORRECT BEHAVIOUR, not a limitation to engineer around** — at real clinic scale (Daniel's own example: five incidents, two children, one term), most comparisons will correctly fail to clear the floor, and that's the honest answer, not a gap to close by lowering the bar. The empty state's own copy must say so plainly — not "All clear." (which claims a verdict this feature was never positioned to make) but something honest about there not being enough evidence yet either way. **The real number is deferred to the trial's own data, deliberately** — not invented here, not guessed at a plausible-sounding value, a genuinely open constant waiting on real incident volume from real use.

  **DECISION 3 — `clinical_plans`-ONLY CLIENTS ARE OUT OF SCOPE FOR THIS QUEUE, NO CALENDAR FALLBACK.** The five Silo 2 placeholder types (crisis/sensory-diet/AAC/care/student-support plans) have no lock or sign timestamp at all — confirmed directly from `clinical_plans`' own migration comment (0244), which states the absence of a lock is deliberate design, not an oversight to route around. Daniel's own reasoning for refusing a calendar-anchored substitute: *"The queue asks whether the plan is working. A client with no signed plan has no intervention to evaluate, and a calendar-anchored version would be answering a different question while looking like the same one."* Must be stated plainly wherever this would otherwise read as a silent absence — a client on this queue's own roster with only a `clinical_plans` entry and no `bsp` should say so, not simply never appear with no explanation.

  **EVERYTHING ELSE CONFIRMED AS PROPOSED, no changes**: rate-per-week-of-exposure as the unit, never a raw count (the before/after windows are unequal lengths — a count comparison between them would be apples to oranges). `bsp.signed_at` (0238) as the one split point — real, non-client-writable, set only by `sign_bsp()` at the exact moment `status` becomes `'active'`, walkable back through `supersedes_id` for a plan's full revision lineage if a "since the very first version" comparison is ever wanted instead of "since the current version." The density-gate PHILOSOPHY already proven in `src/lib/progress/abc.ts`/`comparison.ts` (a minimum-evidence floor on both sides before ever showing a comparison, a ±1 "steady" noise band) reused, not reinvented — the specific numbers are this feature's own, per Decision 2, not copied from the Progress module's. Child as the Entity, always — matching `ClinicDirectorDashboard.tsx`'s own standing constraint and this file's own already-settled principal-incident-sorting precedent (identification is fine, a performance metric is not): practitioner name, if shown at all, is unsortable, ungroupable context text, never a groupable axis, never a league table. Golden Brown, not the Support Button's reserved red — this is a prompt to look, not a safety alert. Copy states the fact and stops: *"Incidents for [child] have increased since their plan began."* is the complete claim; "worth checking in on" is the only permitted addition; a cause is never named — the ambiguity (a plan that needs revision, a new teacher, something changed at home) stays real and unresolved in the copy, exactly as it is in fact.

  **BUILT: `get_institution_incidents_for_director()` (the new director-facing incident read, bulk/institution-scoped, minimal fields only — `passport_id`/`incident_id`/`occurred_at`/`is_restraint`, deliberately not full incident content, per Decision 1's own scoping) and `get_institution_stagnation_queue()` (the computation, calling the first internally rather than re-deriving the withholding filter a second time). Both migration 0260. `is_restraint` derived from `restrictive_practices` row existence, not `incident_action_types.is_restraint` — the more precise, more direct signal of the two. `ClinicDirectorDashboard.tsx` gained its own "Worth A Look" section, deliberately separate from the existing `nothingOutstanding`/"All clear." empty state (its own header comment now says why) — rising clients render as real `WorkQueueRow`s with fact-stating copy and a "Review" link to the child's own record (never the incidents themselves, matching every other bucket); the empty case renders honest, evidence-aware copy built from the roster's own `queue_status` breakdown, never a verdict.

  **A REAL BUG FOUND WHILE BUILDING THE VERIFICATION FIXTURE, INITIALLY FLAGGED RATHER THAN FIXED (OUT OF SCOPE OF THIS MIGRATION) — FIXED PROPERLY IN A LATER PASS, `0261`, ONCE IT TURNED OUT TO BE THE THIRD OF FOUR INSTANCES OF THE SAME PATTERN. See the dedicated Postgres-gotchas entry above ("AN UNORDERED `SELECT ... INTO` AGAINST `institution_staff`...") for the fix itself and the other three instances: `create_bsp()`'s own `institution_id` resolution was unordered and unscoped for a multi-clinic practitioner.** Its query (`select s.institution_id from institution_staff s ... where s.user_id = auth.uid() and s.role = 'clinician' ... limit 1`, no `order by`) picks ONE of a clinician's own institution_staff rows arbitrarily when they hold more than one — found live, not theorised, when a fixture practitioner who was staff at two real clinics had a BSP for a child at Clinic B silently attributed to Clinic A instead (confirmed directly: the row's own `institution_id` was Clinic A's, while `clinician_access.engaged_by_institution_id` for that exact passport correctly pointed at Clinic B). **The correct source of truth is sitting right there and isn't used**: `clinician_access.engaged_by_institution_id` for the specific `(passport_id, clinician_id)` pair being acted on is unambiguous; the current query instead scans the CALLER's own institution_staff membership in general, with no relationship to which child is being discussed at all. Consequence, real and not hypothetical: a genuinely multi-clinic practitioner (an ordinary, supported scenario — nothing prevents it) can have a BSP silently misattributed to the wrong clinic — wrong for `bsp_one_active_per_passport_per_institution`'s own uniqueness scope, wrong for which clinic's own director can read it via `get_bsp_for_director()`, and now wrong for this queue's own roster too. Worked around in the verification fixture at the time (a dedicated single-clinic practitioner for the affected case) — properly fixed shortly after in `0261`, once Daniel asked for it fixed rather than carried.

  **VERIFIED — real fixture (`ZZPRD8STAGE3SCHOOL`/`ZZPRD8STAGE3CLINIC`/`ZZPRD8STAGE3CLINICB`/`ZZPRD8STAGE3EMPTYCLINIC`), real signed-in sessions throughout, 22/22.** `get_institution_incidents_for_director()`'s own refusals proven directly: a director at an unrelated second clinic gets nothing for the first clinic's own institution id; a practitioner (not a director) gets nothing even for their own clinic; a `clinical_lead` gets nothing. The success case: a genuinely current client's incidents ARE returned (12 of 13 real countersigned incidents — the 13th deliberately withheld at countersign, correctly excluded); a second child whose episode was genuinely ended via `end_clinic_episode()` returns ZERO incidents even though 3 real, countersigned, non-withheld incidents exist for them — cross-checked directly against the database to confirm the episode-ended exclusion, not some other cause, is what's hiding them. The queue itself: a real BSP signed 10 weeks into a real 20-week engagement, 5 real incidents before signing and 8 after (1 withheld, so 7 visible) — `incidents_before_count=5`, `incidents_after_count=7`, `incidents_after_rate(0.7) > incidents_before_rate(0.5)`, `queue_status='rising'`. The SAME child's restraint sub-signal (2 before, 3 after — both under the floor of 5) independently reports `restraints_rising=false`, proving each signal's own evidence floor is applied separately rather than one clearing the floor for both. A `no_signed_plan` client and an `insufficient_evidence` client (2 before/1 after) both present in the roster with the correct status, never silently dropped.

  **Then a live browser pass, local dev, confirming the part Daniel named as most likely to be built wrong.** The empty clinic's own director (zero rising clients: one `no_signed_plan`, one `insufficient_evidence`) saw, rendered live: *"Not enough incident history yet to say whether a current plan is working for any client."* plus *"1 doesn't have enough incident history yet to compare either way; 1 doesn't have a signed plan yet."* — never "All clear.", and genuinely a different section from the existing Outstanding Work bucket, which correctly DID say "All clear." for what it measures (nothing pending), proving the two empty states don't collide or borrow each other's copy. The main clinic's own director saw the real rising row: *"Incidents have increased since their plan began — worth checking in on."* with *"0.5/wk before their plan → 0.7/wk since"* as context, and its "Review" link resolved to `/principal/passports/<passport_id>` — the child's own record, confirmed directly from the rendered `href`, never the incidents themselves.

  Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule (none found). Typecheck/lint/build all clean.

  **Stage 3 closes here.** PRD 8's own remaining item — the cross-organisation read audit — is next; not started.

- **PRD 9 STAGE 1 — THE GOOGLE CONNECTION AND AVAILABILITY. MIGRATIONS RUN (0255, 0256), CODE BUILT, VERIFIED AT EVERY LAYER INCLUDING A REAL GOOGLE ROUND TRIP. CLOSED. 20 Sept 2026.** A new PRD: native, parent-facing appointment booking against a clinic's own Google Workspace calendars — the app's own database is the source of BOOKINGS, Google Workspace (via a service account with Domain-Wide Delegation) is the source of clinician AVAILABILITY. Recon-first, per Daniel's own instruction, across three rounds (the connection/mapping/Freebusy mechanics; the bookings table design; the two closure gaps) before any code was written — each round's decisions are recorded in their own migration/code header comments rather than only here.

  **Session types, decided in PRD 9 section 3a, not the original "one duration" model**: online, in-person, and school-observation, all one hour, with in-person and school-observation additionally carrying REAL 30-minute travel-block calendar events immediately before and after the session (never a soft buffer — an actual event, so the clinician's calendar genuinely shows the travel as unavailable to anyone else reading it). The session type is chosen BEFORE availability is computed, since it changes how much clear time a candidate gap needs — online needs 60 minutes clear, in-person/school-observation need 120 (30+60+30) to offer a 60-minute slot at the centre. `school_observation` is real in the data model (a clinician's own calendar can carry one) but is refused at the API boundary for parent-facing booking — proven live: a request with `sessionType=school_observation` returns 400 before authentication even runs.

  **`workspace_email` is director-set, never self-declared — the one design choice Daniel confirmed as the deciding factor from my own reasoning, not a house style copied from elsewhere.** Checked against this schema's own two existing patterns for "a fact about a clinician" — `specialty`/`domain_tags` (self-declared, zero verification, since being wrong costs nothing) vs `verification_status`/`clinician_code` (director/Behaviour-Hive approved, no client UPDATE policy at all). A workspace email sits with the second: a wrong-but-REAL address silently misdirects the Domain-Wide Delegation service account to a different real person's calendar, with nothing failing to say so — a non-existent address fails cleanly, a real-but-wrong one does not fail at all. `set_clinician_workspace_email()` is director-only, clinic-only (`inst.type = 'clinic'`), with a partial unique index preventing two clinicians ever sharing one mapping. Surfaced through the existing Directory > Clinicians screen (a new "Workspace email" block in `ClinicianCoverageDetail.tsx`, gated on `useInstitutionType(institutionId) === "clinic"` — a school principal using the identical shared component never sees the block at all) rather than a new screen, and `get_institution_clinicians()` was widened (DROP+CREATE, since a `RETURNS TABLE` shape change can't go through a bare `CREATE OR REPLACE`) so the director can see the current mapping before correcting it, never a blind overwrite.

  **Every one of the new clinic-scoped RPCs (`set_clinician_workspace_email`, `set_clinic_hours`, `set_booking_buffer_minutes`, `set_booking_window_days`) uses `institution_staff_has_current_standing()`, not hand-written `deactivated_at`/`approved_at` conditions** — this schema's own standing rule, applied from the first draft this time rather than caught in review.

  **The `bookings` table — a row is written before any Google API call; the calendar event is its destination, never its origin.** Built in full now (Stage 1), even though Stage 2 owns the only code path that will ever `INSERT` into it — the reconciliation/orphan-handling shape is part of the table's own design, not something to retrofit once Stage 2 discovers it's missing. No stored status column, matching this schema's own standing convention (`passport_completion_requests`, `incidents`' own derived status) — "confirmed" is `cancelled_at is null`, "completed" is `cancelled_at is null and session_end_at < now()`, both computed at read time.
