Travel blocks are flat columns (`travel_before_start_at`/`travel_after_end_at`, outer bounds only — the inner bounds are already `session_start_at`/`session_end_at` since a travel block is always contiguous with its session), not a child table, since a travel block never exists or is queried independently of its own session. `google_calendar_id` is frozen at booking time, not re-read live off the clinician's current `workspace_email` — a later director correction to a typo must not retroactively misdirect an already-written booking's own sync/cancel operations. `cancellation_policy_snapshot` is copied text, not a foreign key into a versioned policy table, matching `session_notes`' own two-edit-tracking-columns precedent for "what did this person agree to at the time" questions. A structural `EXCLUDE USING gist` constraint (`btree_gist`) backstops the double-booking check independently of Stage 2's own application-level re-check at booking time — proven live: a genuinely overlapping active booking for the same clinician is refused, and the identical overlap succeeds once the original is cancelled (the constraint is scoped to `cancelled_at is null`).

  **Drift is detected, marked, and surfaced to the CLINICIAN — never auto-adopted, never auto-cancelled. Daniel's own explicit override of my own first proposal (auto-cancel-on-drift), and the reasoning is the record, not just the outcome**: a clinician dragging an event in Google must not be silently treated as a reschedule (that changes what the parent agreed to without asking them), and auto-cancelling is worse than the problem it solves (it costs the parent their slot for something nobody asked to happen). `google_sync_status` (`pending`/`synced`/`drifted`/`sync_failed`) marks the fact and leaves the booking exactly as it was; `get_my_bookings_needing_attention()` puts both `drifted` and `sync_failed` rows in front of the clinician specifically — their calendar, their session, only they can tell whether the time is genuinely free — never a director's queue, never a silent log. A human resolves it, by rescheduling or cancelling properly, in the app.

  **The orphaned-`pending`-row gap, the second thing Daniel required closed before Stage 2 starts writing to this table**: if the process dies between the row insert and the Google call that's meant to follow it, the row must not sit there looking like a real booking forever. `fail_stale_pending_bookings()` (service-role only, matching `purge_stale_app_events()`'s own established shape) reclassifies — never deletes — a `pending` row older than 5 minutes to `sync_failed`, which puts it in front of the same clinician queue with no separate mechanism needed. `/api/cron/fail-stale-bookings` mirrors `/api/cron/purge-app-events`'s exact shape and reuses the same `CRON_SECRET`; deliberately NOT wired into `vercel.json` yet (no `vercel.json` exists in this repo at all) — inert until Stage 2 needs it live.

  **Verified at three layers, and the boundary between them stated plainly, not blurred.** (1) Schema/RLS/RPC — real fixture (`ZZPRD9CLINIC`/`ZZPRD9CLINICB`/`ZZPRD9SCHOOL`), real signed-in sessions throughout, 53/53: the director sets and reads back a workspace email; a clinical_lead, a clinic_admin, the clinician themselves, and a genuinely active verified SCHOOL principal are all refused (the school-principal case refused purely on the `inst.type = 'clinic'` check, not an authority check — the sharpest version of the "school sees nothing clinic-shaped" proof, and the RPC-level twin of the render-level proof below); cross-clinic isolation both directions; the partial unique index; every clinic-settings RPC's boundary values; `get_bookable_clinician_details()`'s four real cases (resolves correctly, refuses an unconfigured clinician, refuses an unrelated parent, refuses a clinician not on the caseload); every `bookings` CHECK constraint including the exclude constraint's both directions; `bookings`' RLS across parent/clinician/director/cross-clinic/school-principal; `get_my_bookings_needing_attention()`'s exact filter; `fail_stale_pending_bookings()`'s service-role-only gate and its actual effect. Fixture torn down, zero orphans. (2) Availability computation — 7/7 on the pure function directly (online vs in-person clear-time requirement; the identical free gap yielding one slot for online and zero for in-person, the whole point of choosing type first; the buffer padding every busy block), plus 3/3 against a genuinely running dev server (`school_observation` refused with 400 before auth runs; valid types pass through to a 401; missing params refused). (3) The Google round trip itself — **stated plainly as unverified, not glossed over**: `GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY` in Vercel and the Domain-Wide Delegation grant in Workspace Admin are both still unconfirmed, and nothing in this pass exercised a real Freebusy call. Blocked on Daniel's own setup, not on anything this session can do.

  **THE RENDER-LEVEL PROOF, DONE SEPARATELY, ON THE DEPLOYED APP, ONCE COMMITTED — SIXTH INSTANCE OF "A SCHOOL SEES NOTHING CLINIC-SHAPED" NEEDING PROOF FROM THE SCHOOL'S SIDE, FIVE OF THE PRIOR SIX BEING CLIENT-SIDE GATES, NOT DATABASE ONES.** The RPC-level refusal above proves a school principal cannot WRITE a workspace email through `set_clinician_workspace_email()`. It does not prove the Workspace-email BLOCK never RENDERS at all for them — `ClinicianCoverageDetail.tsx` is the same shared component a clinic director's screen uses, and an RPC refusal happening server-side says nothing about what a client-side conditional does. Proven separately: a fresh real school (`ZZPRD9SCHOOLRENDER`), a real principal account signed in through the actual `/login` form (not a service-role session), consenting through the real screen, navigating to the real Directory > Clinicians segment, and selecting a real engaged clinician (`ZZPRD9SCHOOLRENDER`'s own institution-engaged, verified clinician, appearing exactly as `get_institution_clinicians()` — the widened RPC — returns it). The rendered pane goes straight from the clinician's name and specialty to the coverage checklist ("Select all" / the one covered child / "Save Changes") — confirmed both from the extracted page text and, more rigorously, from a direct DOM search of the full page `innerHTML` for the word "workspace" (case-insensitive) and for a "Set"/"Change" button: both came back negative. Not "hidden," not "disabled" — genuinely absent from the markup, matching what `institutionType === "clinic"` gating in source actually does (the whole block sits inside that one conditional, never rendered to `false` with CSS). Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own codes first per standing rule. A known tooling quirk was hit and worked around during this pass, not the app: the browser tool's coordinate-based clicks on the consent screen's checkboxes and "Continue" button registered on the label text rather than the input/button elements (this repo's own documented quirk) — resolved by dispatching real `click()` calls on the actual DOM elements directly via `javascript_exec`, the same fix this file already prescribes for this exact symptom.

  **The full adversarial suite surfaced one unrelated finding along the way, fixed in the same session — see the dedicated "A REBUILD CAN SILENTLY DROP A GATE" entry above for the full account.** `derive_countersign_fields()` had silently regressed a real, already-shipped fix (`0103`) when `0245` (this session's own earlier PRD 8 Stage 1 work) rewrote it from scratch. Nothing about this was caused by or related to migration 0255 — found only because the gate rule required a full suite run for a migration touching the database, exactly the reason that rule exists. Fixed in `0256`; full suite re-run clean, 1163/1163, zero regressions.

  **THE GOOGLE ROUND TRIP ITSELF, PROVEN LIVE, 20 Sept 2026 — Daniel's own GCP/Workspace Admin setup complete (service account created, Domain-Wide Delegation granted with the calendar scope, `GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY` set in Vercel, redeploy confirmed), run directly against Google's own APIs rather than through the deployed app, since item 4 below needed an arbitrary out-of-domain subject the app's own route could never construct.** Four items, in the order Daniel specified, stop-on-failure — none did.

  1. **Authentication.** A real RS256 JWT, signed with the actual downloaded key, exchanged at Google's own token endpoint impersonating `daniel@thebehaviourhive.com` — `200`, a real `access_token` returned.
  2. **A real Freebusy call, proven to reflect actual calendar state, not assumed.** Baseline Freebusy on a real target day (14 days out, to avoid colliding with anything real) came back genuinely empty. Two real events were created on that real calendar via the Calendar API (impersonating the same subject, same access token) — `09:00–10:00` and `11:30–12:30` UTC, deliberately bounding a 90-minute gap. A second Freebusy call, immediately after, returned exactly those two windows, byte-for-byte matching what was just written — a real write-then-read, not an inference. Both test events deleted immediately after item 3 (both `204`), nothing left on the real calendar.
  3. **Availability computation, fed by that real Freebusy data, not a fixture.** The 90-minute real gap (10:00–11:30, bounded by the two real events) is exactly the differentiating width this PRD's own design turns on: online (60 minutes clear) offered two real slots inside it (`10:00–11:00`, `10:30–11:30`); in-person (120 minutes clear) offered none. The exact "online offers where in-person cannot" proof, this time against a real Google calendar's own real state, not `computeAvailableSlots()`'s own unit-test fixtures from earlier in this stage.
  4. **The impersonation boundary.** A token request for a subject outside the domain (`someone.outside@gmail.com`) was refused outright by Google itself — `401`, `unauthorized_client`, *"Client is unauthorized to retrieve access tokens using this method, or client not authorized for any of the scopes requested."* Confirmed as a genuine authorization-scope refusal, not a routing quirk of one made-up address: Domain-Wide Delegation is scoped to the verified domain at Google's own admin-console level, and the token exchange itself enforces it before any Freebusy/Calendar call is ever attempted.

  Verification script (`scripts/dev/zz-prd9-google-roundtrip-verify.mjs`) reproduces `computeAvailableSlots()`'s own logic locally (a plain-Node ESM script, not a TS import) rather than re-deriving it independently — checked by hand against `src/lib/scheduling/availability.ts` at the time this was written, so item 3 proves the SAME function's behaviour against real data, not a parallel reimplementation that could quietly diverge. `GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY` was added to `.env.local` (gitignored, confirmed before writing it) for this one run, mirroring how `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` already sits there for privileged test scripts — kept there deliberately, since Stage 2's own verification will need it again.

  **Stage 1 closes here, genuinely, all three layers proven.** Stage 2 (booking creation, cancellation, reschedule, the sync loop) is next, per Daniel's own explicit scoping — not started. The clinic-settings UI gap this entry's own earlier draft named as still-owed ("minimal director-facing UI for the three clinic-settings RPCs") is no longer a gap — see the dedicated clinical director's dashboard entry below, which built `/principal/clinic` and gave `set_clinic_hours`/`set_booking_buffer_minutes`/`set_booking_window_days` their real screen.

- **PRD 9 STAGE 2 — BOOKING, THE VISIBLE HALF. MIGRATIONS RUN (0258, 0259), CODE BUILT, VERIFIED LIVE AGAINST THE REAL DEV SERVER AND THE REAL GOOGLE CALENDAR. CLOSED. 20 Sept 2026.** Recon-first per Daniel's own instruction (five points, reported and stopped on, not built) — his confirmations plus two things the recon itself surfaced are the actual scope this stage was built against.

  **Two constraints the recon surfaced that PRD 9 section 6 itself didn't anticipate, both resolved by Daniel directly rather than worked around silently.** (1) One Google Calendar event has one `summary` field and one attendee — it cannot show a parent one title and the clinician a different one, which is what section 6's own language implied. *"PRD 9 section 6 is wrong rather than your code being a workaround."* The real event uses the clinician-facing title (`Clinical Session - <passport reference>`, distinguishing one client from another on a busy calendar, which is the thing that actually matters on that side); the parent's own "your session with [clinician]" framing lives entirely in this app's own UI, never in Google's shared summary. (2) No "passport number" concept exists anywhere in this schema for section 6 to reference. `formatPassportReference()` (`src/lib/scheduling/passportReference.ts`) invents a new, non-security, display-only reference derived from the passport's own UUID — deliberately NOT `passports.passport_code` (a real, live security credential that grants `passport_access`) — flagged in-file as swappable, since nothing about its exact shape is load-bearing anywhere else.

  **THE ALL-OR-NOTHING WRITE, HONESTLY BOUNDED.** `create_pending_booking()` writes the row before any Google call — the row is what `bookings`' own header already promises, its destination is Google, never its origin. Google's Calendar API has no transaction primitive, so "all-or-nothing" is a best-effort rollback (delete whatever succeeded), not a guarantee — a delete can fail the same way the original create did. `mark_booking_sync_failed()` fires regardless of whether the rollback confirmed clean, reaching the clinician's own `get_my_bookings_needing_attention()` queue (Stage 1's "detect, mark, a human resolves it" principle, extended from drift to a failed write). The parent gets one plain, synchronous error — never a spinner, never a silent pending state.

  **FIRST-COME-FIRST-SERVED, TWO DISTINCT SURFACES, PROVEN SEPARATELY.** A fresh, narrow Freebusy re-check at the exact requested window (never the cached slot list the parent's page loaded minutes earlier) is the first surface — cheap, and catches most conflicts including a clinician's own directly-created Google event. `bookings_no_overlap`, a `gist` EXCLUDE constraint from Stage 1, is the second and final backstop — the thing that actually resolves a genuine race between two requests that both saw the slot as free. Both surfaces are wired to produce the IDENTICAL "This time is no longer available. Please choose another slot." message, so a parent can never tell which one refused them, and doesn't need to.

  **`cancelled_via` gained a real, distinct `'clinician'` value — found while re-reading the CHECK constraint Stage 1 shipped, which had `'clinician_google'` but no plain `'clinician'` at all.** Daniel: *"Your read is right, and the distinction matters."* `'clinician'` is a real in-app cancel action, built this stage. `'clinician_google'` stays reserved and unused — a deletion DETECTED in Google and then CONFIRMED by the clinician in-app, the same "detect, mark, a human confirms" shape applied to deletion rather than drift, needing the sync/poll mechanism (PRD section 7) this stage doesn't build.

  **ONE SHARED `cancel_booking()` RPC for all three real actors (parent, clinician, director), authorization derived from WHO IS CALLING, never a client-supplied `cancelled_via`** — matching this schema's own established "never trust a client-supplied role/via value" discipline (the same lesson this session's own `derive_countersign_fields()` regression fix, above, already turns on). Notice-period is a WARNING the booking screen shows before the parent ever confirms — nothing in the database enforces it, since billing is explicitly out of scope and there is nothing else to gate.

  **DISCHARGE AS A SWEEP, NOT A CLIENT-SIDE CLEANUP STEP.** `end_clinic_episode()` is pure SQL with no way to reach Google, so a client-side cleanup step would only ever fire for whichever one path happened to call it. `sweep_bookings_for_ended_episodes()` (same shape as `fail_stale_pending_bookings()`, Stage 1) finds every future, un-cancelled booking whose own episode has already ended, cancels each with `cancelled_via = 'discharge'`, and returns what `/api/cron/sweep-discharged-bookings` needs to delete from Google — not yet wired into `vercel.json`, same inert-until-activated posture as the other two cron routes.

  **HOME = UPCOMING, PASSPORT = HISTORY — the PRD's own open question (section 11), answered from the already-established split (PRD 3): Home is "something is happening," Passport is record material.** `get_my_upcoming_bookings()`/`get_my_booking_history()` split mutually exclusively on `cancelled_at is null and session_end_at > now()` — nothing is ever double-counted or missing between the two. `UpcomingBookingsCard` sits on `parent-dashboard` right after Clinical Support; `BookingHistorySection` sits on `passport/dashboard` right after Clinical Team, and owns its own trailing divider so it never doubles one up when it renders nothing.

  **A REAL BUG, FOUND BY THE DISCHARGE-SWEEP VERIFICATION TEST ITSELF, NOT BY REVIEW — FIXED IN 0259.** `sweep_bookings_for_ended_episodes()`'s own `return query select * from public.cancel_booking_for_discharge(v_row.id)` selected `cancel_booking_for_discharge()`'s FOUR columns (`google_calendar_id`, `google_event_id`, `travel_before_event_id`, `travel_after_event_id`) positionally into a FIVE-column return type (`booking_id uuid` plus those same four) — Postgres lined up column 1 of the inner result (`google_calendar_id`, text) against column 1 of the outer declaration (`booking_id`, uuid) and refused outright: `"Returned type text does not match expected type uuid in column 1."` Every real discharge would have hit this the first time the sweep ever had something to actually cancel — it sat live and untested until this stage's own verification created the first genuine case. Fixed by naming every column explicitly, `v_row.id` supplying the one column `cancel_booking_for_discharge()` itself never returns. Confirmed live: the fix landed, a real diagnostic RPC call immediately swept the two real bookings left over from the earlier failed attempt (one carrying a real Google event, one a `sync_failed` row from an unrelated earlier test that also happened to sit on the same now-discharged episode), both correctly `cancelled_via = 'discharge'`, the one real Google event confirmed deleted.

  **VERIFIED, local dev server (`localhost:3000`) plus the real Google Calendar, fixture `ZZPRD9STAGE2` (director, practitioner with a real workspace email, two clinic clients each with a real parent guardian, a real cancellation policy set via the real director-only RPCs) — real HTTP requests against the actual API routes throughout, not RPC calls alone, using each fixture account's own real signed-in session (a Supabase auth session cookie constructed from a real password sign-in, sent as a raw `Cookie` header — the same format `@supabase/ssr` itself writes — so genuinely concurrent requests from two different real users could be fired via `Promise.all` from a single script, something a browser session alone can't do since one browser origin only ever holds one signed-in user's cookies at a time).**

  Daniel's three priority cases, each proven exactly as specified, before anything else:
  1. **Policy snapshot immutability.** A real booking captured "Policy A" as `cancellation_policy_snapshot` at write time; the director then genuinely edited the live policy to "Policy B" via the real RPC; the existing booking's own snapshot still read "Policy A" afterward, byte-for-byte, while a fresh availability call for a NEW booking correctly returned "Policy B" — proving the snapshot is real per-booking history, not a live reference that would have silently rewritten the past.
  2. **The forced all-or-nothing rollback, forced rather than hoped for.** An in-person booking request, deliberately made to fail right after its real travel-before Google event succeeded but before the session event was ever attempted. Result: the parent got a plain, synchronous 500 with a real error message (no spinner, no pending state); the row existed with `google_sync_status = 'sync_failed'` and no `google_event_id`; a Freebusy re-check across the entire candidate window came back completely clear — no orphaned event survived on the real calendar; and the booking reached the clinician's own `get_my_bookings_needing_attention()` queue.
  3. **First come first served, two genuinely concurrent attempts, not sequential, both surfaces.** (a) An ad-hoc "busy" event created directly on the real calendar, then a real booking attempt overlapping it — refused 409 with the standard message, via the Freebusy surface. (b) Two real HTTP requests fired together via `Promise.all` (each with an artificial delay widening the window between its own Freebusy re-check and its own row insert, so both genuinely believed the slot was free before racing the database) for the identical clinician and time window, from two different real parent sessions — exactly one won (a real booking, 200), exactly one lost (409), and the loser's message was byte-for-byte identical to the Freebusy surface's own message, proving a parent can never distinguish which backstop actually caught them.

  Then the rest: all three cancellation paths (parent, clinician, director), each producing the correct `cancelled_via` value and confirmed to have actually deleted the booking's real Google event(s), checked directly against the Calendar API, not inferred from the database alone. A negative control — an unrelated parent attempting to cancel someone else's booking — correctly refused with 403, the booking left untouched, and the real parent still able to cancel it afterward. The discharge sweep, proven end to end after the 0259 fix (above). And a full live browser pass through the real UI on the real dev server: the contextual "Book" link on `YourTeamCard`, the real 5-step flow (session type → slot, computed from real Freebusy data with real gaps matching the ad-hoc busy blocks created earlier in the same run → the live cancellation policy shown and agreed to → a real "Booked." confirmation), the Upcoming card appearing on Home immediately afterward with a working Cancel action, and — once cancelled — the same booking correctly appearing in Session History on the Passport page with the right "CANCELLED BY YOU"/"CANCELLED BY THE CLINICIAN" attribution and correctly absent from Upcoming.

  Every real Google Calendar event created during this pass (bookings, travel blocks, the ad-hoc busy block) confirmed deleted via a dedicated cleanup script before DB teardown — zero events left on the real calendar. Fixture torn down, zero orphans confirmed. The two ZZ-TEST-ONLY hooks (`x-zz-test-mode: delay:<ms>` / `force_failure`) that made cases 2 and 3 provable from one dev server run without restarts were removed from `src/app/api/scheduling/book/route.ts` immediately after verification, confirmed absent by grep; typecheck, lint, and a full production build all clean afterward.

  **Stage 2 closes here.** PRD 9 section 7's own sync/poll mechanism (detecting a clinician's own direct Google-side changes — drift, deletion — and reconciling them) was the obvious next piece — **explicitly HELD, per Daniel's own instruction, 20 Sept 2026.** He is having the booking flow itself redesigned (a brief is being written from screenshots, after flagging the current design as sub-par) — building a sync mechanism on top of a flow that's about to change is the wrong order. Not resumed until the redesign brief lands.

  **THE CLINICIAN-FACING CANCEL SCREEN — RECORDED AS OWED, NOT FORGOTTEN.** `cancel_booking()` has been proven correct for a clinician caller at the RPC layer (this stage's own verification, above) but has no dedicated client screen — a clinician today has no in-app affordance to cancel a session themselves, only the parent's and the director's own `/passport/book`-adjacent and `/principal/clinic`-adjacent surfaces got one. A fair, deliberate scoping call at the time (this stage's own recon named it and moved on), not an oversight — but real, and worth remembering rather than rediscovering. Likely home: alongside `get_my_bookings_needing_attention()`'s own existing queue on the clinician's dashboard, the same place a `drifted`/`sync_failed` booking already surfaces to them.

- **PRD 9 — THE THREE CRON ROUTES WIRED INTO `vercel.json`, THE HOBBY-PLAN SCHEDULE CORRECTION, AND THE PASSPORT REFERENCE CONVENTION CLOSED. 20 Sept 2026.**

  **THE WIRING, FINAL SHAPE.** `fail-stale-bookings` and `sweep-discharged-bookings` are the two real entries in `vercel.json`. `purge-app-events` deliberately stays OUT — pg_cron is its live mechanism (Daniel's own confirmation, given while scoping this piece of work — *"two are inert"*, meaning this third route is not), and wiring a second, Vercel-side scheduler for the identical job would be exactly the "two active schedulers doing the same job" confusion that route's own header has warned against since it was written.

  **CONFIRMED BY DANIEL: THIS PROJECT IS ON THE HOBBY PLAN, WHICH CAPS VERCEL CRON JOBS AT ONCE PER DAY. `fail-stale-bookings` CORRECTED FROM `*/5 * * * *` TO `0 4 * * *` AS A RESULT** — a five-minute schedule on a plan that only ever fires it once a day is configuration that does not describe what runs, which is worse than an honest daily one, per Daniel's own framing. `sweep-discharged-bookings` was already daily (`0 3 * * *`) and needed no change; the two are now staggered an hour apart, though nothing depends on that spacing.

  **`vercel.json` is strict JSON — it cannot carry an inline comment explaining the schedule.** The "why" lives instead in `fail-stale-bookings/route.ts`'s own header, the established location for this route's schedule rationale, and is restated here: `STALE_AFTER_MINUTES` (still 5) is untouched — that number defines what counts as stale once the cron runs, not how often it runs, and stays correct regardless of cadence. **The real cost, named rather than left implied**: a booking that fails between its row insert and its Google call can now sit ambiguously `pending` for up to 24 hours before this route reclassifies it to `sync_failed` and surfaces it to the clinician's own attention queue — not a lost booking (the parent already saw a plain, synchronous error the moment it failed, via the all-or-nothing write's own design), a DELAYED signal to the clinician, not a silent one. **Upgrading to the Pro plan is what unlocks the tighter, minutes-scale cadence this route was originally built for** — a known, named consequence of staying on Hobby, not something to rediscover the next time this file is read.

  **`CRON_SECRET` — exactly what all three `/api/cron/*` routes expect, confirmed by reading each one's own check directly, not assumed**: all three run the identical line, `authHeader !== \`Bearer ${process.env.CRON_SECRET}\``, against the `Authorization` header. This is Vercel's own native Cron Jobs authentication, not a custom scheme — once a `CRON_SECRET` environment variable exists on the project, Vercel signs every cron-triggered request with `Authorization: Bearer <that value>` automatically, no other wiring needed on the route's own side. **What Daniel needs to set, exactly**: in the Vercel dashboard, Project Settings → Environment Variables → add one variable named `CRON_SECRET` (that literal name — all three routes read `process.env.CRON_SECRET`), value a strong random string he generates himself (e.g. `openssl rand -hex 32` in a terminal) — the exact value doesn't matter to the app, only that it exists and matches what Vercel itself sends, which it does automatically once the variable is set. Scope it to Production at minimum (Cron Jobs run against Production deployments). **Neither wired route can authenticate a real trigger without this** — until it's set, both return a bare 401 and do nothing, silently, every time Vercel tries to call them. A redeploy after adding it is the safe way to be sure the running instance has picked it up.

  **THE PASSPORT REFERENCE CONVENTION — CLOSED. Daniel confirmed: the opaque, UUID-derived reference stays as built, no change.** `formatPassportReference()` (`src/lib/scheduling/passportReference.ts`) — a short, non-security, display-only string derived from the passport's own UUID (`A3F0-9C1B`, the first 8 hex characters uppercased and split into two groups of four), used as the only child-identifying text that ever reaches Google, in the real calendar event's own `summary` field (`Clinical Session - A3F0-9C1B`). Deliberately distinct from `passports.passport_code` (a real, live security credential). Reachable in one place, one pure function, if this is ever revisited.

- **SCHOOL TRIAL PREP — TWO FIXES BEFORE THE TRIAL, FOUND DURING THE SCHOOL-TRACK SMOKE TEST. DONE, MIGRATION RUN (0254), VERIFIED LIVE, CLOSED. 19 Sept 2026.** After five PRDs of clinic-only work (5 through 8), Daniel asked for the school track to be re-walked from scratch before the trial: a smoke test of all five roles end to end, a precise list of what changed since his own last full QA pass, and live proof from real principal/teacher/SNA sessions that a school sees nothing clinic-shaped. All three reports came back clean — the vocabulary translation held, onboarding/enrolment/incident/export all worked — but the smoke test itself surfaced two real, pre-existing gaps, both fixed here.

  **1. THE CLINICIAN TILE AT SCHOOL ONBOARDING LED TO A DEAD END, REMOVED.** PRD 5's own role picker (`SchoolStaffRoleSelectContent.tsx`) added a fourth "Clinician — Employed by or working through this organisation" tile alongside Class Teacher/SNA/Principal, leading to a "not set up yet" placeholder — institution-employed clinician self-service was never built (still parked, per its own dedicated entry above) and the trial school's psychologist is onboarded manually via `approve_clinician()` regardless. A real teacher or SNA signing up at the trial school would see four options and one would be inert, for nothing gained. Removed the tile and its placeholder branch entirely, left a comment pointing at exactly where to re-add it once that self-service path is real. Verified live on the deployed app with a fresh throwaway account: the role picker now shows exactly three tiles.

  **2. NARRATIVE COULD BE COMPLETELY EMPTY AT SIGN-OFF AND COUNTERSIGN, AND NOTHING EVER FLAGGED IT — THE MORE IMPORTANT FINDING OF THE TWO, IN DANIEL'S OWN WORDS "THE MOST IMPORTANT FINDING THIS WEEK."** Found by accident during the smoke test's own incident-logging step: a teacher signed off and a principal countersigned a real (fixture) restraint record with a completely blank "what happened?" field and no category, and the export screen's own honest "not recorded" was the ONLY signal anywhere that anything was missing. Traced to `incident_signoff_issues()` (0085) — read in full, its own header reasons at length about STRUCTURAL CONSISTENCY between related answers (debrief required vs completed, `anyone_injured` vs injury records, CPI ticked vs a restrictive-practice record) but never once considers CONTENT COMPLETENESS as a category of gate. An oversight, not a decision with a suppressed rationale — confirmed by reading every migration that touches the function's callers and finding no discussion of narrative/category anywhere.

  **Why "Missing Info" — the status that LOOKS like it should have caught this — structurally never could**, the sharpest part of the finding, Daniel's own words: `deriveIncidentDisplayStatus()` (`incidentDisplayStatus.ts`) checks `countersigned_at`/`teacher_signed_at` BEFORE `has_blocking_issues` — the moment a teacher signs off, the display status becomes "Awaiting Countersign" (then "Finished"), and `has_blocking_issues` is never consulted again for that record. "Missing Info" was only ever a PRE-signoff indicator of structural inconsistency in a draft, never a completeness check on the narrative, even by design.

  **THE FIX, exactly as decided: narrative blocks, category warns, both flow through the ONE shared function, not a second mechanism.** A new blocking code, `narrative_required`, added directly inside `incident_signoff_issues()` — this alone means `sign_off_incident()`'s own guard-trigger chain refuses it, `get_incident_signoff_summary()`'s `can_sign_off`/`blocking_issues` correctly report it, and SignOffCard's existing generic "Blocking sign-off" list renders it with ZERO client changes (it already renders any `blocking_issues` entry it doesn't specifically own). Category is deliberately NOT added to that function at all — a classification is not the record itself — instead surfaced as a new non-blocking `{value, note}` pair on `get_incident_signoff_summary()`, matching `anyone_injured`'s own already-established shape exactly, rendered in SignOffCard's existing "will not block sign-off" list.

  **Countersign does not need the identical gate — a structural argument, not an oversight.** `guard_incident_immutability()` (0085) freezes every `incidents` column except an explicit allow-list (`countersigned_at`/`_by`/`_role_at_time`, `updated_at`, the withhold columns) the instant `teacher_signed_at` is set — narrative is not on that list and cannot change between sign-off and countersign. So the moment sign-off requires a non-empty narrative, every incident reaching "awaiting countersign" from that point on is structurally guaranteed to still carry it — there is no code path for it to go empty in between. The only way an empty-narrative incident could ever reach countersign after this shipped is a LEGACY one, signed off before the migration ran — checked directly against production first, per Daniel's own explicit instruction, and confirmed: exactly one signed-off incident exists at all, and it already has both a real narrative and a real category. Zero legacy risk, so no duplicate check was added to `countersign_incident()` — that would have been a second definition of the identical guarantee, the exact drift risk this schema's own "one shared function, not a parallel implementation" precedent (0085's own header) exists to avoid.

  **THE FULL ADVERSARIAL SUITE, RUN BECAUSE THIS TOUCHES A NEW TRIGGER AND AN RPC, PER THE STANDING GATE RULE — AND IT SURFACED A MUCH WIDER FIXTURE-COMPATIBILITY SWEEP THAN EXPECTED.** Roughly 50 separate `create_incident_stamp()` fixtures across the suite had always relied on the OLD, permissive behaviour — creating an incident and signing it off immediately with no narrative, since nothing had ever required one — genuinely incidental to whatever each check was actually testing (debrief gates, attestation staleness, CPI consistency, countersign grants, ownership transfers, handover mechanics, none of them about narrative content at all). Every one of those fixtures got a real narrative added at creation, not a workaround to the new gate but the fixture finally doing what a real teacher would always have done. One raw `teacher_signed_at` update (CHECK K's own post-signoff link-rejection test) was missed on a first grep pass because it set both columns on a single line without the two-line format the rest of the sweep's own regex expected — caught by the live run itself, not by the static read, a small reminder that a full live run finds what a grep-based sweep can miss even when the sweep is thorough.

  **A red herring worth naming, because it looked like a live security bug and wasn't: a background suite run, started before every fixture fix had actually landed, showed an ungranted teacher successfully countersigning their own incident.** Investigated by direct reproduction in isolation (a fresh, minimal fixture) before concluding anything — `can_countersign_incident()` and the "Principal can countersign" RLS policy both behaved correctly in isolation. The actual cause: Node loads a script's full source into memory once, at process start; the background run had already started against the OLD, unfixed CHECK J fixture (no narrative on that incident), so the teacher's own sign-off attempt raised my new `narrative_required` exception and left `teacher_signed_at` still null — at which point the SEPARATE "Owning teacher can edit before teacher sign-off" policy (whose own `WITH CHECK` is just `owning_teacher_id = auth.uid()`, no column restriction) legitimately admitted the countersign-shaped write, since by its own lights sign-off had never actually happened. Confirmed by re-running the full suite fresh, with every fixture fix in place: the finding disappeared entirely (1154/1157). Recorded here as a genuine, if narrow, LATENT gap worth knowing about even though it caused no failure this time: if any FUTURE `incident_signoff_issues()` gate raises an exception (any of them can, not just this one) partway through what looks to the client like a completed sign-off, the owning teacher's own pre-signoff edit policy stays technically satisfiable for one more write, including a countersign-shaped one, until `teacher_signed_at` is genuinely non-null. Not fixed here, not asked for, and only reachable by someone deliberately racing a raw client update against a gate they know will fail — recorded for whoever next touches this policy.

  **THE 3 REMAINING FAILURES — A PRE-EXISTING STALE-CHECK FAMILY, NOT A REGRESSION FROM THIS FIX, BUT FIXED IN THE SAME SESSION ANYWAY, PER DANIEL'S OWN INSTRUCTION NOT TO CARRY THEM.** `CHECK S9b`/`T1`/`T8` (migration 0095, long predating this session) asserted the OLD clinician-visibility gate — `status <> 'draft'` / `teacher_signed_at` — that THIS SESSION's own PRD 8 Stage 1 (above) had already deliberately and correctly moved to `countersigned_at is not null and withheld_from_clinic = false`, so that withholding at countersign is a real first-exposure decision rather than a door already open (see that entry's own "THE TIMING FINDING" for the full reasoning). Neither fixture had ever countersigned, only signed off — under the new, deliberate gate they correctly got nothing, and nobody had updated them to match. The exact "a check encoding superseded behaviour" shape this file already documents for `DD-1a` and `NNN13` — a check that was correct when written, made stale by a later, deliberate decision. Daniel's own reasoning for fixing rather than carrying them: "three known-red checks in a suite of 1157 is how a real failure gets scrolled past, and the trial is exactly when you want a clean gate to mean something."

  **Rewritten to prove the CURRENT boundary directly, on both surfaces the gate touches — the notification table (S9) and the read RPC (T) — not just to make the old assertion pass.** Each now proves three things with a real `countersign_incident()` call, not inferred: nothing at sign-off alone (`S9a2`/`T1`/`T6c` — status leaving `'draft'` isn't the gate, `teacher_signed_at` isn't either), real content once genuinely countersigned and not withheld (`S9b`/`T8`), and genuine, permanent absence when countersigned WITH `withhold_from_clinic = true` (`S9e`/`S9f`/`T9`, both new — the withhold half of the gate had never been exercised by either check before, only inferred from PRD 8's own separate fixture). CORE (the indivisible B–U block both checks live inside) re-run clean: 313/313, zero regressions elsewhere. Fixture torn down, zero orphans.

  **1154/1157 on the narrative-gate's own final clean run** (fixture-per-fixture confirmed against the actual database via a dedicated targeted script, `zz-narrative-gate-verify.mjs`, 12/12, before the full run; checked production for legacy risk, zero; the full suite re-run twice more to separate genuine failures from a stale-in-memory-code artifact), **then 313/313 on CORE alone once S9/T1/T8 were rewritten** — the school trial's own trigger for this whole pass, closed. Rate limit raised twice for these two runs, per the standing gate rule — reverted to 30 by Daniel after each.

- **PRD 8 STAGE 2 — CLINICAL EXPORT, BOTH DIRECTIONS. DONE, MIGRATIONS RUN (0246–0253), VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE (58/58) AND A REAL BROWSER PASS ON BOTH ROUTES. CLOSED. 19 Sept 2026.** Section 7 — "the sharpest test of the ownership rule anywhere in this build," Daniel's own framing, because an export that cannot cleanly separate authorship makes the rule decorative rather than real.

  **THE ARCHITECTURE, ENDORSED BEFORE A LINE WAS WRITTEN.** Export is a client screen calling each table's own already-RLS-scoped read — never a single `SECURITY DEFINER` bulk-read re-deriving eight tables' authorization in one place, which would mean one mistake leaking everything at once. This inherits every access guarantee already proven, including the live-relationship rule and the withhold gate, rather than re-deriving them. Daniel's own words: "THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS RECON," recorded directly in `clinicalExport.ts`'s and the export routes' own header comments so it survives past this session.

  **TWO ARTEFACTS, NOT ONE — Daniel's own correction of his own initial ask.** He first asked for one combined download; recon found every option for bundling `window.print()`'s own real, selectable-text document together with attachments paid a structural cost (rasterized text, permanent component duplication, or the file ceasing to be a genuine PDF). His reversal: *"I was optimising a packaging detail at the cost of the thing inside it."* The document stays `window.print()`, unchanged; attachments are a separate "Download attachments" zip (`jszip`, new dependency), fetched via the exact signed-URL-then-real-bytes retry pattern `useAttachments.ts` already uses — a signed URL cannot survive being embedded in a printed PDF, so there was never a version of this that could be one download.

  **THE DIRECTOR-READ GAP, MADE ITS OWN EXPLICIT PIECE, BUILT BEFORE EXPORT.** Recon surfaced that four clinician-authored tables (`fba_reports`, `bsp`, `clinical_plans`, `assessments`) had no director-read branch at all — only domain-matching (a peer-colleague mechanism requiring the caller to hold their own `clinician_access`) or straight authorship. Daniel's own reasoning, given directly rather than left implicit: *"a clinical director is accountable for their organisation's clinical record. That accountability does not stop at their own discipline... Domain matching is a peer-colleague mechanism. A director is not a peer colleague."* Built as five new RPCs modelled on `get_session_notes_for_director()` (the fifth, `passport_clinical_content`, found the same way — its own clinician-read policy requires the caller's own `clinician_access`, which a director isn't guaranteed to have) — `get_fba_reports_for_director`, `get_bsp_for_director`, `get_clinical_plans_for_director`, `get_assessments_for_director`, `get_passport_clinical_content_for_director`. **A `clinical_lead` still reads nothing through any of the five — deliberate, the asymmetry Daniel named as "where it would quietly erode" if not stated plainly.**

  **THE SAME BUG, FOUND THREE TIMES, TWO OF WHICH PREDATE THIS STAGE.** The five new RPCs (0247/0248) never actually called `_clinician_authored_at_institution()` (0246's own time-scoped join helper — checks a clinician held an APPROVED `institution_staff` row at that institution whose tenure window covers the artefact's own `created_at`, not merely "ever held one") despite claiming to — an untensed join that would show a clinician's work at a PRIOR clinic to their CURRENT one. Building the fixture Daniel asked for — a clinician who moves clinics, with a real, precisely backdated FBA authored only while genuinely active at the first — caught it immediately: Clinic A's own director correctly saw it, Clinic B's incorrectly did too. Fixed in 0249, threading the artefact's own `created_at` through in all six functions (the five new ones plus the pre-existing `get_session_notes_for_director()`, which had the identical bug, live since PRD 6, entirely unrelated to this stage). **A third instance, independently found while extending attachment reads to directors**: `_clinic_director_can_read_clinician_material()` (0233, PRD 7 Stage 2) had the same untensed join, live in production on the BASE TABLE policies for `session_notes` and `assessments` directly — not a new RPC's mistake, a standing one. Fixed in 0251 with proper new-signature-first/drop-old-signature-after ordering (this schema's own documented discipline for a function signature change).

  **A REAL REGRESSION FROM EARLIER IN THIS SAME SESSION, FOUND AND FIXED IN THE SAME PASS.** Migration 0244 (this session's own PRD 7 "Silo 2 placeholders" work, closed and reported earlier) silently reverted `_caller_owns_artefact()`'s `'assessment'` arm to a stale, pre-0233 version — dropping both the live-relationship gate (`_caller_has_live_clinician_access`) and the director branch 0233 had added, for the ATTACHMENT specifically (the base `assessments` row itself was unaffected). 0244's own header comment claimed the assessment arm was "inherited wholesale" from the existing bridge; it wasn't. This reopened, for one artefact type's attachments only, the exact "ownership is permanent forever, even after discharge" bug 0233 was built to fix. Fixed in 0251 alongside the time-scoped-join fix, and proven closed with a real revoke: clinicianA's own `clinician_access` for the child was genuinely revoked mid-fixture, and their own previously-working signed-URL generation for their own uploaded attachment was confirmed refused immediately afterward — while the director's own read (a separate, accountability-based relationship, not the caseload grant that just ended) stayed unaffected. Access re-granted afterward for the live browser pass.

  **A COVERAGE GAP IN THE FIXTURE ITSELF, FOUND BY THE LIVE BROWSER PASS, NOT BY THE FIXTURE.** `get_child_incidents_for_staff()` (0166) gated purely on `has_child_access()` — class-derived (`has_class_teacher_access() OR has_sna_access()`) — with no institution-wide principal branch, the same "a principal is not on their own school's team listing either" shape this schema already documents (`get_passport_team()`, PRD 5 Stage 7). The school principal's own export screen called this RPC expecting the same institution-wide read `get_abc_logs()`/`get_passport_clinical_content()` already correctly provide; it silently returned zero incidents for a real principal in the live browser pass. **The fixture's own negative-only assertions ("clinic markers absent from the school export") were vacuously true against this empty result set** — 55/55 had passed with the actual bug live and undetected. Fixed in 0253 (the same institution-wide principal branch pattern already used elsewhere), and the fixture itself corrected to add explicit POSITIVE assertions (both incidents' own real narratives present, the school's own ABC filter including the teacher's entry, the clinic's own filter including the clinician's) alongside every existing negative one — Daniel's own instruction after this was reported: *"Any of those could be passing today for the same vacuous reason. Check each rather than assuming only incidents was affected."* All four paired checks (school incidents present/clinic assessments absent; clinic assessments present/school incidents absent; published guidance present/raw assessment absent; clinician ABC present in clinic export, parent ABC absent from both) were checked and found already correctly paired — the incidents gap was the only one.

  **A second, smaller live-browser-only bug in the same pass**: both export screens showed a blank child name, because `passports` has no policy granting institution staff a direct read of the row — the export screens were doing exactly the direct read this schema's own standing rule warns against ("roster-scoped child names always resolve through a dedicated RPC, never a direct or embedded `passports(...)` read"). Fixed in 0252 with a new narrow RPC, `get_child_name_for_linked_institution_staff()`.

  **THE FBA STANDING RULE, GIVEN DIRECTLY BECAUSE OF THIS STAGE.** The natural-looking move for the export screen's own FBA rendering would have been to reuse `FbaSectionsReadOnly` (the FBA reader/print's own polished component) — rejected because its own AFLS sub-section does a LIVE fetch against the CURRENT VIEWER's session (`useAflsAssessmentsForFba(fbaId)`), which would silently break for a director exporting a colleague's FBA (no director branch on `afls_assessments`, and extending it wasn't asked for, just to make an unrelated screen's borrowed component behave). The export screen instead renders `content_data` generically — humanised keys, JSON-stringified values, `afls` explicitly excluded — never reusing or touching any FBA component. Daniel made this a standing rule, not a one-off note: **"THE FBA IS NOT TO BE TOUCHED,"** recorded verbatim at the very top of this file under its own "Standing rules" section — no changes to its components, sections, rendering, save paths, or data shape, for any reason, ever, unless he says otherwise. If a future piece of work seems to need one, stop and say so rather than making it.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, deployed app, real signed-in sessions throughout, 58/58**, fixture `ZZPRD8EXPSCHOOL`/`ZZPRD8EXPCLINIC`/`ZZPRD8EXPCLINICB` (a school; two clinics; a director each; clinicianA, domain `cognition`, no overlap with Clinic A's own director, domain `communication` — the exact case domain-matching would hide; a `clinical_lead`; clinicianD, moved from Clinic A — approved 90 days ago, deactivated 30 days ago — to Clinic B — approved 10 days ago). One shared passport linked to all three institutions. Proved, in order: **Part A**, the negative proof itself — a clinic export for a child with real school incidents, both incidents' real narratives provably absent from every one of the six director-read RPCs' combined serialized output; the withhold gate re-confirmed via `get_clinician_incidents()` directly; a school export with the raw assessment's own interpretation text and every FBA/BSP/care-plan/session-note marker absent, published guidance (`strategy_school`) present, the home-only strategy (`strategy_home`) absent; the parent-logged ABC entry excluded from both the school's and the clinic's own export filters, the teacher's and clinician's own entries correctly included in each. **Part B**, all five director-read RPCs proven against a genuinely non-overlapping domain pair — a raw select refused (except `assessments`, whose base policy now correctly grants it directly per 0233/0251, confirmed as the one deliberate exception, and confirmed a DIFFERENT clinic's own director is still refused by that same policy) — and a `clinical_lead` getting nothing from any of the five. **Part C**, the time-scoped join proven with the real personnel move: Clinic A's own director sees clinicianD's earlier, genuinely-backdated FBA; Clinic B's does not, even though clinicianD holds a real, approved row there today — the exact case the naive (pre-0249) join would have gotten wrong. **Part D**, the practitioner toggle off (screen refused, underlying RLS unaffected) then on (own-caseload-only — clinicianA's own FBA included, clinicianD's excluded even with the toggle on). **Part E**, `record_clinical_export()` writing a real `activity_log` row with the correct actor/passport/event_type, and refusing a caller with no staff relationship to the named institution. **Part F**, both real attachments' signed URLs fetched to genuine byte-identical content, and the real AFLS assessor-name marker absent from every combined RPC result. **Part G**, the 0244 regression proof above.

  **Then a live browser pass on both routes, deployed app, genuinely fresh sessions.** The school principal's own export: both incidents present with their real, correct narratives (withheld only from the clinic, never from the school itself), exactly one ABC log (the teacher's, not the parent's), published guidance present, nothing clinical rendered anywhere — confirming the 0253 fix live, not just at the data level. The practitioner's own export (never previously checked live): FBA, BSP, care plan, the raw WISC-V interpretation, session note, and BOTH published-guidance rows (school and home — correctly, since this is the clinician's own material, not the school's) all rendered; "Download attachments (2)" produced a real "Downloaded 2 files." confirmation. The toggle-off refusal copy ("Exporting your own clients isn't enabled for your clinic. Ask your clinical director.") confirmed live by toggling off, reloading, and toggling back on afterward. Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule (none found).

  **Stage 2 closes here.** Per PRD 8's own sequencing (section 9): consent, the grant mechanism, incident flow + withholding (Stage 1), clinical export (Stage 2, this entry) — then the cross-organisation read audit, and the stagnation queue last. Not started.

- **PRD 8 STAGE 1 — THE GRANT MECHANISM (CLINIC → SCHOOL) AND INCIDENT WITHHOLDING. DONE, MIGRATION RUN (0245), CLIENT BUILT (THE COUNTERSIGN CARD'S WITHHOLD CONTROL), VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE (36/36) AND A REAL BROWSER PASS. CLOSED. 19 Sept 2026.** Stage 1 per PRD 8's own sequencing (section 9): the grant mechanism, resolving through a sibling chokepoint, plus section 12's withholding decision. Recon-first, per Daniel's own explicit instruction — and the recon changed the shape of the build.

  **THE INCIDENT-FLOW CHECK, THE THING THAT ACTUALLY SIZED THIS STAGE.** Section 4 names "organisation-to-organisation access grants — a clinic granting a school, and the reverse" as one item. Checked before building either direction, per Daniel's own instruction: does a clinic-engaged practitioner already see a school's incidents for their client today, and through what path? Yes — `get_clinician_incidents()` (0095) and `get_abc_logs()` (0190) both already grant any clinician holding a live `clinician_access` row full read of a child's incidents and ABC logs, zero institution-linkage check, zero grant table involved, live since the clinician track shipped. **"The reverse" needed no grant mechanism at all.** The only genuinely missing piece in that direction was section 12's own withholding decision, built as a small, targeted addition to the existing countersign path — not a grant, and not the "second half of a symmetric mechanism" the PRD's own section-4 phrasing implied.

  **THE TIMING FINDING THAT ACTUALLY DROVE SECTION 3's BUILD.** Section 12 reads: *"A principal chooses whether to withhold an incident from an engaged clinic at the moment they countersign it... Not a separate step, and not available afterwards."* That framing presupposes nothing is visible to the clinic before that one decision point. Traced precisely, not assumed: `derive_incident_status()` (0089) can move `incidents.status` out of `'draft'` as early as `attestations_requested` — often days before teacher sign-off, let alone countersign — and `get_clinician_incidents()`'s own gate was `status <> 'draft'`, satisfied at that same earliest point. `notify_clinicians_of_incident_signoff()` (0094) then actively notified the engaged clinician at teacher sign-off, earlier still than a passive query would need. Section 12's own "not available afterwards" was true of the WITHHOLD DECISION but false of the underlying visibility it was trying to gate — a clinician could already have read a serious incident's full narrative, and been told about it, for the entire multi-day attestation-and-signoff window before a principal ever reached the countersign screen.

  **TWO REAL DESIGNS FOLLOWED FROM THAT FACT, NOT ONE — RECORDED SO THE TRADE IS LEGIBLE, NOT BURIED IN A COMMIT.** Option B: leave the early gate exactly as it was, and read section 12's "not available afterwards" as being about the WITHHOLD FLAG only — a clinician who already saw it stays able to see it; withholding just stops it recurring for anyone re-reading from that point. Option A: move the READ GATE ITSELF to `countersigned_at is not null`, so nothing is visible to the clinic until the one moment section 12 describes, making the countersign decision a REAL first-exposure choice rather than a door already standing open. **My own first report recommended B — the literal PRD line, treated as settled — without flagging that the recon itself was better information than the document it was checking against.** Daniel's own correction, given after reviewing the build: *"You recommended B and built A, following the PRD line over your own finding. The finding was the better input — the PRD was written without knowing the exposure window existed. When a recon contradicts a decision's premise, stop and say so rather than building the decision as written."* Recorded as its own standing feedback memory (`feedback_recon_contradicts_decision.md`) — the same instinct that correctly flagged the FBA carry-over question and the BSP revision cascade trap earlier in this project, applied inconsistently here.

  **THE COST OF OPTION A, RECORDED PLAINLY SO IT READS AS A DECISION, NOT AN OVERSIGHT — DANIEL'S OWN WORDING.** A clinic-engaged clinician now sees NO incident until it is countersigned. Before 0245 they saw it from the moment attestations were requested, often days earlier, and were notified at teacher sign-off. That earlier visibility is deliberately removed so withholding at countersign is a real decision rather than closing a door already open. The cost is latency: a serious incident reaches the child's clinician when the principal countersigns, not before. **If countersign latency turns out to be days rather than hours in real use, this trade should be revisited.**

  **`cross_organisation_grants` — clinic proposes, parent confirms (section 3, explicitly decided, not relitigated).** Scope structurally limited to `fba_report` and `bsp` — a CHECK-enumerated allow-list (`scope_items <@ array['fba_report','bsp']`), never a runtime judgment call that could admit `session_note`/`assessment` by mistake later. `clinical_plans` (the Silo 2 placeholders, 0244) deliberately excluded from scope — it already crosses to school by default via type-level `default_school_visibility`, no grant needed, already shipped. `session_notes` and `assessments` (Silo 1 raw instrument data) never cross, full stop, per section 8's own table — excluded by the vocabulary simply never naming them, the same structural-guarantee idiom `clinical_plans.plan_type`/`attachments.artefact_type` already established, and the thing Daniel singled out from the recon as "the sharpest thing in this recon." A `before insert` trigger enforces the direction itself — `granting_institution_id` must be `type = 'clinic'`, `receiving_institution_id` must be `type = 'school'` — structurally, not by convention. Two partial unique indexes (`one_active`, `one_proposed`, both scoped to `(passport_id, granting_institution_id, receiving_institution_id)`) prevent duplicate live grants for the same triple while still allowing a fresh proposal once a prior one is revoked or declined — proven live, not assumed. No client INSERT/UPDATE policy on the table at all; every transition (`propose_cross_organisation_grant`, `confirm_cross_organisation_grant`, `decline_cross_organisation_grant`, `revoke_cross_organisation_grant`) goes through a dedicated SECURITY DEFINER RPC, matching `bsp`'s own "no INSERT/UPDATE policy, functions only" posture — each transition has real, distinct authorization (director-of-the-linked-clinic to propose; the passport's own guardian to confirm/decline; the granting director, with a required reason, to revoke) that a raw RLS policy on one table could only express badly.

  **`has_cross_org_grant_access()` — the sibling chokepoint PRD 8 section 5 asks for, resolved the way PRD 5 and 6 already resolved the identical tension against `has_child_access()` itself.** Section 5: *"A cross-organisation grant must resolve through `has_child_access()`, not around it... this build has found six functions that hand-rolled that logic and drifted."* `has_child_access()` backs 30 call sites, 3 of them write paths, and none of them should carry a branch relevant to exactly two tables. Daniel's own concession, given directly: *"I wrote 'resolve through `has_child_access()`' when what I meant was 'resolve through ONE place, not six callers'. A sibling function satisfies the real requirement and leaves 30 call sites and 3 write paths untouched. Build the sibling."* `has_cross_org_grant_access(p_user_id, p_passport_id, p_artefact_type, p_artefact_created_at)` is that one place — composed via additive `OR` policies at `fba_reports` (a genuinely new branch; that table had zero school-facing access at all before this, by omission, since 0040) and `bsp` (also new — until now only BSP's own extracted strategies crossed, via `passport_clinical_content`, never the document itself), plus folded into `_bsp_is_readable_by_caller()` so `bsp_strategies` inherits it for free rather than needing a second, independently-derived policy. Forward-only by default (section 2, decided): `p_artefact_created_at >= g.confirmed_at`, proven live both directions on the SAME grant — an FBA created before `confirmed_at` stayed invisible after the grant went active; a second FBA created after `confirmed_at` became visible immediately. Scope enforcement proven the same way: a BSP created AFTER `confirmed_at` — which would pass the forward-only check — stayed invisible because this grant's own `scope_items` was `['fba_report']` only.

  **Incident withholding, decided exactly once, enforced at the database, not by client discipline.** Four new columns on `incidents` (`withheld_from_clinic`, `_reason`, `_decided_at`, `_decided_by`), a reason required by CHECK constraint whenever withholding is true. `countersign_incident()` grows two new trailing parameters (`p_withhold_from_clinic`, `p_withhold_reason`) — old signature dropped first per this schema's own hard-learned `send_message()` rule, never a bare `CREATE OR REPLACE` for a new trailing parameter. The "not available afterwards" half of section 12 is a real structural guarantee, not a UI convention: `derive_countersign_fields()` (extended, 0090) auto-stamps who/when at the exact `countersigned_at` null→not-null transition, and force-reverts these four columns to their already-decided value on every write after that — proven live with a genuine tamper attempt (a raw `.update({withheld_from_clinic: true, ...})` from the same principal, post-countersign, who still passes the RLS `USING` clause: no thrown error, and a service-role read confirmed the row genuinely unchanged, the same "RLS-on-UPDATE silently filters" shape this file already documents, here produced by the trigger's own revert rather than RLS refusing the row outright). `notify_clinicians_of_incident_signoff()` moved from firing at `teacher_signed_at` to firing at `countersigned_at`, and skips a withheld incident entirely — left wired to the old point, it would have notified a clinician about an incident they then couldn't open until countersign (and, if withheld, never). ABC logs deliberately untouched by this section — no countersign step to gate on, section 2's "incidents flow" language names incidents specifically, and their own already-shipped default-share behaviour predates this PRD.

  **THE CLIENT HALF, PER DANIEL'S OWN TWO REQUIREMENTS.** `CountersignCard.tsx`'s confirm sheet gained a withhold checkbox, shown only when `get_countersign_summary()` reports `has_engaged_clinic: true` (no clutter for a child with no clinic involvement at all). Ticking it does NOT just toggle a bare label — it expands a Golden Brown block stating the real consequence in the child's own name ("A clinician working with [child]'s behaviour will not see what happened here. Once you countersign, this cannot be changed — reversed, or added later.") alongside a required reason field; the Countersign button itself stays disabled until the reason is filled, matching section 4's "requires a reason" as an enforced precondition, not a suggestion. Once countersigned, the decision renders plainly on the card afterward (a Golden Brown banner: "Withheld from this child's engaged clinician." plus the quoted reason) rather than being recorded silently with no visible trace.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, deployed app, real signed-in sessions throughout, 36/36** (`ZZPRD8SCHOOL`/`ZZPRD8CLINIC`/`ZZPRD8CLINICB`/`ZZPRD8SCHOOLB` — the last two wholly unrelated, for the negative controls). Baseline (nothing crosses before any grant); the direction trigger refusing both a school-as-grantor insert and a grant proposed to a clinic; authorization (a non-director clinician refused, a director of an unlinked clinic refused); the full propose→confirm lifecycle with a non-guardian confirm refused; forward-only proven both directions on one grant; scope enforcement proven with an artefact that would otherwise pass forward-only; an unrelated second school still refused; revoke (non-director refused, empty-reason refused, a real revoke with a reason succeeding and immediately closing access); a fresh proposal correctly re-raisable for the same triple once the prior grant is revoked; a second proposal genuinely declined by the guardian, granting nothing; the core fix itself — a teacher-signed-but-not-countersigned incident confirmed invisible to the engaged clinician, the exact case the old gate would have shown; reason-required refused; a real countersign (not withheld) making the incident visible immediately and producing a real `clinician_incident_notices` row; the tamper-attempt-after-countersign proof above; a second incident countersigned WITH withholding, confirmed invisible to the clinician, confirmed to produce NO notice row, and confirmed to report its own reason back through `get_countersign_summary()`; and `who`/`when` confirmed auto-stamped, not client-suppliable. **Then a real browser pass** on the deployed app: signed in fresh as the school principal, a genuine consent-screen completion (the fixture's own bootstrap-inserted `institution_staff` row had no `consents` row, so this incidentally proved `resolveOnboardingDestination()`'s "joined, not consented" gate still works correctly), then the real Countersign confirm sheet on a fresh teacher-signed incident — ticked the withhold checkbox, watched the Golden Brown consequence block and required-reason field appear, confirmed the Countersign button stayed disabled until the reason was filled, submitted, and read back the exact persisted outcome on the card: "Countersigned by someone (Principal) on 19 Sept 2026 · 15:31." / "Withheld from this child's engaged clinician." / the reason, quoted verbatim. Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule (none found).

  **Stage 1 closes here.** Per PRD 8's own sequencing (section 9): consent (done, this stage), the grant mechanism (done, this stage), incident flow + withholding (done, this stage, smaller than originally scoped once the incident-flow check landed), then clinical export (both directions), the cross-organisation read audit, and the stagnation queue last. Not started.

- **PRD 7, ITEM (a) — THE SILO 2 PLACEHOLDERS: CRISIS, SENSORY DIET, AAC, CARE, STUDENT SUPPORT. DONE, MIGRATIONS RUN (0244, THE FINAL OF THREE ATTEMPTS — SEE THE TWO ORDERING BUGS BELOW), CLIENT BUILT, VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE (26/26) AND A REAL BROWSER PASS THAT CREATED A GENUINE PLAN THROUGH THE REAL UI. CLOSED. 19 Sept 2026.** One generic `clinical_plans` table (`plan_type` discriminator), five `clinical_artefact_types` rows at `silo = 'framework'`, inheriting Stage 3's domain-tag machinery and the attachments bridge (0232) wholesale, per Daniel's own framing. Not "coming soon" cards — a clinician creates one today (name, date, free-text body, optional attachment) and it appears on the child's record, live, right now.

  **WHY THESE DO NOT LOCK, RECORDED WHERE A FUTURE READER WILL ACTUALLY SEE IT.** A real Postgres `COMMENT ON TABLE`, not just a migration-file comment: the attachment (when one exists) is the authoritative, already-immutable artefact; this row is metadata about it, and locking a pointer to an immutable file is theatre. When any of the five graduates to a built-in-full structured form, it inherits BSP's own lifecycle — draft, locked on sign, revision as a new row, never an edit. Written into the table's own schema-level comment specifically so whoever builds a structured crisis plan later on this already-existing unlocked table has no reason to miss that the lock needs adding at that point, not before.

  **REQUIREMENT 2 — THE ONE PLACE TWO RULES MEET AND COULD CONTRADICT — PROVEN, NOT ASSERTED, TWICE OVER.** Silo 3's "shareable by default" (these default `shareable`, unlike diagnostic assessments — the confirmed type default Stage 3's own recon already named as "BSP and plans shareable," finally attached to real rows) against Stage 2's "the attachment stays clinic-only, always." The two hold simultaneously because they're structurally two separate authorization surfaces, not one rule qualified by another: `clinical_plans`' own name/date/body is gated by a new `_clinical_plan_is_school_visible()` helper (folding a per-instance `school_visibility_override` over the type's own default); the attachments bridge's new `'clinical_plan'` arm in `_caller_owns_artefact()`/`_caller_can_modify_artefact_attachments()` is author-only, identical in shape to the existing `'assessment'` arm — no column on `attachments` inherits the plan's own visibility setting, because no such column exists at all. **Proven live against a real uploaded PDF**, not a hypothetical: the class teacher read the Student Support Plan's real body (byte-for-byte, via both the RPC and a raw table SELECT — a real, independent RLS grant, not just the RPC), got zero rows from `attachments` for that same plan, couldn't generate a signed URL, couldn't download the object directly — while the author's own signed URL worked and fetched the real bytes, confirming the refusal was real and specific, not a blanket bucket failure. **Proven again live in the browser**, differently: `ClinicalPlansSection` (the school-facing component) simply has no attachment-related UI at all — a teacher is never even offered the affordance to try, the same guarantee holding at the UI layer as at the API layer.

  **THE CRISIS PLAN'S OWN OPEN QUESTION, RECORDED AGAINST IT SPECIFICALLY, NOT SOLVED HERE.** PRD 7 section 16 names the real requirement: where does the worst-case plan live, and how does a cover SNA find it on a phone in three seconds? A row holding a name, a date, a free-text body and an attachment does not answer that — there is no fast-path surface, no "break glass" affordance, nothing privileging `crisis_plan` over the other four at read time; it sits behind the same Clinical Team tab and the same generic list every other type does. Recorded in the migration's own comment, next to the seed row, so whoever eventually builds crisis_plan's own structured form starts from the real requirement, not from this placeholder's shape.

  **"STATUTORY" WAS A FALSE LEGAL CLAIM, CAUGHT BY DANIEL DIRECTLY, FIXED AT THE SCHEMA KEY — NOT JUST THE DISPLAY LABEL.** The first draft seeded a `statutory_plan` type labelled "statutory (IEP and equivalents)." IEPs have never been statutory in Ireland — the EPSEN Act's own IEP provisions were never commenced. The real, current DES-inspected structure is the Student Support File under the NEPS Continuum of Support (Guidelines for Primary Schools, Circular 002/2024); the document within it is the Student Support Plan, at three tiers (Classroom Support, School Support, School Support Plus) — "IEP" survives only as informal shorthand at the top tier. **Renamed throughout, including the schema key itself: `statutory_plan` → `student_support_plan`.** Daniel's own reasoning for why the key mattered, not just the label: "statutory" sitting in the schema is a claim a future reader of the SCHEMA sees and could believe, independent of whatever a screen happens to say — a wrong key is a wrong claim that propagates every time it's read, copied, or extended, in a way a display-string fix alone would never have caught. `PLAN_TYPE_LABELS` (`src/lib/clinicalPlans.ts`) is the one place a `plan_type` gets a display label, matching `SOURCE_DOCUMENT_LABELS`' own established role — `student_support_plan` reads "Student Support Plan."

  **THE PPP — RECORDED, NOT BUILT, NOT A SIXTH TYPE.** The Pupil Personal Plan is a real Irish document for pupils with SNA access, genuinely relevant given the trial school is a special school with four SNAs. It may be what `student_support_plan`/`care_plan` already cover, or it may want its own type — not decided here. Recorded against `care_plan`'s own seed row as an open question for Catherine, not resolved by guessing either way.

  **TWO REAL ORDERING BUGS, BOTH FOUND BY POSTGRES ITSELF ON A LIVE RUN, NEITHER FOUND BY REVIEW — WORTH RECORDING AS A PAIR SINCE THEY PULL IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS.** (1) `CREATE POLICY`'s own `USING` clause resolves function references IMMEDIATELY, at policy-creation time, unlike a plpgsql function body (opaque until first called) — the first draft (0242) defined `_clinical_plan_is_school_visible()` AFTER `clinical_plans` and its own policies that call it, and failed outright: "function ... does not exist." (2) The fix (0243) moved that helper AND the read RPC ahead of the table — which broke the RPC instead: a `language sql` function's body IS parsed against real, existing objects at CREATE FUNCTION time (unlike plpgsql), and `get_clinical_plans_for_passport()` queries `clinical_plans` directly in its own body — "relation public.clinical_plans does not exist" on the second attempt, confirmed via live state to have rolled back completely, same as the first. **The actual constraint, only fully correct on the third attempt (0244): a policy needs the FUNCTIONS it calls to exist first; a `language sql` function needs the TABLES it queries to exist first — both true at once here because one function serves the table's own policies (must precede it) while a second function needs the table itself (must follow it).** Fixed by splitting what the second attempt treated as one movable unit: the visibility helper (queries only `clinical_artefact_types`, no conflict) stays ahead of the table; the RPC (queries `clinical_plans` directly) moves to after it, alongside the attachments bridge's own two helpers, which had the identical constraint and were correctly placed after the table in every attempt. Confirmed via direct live-state queries after each failed run, not assumed, that each attempt rolled back completely before writing the next fix — this Supabase SQL editor wraps a full pasted script in one transaction, contrary to an earlier, different session's own documented experience with a different execution path.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, deployed app, real signed-in sessions throughout, 26/26** (`ZZPRD7PLANSSCHOOL` + `ZZPRD7PLANSCLINIC`, a school principal/teacher, a clinic director, an author clinician, a domain-matched colleague, a non-domain-matched colleague, a parent). Creation across types with the FK+CHECK double-validation (`clinical_artefact_types` itself legally contains `'fba_report'`, refused anyway by `clinical_plans`' own narrower CHECK); the full requirement-2 proof above; a `clinic_only` override closing both reads (RPC and raw SELECT) the same way; domain-tag colleague read (a real match reading, a real non-match refused, both against the same row); write authority (a reading colleague's edit attempt silently touching nothing — the RLS-on-UPDATE trap, checked directly; the author editing freely, no lock, ever; no DELETE policy at all, even for the author, confirmed via a service-role read that the row survived the attempt); and the deliberate absence of any parent branch, proven both via the RPC and a raw table SELECT, matching BSP's own precedent. **Then a real browser pass** — an authenticated clinician creating a genuine Crisis Management Plan through the real "+ New Plan" picker, typing and saving a real summary (confirmed via service-role read immediately after), then a genuinely fresh teacher account reading the school's own "Clinical Team" tab and seeing all three shareable plans (including the one just created moments earlier) with their real bodies and correct type labels, with the clinic-only plan correctly and silently absent. Fixture torn down, zero orphans (including the real uploaded Storage object), checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule.

  **This browser pass is also where the deployment lesson above was found and resolved** — see "A DEPLOYMENT BEING 'Ready' IS NOT THE SAME AS IT BEING LIVE" under Verification environment.

  **Item (a) closes here.** Per Daniel's own ordering, the rejection loop is last — it needs real BSPs in real use to have anything to reject. Not started.

- **PRD 7, ITEM (c-PUSH) — A SIGNED BSP PUSHES ITS SCHOOL/SHARED STRATEGIES INTO THE CLASSROOM. DONE, MIGRATIONS RUN (0240, PLUS A TANGENTIAL 0241 FIX FOUND WHILE VERIFYING), VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE (36/36) AND A REAL BROWSER PASS. CLOSED. 18 Sept 2026.** Short stage, per Daniel's own framing — the point was proving an inheritance claim before building anything, not building something new.

  **THE CLAIM, PROVEN BEFORE A LINE OF CODE CHANGED, NOT REASONED ABOUT.** `bsp_strategies.placement` (home/school/shared) and `passport_clinical_content` were both already there; the question was whether pushing school/shared strategies into the latter, tagged `source_document_type = 'bsp'`, would work through every existing consumer for free. Traced, not assumed: `passport_clinical_content`'s teacher SELECT policy and `get_passport_clinical_content()` (0104) are both `has_child_access()`-gated and `source_document_type`-blind — a BSP row satisfies them identically to an FBA row. `EodWizardForChild.tsx`'s own `ratableStrategies` filter is `item.itemType === "strategy_school" || item.itemType === "strategy_shared"`, no source branch anywhere in that file. `get_strategy_effectiveness()` and `get_clinician_strategy_type_insights()` both read `passport_clinical_content` unfiltered by source. **Verdict: all five things Daniel asked to see proven — push with source_document_type='bsp', home never crossing, the class teacher's own read, the EOD wizard picking it up, the insights aggregate including it — inherited with zero code change.** The one honest caveat, not a gap: `bsp_strategies` has no `strategy_type_id` concept, so a BSP-sourced rating buckets into the caseload rollup's existing `'Untagged'` group rather than a specific category — recorded below as its own item, not fixed here.

  **THE REVISION TRAP — THIS STAGE'S OWN LOAD-BEARING FINDING, AND THE REASON THE OBVIOUS APPROACH WAS WRONG.** `finalize_fba_report()`'s own delete-then-insert (scoped by `source_document_id`) is safe in its own context purely because an FBA is never revised — the delete can only ever clear rows the SAME call wrote a moment earlier. Naively reusing that shape for a BSP REVISION — deleting the PRIOR plan's own pushed rows the moment a new version is signed — would have been a different operation wearing the same syntax: `strategy_feedback.strategy_content_id references passport_clinical_content(id) on delete cascade`.
