Deleting a superseded plan's own rows would silently destroy every "helped/partly/not" rating a teacher had already logged against them, at the exact moment a plan changes hands. **FBA was never at risk of this because FBA has no revision concept; BSP is the first document in this schema that can be superseded, and that is precisely what made the borrowed pattern wrong here.** The general lesson, stated plainly because it will recur: a precedent proven safe in its own context is not automatically safe in a new one — the thing that changed was not the code, it was what could now reference the row being deleted.

  **THE FIX: never delete. Gate visibility on the sourcing plan's own `status = 'active'`.** `_bsp_source_still_active(source_document_type, source_document_id)` — a no-op (`true`) for any row that isn't `source_document_type = 'bsp'`, so FBA rows sail through untouched. Applied to the parent's and teacher's own `passport_clinical_content` SELECT policies, `get_passport_clinical_content()`'s owns_passport/staff/principal branches, and `strategy_feedback`'s own teacher INSERT policy (closing the direct-write path the UI can no longer construct anyway, matching the "validate at write time, don't trust the UI" standard already applied to `assign_assessment_respondent()`, item (b) above). **The clinician's own reads — the raw RLS branch, `get_strategy_effectiveness()`, `get_clinician_strategy_type_insights()` — are all left completely unconditional, deliberately, per Daniel's own instruction: "we tried this in September, it didn't help, that's why the plan changed" is exactly the evidence that justified the revision, and narrowing it would remove the reason the change happened, not just old data.**

  **THE SELF-REFERENTIAL RLS TRAP — FOURTH INSTANCE OF THIS SCHEMA'S OWN DOCUMENTED FAILURE MODE, AND THE FIRST TIME IT WAS CAUGHT BEFORE SHIPPING RATHER THAN FOUND LIVE BY A FIXTURE.** A naive `exists (select 1 from bsp where id = source_document_id and status = 'active')` written directly inside `passport_clinical_content`'s own policy would run under the CALLING session's own RLS on `bsp` — and `bsp`'s own SELECT policy is clinician-author-or-domain-colleague only. A teacher or parent querying through that inline subquery would get zero rows back from `bsp`, every time, making the check silently always-false and hiding every current, legitimate BSP strategy from every teacher and parent, permanently — the identical shape that hit `clinical_lead_scope` twice (0207/0217) and `assessments` once (0233/0234), each time found live, after shipping, by a verification fixture. `_bsp_source_still_active()` is `SECURITY DEFINER` for exactly that reason, checked against the live pattern before a single policy was written to call it — not discovered afterward. Daniel's own framing: "the fourth instance, first pre-emption."

  **THE CORRECTION ON DESCRIPTION COMPOSITION — WHY, FIRST.** The original proposal left `why` unpushed, reasoning by analogy to FBA's own hypothesis fields never crossing. Daniel corrected this directly: an FBA's hypothesis is genuinely clinical ("maintained by escape from adult-led demands"); a BSP strategy's `why`, in the real document, reads "Rian benefits from predictability and clear visual information about what is happening now, what is finished, and what is coming next" — context a teacher acts on, not clinical reasoning to shield them from. Same word, different things. `description` composition, in order: `why`, then `how`, then `scripted_language`/`materials_and_setup` when present, then `caveat` last, newline-joined (matching `ClinicalTeamSection`'s own bullet-list rendering and FBA's own `\n`-joined convention). The caveat travels on ANY strategy that has one, school or shared, not just shared — Daniel's own correction to the original proposal: "if a clinician wrote it, they meant to convey it."

  **RECORDED, NOT FIXED HERE — THE STRATEGY_TYPE_ID GAP, FRAMED THE WAY DANIEL SPECIFIED, NOT AS A MISSING FIELD.** `get_clinician_strategy_type_insights()`'s caseload rollup groups by `content->>'strategy_type_id'`, a concept `bsp_strategies` has no equivalent of — every BSP-sourced rating buckets into the rollup's existing `'Untagged'` group. Nothing crashes, nothing is excluded, this works today. The correct framing, Daniel's own: **not "a missing field" but "a rollup that loses resolution as BSP adoption grows"** — the more classroom strategies originate from BSPs rather than FBAs, the more of the caseload-wide view collapses into one undifferentiated bucket instead of distinguishing anything. The likely fix is a `strategy_type` on the bank itself (`strategy_bank`/`bsp_strategies`), not a change to this rollup's own SQL. Not this stage's problem to solve.

  **A REAL, TANGENTIAL BUG FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, CHASED AND FIXED BEFORE CONTINUING (0241), MATCHING THIS SESSION'S OWN STANDING DISCIPLINE.** `create_bsp(p_passport_id, p_source_fba_id)` has always supported a standalone plan with no source FBA — the client's own picker (`ClinicalFileBspTab.tsx`) explicitly offers "none" as a real, selectable option, not a hypothetical. That path has been broken since 0238 shipped: `v_fba record;` is never assigned when `p_source_fba_id` is null (the `if p_source_fba_id is not null then select into v_fba ... end if` block simply never runs), and PL/pgSQL raises "record ... is not assigned yet" the moment any field of an unassigned record variable is dereferenced — which the function's own final INSERT does four times, all against `v_fba.content_data`. Every attempt to create a BSP without picking a source FBA has failed outright since the day this shipped, found only because this verification fixture was the first thing to have ever exercised that branch, real or automated. Fixed by explicitly assigning `v_fba` a null-`content_data` row in the no-FBA branch, mirroring what the real-FBA branch already does — nothing else in the function changed. **A correction worth recording about the fix itself, not just the bug**: the first draft of this fix wrote `v_fba.precursors` in the final INSERT, inventing a field the original code never had — the live function extracts `precursors` from the jsonb blob (`v_fba.content_data ->> 'precursors'`), not a standalone column. Caught before committing by re-reading the original expression directly rather than trusting a remembered paraphrase of it — the exact discipline this file's own "read the live definition, not the first one you find" rule already demands, just applied to a fix-in-progress instead of a stale migration.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, deployed app, real signed-in sessions throughout, 36/36** (`ZZPRD7BSPSCHOOL` + `ZZPRD7BSPCLINIC`, a school principal/teacher, a clinic director/clinician-author, a parent). Pre-sign state (zero pushed rows); the sign and its push (exactly two rows, school + shared, home never among them; description ordering proven byte-for-byte for both the school strategy — why/how/caveat — and the shared strategy — why/how/scripted_language/caveat; a `clinical_content_added` activity_log entry written); teacher, principal, and parent all reading the current plan's strategies, with the teacher's own zero-SELECT-on-`bsp` confirmed directly (the exact condition that would have broken a naive inline check); the EOD wizard's real write path (a teacher rating the pushed shared strategy); both insights RPCs including it (`school_helped = 1`, and the caseload rollup's `'Untagged'` bucket picking it up); the full revision sequence (a revision created and signed, the original correctly superseded, its own pushed rows confirmed to STILL PHYSICALLY EXIST via service-role read, the teacher/principal/parent all losing sight of them the instant the revision activated while the clinician's own read kept BOTH old and new, the teacher's EARLIER rating against the now-superseded row confirmed NOT cascade-deleted and STILL counting in both insights RPCs); and the hardened `strategy_feedback` INSERT policy refusing a raw attempt to rate the superseded row while a regression control confirmed rating the new plan's own current strategy still works. **Then a real browser pass**, signed in as the class teacher on the deployed app: "From your Clinical Team" rendered both strategies with the exact why→how→caveat bullet ordering live, unstyled from any test scaffolding; the EOD wizard's own optional fifth step ("Did you use any of ZZ's strategies today?") listed both by name; tapping "Helped" on the school strategy produced a real, immediate ✓ in the UI, confirmed via a direct service-role read afterward to be attributed to the REVISION's own current row, not the superseded one. Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule.

  **Item (c-push) closes here.** Per Daniel's own ordering: the Silo 2 placeholders next, then the rejection loop last (needs real BSPs in real use to have anything to reject). Not started.

- **PRD 7, ITEM (b) — THE PARENT/SCHOOL-FACING RESPONSE-SHEET COMPLETION FLOW. DONE, MIGRATION RUN (0239), CLIENT CODE BUILT AND DEPLOYED, VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE (44/44) PLUS A REAL BROWSER PASS THAT FOUND AND FIXED A REAL GAP. CLOSED. 18 Sept 2026.** Finishes what Stage 1 deliberately left half-built (this file's own Stage 1 entry, above): a clinician could record a response sheet, never send one, and the "Sent to parent"/"Sent to school staff" pills had been lying since the day Stage 1 shipped — nothing had ever actually been sent.

  **THE DECISION, Daniel's own: one assessment = one administration = one respondent. Column, not a table.** A clinician wanting a MAS from a parent AND a teacher creates TWO assessment rows — two genuine administrations, comparable the same way two WISC-V records are, not one row fanning out to multiple recipients the way `fba_instrument_requests` does for the FBA's own multi-recipient indirect-assessment synthesis. `assigned_respondent_id`/`assigned_at`/`last_reminded_at`/`instruction` land directly on `assessments`. Status (sent/in_progress/completed) is derived, never stored — from whether `assigned_respondent_id` is set, whether `responses` has any keys, and `completed_at`.

  **THE LEAK DANIEL NAMED, AND WHY THE RESPONDENT NEVER TOUCHES THE `assessments` TABLE DIRECTLY AT ALL — NOT THROUGH RLS, EITHER DIRECTION.** A respondent granted raw SELECT would also see the clinician's own interpretation, scores, and everything else on the row — silently wrong, a parent reading a clinician's interpretation of an assessment they're still contributing to. Fixed by never granting raw SELECT at all: `get_assessment_to_complete()` and `submit_assessment_response()` are the respondent's ENTIRE interaction surface, each returning or touching only what's actually needed. `assessments`' own RLS is completely unchanged by this migration — no new SELECT/UPDATE policy at all.

  **A SECOND, EQUIVALENT LEAK, SURFACED WHILE DESIGNING THE WRITE SIDE, NOT ASKED FOR DIRECTLY BUT THE SAME SHAPE — AND THE REASON A SCOPED RLS POLICY ALONE COULD NEVER HAVE CLOSED IT.** Postgres column-level GRANTs are per DATABASE ROLE, not per RLS POLICY — every caller in this app authenticates as the same `authenticated` role regardless of "kind" of caller. A raw RLS UPDATE policy scoped to `assigned_respondent_id = auth.uid()`, even with a column grant narrowed to `responses` only, would not actually stop the AUTHOR (admitted via their own separate policy, sharing the identical column-privilege surface) from writing `responses`/`subscale_totals`/`interpretation` with equal freedom either way — column grants can't be conditioned on which policy admitted the row. The fix is the one already used everywhere else in this schema a column needs to be writable by one kind of caller and not another sharing the same DB role: a SECURITY DEFINER RPC, never a raw RLS policy plus a column grant.

  **TWO CORRECTIONS DANIEL MADE TO THE FIRST DESIGN, BOTH BUILT AS STATED.** (1) The respondent writes `responses` ONLY, never `subscale_totals` — `submit_assessment_response(p_assessment_id, p_responses)`'s own signature has no parameter for totals at all; per 13a, the clinician enters subscale totals from the paper's own scoring key, the whole reason the app never computes them, and a respondent writing totals would defeat that. (2) The pills were lying — "Sent to parent" when nothing was sent and the clinician typed every row themselves is worse than no label. `AssessmentResponseSheetEditor.tsx`'s own manual-entry pills now read "On behalf of a parent"/"On behalf of school staff"/"Interview", honest for the case where `respondent_type` is metadata the clinician is asserting about their own transcription, never a claim that something was sent.

  **THE THREE THINGS FLAGGED AS "BUILD THESE, DON'T INHERIT THEM" — ALL THREE BUILT.** (1) `assign_assessment_respondent()` re-validates the candidate against `get_assessment_respondent_candidates()` SERVER-SIDE, at write time — `fba_instrument_requests`' own INSERT policy is only safe today because nothing in the UI ever offers a bad `recipient_id`; a raw insert with an arbitrary one would succeed there. This migration closes that gap for the new mechanism rather than inheriting it — proven live (Part B, below): assigning a genuine outsider is refused with "That person is not a valid respondent for this child," not silently accepted. (2) Auto-cancel on completion, matching 0199's own reasoning (a document that no longer accepts writes should say so, not fail silently): `submit_assessment_response()` raises an explicit, readable exception once `completed_at` is set — "This assessment has already been completed — your response is no longer needed" — rather than the "RLS-on-UPDATE silently filters" no-op this file already documents elsewhere. There's no separate `'cancelled'` STATE to transition through here, unlike `fba_instrument_requests` — a single-respondent design has no other request to cancel independently; `completed_at` being set IS the terminal state. (3) The recipient-scoped RLS shape is copied from 0040 EXACTLY, restated as the standard both respondent RPCs check: `assigned_respondent_id = auth.uid()` alone, no re-check of `has_child_access()`/current standing at all. Being named as respondent is sufficient, permanently, even after their access to the child ends. Proven live, Part E below — not asserted, PROVEN: a school teacher was assigned, submitted a real response, then had BOTH their `passport_access` grant AND their `institution_staff` row deactivated (full, real access revocation, not a token gesture), and could still reach `get_assessment_to_complete()` and still `submit_assessment_response()` afterward — while `get_assessment_respondent_candidates()`, re-queried in the same moment, correctly stopped listing them. The two RPCs are deliberately decoupled from access currency; the candidate list is deliberately not.

  **THE CROSS-ORGANISATION QUESTION DANIEL ASKED TO HAVE CONFIRMED, NOT ASSUMED — TRACED AND PROVEN, NOT JUST READ.** `get_assessment_respondent_candidates()` resolves the institution-staff arm via `passport_institution_links` — the CHILD's own linked institutions, plural, independent of the calling clinician's own institution — never through the clinician's own `institution_staff` row. `has_child_access()` is a purely school-side primitive (`has_class_teacher_access()` OR `has_sna_access()`, reconfirmed against the live 0130 definition before building this), so a clinic's own OTHER clinicians can never appear as candidates through this arm even when the same passport is also clinic-linked — they simply never satisfy `has_child_access()` at all, structurally. Proven live with a genuine two-institution fixture — a clinic where the assessment was authored, and a WHOLLY SEPARATE school where the respondent teacher actually works, the same passport linked to both: the cross-org teacher resolved correctly as a candidate; a second clinician at the SAME clinic, holding real `clinician_access` to the same child, did NOT; the clinic director did not; a genuine outsider did not.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, deployed app, real signed-in sessions throughout, 44/44** (`ZZPRD7RESPSCHOOL` + `ZZPRD7RESPCLINIC`, a school principal/teacher, a clinic director/two clinicians, a parent, an outsider). Candidate resolution (the cross-org proof above, plus the clinic-colleague/director/outsider exclusions); assignment (server-side validation refusing an invalid candidate, refusing a non-author clinician, refusing an external-record instrument, then a real assignment succeeding with `responses` reset to `{}` and `respondent_type` correctly derived); the SELECT/UPDATE leak (a raw client `SELECT` on `assessments` returned zero rows, RLS-silent; a raw `UPDATE` attempting to tamper `subscale_totals` touched nothing, confirmed unchanged via service-role read; `get_assessment_to_complete()`'s own returned object structurally has no `interpretation`/`scores`/`subscale_totals` key at all — not merely unrendered); submit (responses persisted exactly as sent, `subscale_totals` untouched); recipient permanence (the full sequence above); unassign (clears the three assignment columns, LEAVES partial responses exactly as they are, so the clinician can pick up where the respondent left off — the former respondent immediately loses all further access); remind (refused with a real message when there's no active assignment, succeeds and sets `last_reminded_at` when there is); and auto-cancel (refused with the explicit message, absent from both `get_assessment_to_complete()` and `get_my_assessments_to_complete()` — the dashboard prompt card's own query — once completed, and a reassignment attempt on a completed assessment refused the same way).

  **THEN A REAL BROWSER PASS ON THE DEPLOYED APP, which found and fixed a real gap** — the fourth time in this session a browser pass has caught something a fixture's own structural assertions couldn't. `AssessmentResponseSheetEditor.tsx`'s own candidate-name mapping had no fallback for a null `full_name` — `useFbaInstrumentRequests.ts` (the sibling hook backing the FBA's own recipient picker) already established `full_name ?? "Unknown"` for this exact RPC shape; this editor's own mapping had none, and a candidate with no name set rendered as a blank, unlabelled button in the assign sheet — confirmed live, not guessed, on a genuinely fresh fixture account. Fixed, typechecked, linted, built clean, committed and pushed alone (client-only, no RLS/RPC/query-shape change — shipped on typecheck/lint/build/live-verification alone, per this file's own standing gate rule). The rest of the pass confirmed the real, substantive mechanics live: the editor's honest pills and "Assign a respondent" button rendering correctly on a brand-new response sheet; a real assignment via the picker locking every response-row button (confirmed via `button.disabled`, not just visual styling) and showing "Not started"; the parent's own dashboard prompt card (`AssessmentRequestPromptCard`) rendering under "Clinical Support" with honest copy, opening the real `AssessmentResponseFlow` (numbered rows, the instrument's own scale, no item text); a real tap-to-save round trip confirmed via service-role read immediately after, and confirmed still showing on the dashboard afterward (correctly NOT disappearing on a merely-partial response, only on completion); the clinician's own view reflecting "In progress" and the parent's real answer live; Remind producing "Reminder sent. Last reminded 18/9/2026"; and Unassign correctly returning the editor to its unassigned state while the parent's one submitted answer stayed exactly as they left it.

  Fixture torn down, zero orphans confirmed, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule.

  **Item (b) closes here.** Per the ordering Daniel set (this file's own entry immediately below): push-to-school is next, then the Silo 2 placeholders, then the rejection loop last. Not started.

- **PRD 7 — WHAT'S LEFT AFTER STAGE 4, SCOPED AND ORDERED. 18 Sept 2026. ITEMS (b), (c-PUSH), AND (a) ARE NOW ALL DONE — SEE THE THREE DEDICATED ENTRIES IMMEDIATELY ABOVE. ONLY THE REJECTION LOOP REMAINS.** Four items named, none started at the time this entry was first written. Recorded here so the reasoning survives even if a future session picks these up individually rather than in one sitting.

  **Four items collapse to three stages.** (b) the parent/school-staff-facing response-sheet completion flow (Stage 1 explicitly left this half-built — a clinician can record a response sheet today, never send one); (c) pushing BSP strategies to school PLUS the rejection loop, split into two short stages rather than one, in this order — push first, rejection second; (a) the Silo 2 placeholder plan types (crisis, sensory diet, AAC, care plans, statutory).

  **THE ORDER, Daniel's own reasoning: (b) first.** It finishes something already half-built rather than starting something new, and it's the piece that actually replaces paper chasing in the real clinic. Then push-to-school (the first half of the old item (c)), then the placeholders (a) — "useful, blocks nothing" — then the rejection loop (the second half of (c)) last, because it needs real BSPs in real use to have anything to reject.

  **WHY (c) SPLIT IN TWO, NOT BUILT AS ONE STAGE, even though push-and-feedback are conceptually one cycle**: recon found that `bsp_strategies.placement` already existing, plus `passport_clinical_content`'s own proven extraction pattern, means the EXISTING `strategy_feedback`/EOD-wizard/insights pipeline should start working for BSP-sourced strategies "for free" once they're pushed — zero new schema for that half. But "should start working" is a claim, not a proven fact, and Daniel's own instruction was explicit: prove the inherited thing actually works before building the rejection loop on top of it — the exact same discipline that caught the clinician-role gate, the strategy-bank gate, and the assessments discharge case, all in this same file. If the EOD wizard doesn't cleanly pick up BSP-sourced strategies, the rejection loop's own design might need to change, and building it first would mean building on an unverified assumption.

  **THE ROLE OF STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS, AND WHY THE REJECTION LOOP IS NOT AN EXTENSION OF IT.** `strategy_feedback` (0055) already exists and already has a real teacher-facing capture UI (the EOD wizard's "Helped / A little / Not this time") feeding a clinician-facing AGGREGATE, never-individually-readable ranked view. That's an OUTCOME signal — it always presupposes the strategy was attempted. The rejection loop is an INFEASIBILITY signal — "we structurally cannot run this here," with a reason, meant to land as individually-readable clinical information tied to one child and one strategy, not folded into a caseload-wide count. Widening `strategy_feedback.rating` with a fourth value would bury exactly the signal that matters inside a table whose entire design principle is the opposite (counts, never individually surfaced). **Decided: a new, small table, reusing `strategy_feedback`'s own proven RLS shape (teacher writes, tied to the strategy's own `passport_clinical_content` row) but with real clinician-facing SELECT, which `strategy_feedback` deliberately does not have.** Not built yet.

  **THE SILO 2 PLACEHOLDERS (crisis, sensory diet, AAC, care plans, statutory) — ONE GENERIC TABLE, AND THEY DO NOT LOCK, BUT SAY WHY IN THE TABLE'S OWN COMMENT WHEN THIS IS BUILT.** A `plan_type`-discriminated table, name/date/free-text summary/attachment, five new `clinical_artefact_types` rows (`silo = 'framework'`), inheriting Stage 3's domain-tag/colleague-read machinery and the attachments bridge (a new `artefact_type` arm) — nothing new to design there. **No lock, no sign action, no supersede lifecycle — Daniel's own reasoning, which matters more than the answer itself**: in real practice these ARE signed documents, but the placeholder is not the document — the attachment is. The app's own row is a name, a date, a summary, and a pointer to the signed PDF that lives as a file; locking a pointer to an already-immutable file is theatre. **BUT this must be written explicitly into the table's own migration comment when it's built, not left implicit**: the attachment is the authoritative artefact, the app record is metadata about it, AND when any one of these five graduates to a built-in-full STRUCTURED form (matching how FBA/BSP work today, own fields, own editor, no longer just a name+attachment pointer), it inherits the BSP's own lifecycle — draft, locked on sign, revision as a new row, not an edit. Without that comment on record, whoever builds a structured crisis plan later on an already-existing unlocked table has no reason to notice the lock needs adding at that point, not before.

- **STEP 0 FINDINGS — AFLS, MAS AND QABF, WHAT EACH ACTUALLY STORES. A DECISION DEFERRED, NOT MADE. RECORDED, NOT CHANGED. 18 Sept 2026.** PRD 7's own Step 0 recon, done before any Stage 1 build work, applying the PRD's own literal test to each of the three existing implementations: *could a clinician administer or score the instrument using only what's on the screen?* Daniel's own instruction after this recon: leave all three exactly as they are — licensing is his to handle separately, not this session's to resolve or work around. This entry is the standing factual record of what was found, so it isn't rediscovered from scratch and isn't silently treated as settled either way.

  **MAS (Motivation Assessment Scale, Durand & Crimmins) — reproduction, unambiguous.** `fba_instruments` (migration `0045`) stores all 16 items verbatim — full question text, subscale (`category`: Sensory/Escape/Attention/Tangible), and the complete 7-point response scale. `QuestionnaireFlow.tsx` renders the instrument's own question text directly to the parent/teacher completing it; `instrumentScoring.ts` computes per-category totals automatically from the stored responses. A respondent completes the actual MAS start to finish inside the app; a clinician gets the scored result with no paper involved at any point.

  **QABF (Questions About Behavioral Function, Matson & Vollmer) — reproduction, unambiguous, further over the line than MAS.** `fba_instruments` (migration `0047`) stores all 25 items verbatim, subscale membership (Attention/Escape/Non-social function/Physical/Tangible), and the exclusion-aware `X/0/1/2/3` scale — including the "X" exclusion rule reproduced directly in `instrumentScoring.ts`'s own `hasExclusionOption()`. Same rendering and auto-scoring path as MAS. A complete, working reimplementation of the instrument, exclusion rule and all.

  **AFLS (Partington & Mueller) — also falls on the reproduction side, but a genuinely narrower case than MAS/QABF.** `afls_assessments` + `fba_instruments` (migration `0060`, item bank in `scripts/afls/afls_items.json`) store a 225-task item bank — task code, a short task description (e.g. *"Follows instructions"*, *"Remains calm when schedule changed, preferred items restricted, told No or made to wait"*), max score, and NA rules, organised by the AFLS's own domains. The source file's own header is explicit that it deliberately stops short of the full protocol: *"the full question/criteria/example text deliberately stays on paper."* No scoring criteria, no examples, no rubric — just the task title plus a 0–max tap-to-score control, rendered directly beside the item text in `AflsSection.tsx`. Many task descriptions are self-explanatory enough (*"Shoes on and off"*) that a clinician could plausibly score them from the app alone; others would still need the paper's own criteria to separate a 2 from a 3. A genuine grey area, not a clean pass — recorded as such rather than rounded down to "fine."

  **All three: left exactly as they are.** Not disabled, not changed, not removed, not migrated, not reconciled with the new PRD 7 catalogue (below). Their own tables, their own RLS, their own client code — completely untouched by this entry or by PRD 7 Stage 1's own build. This is a decision deferred to Daniel, not a decision this session made on his behalf.

- **PRD 7 STAGE 4 — THE BSP AND THE STRATEGY BANK. DONE, MIGRATION RUN (0238, a self-caught repeat of the subquery-in-DEFAULT mistake fixed before handoff, not after), VERIFIED LIVE (43/43, PLUS A REAL BROWSER PASS THAT FOUND AND FIXED A REAL ACCESS BUG), CLOSED. 18 Sept 2026.** Sections 1–10, 7a — PRD 7's own core, per section 2's own argument: build the bank first, because a document editor is a commodity and a clinic's own accumulated, refined library of what works is not. No PRD 7 document or sample BSP exists anywhere in this repo; the whole build is from Daniel's own verbal description, confirmed piece by piece across two rounds of recon before a line of it was written.

  **THE FBA GOT BETTER AS A SIDE EFFECT, NOT JUST THE BSP.** Recon found the FBA's own `hypothesisedFunctions` was a single whole-case narrative — a function is a property of a SPECIFIC behaviour, the whole point of functional assessment, and an FBA that can't say "refusal is maintained by escape; screaming is maintained by access to tangibles AND escape" was under-structured for its own purpose. Fixed by adding an optional `function` field to `TargetBehaviourEntry` (client-side only — `content_data` is unstructured jsonb, existing FBAs have none, nothing migrates). `current_frequency` deliberately did NOT get the same treatment — the real document's own "Current Frequency/Level of Behaviours" is a synthesis paragraph the clinician writes for the PLAN, not per-behaviour structured data, and Daniel was explicit this should never carry over, ever.

  **TWO REGISTRIES BECOME THREE ARTEFACT KINDS, VIA THE SAME SINGLE RESOLUTION FUNCTION BUILT IN STAGE 3 — NO NEW LOOKUP MECHANISM NEEDED.** `bsp` joins `clinical_artefact_types` as the schema's first real `silo = 'framework'` member with actual data behind it (`fba_calm_cards` established the category; `bsp` is the first row IN the registry itself). `default_school_visibility = 'shareable'` is the first real use of that value anywhere — Stage 3's own recon named it as the confirmed type default and it sat unused until this stage gave it something to attach to.

  **THE STRATEGY BANK IS DELIBERATELY OUTSIDE THE SILO MODEL ENTIRELY, NOT A FOURTH SILO.** `strategy_bank` and `bank_assets` are institution-scoped reference libraries, not per-child clinical artefacts — no `clinical_artefact_types` row, no domain-tag gating, no `clinician_access` chain. RLS closer in kind to `institution_tags` (director-scoped catalogue) than to `assessments`. `bsp` itself — the plan, built FROM the bank — is the real Silo-2 member, and gets Stage 3's own colleague-read composition (`_clinical_colleague_domain_match`) as a direct, mechanical extension, reusing the exact same generic helpers with zero new predicate logic.

  **THE COPY RULE, THE THING DANIEL NAMED AS MOST LIKELY TO BE BUILT WRONG, BUILT AND PROVEN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** `add_bank_strategy_to_bsp()` is a SECURITY DEFINER RPC that reads the bank strategy SERVER-SIDE at the moment of copying and writes every field directly — never a client-supplied echo of what it read. `bsp_strategies`' own INSERT policy refuses any client-supplied `source_bank_strategy_id`, so a row carrying real provenance is GUARANTEED, structurally, to have gone through the real copy path. Proven live, both directions: editing the plan's own copy afterward never touched the bank's original; editing the bank's original afterward (a director's own later "curates" edit) never touched the already-copied plan strategy. This is the same shape `finalize_fba_report()` already established (copy into a new permanent home, never a live reference) — the contrast Daniel drew against `episode_tags`' own deliberate move to a real FK (opposite direction, for the opposite reason — tags ARE meant to stay live-linked) is the right way to read why the bank wants the first shape and tags wanted the second. **Confirmed by Daniel as the right call, generalised: proving a copy rule in only one direction proves half of it** — the bank-doesn't-leak-into-the-plan direction is the one every obvious test reaches for, but it's the plan-doesn't-leak-into-the-bank direction (an editor accidentally mutating shared reference data through what they believe is their own private copy) that a reference-instead-of-copy bug would actually produce first in practice. Test both, every time this shape recurs.

  **BANK ASSET IMMUTABILITY CLOSES A GAP THE RECON'S OWN "COPY THE REFERENCE" ANSWER LEFT OPEN.** Copying a pointer (`image_asset_id`, `reference_asset_id`) is only safe if what it points at can never change underneath an existing plan — otherwise replacing a file reaches into every plan that referenced it through the back door, defeating the copy rule one level down. Enforced structurally, not by convention: no UPDATE grant on `storage_path`/`original_filename`/`content_type`/`size_bytes` at all, ever, on `bank_assets` — only `label` (a curation typo-fix) and `is_active` (retirement) are ever mutable, and only by the director. "Replacing" a First-Then board means uploading a NEW row; the old one stays, forever, for every plan still pointing at it.

  **THE LOCK, BUILT RIGHT THE FIRST TIME, NOT DISCOVERED AS A REGRESSION LATER — THE AFLS LESSON APPLIED ON PURPOSE.** Draft is editable; active is locked; a revision is a NEW ROW (`supersedes_id` points at what it replaces), never an edit to the signed one. `bsp`'s own UPDATE policy's WITH CHECK requires the row STAY `'draft'` after any ordinary client write — not just USING, which only gates which rows are touchable — so a raw client `.update({status: 'active'})` is refused outright by RLS itself, forcing every real transition through `sign_bsp()`, the only place that can also handle the atomic supersede side effect correctly (the prior plan is demoted BEFORE the new one is promoted, so the one-active-per-passport-per-institution unique index is never transiently violated within the transaction). `bsp_strategies` locks TOGETHER with its parent, deliberately UNLIKE `fba_calm_cards`' own exemption from the FBA's lock — a Calm Card only ever references an already-locked conclusion and never constitutes it; a plan's own strategies ARE its core content, and letting them stay editable after signature would make the lock on the plan itself meaningless.

  **ONE ACTIVE PER PASSPORT PER INSTITUTION, MATCHING `episodes_of_care`'S OWN ESTABLISHED PRECEDENT EXACTLY (PRD 5) — AND ITS OWN KNOWN EDGE CASE, INHERITED HONESTLY, NOT HIDDEN.** `bsp` needed an `institution_id` column no other Silo 1/2 artefact carries (`assessments`/`fba_reports`/`session_notes` are deliberately institution-less, identical across parent/school/clinic engagement) — a genuine, flagged departure from precedent, because Daniel's own instruction was specifically "per institution," not "per passport" alone. Nullable, for a purely independent clinician with no `institution_staff` row — and a standard Postgres unique index treats every NULL as distinct from every other NULL, so the one-active constraint does NOT actually bound an independent clinician to one plan at a time. Accepted as a known edge case rather than built around, since every real scenario this stage's own recon considered was clinic-team-shaped.

  **A REAL ACCESS BUG, FOUND BY THE BROWSER PASS ITSELF, FIXED BEFORE THIS STAGE WAS CALLED CLOSED — THE FIFTH INSTANCE OF THIS EXACT SHAPE, AND THE SECOND TIME THE BROWSER-PASS RULE ITSELF IS WHAT CAUGHT IT.** `/clinician/strategy-bank` was gated `useRequireRole("clinician")` — structurally unreachable by a `principal` (a clinic director), the exact role Daniel's own instruction names as the one who curates and retires. The backend was never wrong (`_is_director_of_institution()` correctly authorized the director's own UPDATE, proven directly in the fixture); the client gate was the only thing standing between the director and the page at all. Found by deliberately signing in as the director during verification, not assumed correct because the RLS was. Fixed (`useRequireRole(["clinician", "principal"])`), redeployed, re-verified: the director reached the page, saw the "Retire" control (absent for the non-director practitioner, confirmed by contrast), clicked it for real, and the retirement was confirmed via a service-role read afterward — the full loop, not just the fix landing.

  **THE PATTERN, NAMED PRECISELY NOW THAT IT'S FIVE FOR FIVE, DANIEL'S OWN OBSERVATION: every single instance was a CLIENT-side or ROUTE-level gate that predated a new role or capability. The database has never once been the thing that was wrong.** Not five different bugs — one bug, found five times, in five different gates: the Stage 2 inert-clinician-role finding, the `get_passport_team()` Stage 7 blocker, a third instance recorded alongside those two, the clinician-track specialty gate (`ClinicianAccessGate.tsx` + its own un-migrated dashboard duplicate), and now this one. This is a diagnostic heuristic worth applying BEFORE re-auditing RLS/RPCs the next time a properly-authorised person reports being unable to do something this schema's own policies already permit: check the client-side gate — `useRequireRole`, a role-keyed conditional render, a nav item that was never added — first, not last. The database's own track record on this specific failure family is 5 for 5 correct.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, deployed app, real signed-in sessions throughout, 43/43.** The same MDT-shape fixture as Stage 3 (director, author A, domain-matched colleague B, non-matching colleague C, all at one clinic; a fourth clinician D at a wholly separate second clinic) plus a real completed FBA with genuine per-behaviour functions, proved in order: the bank (upload a real asset with a real signed-URL byte-for-byte fetch, add a strategy, a non-director's retire attempt silently touching zero rows — the RLS-on-UPDATE trap, checked directly rather than assumed — the director's own real retire and restore); FBA carry-over (target behaviours WITH their new function field, triggers, setting events, precursors all copied verbatim; a draft/uncompleted FBA correctly refused as a source; `current_frequency` starting genuinely empty); the copy rule both directions; a domain-matched colleague reading a still-DRAFT plan (bsp's own SELECT policy never checks status, matching `assessments`); the cross-clinic guarantee (D refused regardless of domain, cannot even attempt the copy RPC on a plan they can't read); the lock (a reader's edit attempt silently touching nothing, a raw status flip refused by WITH CHECK, `sign_bsp()` genuinely locking it, post-lock edits and strategy additions both refused); the one-active constraint (signing an unrelated second draft for the same child+institution refused by the unique index); and a full, genuine revision cycle (seeded from the prior plan's own content AND strategies, signed, the original atomically superseded, both the original author and the domain-matched colleague still reading the superseded plan afterward, permanently). Then the real browser pass: the Clinical File's own new "BSP" tab correctly showing the active plan, its history, and the superseded original; the workspace page rendering every carried field (including the new per-behaviour function display) and correctly marking everything read-only once locked; the bank page correctly hiding the Retire control for a non-director and showing it for the director, whose real click produced a real, persisted retirement. Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule.

  **Stage 4 closes here.** Whether PRD 7 has further stages beyond the bank and the plan is Daniel's own call, not assumed either way — nothing past this is scoped or started.

- **PRD 7 STAGE 3 — THE ARTEFACT MODEL: SILOS, DOMAIN TAGS, SCHOOL-VISIBILITY DEFAULTS. DONE, MIGRATIONS RUN (0235, then a corrective 0236 for a real Postgres syntax error), VERIFIED LIVE (23/23, PLUS A REAL BROWSER ROUND TRIP), CLOSED. 18 Sept 2026.** Sections 11, 13b, 15 — the layer that makes every future instrument/artefact type consistent rather than decided case by case.

  **The silos: a property of the artefact TYPE, not a column stamped on every instance.** Two registries, deliberately, not one — `assessment_instruments` (already the correct per-INSTRUMENT registry, since MAS and WISC-V genuinely want different defaults; extended with `silo`/`default_domain_tags`/`default_school_visibility` rather than duplicated) and a new `clinical_artefact_types` (one row per TABLE-granularity kind — `fba_report`, `session_note`, `bsp` once built — same closed-vocabulary, select-only, Behaviour-Hive-controlled posture as `cpi_reason_types`/`discharge_reasons`). Forcing both granularities into one table would have meant mixing a table-name key with a per-instrument uuid key in the same column — an honest tradeoff, not a fully unified shape, flagged as such rather than smoothed over. `incidents` is a genuine Silo-3 shape but deliberately excluded — a structurally separate track, teacher/SNA/principal-authored, zero clinician involvement, already 100% school-side with no clinic boundary to cross and no clinician author for a domain tag to mean anything about.

  **ONE RESOLUTION FUNCTION, per Daniel's own explicit condition, not a design nicety**: `resolve_clinical_artefact_type(p_artefact_type, p_instrument_id)` is the only place that decides which registry to query, ever — `'assessment'` means look in `assessment_instruments` by instrument id, anything else means look in `clinical_artefact_types` by that key directly. Two registries is fine; two registries plus every future caller re-implementing the which-registry decision is the exact "nine independent lineages check institution_staff membership" shape this schema already has a standing entry against. Even the ONE place that already knows it's instrument-backed (`assessments`' own insert trigger) goes through the shared function rather than querying `assessment_instruments` directly a second way.

  **DOMAIN TAGS ADD A LAYER, THEY DO NOT REPLACE THE ACCESS MODEL — proven directly, not just asserted.** `_clinical_colleague_domain_match()` is ANDed onto the existing author-or-director predicate on `assessments`/`fba_reports`, never ORed as an alternate route in: it requires the caller to be a DIFFERENT, verified clinician with LIVE `clinician_access` to the SAME passport before domain overlap is ever even consulted. Verified with a genuine cross-clinic negative control: a real, verified, currently-standing clinician at a WHOLLY UNRELATED clinic — zero `clinician_access`, zero relationship to the child at all — declared an EXACT matching domain tag (`cognition`, deliberately, to make the test meaningful) and was still refused both the assessment and the FBA. A domain tag can never grant access to a child someone cannot already reach; this is that guarantee, proven with the sharpest case that could break it, not the easy one.

  **THE THING THAT WOULD HAVE BROKEN THIS ON DAY ONE, FOUND BEFORE SHIPPING — the structurally-inert-role shape, a third time.** Every organisation-verified practitioner has `clinicians.domain_tags = '{}'` today (their `specialty` itself is still the `'unspecified'` placeholder from `0222`, and until this stage nothing had ever prompted a change) — a naive `artefact.domain_tags && caller.domain_tags` AND-gate is false for literally everyone, and would have locked every practitioner out of every colleague's assessment the moment this shipped. Fixed by design, not patched after: the domain clause applies only when BOTH sides carry real tags; if either the artefact or the calling practitioner is untagged, the layer falls through to reachability alone — today's behaviour, unchanged, for anyone who hasn't declared yet. Honest about "not yet declared" rather than treating an empty set as a decision — the feature turns ON as declarations happen rather than breaking BEFORE they do. Proven as a REAL gate, not just a permissive fallback: once both a matching and a genuinely non-matching colleague declared real tags, the matching one kept reading and the non-matching one was refused — on both `assessments` and `fba_reports` independently, through the one shared helper.

  **THIS MADE THE SELF-DECLARATION SCREEN A REAL DEPENDENCY, BUILT IN THIS STAGE, NOT LEFT AS A LOOSE END.** `/clinician/specialty` now collects specialty AND domain tags together, in one screen, one submission — closing the `'unspecified'` placeholder loose end (recorded under PRD 5 Stage 2's own entry) as a direct side effect of building this, exactly as anticipated rather than deferred again. Self-set, no verification, matching `select_clinician_specialty()`'s own precedent — nobody needs to check a fact a person is simply stating about themselves. Reachable two ways: during onboarding, unchanged in shape (`allowBeforeConsent: true`, confirmed a genuine no-op for an already-consented return visit, not just assumed); and now, newly, on-demand from a "Specialty & Clinical Domains" section on `/more`, with a visible golden-brown nudge — this app's own attention colour, deliberately, because an undeclared practitioner genuinely is an outstanding task now — when `domain_tags` is empty. A real, previously-latent inconsistency surfaced and fixed as a direct byproduct of wiring this up: the specialty picker's own "Coming Soon" detour never checked `verification_route`, unlike the dashboard's own gate, so an organisation-verified practitioner revisiting this screen would have hit a needless "not supported yet" sheet despite never having been gated on specialty at all. Now skipped for `verificationRoute === 'organisation'`, matching the dashboard.

  **SCHOOL VISIBILITY — type-level default plus per-instance override, and one deliberate asymmetry that survives every level.** `assessments` gained `school_visibility_override` (nullable — null inherits the instrument's own `default_school_visibility`) as forward infrastructure only, not yet consulted by anything — the school-facing READ path is a real, separate stage, same posture Stage 1's own SELECT-policy comment already took for the director branch Stage 2 later built. FBA gets NO such column at all, confirmed correct rather than assumed: its own `passport_clinical_content.item_type` mechanism (trigger/setting_event/strategy_home/strategy_school/strategy_shared, chosen per-section by the clinician at authoring time) is already more expressive than any single type-level default could be, and this stage's own recon found that out — the original instruction ("FBA conclusions only") was read, correctly per Daniel's own confirmation, as "keep the existing mechanism, add nothing," not "give FBA a default too."

  **THE SHARPEST POINT IN THIS STAGE, DANIEL'S OWN WORDS: "uploaded files clinic-only ALWAYS — a raw report crossing is a deliberate act, never a consequence."** `attachments` is deliberately, completely untouched by this migration — no column added that a type-level default could ever reach, structurally, not by convention. A domain-matched colleague can now read a colleague's assessment interpretation/scores/subscale_totals but NOT any file attached to it — a real, known, flagged asymmetry, not silently resolved either way, because extending the attachments bridge to a new class of reader is a genuine additional decision nobody has asked for yet (the same caution `0233`'s own header already applied to the write side of this exact bridge). Proven directly: a domain-matched colleague who successfully reads the assessment ROW is still refused the `attachments` row and cannot even generate a signed URL for the file. The shape a real "share this file with the school" action would eventually need — a genuine `shared_with_school_at`/`_by` pair, set only by deliberate act, plus its own `storage.objects` read branch — stays exactly what `0232`'s own header already sketched: a sketch, not built here.

  **SESSION_NOTE SITS IN THE REGISTRY WITH NO REAL DOMAIN LAYER OF ITS OWN, DELIBERATELY, COMMENTED DIRECTLY AT THE SEED ROW SO NOBODY ADDS ONE "FOR CONSISTENCY" LATER.** `session_notes`' own SELECT policy is already STRICTER than any colleague-read branch would produce — author-with-live-access or the clinic's own director, no peer-colleague branch at all, for anyone, regardless of domain. Adding domain-tag gating here would not add a restriction on top of an open policy; it would OPEN a policy that is deliberately closed to every colleague today. A session note is a clinician's own write-up of an encounter — the raw material that FEEDS a Silo-1 analysis, never itself something a same-domain colleague is meant to browse. Enforced by absence (there is no peer branch on that table for a domain tag to modify), not a special-cased skip — the registry row exists only so silo classification lives in the same place as every other kind's.

  **TWO REAL BUGS FOUND WHILE BUILDING, BOTH FIXED, BOTH WORTH NAMING SEPARATELY (their own standing entries, above, in the Postgres-gotchas section) — a subquery-in-column-`DEFAULT` syntax restriction that isn't a logic bug at all (fixed with a scalar-returning wrapper function), and the verification fixture's own attempt to revoke a `clinician_access` row via a raw `.update()` — this schema's own "RLS on UPDATE silently filters" trap, hit again, this time by the verification tooling itself rather than the product: `clinician_access` has had no client-facing UPDATE policy at all since `0123` (write-only via `revoke_clinician_access()` and its two sibling RPCs), so the raw update reported no error while touching zero rows, and the test's own first run "failed" — correctly, since it was checking real persisted state — for a reason that was a fixture mistake, not a regression. Fixed by using the real production RPC, matching this file's own standing instruction to prove every write path with the actual mechanism, never a shortcut around it — including inside a verification script.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, deployed app, real signed-in sessions throughout, 23/23.** A genuine MDT case — three clinicians (A author, B and C colleagues) all holding real, independent `clinician_access` grants to the same child at one clinic, plus a fourth clinician at a wholly separate second clinic — proved, in order: day one (B and C, both undeclared, both still read A's tagged artefacts via the fallback); the cross-clinic guarantee, both before and after D declares a matching tag; the real gate once B (matching) and C (genuinely non-matching) both declare, on both `assessments` and `fba_reports` independently; the author's and the director's reads staying unconditional throughout; the attachments asymmetry; discharge interaction (A revoked via the real RPC, A's own read correctly ends, B's colleague-branch read — which never depended on A's standing — stays completely unaffected, the director's stays unaffected too); and the raw helper's own both-sides-empty fallback. Then a full browser round trip on the deployed app: a fresh organisation-verified practitioner (`specialty: 'unspecified'`, `domain_tags: []`, the exact real day-one state) saw the golden-brown nudge on `/more`, opened `/clinician/specialty` (correctly rendering the return-visit copy, not the onboarding copy), selected OT plus two domains, saved, was redirected back to `/more`, and the section correctly showed the saved summary — matching a service-role read of the same row exactly, `verification_status` confirmed unchanged (still `verified`, the route-aware reset holding). Fixture torn down both times, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule.

  **ONE UNRELATED FINDING SURFACED DURING THE BROWSER PASS — CHASED DOWN AND FIXED IMMEDIATELY AFTER, BEFORE STAGE 4, PER DANIEL'S OWN INSTRUCTION. See the dedicated entry below ("A REAL, LIVE BUG...") for the full root cause and fix — a trigger predating `verification_route` by seven months, never taught about the route it couldn't have known would exist.**

- **A REAL, LIVE BUG: THE ROSTER-ASSIGNMENT PATH GENERATED A CLINICIAN CODE IT WAS NEVER SUPPOSED TO — A TRIGGER, NOT EITHER RPC, AND SEVEN MONTHS OLDER THAN THE CONCEPT IT DIDN'T KNOW ABOUT. FOUND DURING A ROUTINE PRD 7 STAGE 3 BROWSER CHECK, CHASED AND FIXED (MIGRATION 0237) BEFORE STAGE 4 STARTED, PER DANIEL'S OWN EXPLICIT INSTRUCTION NOT TO LEAVE A SMALL CONTRADICTION UNRESOLVED. 18 Sept 2026.** A fresh organisation-verified practitioner, created live on the deployed app purely to test PRD 7 Stage 3's own domain-tags nudge, showed a real, non-null `clinician_code` under "My Clinician Code" on `/more` — contradicting both that page's own copy for `verificationRoute === 'organisation'` ("You don't need a code — your director assigns your caseload directly") and the standing design belief that the roster-assignment path (`bulk_grant_clinician_access()`'s `p_roster_user_id` arm, PRD 5 Stage 6) never produces one.

  **Neither RPC is the writer. `ensure_clinician_code_on_verify` (migration `0031`) is** — `before insert or update on clinicians`, firing whenever a row's own `verification_status` reaches `'verified'` with `clinician_code` still null. It predates `verification_route` by seven months (that column arrives in `0221`) and has no knowledge of it at all — it checks `verification_status` alone. `0031`'s own header comment is the tell, read in hindsight: it exists to guarantee a code "regardless of whether that happened via `approve_clinician()`, a direct Table Editor edit, **or any other future path**" — correct and complete as written at the time, but "any other future path" meant only "someone hand-edits the row," never "a second, deliberately code-less verification route arrives later." `approve_staff_join()`'s own clinic branch (`0222`) does `insert into clinicians (..., verification_status, verification_route) values (..., 'verified', 'organisation')` — its own SQL never asks for a code, but that INSERT is enough to fire the trigger, which sees a null `clinician_code` on a row about to be `'verified'` and mints one before the row is ever written. The RPC's own logic was exactly as designed the whole time; the bug was entirely downstream, in a trigger nobody thought to re-check when the second route was added.

  **This means the earlier "My Clinician Code" fix (same file, above) was built on a false premise — recorded there, not silently corrected.** That fix branched the DISPLAY correctly on `verificationRoute`, but its own justification — "an organisation-verified practitioner never gets a `clinician_code` at all, by design" — was never actually true; `clinician_code is null` merely happened to hold for every real organisation-route account that existed at the time, for a reason unrelated to the design intent (nobody had hit this trigger's own blind spot yet). The display fix itself remains correct and needed no further change; the premise underneath it needed correcting, which is what this entry and `0237` do.

  **Checked before fixing, not assumed: queried production directly for `verification_route = 'organisation' and clinician_code is not null` — zero real rows.** The only account this ever hit was this session's own already-torn-down Stage 3 verification fixture. No backfill needed — a pure forward fix, `0237`, teaching the trigger the one condition it was missing (`new.verification_route is distinct from 'organisation'`), leaving `approve_clinician()`'s own explicit generation and every other path into `'verified'` completely unaffected.

  **The standing lesson, generalised beyond this one trigger**: a trigger written to be a safety net for "any future path" is only actually correct for the paths that existed when it was written — a genuinely new path arriving later needs its own conscious check against every existing trigger/constraint that fires on the same condition, not just against the RPCs and RLS policies that are usually where this kind of review looks first. `0221`/`0222`'s own recon (PRD 5 Stage 2/6) swept RLS policies, RPCs, and client-side gates thoroughly, by this file's own account — a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger on the same table was the one thing that sweep never had reason to look at, because on its face it looks like generic plumbing, not a place a new column's own meaning could be silently ignored.

- **PRD 7 STAGE 2 — FILE STORAGE. DONE, MIGRATION RUN, VERIFIED LIVE (LOCAL DEV, REAL SESSIONS, 18/18). A REAL FINDING THAT CONTRADICTS THE ORIGINAL EXPECTATION, REPORTED EXACTLY AS FOUND, NOT SMOOTHED OVER. 18 Sept 2026.** Section 14. The first thing this app has ever stored as an uploaded document.

  **THE FOURTH INSTANCE, CAUGHT BEFORE IT SHIPPED THIS TIME, NOT BY A FIXTURE.** "A policy that looks up someone else runs under the caller's own RLS" — already hit three times (`clinical_lead_scope`'s write policy, its read policy, `session_notes`' own first-draft director-read policy), each time found live by a verification fixture rather than by review. Daniel's own instruction, given directly in response to the recon before any code was written: the storage bridge must go through a `SECURITY DEFINER` helper from the start, never a raw `EXISTS`. Built that way — `_caller_owns_artefact()`/`_caller_can_modify_artefact_attachments()` are the two sources of truth every other check (the table's own RLS, both storage-side helpers, the storage INSERT policy) composes from, never re-derived.

  **`attachments` — one generic table, `artefact_type` a closed enum plus a bare `artefact_id uuid`, NOT a per-type foreign key.** This deliberately trades away a database-enforced referential guarantee (Postgres can't express a single FK column pointing at different tables by discriminator) for one pair every future artefact type reuses without a new column. What keeps it safe instead: every helper requires the referenced row to EXIST and pass LIVE authorization — a dangling `artefact_id` just fails every check, the same practical outcome an `ON DELETE CASCADE` would have given by removing the row outright. `uploaded_by` tolerates a non-clinician (section 13a's own parent-uploads-the-paper-form case) — a column decision, made now; the INSERT policy stays clinician-only, Stage 2's own first and only caller.

  **A REAL BUG, FOUND BY THE VERIFICATION FIXTURE ITSELF, NOT BY REVIEW — the fourth-instance discipline caught the RLS shape, but a different, plainer bug slipped past typecheck/lint/build entirely.** `useAttachments.ts`'s own `upload()` never set `uploaded_by` on the `attachments` insert — the column is `not null`, the INSERT policy requires `uploaded_by = auth.uid()`, and the omission meant EVERY real upload through the real app would have failed with a bare RLS violation, "new row violates row-level security policy for table \"attachments\"" — a message that gives no hint the actual defect is a missing column, not a broken policy. Caught on the very first fixture run, before any deployed exposure. Fixed by fetching the caller's own id via `supabase.auth.getUser()` (matching `useRequireRole`'s own established pattern) and setting it explicitly. Daniel's own standing warning about this pattern — "the scores I entered disappeared" reads as flakiness and never gets reproduced — applies to this class of bug too: a silent, total upload failure with a generic RLS error is exactly the shape that gets reported as "attachments don't work" and closed as unreproducible if the fixture hadn't caught it on the first real attempt.

  **SIGNED URLS: five minutes, generated fresh per view, a real fetch-with-one-retry, never a bare `window.open(signedUrl)`.** `useAttachments.ts`'s own `fetchAttachment()` fetches the signed URL's content directly (so a failure — expired token, a dropped connection resuming after the window closed — can actually be detected), retries exactly once with a freshly-generated URL, and only then surfaces a real error. The comment beside it is explicit about why the retry exists — Daniel's own instruction, given because a future reader seeing "retry on 403" with no context would read it as covering up an auth bug rather than handling an expected, deliberately-short expiry.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, LOCAL DEV, real signed-in sessions throughout, 18/18** (`ZZPRD7S2CLINICA`/`ZZPRD7S2CLINICB`/`ZZPRD7S2SCHOOL`). **The real read, proven the way Daniel specified — not the RPC above it, not a service-role fetch**: the practitioner's own session generated a signed URL and `fetch()`-ed it directly; the returned bytes matched the uploaded file byte-for-byte. A different clinic's own clinician could not even GENERATE a signed URL (`createSignedUrl()` itself refused, before any fetch was attempted) and could not upload into the folder either. A school principal — never a clinician at all — got the identical refusal. A file over the bucket's own 25MB limit was refused by Storage itself, independent of any RLS check. **The signed URL, the real 300-second value the app actually uses (not a shortened proxy), confirmed genuinely dead after 310 real seconds**: an immediate fetch succeeded (200); the identical URL, refetched after waiting the real 5 minutes plus a 10-second buffer, failed with Supabase Storage's own `InvalidJWT` / `"exp" claim timestamp check failed` — the literal mechanism, not inferred.

  **THE DISCHARGE CASE — REPORTED EXACTLY AS FOUND, CONTRADICTING THE ORIGINAL EXPECTATION.** Daniel's own framing going in: *"Refused — because the record's authorisation ended, not because anything storage-specific changed. That is the whole argument for the bridge."* Built and run precisely: PA uploads a real file to a real WISC-V assessment; a genuine baseline confirms she can list and sign it; the director discharges the client for real, via `end_clinic_episode()` as her own session (the actual production path, not a raw update) — confirmed via service-role read that `clinician_access.is_active` genuinely flips false and the episode genuinely ends; PA, same session, attempts the same read again. **Result: still listed, still signable, still fetchable — not refused.** The reason, traced precisely rather than guessed: `assessments`' own Stage 1 SELECT policy is `clinician_id = auth.uid()` alone, with NO dependency on `clinician_access.is_active` — built that way deliberately, matching `session_notes`' own "ownership is permanent" precedent (an author keeps their own record readable regardless of what happens to the underlying relationship, the same reasoning that lets a departed teacher's own incident content stand unaffected by their own departure). The attachment bridge calls this EXACT predicate — `_caller_owns_artefact()`'s `'assessment'` arm is byte-for-byte the same check — so it faithfully, correctly inherits Stage 1's own design rather than introducing a stricter, storage-specific standing check of its own. **The bridge is not broken; it is working exactly as built. What's genuinely open is whether Stage 1's own "author-permanent" answer was the right one for `assessments` (and now, by direct inheritance, for `attachments`) in the first place** — a real, live question about a decision made deliberately in Stage 1, not a defect introduced in Stage 2. Recorded here rather than silently "fixed" by changing the bridge's own predicate, which would have been a unilateral policy change dressed up as a storage fix.

  **A SECOND, RELATED FINDING FROM THE SAME PASS, discovered live in the browser, not the fixture: the Clinical File page's own access gate and `assessments`'/`attachments`' own RLS DISAGREE about a discharged client, and which one governs depends entirely on which path a person takes to get there.** After the same discharge above, PA's own browser session hit "We couldn't find this passport, or you don't have access to it" navigating to the Clinical File (`/clinician/passport/[passportId]`) for that same client — a DIFFERENT, pre-existing gate (tied to current caseload standing, unrelated to PRD 7) correctly refused her. But navigating DIRECTLY to the assessment's own URL (`/clinician/passport/[passportId]/assessment/[assessmentId]`) — no Clinical File detour — loaded cleanly, attachment and all, because that route has no gate of its own beyond `assessments`' own RLS. The exact shape this file's own "the suite tests the database, not the journey" entry already names: a database-level check being true doesn't mean the page a person actually uses agrees, and here it's sharper than that — the SAME record is reachable or refused purely depending on which of two real, working routes someone takes to it. Not a security hole (nothing is reachable that RLS itself doesn't already permit) but a real inconsistency in what "access has ended" means depending on where you're standing, worth resolving deliberately once the discharge-case question above is decided, not before.

  Fixture torn down, zero orphans (including a manually-confirmed empty check on the actual Storage bucket, not just the database rows) — the first run's own orphaned Storage object (from the `uploaded_by` bug above, uploaded to Storage successfully before the metadata insert failed) was found and removed by hand first, since it had no matching `attachments` row for the teardown script's own storage-cleanup logic to find it by.

- **PRD 7 STAGE 1 — THE INSTRUMENT CATALOGUE AND THE ASSESSMENT RECORD. DONE, MIGRATION RUN, VERIFIED LIVE (LOCAL DEV 26/26, FULL ADVERSARIAL SUITE 1157/1157, AND THE DEPLOYED APP), CLOSED. 18 Sept 2026.** Built AROUND the three implementations in the entry immediately above, never on top of them — `assessment_instruments`/`assessments` (migration `0231`) share no table, no RLS, no client code with `fba_instruments`/`afls_assessments`/`fba_instrument_requests`, and the migration's own header says so explicitly, so nobody reads MAS/QABF/AFLS's existing shape as this catalogue's template.

  **`assessment_instruments` — the catalogue, Behaviour-Hive-controlled, same closed-vocabulary posture as `cpi_reason_types`/`discharge_reasons`**: no client write policy at all, select-only. Global, not per-institution — every clinic picks from the same fixed list. `record_type` is PRD 7 section 13b's three patterns (`response_sheet`/`external_record`/`built_in_full`) plus nothing else. Seeded with exactly the instruments THE FLOW section names — FBA (`built_in_full`), MAS and QABF (`response_sheet`, the NEW, safe 13a shape — item_count + response_scale only, no item text, no subscale mapping, coexisting with the older `fba_instruments`-based MAS/QABF the FBA's own indirect-assessment flow still uses unchanged), and WISC-V/ADOS-2/Vineland-3/BASC-3/CARS-2/ADI-R (`external_record`). FAST and IISCA deliberately NOT seeded — FAST because PRD 7 section 12 itself flags it as an unresolved licensing question of its own; IISCA because it isn't named in Stage 1's own flow section. Both are calls for whoever scopes them deliberately, not a default extension of this list.

  **Deliberately absent from the catalogue: any subscale/category structure.** "The clinician enters subscale totals from the paper's own scoring key" (13a, and Daniel's own Stage 1 brief) means the app holds neither an item-to-subscale mapping NOR even a pre-seeded list of subscale names — the clinician builds a free-form `(label, total)` list themselves on the assessment record. Keeps the catalogue clear of the instrument's own scoring structure entirely, not just its item text.

  **`assessments` — the record, keyed on `passport_id` not `episode_id`**, the identical structural reason `session_notes` (PRD 6) used it: a school-engaged or parent-engaged clinician never gets an episode row at all. Two shapes in one table (response_sheet fields vs. external_record fields), mutually exclusive via CHECK constraint, discriminated by a `record_type` column the client never sets directly — a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger (`_assessments_set_record_type()`) derives it from `instrument_id` and refuses outright if the target instrument is `built_in_full`, so an FBA can structurally never get a row here regardless of what any client code does. FBA itself never touches this table at all — picking it from the catalogue replicates `clinician/fba/page.tsx`'s own creation logic (same insert, same unique-violation handling, same `fba_started` activity log entry) scoped to the current passport, then opens the existing builder unchanged, exactly as the flow describes.

  **SILO 1 BEHAVIOUR — LOCKED ONCE COMPLETED, built into the UPDATE policy's own `USING` clause from this migration's first version, not added after a regression.** This is the AFLS lock regression's own lesson, applied deliberately rather than repeated: `0060` dropped `fba_afls_data`'s original completed-lock and it went unnoticed for months (see this file's own dedicated entry on that incident). `assessments`' own UPDATE policy requires `clinician_id = auth.uid() and completed_at is null` from day one.

  **SELECT is author-only, a deliberate Stage 1 scoping choice, not an oversight.** Daniel's own "NOT IN STAGE 1" list names school visibility explicitly and says nothing about a director's own read — unlike `session_notes`, which built its director-read branch as its own later, explicitly-requested stage (PRD 6 Stage 4). Do not add a director/school branch here by analogy to that precedent; it is a real, separate decision for whichever future stage asks for it.

  **External records: generic `scores` (free-form label/value pairs) and no attachment field or upload control at all.** File storage (PRD 7 section 14) is its own, not-yet-built piece of infrastructure — the editor says so plainly in its own copy ("Attaching the full report isn't available yet") rather than showing a control that doesn't work. Per section 13: start generic, add a structured per-instrument form only once Catherine supplies real output structures — nothing in `scores` migrates when that happens, a structured form is offered alongside the same column.

  **Response sheets: numbered rows (1..`item_count`) and the instrument's own `response_scale` as tap options, never item text.** Respondent type (parent/school staff/interview) is recorded as metadata; for Stage 1 the CLINICIAN enters every response regardless of who conceptually answered it — there is no remote send-to-parent/send-to-school-staff completion mechanism yet (mirroring `fba_instrument_requests`' own async request/notify/complete shape would be real, separate scope, not implied by "pick the respondent" in the flow description). Flagged here as a scoping call made, not a silent omission — worth a real decision if a future stage needs genuine remote completion.

  **VERIFIED LIVE, LOCAL DEV, real signed-in sessions throughout, 26/26.** A two-practitioner clinic fixture (`ZZPRD7CLINIC`) proved: the catalogue readable by any clinician and unwritable even by the director (9 rows, correct shapes per type); a MAS response sheet created, answered, and its subscale totals entered exactly as typed (never computed); a WISC-V external record created with generic scores and the clinician's own interpretation; the FBA branch replicating the real `fba_reports` insert and refusing correctly on an active-FBA unique violation, WITH the structural guarantee proven directly — a raw attempt to insert an `assessments` row against the FBA instrument refused by the trigger itself (*"This instrument opens its own existing builder..."*), not merely avoided by client code; the completed lock proven with a real post-lock tamper attempt (200, empty array, the exact "RLS on UPDATE silently filters" shape this file already documents — confirmed unchanged via service-role read, not trusted at face value) and a second confirmation that even the director can't un-complete it; cross-clinician isolation (a second practitioner with no access reads nothing and can't insert on the first's behalf); and zero rows created in `fba_instrument_requests`/`afls_assessments` as a side effect of any of it.

  **Then a real browser pass, local dev, same fixture**: the Assessments tab listed both records correctly with type/status badges; the locked MAS response sheet rendered all 16 rows genuinely disabled (confirmed via `button.disabled`, not just visual styling), no item text anywhere, the two subscale-total rows shown exactly as entered; the WISC-V's fields all correctly re-seeded from the database, its "Mark as Completed" button locked it live (confirmed via service-role read immediately after); the picker created a real QABF response sheet (25 rows, `X/0/1/2/3`), and a genuine tap-to-save round-trip persisted correctly, keyed by row number; picking FBA a second time correctly surfaced "An active FBA already exists for this child" (the fixture's own pre-existing draft), confirming the client-side branch's error handling matches the existing FBA list page's own. Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution code first.

  **THE FULL ADVERSARIAL SUITE — run with the rate limit raised to 300, 1157/1157 passed, all 81 blocks (B through SSS), zero failures, zero crashes.** Matches the exact count PRD 5 Stage 6 last recorded as the known-good full-suite total — nothing regressed from PRD 6 or from this stage's own new, isolated table. Rate limit reverted to 30 immediately after, per standing rule.

  **THEN THE DEPLOYED-APP PASS, real sessions throughout, service-role reads for persisted state, against `https://behaviour-hive-app.vercel.app`.** Every case named explicitly:
  - **The completed lock, enforced at the database, not the UI** — the practitioner's own real deployed session, after completing a MAS response sheet, attempted a raw `PATCH` directly against `assessments` (bypassing the UI, which has no edit control at all once locked) with a tampered `responses`/`subscale_totals` payload. `200`, empty array, no thrown error — the exact "RLS on UPDATE silently filters" shape this file already documents. Service-role read confirmed the row genuinely unchanged, not trusted at face value.
  - **An external record (WISC-V) captured end to end through the real UI** — version, date, administrator, location, two scores, and the clinician's own interpretation, all typed live and confirmed via service-role read at each step, then locked with "Mark as Completed" and reconfirmed read-only.
  - **A response sheet (MAS) showing numbered rows and no item text** — screenshotted live on the deployed app: "Row 1" through "Row 16", the instrument's own 7-point scale as tap options, nothing else. A live tap-to-save round trip confirmed persisted, keyed by row number.
  - **The catalogue rendering every instrument with its correct type, the form shape following from it** — all 9 seeded instruments listed alphabetically with the correct badge (`External record` ×6, `Response sheet` ×2, `Built in full` ×1), screenshotted live.
  - **A clinician at one clinic unable to reach another's assessment record** — a second, entirely unrelated clinician (Clinic B, never approved there, but genuinely signed in with a real token) attempted both a direct `SELECT` and a raw `PATCH` against Clinic A's own WISC-V record. Both returned `200`/empty — refused at the RLS layer itself, not merely hidden by a UI gate — and a service-role read confirmed the tamper attempt made zero change.

  **TWO REAL FINDINGS FROM THIS SAME PASS, BOTH FIXED, NOT LEFT OPEN.** Found while typing quickly through the external record's own Scores rows (label then value, typed close together): the second field's own save could read `assessment.scores` before the first field's own round trip had updated it, silently overwriting the first edit when the second one's write landed — a genuine, if narrow, data-loss risk on any array-shaped field edited via two sibling inputs in quick succession (`scores` on the external record, `subscale_totals`/`responses` on the response sheet, all of which shared the identical read-modify-write-off-a-round-trip-delayed-object shape). Fixed in both editors by introducing local draft state for these array fields — seeded once from the loaded assessment (same `hasSeededRef` discipline `SessionNoteEditor` already uses), mutated synchronously on every edit, independent of the network round trip — so a second edit's own patch is always built from the first edit's own latest value, never a stale one. Confirmed fixed with a genuine rapid-fire re-test (two sibling fields set in the same tick, no wait between): both landed correctly together. Second, smaller finding: the external record's `SavedStateIndicator` had `isDirty` hard-coded `false`, meaning "Saved" could show even for a value typed but not yet blurred — fixed with the same `changeVersionRef`/`versionAtSaveStartRef` discipline `FbaSectionEditor`/`SessionNoteEditor` already use, so the indicator now genuinely reflects an unflushed edit for the plain text fields. Both fixes typecheck/lint/build clean; the response-sheet's own tap-based fields keep `isDirty={false}` deliberately — every tap is already an immediate, independent save with no real "typed but unflushed" window to represent.

  Deployed fixture (`ZZPRD7DEPLOYA`/`ZZPRD7DEPLOYB`) torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule.

- **PRD 6 CLOSED — STAGES 2–4 VERIFIED ON THE DEPLOYED APP, ONE COMBINED PASS. 18 Sept 2026.** Every case Daniel named explicitly, real signed-in sessions throughout, service-role reads for persisted state, against `https://behaviour-hive-app.vercel.app` — not local dev.

  **The SHA/build-green caveat, stated plainly rather than glossed over.** This session has no GitHub or Vercel API credential (`gh` isn't installed, no token in the environment, no `.vercel` project link) — there was no way to query a commit's own check-run status or Vercel's own build log directly. What's actually confirmed: `origin/main` is at `af53a461600c4d16731a3469f20a6b365e87203d` (the Stage 4 verification-record commit; the last commit touching application code is `29c79ef`, Stage 4's own build, which passed a clean local `npm run build` before it was pushed). The deploy itself was confirmed functionally rather than by reading a dashboard: Stage 2's "Session Notes" tab, Stage 3's parent-facing section and activity events, and Stage 4's director-only tab and RPC all rendered and behaved correctly live on the production URL — none of this code existed before this session, so its presence and correct behaviour on the deployed app is itself proof the deploy is current at least through `29c79ef`. Worth naming as a real gap for next time: this session could not independently confirm a green Vercel check the way a `gh`/Vercel-CLI-equipped session could.

Three notes written live through the actual deployed editor for the clinic client (not seeded via script) — one unshared, one shared-then-parent-note-edited, one shared-then-clinical-record-edited — plus one written live by SC1 for the school client.

  **Every case proven, in Daniel's own order:**
