At 1280px, Clinical Record and Parent Note render as two genuine mounted columns with the sidebar present; at 375px they stack vertically, both fields fully present, reachable by scroll, correctly re-seeding from the database on a hard reload (proving persistence, not local-only state).
  - **The copy toggle proven not a link** — typed into Clinical Record, clicked "Copy into parent note →" (confirmed both fields held identical text), then edited Clinical Record further and blurred. Service-role read afterward: `clinical_record` carries the new edit, `parent_note` still holds exactly the originally-copied text, untouched.
  - **Sharing, and the parent seeing it** — shared two notes live through the real confirm sheet (which previewed the exact parent-note text before committing). Signed in as the real parent guardian: `parent-dashboard/activity` showed both `session_note_shared` events with correct copy; `passport/dashboard` showed both notes in full, `clinical_record` structurally absent from either.
  - **Editing the parent note after sharing** — edited Note 2's `parent_note` as the practitioner. Service-role read confirmed `parent_note_edited_after_share_at` set and a real `session_note_updated` activity_log row. As the parent: the note carried the golden-brown "Updated 18 Sept 2026" marker with the corrected text.
  - **Editing the clinical record after sharing** — edited Note 3's `clinical_record` only. Service-role read confirmed `parent_note_edited_after_share_at` stayed null and NO `session_note_updated` row was created for this edit (only the one from the case above). As the parent: Note 3 showed no marker at all and its original, unedited `parent_note` text.

Then the stronger proof: extracted the director's own live session token from the deployed app's own auth cookie and attempted a raw `PATCH` against `session_notes` directly (bypassing the UI entirely, which has no edit control at all) — `200`, empty array, no thrown error, the exact "RLS on UPDATE silently filters" shape this file already documents. Service-role read confirmed the row genuinely unchanged — the silent refusal wasn't trusted at face value.

Then, using the same token-extraction technique, called `get_session_notes_for_director()` directly AND a raw `select` against `session_notes` directly, both as the school principal's own real session: both returned `200`/empty, proving the `inst.type = 'clinic'` check closes this leak at both the RPC layer and the base table's own RLS, not merely by hiding a tab client-side.

Edited a field, blurred: the indicator showed "Offline" (`aria-label`: "Waiting for connection — tap to retry saving"). Service-role read confirmed the edit had NOT persisted while blocked. Restored `navigator.onLine = true`, unblocked `fetch`, dispatched a real `online` event: the save queue resumed on its own (no manual retry tap needed) and the indicator returned to "Saved".   **PRD 6 closes here.** All four stages done, migrations run, verified live both locally and on the deployed app. The `clinical_lead` reading surface remains deferred, its own recorded item (below) — not part of PRD 6's own scope as closed. Next: PRD 7, blocked on Catherine's own instrument output structures — not started.

- **THE CLINICIAN-TRACK SPECIALTY GATE — A LIVE PRODUCTION BUG, FOUND ONLY BY SIGNING IN AS THE ROLE IT LOCKED OUT. FOUND DURING PRD 6 STAGE 2 BROWSER VERIFICATION, FIXED. 18 Sept 2026.** `ClinicianAccessGate.tsx` — which wraps the dashboard, the caseload list, messages, insights, log, FBA, and (as of this stage) the Clinical File and Session Notes — refused access to anyone whose `clinicians.specialty !== 'behavioural_psychologist'`, regardless of verification status. `clinician/dashboard/page.tsx` carried its own separate, never-migrated COPY of the identical logic (despite `useClinicianReviewState`'s own header comment claiming to have been "extracted from" this file) — including a SECOND instance of the same specialty check gating whether dashboard stats load at all. Both were written when the independent, `behaviour_hive`-reviewed path was the only path, and `behavioural_psychologist` was the one specialty that verification form actually supported. Neither was ever updated when PRD 5 Stage 6 added the organisation-verified path — a director-approved clinic practitioner, whose `specialty` starts at `'unspecified'` BY DESIGN and is never something anyone is asked to pick before being let in, because their director's own approval already is their verification.

  **The consequence: every organisation-verified clinic practitioner — the entire population PRD 6 is built for — was completely locked out of the ENTIRE clinician track**, seeing "Verification for this clinical role is currently in development... working closely with regulatory bodies..." on literally every page, despite being fully, correctly verified at the database level. `is_verified_clinician()`, `clinician_access`, every RLS policy and RPC gating clinician-track data all worked exactly as designed — the block was purely client-side, in a gate two migrations and zero browser sessions never touched.

  **THE REASON IT SURVIVED IS THE MORE VALUABLE HALF OF THIS FINDING.** Stage 6's own verification — 46/46, thorough, real signed-in sessions throughout — went entirely through RPCs and direct table calls. It never opened a browser. The database said the practitioner could reach everything; the app never let them try. This is Daniel's own count, and it should stand as recorded: **the fourth time across PRDs 5 and 6 that a check correct on its own terms refused a properly authorised caller because it encoded a premise that is false for clinics** — the same shape as the Stage 2 inert-clinician-role finding (above) and the `get_passport_team()` Stage 7 blocker (above), both already recorded here, now joined by this one and its own root cause being purely client-side rather than a database policy.

  **THE STANDING RULE THIS EARNS, addition to the existing pattern rather than a new one: for any new ROLE, verification must include signing in as that role in a real browser and reaching the surfaces it is meant to use — not the RPCs behind them.** A role that passes every database check and is locked out by a client-side gate is still a role that does not work. This is the client-side twin of this file's own "prove a write policy with a real session doing the real operation" rule — that rule closes the gap between "the RPC exists" and "a real session can call it"; this one closes the gap between "the RPC and the RLS both work" and "a real person can ever reach the screen that calls them." Neither is provable from the other.

  **THE FIX**: both instances (`ClinicianAccessGate.tsx`, and the dashboard's own separate copy, both its render gate AND its stats-loading guard) now skip the specialty check entirely when `verification_route === 'organisation'` — the director's approval already stands in for the credential check this gate exists to enforce, exactly Stage 6's own reasoning, applied to the one place it was never carried through. `useClinicianReviewState`'s own `ClinicianReviewProfile` gained `verificationRoute`, propagating the fix to every one of its nine call sites at the source; only the dashboard's own un-shared duplicate needed a second, parallel fix.

  **THE SWEEP, per instruction — report what's found, not fix it all:**
  - `src/lib/hasJoined.ts` — checked directly, already correct: its own `'clinician'` branch is an OR across BOTH paths (a real `institution_staff` row, or a set `clinicians.specialty`), with its own header comment already explaining why neither alone would suffice. No bug.
  - `src/app/clinician/specialty/page.tsx` — the independent path's OWN onboarding screen, reachable only via `allowBeforeConsent: true` from the "I don't have a code" / "I'm an independent clinician" route. An organisation-verified practitioner's real onboarding (institution code → self-link → `approve_staff_join()`) never routes through this page at all. Correctly scoped as-is, not a bug.
  - **`src/app/more/page.tsx`, "My Clinician Code" — FIXED, 18 Sept 2026, same day, per Daniel's own instruction not to carry it as a reported finding. THIS FIX WAS BUILT ON A FALSE PREMISE, CORRECTED SIX DAYS LATER — see the dedicated entry below ("A REAL, LIVE BUG...").** Was: "Your code will appear here once your credentials are verified" whenever `clinician_code` is null — assumed permanently false for an organisation-verified practitioner, on the belief that this route never generates a `clinician_code` at all (caseload arrives via the roster path, not a code to hand out), by design. Branched on `verificationRoute`: `'organisation'` shows "You don't need a code — your director assigns your caseload directly." The COPY fix was correct and is unchanged; the PREMISE it was fixed under — that the roster path genuinely never produces a code — was never actually true. `clinician_code is null` was the wrong signal to gate on all along; it just happened to be null for every real organisation-route account that existed at the time, for reasons unrelated to the design intent (see below).

  **THE SPECIALTY PLACEHOLDER IS NOW A LOOSE END IN ITS OWN RIGHT, NOT SOLVED HERE.** `'unspecified'` sits on an organisation-verified practitioner forever, with nothing in the product ever prompting a change — and `specialty` is DISPLAYED, not just stored: `get_passport_team()` and `get_passport_clinicians()` both surface it directly, so a parent or a director reads "unspecified" where a real discipline belongs. This mattered less when Stage 6 chose the placeholder (a single fact nobody needed yet); it matters more in a real MDT clinic, where an OT, an SLT, and a BCBA are meaningfully different to everyone reading the record, not an implementation detail. Daniel's own instinct, recorded rather than built: a practitioner sets their own specialty after joining — no verification step needed, since nobody needs to check a fact a person is simply stating about themselves, the same reasoning that already lets `select_clinician_specialty()` exist unguarded for the independent path. Not decided as the final shape; recorded so it's answered deliberately when picked up, not guessed at under a later deadline.

- **THE `clinical_lead` ROLE HAS NO CLIENT SURFACE ANYWHERE — DEFERRED, ITS OWN ITEM, NOT PART OF PRD 6 STAGE 4. 18 Sept 2026.** Checked before building Stage 4's session-notes reading surfaces, not assumed: `clinical_lead` has zero UI in this app today. No dashboard, no route gated to it (`useRequireRole("clinical_lead"` — zero matches anywhere), no per-client page. `getPostAuthRedirect()` has no `case "clinical_lead"` and falls through to `/role-select` — a signed-in lead lands on the generic code-entry placeholder, indistinguishable from someone who hasn't joined anything yet. Stage 4's own instruction (build a session-notes surface that explicitly SAYS it cannot read notes, rather than silently showing an empty section) presupposed a surface to put that message on, and none exists. Daniel's own call: build a page now, as a side effect of a session-notes stage, would be the wrong order — the same category as PRD 5 Stage 7's own deferred director-dashboard screen, and the argument is stronger here since the role has no UI at all, meaning a page built now would be a screen designed to carry one negative message. **Stage 4 is director-only.** Two things to carry into whichever future piece builds the lead's first real screen, so neither is rediscovered from scratch:

  1. **The negative-disclosure rule still applies the moment that screen exists.** A session-notes section on a lead's own client view must explicitly state notes are not visible to a lead — never silently omit the section. A lead with real scope over a client seeing no notes section at all will assume there are none, which is worse than being told plainly they cannot see them.

  2. **The data layer is already built, which makes this smaller than it sounds.** `get_child_passport_profile_for_lead()` and `get_institution_episode_roster_for_lead()` (PRD 5 Stage 4, migration `0216`) exist, work, and currently have zero client-side callers — confirmed via `grep -rln` across `src/`, no results for either. Whoever picks up the lead's first screen is not starting from an empty data layer, only an empty client.

DONE, MIGRATION RUN, VERIFIED LIVE (LOCAL DEV, REAL BROWSER SESSIONS), CLOSED. 18 Sept 2026.** `get_session_notes_for_director()` (migration `0230`) re-derives, byte-for-byte, the same predicate `session_notes`' own SELECT policy (0228) already proves live — a SECURITY DEFINER function bypasses RLS for every table it touches, including `session_notes` itself, so the director check has to be restated explicitly inside the function rather than assumed to still apply. Exists only for the `auth.users` join (a clinician's display name), the identical reason `get_shared_session_notes()` exists as a function rather than a raw client-side `select()`. Unlike the parent's own read, this one is TOTAL — `clinical_record` included, no redaction boundary, matching the base policy's own director-reads-everything shape exactly.

  `DirectorSessionNotesTab.tsx` (new) — read-only, no write path (no write policy has ever granted a director anything; stated in 0228's own policy comment, not inferred). Wired into `ChildDetail.tsx` as a new `"sessionNotes"` tab, positioned right after "Clinical Team" — **filtered out of the tab strip entirely for a school principal**, not merely empty for one: the RPC's own director check already requires `inst.type = 'clinic'`, so a school principal would see nothing there regardless, and an always-empty tab for 9 out of 10 principals is clutter, not information. Cards show clinician attribution, the full clinical record, and the parent note (when present) with the identical golden-brown edit-after-share marker `SharedSessionNotesSection` already established — visual consistency between the two roles' own readings of the same fact.

Then the actual rendering, real browser sessions: the clinic director's own consent screen showed the correct clinic-branch copy ("You can see every client at your clinic..."), and `ChildDetail.tsx`'s tab strip rendered "Session Notes" with all three cards correct — the edited note carrying the golden-brown border and "Updated 18 Sept 2026" pill, the never-edited shared note carrying neither, and the unshared note still showing its own (unshared) parent-note draft, correctly, since a director's read is total. **The one claim that mattered most — the tab is genuinely ABSENT, not merely empty, for a school principal** — proven with a real second fixture institution and a real school-side passport (created directly, service-role, since this was a rendering check, not a re-proof of RLS): the school principal's own consent screen showed the correct school-branch copy (countersigning language), and their `ChildDetail.tsx` tab strip, read directly off the live DOM, listed ten tabs with no "Session Notes" among them. Both fixtures torn down, zero orphans, including the ad-hoc school passport (picked up correctly by the existing passport-link cleanup loop).

  **Deployed verification still owed** — folded into the combined Stages 2–4 deployed-app pass, per Daniel's own instruction.

- **PRD 6 STAGE 3 — THE PARENT'S VIEW. DONE, VERIFIED LIVE (LOCAL DEV, REAL BROWSER SESSION), CLOSED. 18 Sept 2026.** `get_shared_session_notes()` (Stage 1) had zero callers until this stage. Two pieces, per Daniel's own instruction: a dedicated reading surface, and the activity_log pointer as notification.

  **WHERE IT LIVES, per Daniel's own "passport view" framing, not the inbox.** `SharedSessionNotesSection` (new) mounts on `passport/dashboard/page.tsx` — the deep, accordion-based full record (Clinical Team, "From your Clinical Team", ABC Logs, Incidents) — not `parent-dashboard/page.tsx` (the inbox PRD 3 deliberately simplified). A growing list of session notes over time is record material, the same category the sections either side of it already sit in, not something-new-just-happened inbox material. Self-fetching, calls `get_shared_session_notes(passportId)` directly (no new hook needed — the RPC already shapes exactly what a parent may see: no `clinical_record` key on the row at all, not filtered client-side, structurally absent).

  **THE EDIT-AFTER-SHARE MARKER — Daniel's own instinct, built as specified: on the note itself, not just a feed entry that scrolls away.** Two signals, never colour alone (this codebase's own standing accessibility rule): a golden-brown left border on the whole card, PLUS an explicit "Updated [date]" pill using `parent_note_edited_after_share_at`. A plain shared-and-never-edited note renders with neither.

  **THE FEED HALF — `activityEvents.tsx` gained `session_note_shared`/`session_note_updated`**, mirroring `clinical_content_added`'s own registration exactly: no `href`, `ClinicalFileIcon`, a non-linking `ActivityRow`. No RPC/RLS change needed for the parent's feed to pick these up — migration 0228 already reasoned this through at write time (`get_parent_activity_feed()`'s own exclusion list and the parent SELECT policy's exclusion list are both short, explicit denylists; anything not named is visible by default, and neither new type was added to either list) — this stage was purely the frontend type/icon registration.

Signed in as the real parent: `passport/dashboard` rendered both notes correctly under a new "Session Notes" heading, positioned between "How I Support My Child" and "ABC Logs" — the edited note showing the golden-brown border and "Updated 18 Sept 2026" pill, the plain note showing neither, `clinical_record` absent from both (confirmed via the rendered content itself, not inferred). `parent-dashboard/activity` showed both event types with correct copy and the shared `ClinicalFileIcon`, non-linking (no href, no chevron), exactly matching `clinical_content_added`'s established rendering. Fixture torn down, zero orphans.

- **PRD 6 STAGE 2 — THE WRITING SURFACE. DONE, VERIFIED LIVE (LOCAL DEV, REAL BROWSER SESSION), CLOSED. 18 Sept 2026.** Client-side, against Stage 1's own infrastructure — no migration in this stage. `useSessionNote` (single note: load, per-field `saveField()` queued like AFLS's own `saveQueueRef`, not aborted-and-restarted like FBA's whole-blob path, since these are independent columns with no reason to cancel one field's save because another field changed; a dedicated `share()` that re-fetches afterward rather than guessing what the DB trigger wrote), `useSessionNotes` (the list, no client-side "mine only" filter needed — RLS already scopes it), `ClinicalFileSessionNotesTab` (the list + "+ New Session Note", which inserts a bare row immediately and navigates straight into the editor for it — the row's own existence, not a separate draft flag, is what makes "started during, finished after" work), and `SessionNoteEditor` (the composer, its own route: `/clinician/passport/[passportId]/session-note/[noteId]`).

  **WHERE IT LIVES: the Clinical File, confirmed as the right call rather than assumed.** A new `"sessionNotes"` tab, second in the list (right after Summary) — deliberately more prominent than FBA/Incident Log/Clinical Team further down, matching PRD 6 section 1's own framing ("the highest-frequency clinical task in the organisation"). Reuses `ClinicalFileDetail`'s own established tab-per-component pattern exactly; no new page-level architecture invented.

  **THE REQUIREMENT THAT MATTERED MOST: a plain `lg:grid-cols-2` on the two fields, nothing more elaborate.** Both mounted, both visible, both editable at once at 1280px — no tab, no second screen, no scroll between them. Deliberately NOT capped at `FbaSectionShell`'s own `lg:max-w-[66.6667%]` — right for one column of form fields, wrong for two genuinely-sized text columns, which is why this screen builds its own shell rather than reusing that one.

  **MOBILE (375PX): stacked, by elimination, not by default.** The three alternatives named as ruled out (two screens, tabs, a scroll between stacked fields) were reasoned about in the context where side-by-side is achievable and preferred — desktop. At a width where it genuinely isn't achievable, two screens and tabs fragment the writing flow WORSE than a scroll does (losing all context of the other field, not just visual simultaneity), which is what makes stacking the correct answer at this width specifically, not merely the fallback. Both fields stay mounted on the same screen throughout, reachable by an ordinary scroll — proven live, not just styled: reloading the note at 375px re-seeded both fields and the persistent share banner correctly from the database, confirming resumability works identically at both widths.

  **THE TOGGLE IS A ONE-TIME COPY, NOT A LIVE LINK, PROVEN NOT JUST BUILT THAT WAY.** A plain button, not a persistent checkbox — a checkbox that kept syncing on every keystroke would recreate, client-side, the exact link `0228`'s own schema explicitly refuses to build. Proven live: copied `clinical_record` into `parent_note`, then edited each field independently afterward — the earlier copied text in the untouched field stayed exactly as it was, confirming there is no ongoing behaviour left to misfire.

  **SHARING: ITS OWN DELIBERATE ACT, WITH A PREVIEW, NEVER A TOGGLE THAT FIRES ON SAVE.** A `BottomSheet` confirm showing the exact `parent_note` text about to cross the boundary — same shape as finalising an FBA and sharing an incident amendment, not a new pattern. Proven live end to end: `shared_at` and a real `session_note_shared` activity_log row both appeared the moment "Share with Parent" was confirmed, and neither fired a moment before.

  **ONCE SHARED, THE PRACTITIONER KNOWS BEFORE THEY EDIT, NOT AFTER — proven as the specific claim it is.** A persistent banner sits above `parent_note` itself the entire time the note is open, not a one-time toast after the original share: "Shared with the parent on 18 Sept. Editing this field will tell them it changed." Proven live, both halves separately: editing `clinical_record` on an already-shared note left `parent_note_edited_after_share_at`/`by` and the activity log completely untouched (confirmed via direct query, not inferred from the UI); editing `parent_note` on the same note immediately set both columns and produced a real `session_note_updated` activity_log row, and the banner itself then updated to show "last updated after sharing today."

  **Offline: nothing new, as instructed.** `insertWithOfflineRetry` and `SavedStateIndicator` reused unchanged — no third save mechanism.
