Fixture torn down. **Stated plainly: this was verified against local dev, not the deployed app** — the change hasn't been pushed yet, so deployed-build verification is still owed once it is.

  **Next, per PRD 6 section 7's own sequencing:** stage 3 (sharing UI refinements and the parent's own view of a shared note — `get_shared_session_notes()` already exists from Stage 1, unused by any client surface yet) and stage 4 (reading — a director's and a lead's own view of session notes, per the read rule Stage 1 already built and proved). Not started.

- **PRD 6 STAGE 1 — SESSION NOTES, THE NOTE ITSELF. DONE, MIGRATIONS RUN, VERIFIED LIVE (35/35), CLOSED. 18 Sept 2026.** The daily clinical workload — a note per session, every practitioner, every day — versus PRD 5's once-or-twice-a-year BSP. `session_notes`: `passport_id`, `clinician_id` (a direct `auth.users` FK, not `clinician_access.id` — access can be revoked, the note must survive that), `session_date`, two independently-authored text fields (`clinical_record`, `parent_note`), `is_shared_with_parent` + `shared_at`, and two edit-after-share columns.

  **`passport_id`, not `episode_id` — structural, confirmed by recon before writing anything.** `episodes_of_care` is created by exactly two functions (`onboard_clinic_client()`, `reopen_clinic_episode()`, both `0210`), both gated on `inst.type = 'clinic'`. A school-engaged or independent (parent-engaged) clinician never gets an episode row at all — `clinician_access` is the one relationship table identical across all three engagement types. An episode-keyed note would have been structurally unwritable for two of the three clinician tracks this schema already supports. `abc_logs`' own `passport_id + logged_by` shape (`0019`) was the template, not a coincidence.

  **Two real columns, not read-time redaction** — the `incidents.narrative` / `incidents.parent_summary` precedent (`0068`), independently authored, neither derived from the other. **A boolean, not `passport_clinical_content`'s row-per-audience shape** (`0040`) — a session note only ever has ONE boundary to cross (parent, or not), not three simultaneous audiences, so one flag is the right size. **Two edit-after-share columns, not a revision table** — `parent_note_edited_after_share_at`/`by`, a flag ON the record, answering "has this changed since I read it," the actual question a parent has; a full history table would have been a bigger feature than asked for.

  **The read rule — not `has_child_access()`, and the two mistakes it would have been easy to make in the opposite direction of each other, both caught before shipping.** Author reads/writes their own, always — no clinician_access check on UPDATE or SELECT-self, since ownership is permanent (a practitioner correcting a note about a client whose access has since ended must still be able to, the same way a departed teacher's own incident content stands unaffected by their own departure). The clinic's own director reads, never edits — no write policy branch exists for them at all, so "reads, never edits" is a fact about what's absent, not a convention. **No `clinical_lead` branch, anywhere** — the identical posture `get_episode_overseeing_leads()` (`0227`) already established for a different question: director-only, no lead branch, regardless of scope.

  **THE `inst.type = 'clinic'` CHECK ON THE DIRECTOR BRANCH IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT DEFENSIVE STYLE — a real leak caught in Stage 0 recon before it ever shipped, worth a future reader's attention if they wonder why it's there.** A clinician can be `institution_staff` at a SCHOOL too (the original, pre-PRD-5 clinician track, still live). Without this check, a school-engaged clinician's session notes would be readable by their SCHOOL'S OWN PRINCIPAL — exactly the leak "never visible to a school" (PRD 6 section 3) exists to prevent, and "a school" means the principal too, not just teachers and SNAs. Do not remove this check to "simplify" the policy.

  **THE STANDING DISTINCTION — the two halves read identically in a policy and only one of them is safe, confirmed explicitly before writing either.** The AUTHOR's own current standing is NOT required for the director's read — a deactivated practitioner's notes must stay readable by their own director. The DIRECTOR's own current standing IS required — a departed director must not go on reading a clinic's clinical notes, exactly the departure-cascade gap PRD 5 found three separate times. Getting this backwards either locks a departed practitioner's own notes away from their own director, or lets a departed director keep reading current clinical material — proven as two SEPARATE live assertions (deactivate the author, director still reads; deactivate the director, they're refused), not inferred from one.

  **A REAL BUG, FOUND LIVE, IN THE FIRST VERSION OF THE DIRECTOR-READ POLICY — the exact shape this file's own "a policy that looks up someone else runs under the caller's own RLS" entry already warns about, made again despite it.** The first draft's director-read branch did a raw `EXISTS` against `institution_staff`, looking up the NOTE'S AUTHOR's own row — someone other than the calling director. `institution_staff`'s own SELECT policy has been self-only since `0009`. A subquery inside another table's RLS policy runs under the CALLING session's own permissions — so the author lookup could only ever see the director's own row, never the author's, and the whole check was silently false for every caller except a note's own author. Found live via the verification fixture itself (both the "director reads a colleague's note" and "director still reads after the author is deactivated" assertions failed for the identical reason), fixed in `0229` with the exact same `SECURITY DEFINER` helper pattern this schema already established for `clinical_lead_scope`'s own identical mistake (`_staff_can_view_lead_scope()`, `0217`) — `_clinic_director_can_read_clinician_session_notes(p_author_clinician_id)`, a single `EXISTS` (not a two-step resolve-then-check, since an author can hold more than one `institution_staff` row over their career).

  **THE TOGGLE IS NEVER A LINK.** "Copy `clinical_record` into `parent_note`" is a write-time client convenience — no trigger, no generated column, no constraint keeps the two columns in sync afterward. The absence of any syncing mechanism IS the enforcement, not an oversight; proven live by editing one field after the other and confirming the untouched field holds exactly what it held before.

  **Sharing and edit-tracking is a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger, not an RPC** — the client keeps writing via plain `.update()` calls, the exact shape `insertWithOfflineRetry()`/AFLS's own per-field save pattern already assumes (reused unchanged, per PRD 6 section 5 — no third save mechanism written). `false → true` on `is_shared_with_parent` sets `shared_at` and writes an `activity_log` row (`session_note_shared`) — the same pointer mechanism `finalize_fba_report()` already uses for `clinical_content_added` (`0197`), reused, not invented. A `parent_note` edit AFTER sharing sets the two edit-tracking columns and writes `session_note_updated`; a `clinical_record` edit after sharing touches NEITHER — proven live as the specific claim it is ("enforced, not implied by column naming"), not assumed from the naming.

  **The parent's own read (`get_shared_session_notes()`) structurally excludes `clinical_record`** — the column does not appear anywhere in the RPC's own SQL, matching `get_parent_incidents()`'s own shape (never selects `incidents.narrative`) over a raw RLS SELECT policy that would need a column-level REVOKE on top for the same guarantee. Proven live: the returned object has no `clinical_record` key at all, not a null one.

  **VERIFICATION — two-institution fixture (`ZZPRD6CLINIC`: director, two clinic practitioners, a clinical_lead; `ZZPRD6SCHOOL`: principal, a school-engaged verified clinician; plus an independent clinician with no institution at all, an unverified clinician, and a parent), 35/35, real signed-in sessions throughout.** THE critical case proven first and explicitly: the school's own principal refused reading the school-engaged clinician's note, silently empty, not an error. INSERT boundaries (unverified clinician refused despite active access; verified clinician with no access to that passport refused). The clinical_lead refused regardless of scope. The independent clinician's notes readable by nobody but themself — no director exists to extend oversight to. Both halves of the standing distinction proven as separate live assertions. The full sharing/edit-tracking sequence: before-share nulls confirmed, share sets `shared_at` and fires the activity_log pointer, a `clinical_record`-only edit afterward touches nothing and fires nothing, a `parent_note` edit afterward sets both tracking columns and fires `session_note_updated`, and the earlier `clinical_record` edit is confirmed still intact (the toggle was never a link). The parent's own RPC: sees only their own child's shared notes, structurally no `clinical_record` key, and the activity_log pointer reaches their real `get_parent_activity_feed()`. Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first.

  **Stage 1 closes here.** Per PRD 6's own sequencing (section 7): stage 2 (the clinical record and parent summary UI, whichever shape stage 1 settled — settled above), stage 3 (sharing UI and the parent's own view), stage 4 (reading — practitioner/lead/director surfaces) are next. All backend infrastructure for reading is already in place from this stage; what remains is client-side screens, not schema.

- **"REUSE THE ENROLMENT SHAPE" MEANS THE SHAPE, NEVER THE TABLE — THE REASON IS PRD 8's ENTIRE PREMISE.** `enrolments_one_active_per_child` (0121) is `unique (passport_id) where ended_at is null` — GLOBAL across the whole system, not scoped per institution. If PRD 5's episodes of care had been built by inserting into `enrolments` directly, a child could never be an actively-enrolled school pupil AND an active clinic client at the same time — the exact cross-organisation case an entire future PRD (8: cross-org sharing, the stagnation queue, clinical export) is built around. Found during PRD 5 Stage 3's own recon, confirmed by reading the live partial index rather than assuming the PRD's own "same shape as enrolments" phrase meant "same table." Episodes of care live in their own table (`episodes_of_care`, migration `0209`), same columns and paired-CHECK shape as `enrolments`, but its own uniqueness scope: `unique (passport_id, institution_id) where ended_at is null` — one active episode per client PER ORGANISATION (PRD section 8's own words), not per client globally. When any future PRD says "reuse the shape of" an existing table, treat that as license to copy the STRUCTURE, and check the existing table's own constraints for scope assumptions that may not transfer — "same shape" and "same table" are not the same instruction, and confusing them here would have silently made two live product tracks mutually exclusive for every real family that uses both.

- **THE FULL ADVERSARIAL SUITE CAN CRASH RATHER THAN FAIL, AND WHEN IT DOES, THE UNCLEANED FIXTURE IS THE MORE EXPENSIVE HALF OF THAT — SCOPED, NOT BUILT. 17 Sept 2026.** Found live: `0204` (PRD 5 Stage 2) silently dropped four keys from `hand_over_principal()`'s returned jsonb (see the entry below), and CHECK X's own read of one of those keys threw on `null`, killing the whole process. Every block from Y onward — dozens of them, entirely unrelated to `hand_over_principal()` — never ran, and the gate result was unknown rather than partial. Separately, and worse: CHECK X's own fixture (three institutions, eleven accounts, one incident) was mid-flight when the process died, so its own end-of-block cleanup never ran either — it sat in production until this was reported and torn down by hand. **A block that dies without cleaning up is exactly how fixtures accumulate invisibly — this build has already found and removed 134 orphaned rows from earlier instances of this same failure mode** (see "TEST ACCOUNT CLEANUP" below).

  **The right scope, not "never crash everywhere"**: the suite's own header comment already draws the boundary that matters. Checks V onward are independently self-contained (own institution, own accounts, own cleanup, individually selectable via `ONLY_CHECKS`) — a bug in one has no bearing on any other, so those blocks should be wrapped in try/catch, record a CRASHED result with the stack trace, and continue to the next block rather than dying. B–U is one deliberately interleaved unit where later checks depend on state earlier ones left behind — that region should stay fail-fast, since continuing past a broken shared fixture risks corrupting or misreading everything downstream, not just losing coverage.

  **The cleanup half is the one that actually matters most, not the continue-past-it half.** Losing visibility into unrelated blocks is a coverage gap, found and fixed the same day. An uncleaned fixture sitting in production, unnoticed, is a standing liability — the 134-row cleanup and the CHECK NN/OO fixture leak are what that failure mode costs when nobody happens to be watching. The catch path for each V-onward block should run that block's own existing cleanup code (or an equivalent), not just skip past the failure.

  **Scope, so this isn't mistaken for small**: roughly 50 block boundaries (V through the last, per the suite's own header list: V, W, X, Y, Z, AA, BB, CC, DD, EE, FF, GG, HH, II, JJ, KK, LL, MM, NN, OO, PP, QQ, RR, SS, TT, UU, VV, WW, XX, YY, ZZ, AAA, BBB, CCC, DDD, EEE, FFF, GGG, HHH, III, JJJ, KKK, LLL, MMM, NNN, OOO, PPP, plus RRR), each needing a try/catch/finally wrapped around its existing body without breaking the `ONLY_CHECKS` selective-run feature those same blocks already support. Mechanical, but wide — not something to fold into an unrelated fix. Not started.

  **Worth naming plainly, not a process change to make now**: this full run was the first time the suite had executed since `0204` shipped, and `0204` is what broke it. The gap between a migration landing and the suite actually proving it is where this hid — not because the standing gate rule wasn't followed (it was: `0204` got targeted live verification at the time, per this file's own gate rule, and passed everything it was checked against), but because the FULL suite is expensive enough (the 30-per-5-minute Auth rate limit, raised only for a real gate run) that it doesn't run on every database-touching change, only when a stage explicitly calls for the full gate. That's a deliberate trade — the alternative is raising the rate limit for every migration, which nobody wants — but it means a regression in an area no targeted check happens to touch can sit unnoticed for as long as it takes to next run the full suite. Named here as the actual cost of that trade, so it doesn't get rediscovered as a surprise.

- **`hand_over_principal()`'S RETURN CONTRACT — REGRESSION FROM 0204, FIXED IN 0212, RUN. 17 Sept 2026.** `0204` (PRD 5 Stage 2's own type-aware staying-role rewrite, `CREATE OR REPLACE`) silently narrowed the function's returned jsonb from six keys (`handed_over`, `outcome`, `handover_id`, `successor_institution_staff_id`, `predecessor_new_institution_staff_id`, `grants_revoked`, the original `0102` shape) down to two (`handover_id`, `grants_revoked`). Exactly this file's own "WHEN A FUNCTION'S RETURN CONTRACT CHANGES, UPDATE ITS CALLERS IN THE SAME PASS" gotcha, applied to a migration written earlier in this same session. Found by the full adversarial suite crashing in CHECK X (`successor_institution_staff_id` read off the return value, `undefined`, the subsequent lookup returned no row, next line threw on `null`) — not by review, not by the targeted Stage 2 verification that shipped `0204`, since that verification never happened to read the RPC's own `data`. **Checked whether this reached production before fixing anything: it didn't** — `HandOverPrincipalSheet.tsx`, the one live caller, destructures only `{ error }`, never `data`. Fixed in `0212` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, same signature, only the `RETURN` statement restored) — the function's contract, not a caller worked around, since a caller reading `data` today would have silently broken and the only reason none did is luck, not design.

- **THE FIRST CLEAN FULL ADVERSARIAL RUN SINCE `0204` SHIPPED — 1140/1145, 80 BLOCKS, FULL COMPLETION. 17 Sept 2026.** Re-run immediately after `0212` landed: reached the final block (`RRR summary complete.`), printed the real summary, exited only on the 5 remaining failures (script's own normal behavior for `failed.length > 0`) — no crash marker anywhere in the log. `hand_over_principal()` itself is clean: CHECK X ran in full, `X1b` correctly resolved the successor's row off the restored return shape, both `X-bonus` checks passed against the dynamic message.

  **The 5 failures investigated (not fixed) — three different findings, not one shape, exactly the distinction this file's own "wrong-reason pass" and "coverage gap" entries already draw:**

  **`DD-1a` — a check encoding genuinely superseded behaviour, not a regression.** Written when `get_teacher_activity_feed()`'s `activity_log` branch was still hand-rolled (pre-`0188`) and explicitly joined `passport_institution_links` — confirmed by reading `0155`'s own body directly, which does carry that join. `0188`'s Group B pass deliberately removed it, replacing the hand-rolled logic with `has_child_access()` as "the one and only authority" (its own stated goal, not an accident) — and `has_child_access()`'s own class-derived branch (`has_class_teacher_access()`, live since `0104`/`0130`) has never checked `passport_institution_links` at all. This wasn't a decision to loosen the FEED specifically; it made the feed consistent with every other surface reading the same child's data, none of which ever required that link either. No real gap: the actual boundary — a principal at the teacher's own institution must have put them in a class with this child — still holds regardless of another institution's own separate link. `DD-1a` needs updating (or removing, if nothing else here still needs proving) to match the deliberately-consolidated model, not the pre-`0188` one.

  **`EEE4a`/`EEE5b`/`EEE5c` — a fragile test fixture, not a rule change either way.** The fixture resolves a category via `.contains("allowed_sender_roles", ["class_teacher"])` with no `applies_to` filter and no `ORDER BY` — Postgres gives no row-order guarantee without one. Queried directly: three `applies_to: 'staff'` rows ("Cover / Rota", "Class / Roster", "General") return before any `applies_to: 'child'` row for that exact filter, today. The fixture picks the first row, gets a staff category, and `send_message()` correctly refuses it for a child conversation — the RPC is doing exactly the right thing; the failing assertion is proof of that, misdirected at the wrong actor. The real client (`useMessageCategories.ts`) always passes an explicit `appliesTo` and orders by `sort_order` — no real caller could ever reproduce this. Most likely destabilized by `0168`/`0169` adding new staff categories that shifted unordered return order; this may have been passing by accident for a while, not a new break. Fix belongs in the fixture's own query (add `.eq("applies_to", "child")`), not in `send_message()`.

  **`NNN13` — a check encoding superseded behaviour, in the good direction: a feature it expected to still be missing shipped.** Migration `0196` ("SNA child-scoped messaging") explicitly built the capability `NNN13` was written to prove "still deferred," citing `0188`'s own header ("now decided (SNAs get it)... scoped as its own piece") almost verbatim. The send now succeeds — no error — which is exactly why the check fails: it still expects a refusal. Not a regression; a shipped feature silently misreported as broken because the check was never updated after `0196` landed. **Recommend against a named skip here, unlike `W11c`** — `W11c`'s value is permanently unreachable by construction (a one-time historical backfill no live path can ever reproduce); this one is reachable and working, so the right fix is flipping the assertion to prove the now-correct behaviour (the SNA's message succeeds, and ideally that it reaches the right recipient), which is real coverage a skip would throw away, not a substitute for it.

  **None of the three requires anything in `0209`–`0212` to change.** All predate this session's PRD 5 work (`0155`/`0188` for DD, `0168`/`0169` for the EEE fixture, `0188`/`0196` for NNN). Not fixed yet, per instruction — findings only.

- **DIMENSION-LEVEL, CLINIC-WIDE SCOPING IS THE INTENDED DESIGN, NOT A CONSERVATIVE FALLBACK — RECORDED SO IT IS NEVER "IMPROVED" INTO THE NARROWER VERSION THAT LEAKS.** A tag-change request touches a scoping dimension if ANY lead at the clinic has a `clinical_lead_scope` row on that dimension — clinic-wide, not just the approving lead's own scope, not value-specific. Daniel's own correction during PRD 5 Stage 5 recon, worked through rather than taken on faith: in practice, the dimensions leads actually use to define their own remits (funding, location) are exactly the ones where a change reshuffles the organisation, and those are correctly a director's call; dimensions no lead uses (service, team — until a clinic scopes someone on them) are correctly lead-approvable. **A simple rule arriving at the correct behaviour is a good sign, not a compromise.** The narrower check — "does the APPROVING lead's own scope reference this specific value" — would leak: a lead could approve a change to a value they don't personally track that still silently moves a client out of a DIFFERENT lead's scope, or out of their own via a dimension they weren't thinking about. Provable, not assumed: because scope-matching itself (`_lead_episode_in_scope`, `0215`) only ever looks at dimensions a lead has rows for, a genuinely non-scoping dimension can never move any client into or out of any lead's own scope by construction — the dimension-level, clinic-wide check is airtight for exactly that reason. Do not narrow this to "the approving lead's own scope" later on the theory that it's more precise; that precision is the leak.

- **STAGE 7 BLOCKER, FOUND DURING STAGE 6 VERIFICATION, NOT SOLVED — RECORDED NOW SO IT ISN'T REDISCOVERED FROM SCRATCH. 17 Sept 2026.** `get_passport_team()` returns empty for every clinic client, for any caller, always. Both of its own branches — the institution-staff union arm and the clinician union arm — end in `and public.owns_passport(p_passport_id)`, caller-scoped against `passport_guardians`; `onboard_clinic_client()` (0210) never inserts a `passport_guardians` row, and nothing else in the clinic-side schema does either. A clinic client has no guardian, ever, by construction. Daniel's own framing, correct and worth keeping verbatim: **the same shape as the Stage 2 inert-clinician-role finding — a function that works, called by someone authorised, returning nothing because a premise it depends on is false for clinics.** Not a Stage 6 regression (`get_passport_team()` was never touched by 0221–0225) and deliberately not folded into Stage 6's own fix — but real, and the director's dashboard (PRD section 10, deferred until "the foundation is used") is going to want exactly this: "who is on this client's team."

  **What is NOT actually blocked, checked directly rather than assumed from the name — important, because conflating the two would overstate the gap:** `get_passport_clinicians()` already answers "which practitioner(s) are engaged with this client" for a clinic director, right now, live — its own principal branch (0124, reused unchanged by 0221) has no `owns_passport()` dependency at all, and Stage 6's own verification proved it directly (test 1q: the director's real session saw the newly-approved practitioner on `get_passport_clinicians()` for a guardian-less clinic client). So the clinician-membership half of "who's on the team" already works for a director today. What's actually still broken is narrower than it first reads: `get_passport_team()` specifically, which is the function that COMBINES staff-with-child-access and clinicians into one parent-facing list — and every path into that combined list is gated the same broken way, regardless of which half of the union a caller is really asking about.

  **The three candidate fix shapes, weighed, not decided — Stage 7's own recon should confirm before building, the same way Stage 4/5's own recon did before code:**

  1. **A guardian row created when a parent claims a clinic client.** Presupposes a real, decided product feature — a claim-code flow for clinic clients, mirroring the school side's `generate_passport_claim_code()`/`/passport/claim`. Nothing in PRD 5 as built through Stage 6 gives a clinic client a parent-facing surface at all; every clinic-side mechanism so far (episodes, tags, scope, verification) is staff-only. Building this shape FIRST would mean deciding, as a side effect of fixing one RPC, that clinic clients get parent accounts at all — a bigger and more consequential call than this bug warrants, and not what Daniel asked this entry to resolve. Weakest candidate, and shouldn't be reached for by default.

  2. **A clinic branch bolted onto `get_passport_team()` itself.** Technically possible — a third `union all` arm, or a second predicate replacing `owns_passport()` with something institution-staff-aware for clinic-type institutions. Weighed against this codebase's own established precedent (`get_child_passport_profile_for_lead()`, `get_institution_episode_roster_for_lead()` — both Stage 4, both built as clinic-side SIBLINGS to an existing school-shaped function rather than widening it, explicitly because the chokepoint they'd have widened was "entirely school-side... backing 45 references," per that migration's own header): `get_passport_team()`'s institution-staff arm is exactly that same kind of chokepoint — `class_children`, `class_teachers`, `class_sna_assignments`, `temporary_access`, all structurally meaningless at a clinic. Entangling clinic-awareness into that arm risks the same class of mistake this file already has a dedicated entry against.

  3. **A dedicated clinic-side function, OR — cheaper still — no new function at all, because the real need is already served.** The likeliest actual answer, given point (1) above: if "who is on this client's team" for a clinic means "which practitioner(s) are engaged," that is precisely `get_passport_clinicians()`'s own job, already working for a director. Stage 7 may not need to fix `get_passport_team()` for clinics at all — it may only need its own UI/RPC layer to call `get_passport_clinicians()` for a clinic client instead of `get_passport_team()`, the same way Stage 4 already reaches for a lead-specific sibling rather than a shared one. The one real open question this doesn't resolve on its own: does a clinic's own notion of "team" also want a `clinical_lead`'s scoped oversight surfaced (not caseload — Stage 4's `clinical_lead_scope`, an authority relationship with no dedicated read-facing "this lead can see this client" listing yet), or is engaged-practitioner membership the whole of it? That is a genuine product question for Stage 7's own recon, not something to guess here.

  **Recommendation, not a decision:** start Stage 7's own recon from option 3 — confirm whether `get_passport_clinicians()` already covers the real need before building anything new, and only reach for a dedicated clinic-side function (option 2's shape, built as a sibling per precedent, never as a widening of `get_passport_team()` itself) if a genuine additional fact (scoped-lead visibility, something else) turns out to be missing from it. Option 1 stays parked unless a future PRD explicitly decides clinic clients get parent-facing accounts, independent of this bug.

  **DECIDED, Daniel confirmed, 17 Sept 2026 — option 3, and the REASONING is the record, not just the outcome.** A clinic client's team is its actively-engaged practitioner(s) — `get_passport_clinicians()`, caller-side, no migration. The argument that settled it: checked the SCHOOL precedent before picking a shape, not after — `get_passport_team()`'s own institution-staff arm requires `has_child_access()` (class-derived teacher, SNA, a direct `passport_access` grant), and a **principal is not on their own school's team listing either**, unless separately a class teacher too. Institution-wide oversight and per-client team membership are already, deliberately, two different things in this schema — a clinic director and a `clinical_lead`'s scope are the exact same category as a principal's own institution-wide reach, not team membership, for the identical reason. **Record this so nobody later "fixes" `get_passport_team()` to fold directors (or leads) into it** — that would be reversing a distinction this schema already draws correctly on the school side, not fixing an oversight. `get_passport_team()` itself stays exactly as broken as it is for clinic clients, correctly, because nothing should be calling it for one.

  **A separate, genuine question this raised: "which leads oversee this client" — a director's own real question, and nothing answers it today.** `_lead_episode_in_scope()` (0215) only ever answers "is THIS caller in scope" — every scope-matching function in this schema is written from the lead's own point of view, never the reverse. Confirmed by Daniel as its own Stage 7 item, not folded into the team fix above and not the same question: a lead's scope is oversight, not team membership (per the reasoning immediately above), but a director still needs to SEE who holds that oversight for a given client — informational, not a membership list. Not built yet; the reverse lookup is a straightforward reshape of `_lead_episode_in_scope()`'s own join (every lead at this institution whose scope dimensions all intersect this episode's tags), not a new mechanism.

- **A REAL, LATENT STAGE 4 GAP, FOUND CHECKING WHETHER STAGE 7'S OWN "GROUP BY WHICHEVER DIMENSION THE DIRECTOR PICKS" DASHBOARD IDEA WAS SAFE TO BUILD ON. 17 Sept 2026. Not fixed — recorded, per Daniel's own instruction to check before Stage 7 builds on top of it.** PRD 5 section 6 names renaming a tag dimension ("Location" to "Region") as its own flagship example of something a clinic should be able to do without Behaviour Hive. Checked directly: **no rename operation exists anywhere — not as an RPC, not in any client code (`grep -rln "institution_tags" src/` returns nothing at all), not even as an unused/unwired function.** The only thing that technically permits it is `institution_tags`' own RLS write policy (0213), which is `for all` (not just `for insert`) to a director, matching `institution_vocabulary_overrides`' own established "direct action, hand-run SQL, no UI yet" posture (0200) — but that precedent doesn't transfer cleanly here, and that's the actual defect.

  **Why it doesn't transfer: `institution_vocabulary_overrides` has nothing referencing it by string; `institution_tags` has two.** `episode_tags.dimension`/`value` and `clinical_lead_scope.dimension`/`value` are BOTH plain text with NO foreign key into `institution_tags` — confirmed directly in `0213`'s own header ("Each value is still checked against the clinic's own institution_tags catalog at write time... not by a table-level FK"). Validation against the catalog happens ONLY at write time (`_replace_episode_tags()`, `clinical_lead_scope`'s own INSERT/UPDATE policy, 0215) — nothing ever re-validates or migrates an EXISTING row when the catalog changes underneath it.

  **What actually happens if a director renames (or the only realistic alternative — retires the old value and adds a new one, since no rename UI exists either): every existing `episode_tags`/`clinical_lead_scope` row keeps saying "Location" forever, silently orphaned from a catalog that no longer lists it.** Nothing breaks LOUDLY: `_lead_episode_in_scope()` (0215) is pure string equality between `clinical_lead_scope` and `episode_tags` directly — it never consults the catalog at all, so existing authority matches keep working exactly as before, which is precisely what makes this dangerous rather than obviously broken. What DOES break, silently: (1) nobody can ever tag or scope anything with "Location" again — the catalog validation at write time now refuses it; (2) a director can never migrate the OLD rows to the NEW name — `_replace_episode_tags()` only writes what's explicitly passed, nothing cascades; (3) **any feature that reads the catalog to discover "what dimensions currently exist" — exactly Stage 7's own "group by whichever dimension the director picks" idea — would offer "Region" as a pickable option and silently return an undercount or zero results for every episode still tagged "Location," with no error anywhere to explain why.** A director renaming their own vocabulary, exactly as PRD section 6 promises they can, would quietly corrupt every dashboard view built on top of the catalog from that point on.

  **Confirmed: this is LATENT today, not live.** No client code has ever written to `institution_tags` at all — every real (fixture) catalog row across Stages 4–6 was inserted once and never touched again, so no historical drift has actually occurred yet. Stage 7's dimension-grouping dashboard idea is safe to build TODAY, on the current, genuinely-consistent data. **But it must not be built as though renaming is safe** — the moment Behaviour Hive builds the tag-catalog management UI PRD section 6 promises (even a simple "edit this dimension's label"), this becomes a real, silent data-integrity bug on the first clinic that uses it. Two real fix shapes for whenever that UI gets built, not decided here: (a) a rename RPC that cascades the string change through `episode_tags` and `clinical_lead_scope` atomically, kept in sync by construction; or (b) the more durable fix — give `institution_tags` a stable id that the other two tables reference by FK instead of by copied string, so a rename changes only the display label and nothing else ever needs to move. Recorded now so it's answered before it's built, not discovered the way the `clinical_lead_scope` RLS bug and the Stage 2 inert-role bug both were — live, by a real director, after shipping.

  **FIXED, OPTION (b), MIGRATION 0226, RUN AND VERIFIED LIVE (26/26). 17 Sept 2026.** Daniel's own call on timing, not elegance: fix it now, while there is genuinely nothing to migrate, not after a catalog UI ships and this becomes a live migration of real clinical data and real authority grants with live orphans in both tables. `institution_tags` already carried a stable id (its own PK since `0213`) — the fix is `episode_tags.institution_tag_id`/`clinical_lead_scope.institution_tag_id`, real foreign keys, replacing the copied `dimension`/`value` text columns entirely (both dropped). Confirmed via `grep -rln` before writing anything: zero client code anywhere touches `institution_tags`, `episode_tags`, or `clinical_lead_scope` — PRD 5 has been backend-only through Stage 6, so the shape changed freely with no UI contract to preserve. `tag_change_requests`' own `base_tags`/`proposed_tags` were deliberately left untouched — still jsonb `(dimension, value)` TEXT snapshots, because a request is a historical record of what was asked in human terms and should read the same even after a later rename; only the two LIVE tables moved to the FK.

  **What the FK removes from `0213`'s own validation, precise, not by feel (Daniel's own question):** the EXISTENCE half of every `exists(select 1 from institution_tags where dimension=... and value=... and is_active)` check across `_replace_episode_tags()`, `raise_tag_change_request()`, and the old `_can_manage_clinical_lead_scope()` — genuinely removed, not duplicated, since a bogus id is now structurally impossible to store. What the FK CANNOT express, and stays as an explicit check everywhere: `is_active` (a deactivated catalog row still satisfies referential integrity) and same-institution (a FK only proves the target exists somewhere, not that it belongs to the institution doing the tagging or scoping). Both proven live and refused correctly (below), distinctly from the pure FK-violation cases.

  **`_lead_episode_in_scope()`'s own semantics — the part most likely to be quietly wrong — confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged, worked through with concrete cases before writing the SQL, then proven live through the real caller.** The naive version of this migration (group by `institution_tag_id` instead of dimension name) would have silently turned Stage 4's own "OR within a dimension" rule into a second AND requirement — a lead scoped to `funding=Tusla` and `funding=HSE` would count as 2 distinct groups instead of 1. Fixed by keeping every grouping operation on DIMENSION NAME throughout (recovered via a join to `institution_tags`), while the actual per-row match test became a plain `institution_tag_id` equality — simpler than the old compound `(dimension=dimension and value=value)` equi-join, and incidentally exact in a way text comparison never fully was.

  **TWO REAL MISTAKES MADE AND CAUGHT WHILE WRITING THIS, BOTH KEPT ON THE RECORD IN THE MIGRATION'S OWN HEADER, NOT SMOOTHED OVER — worth restating here since they're the actual lesson, not just the fix.** Mistake 1, caught live by Postgres itself on the first run attempt: the original draft dropped `dimension`/`value` from both tables (and dropped the old `_can_manage_clinical_lead_scope(uuid,text,text)` overload) before repointing everything that still referenced them — refused outright, `2BP01`, "other objects depend on it" — a function or column referenced by a live RLS policy is a real, tracked dependency, the same as a view depending on a table. Fixed by moving both column drops to the very end of the migration, after every function and the policy were repointed. Mistake 2, caught reviewing that exact fix before ever running it a second time: the obvious correction — create the new `(uuid, uuid default null)` overload first, repoint the policy, THEN drop the old `(uuid, text default null, text default null)` one — would have let both overloads coexist for the duration of the `ALTER POLICY` statement. Both signatures accept a call with exactly one `uuid` argument (every parameter past the first has a default in both) and share an identical first parameter type — exactly the call the policy's own `USING` clause makes. Postgres cannot resolve that between two equally-valid candidates and refuses it as "not unique" — the same overload-ambiguity trap this file's own `send_message()` gotcha already documents, in the opposite shape (a shorter call matching two candidates, instead of a longer one silently creating a second signature). The actual fix: `DROP POLICY` (removing the dependency entirely, not repointing it) → `DROP FUNCTION` the old overload → `CREATE FUNCTION` the new one → `CREATE POLICY` fresh. At no point do two overloads coexist while the policy is live.

  **VERIFICATION — dedicated fixture (`ZZPRD5S7CLINIC` + `ZZPRD5S7OTHER`, a director and two leads), 26/26, real signed-in sessions throughout.** The FK itself: a raw service-role insert with a non-existent `institution_tag_id` refused at the database level on both tables (a genuine FK-violation error, not an application check). `is_active` and same-institution both proven as SEPARATE, surviving checks: a director's own real session refused scoping a lead to a real-but-inactive catalog tag, and separately refused scoping to a real tag belonging to a DIFFERENT clinic — both cases the FK alone would have permitted. `set_episode_tags()` end to end through the rewritten `_replace_episode_tags()`: succeeds with a real pair, persists `institution_tag_id` (not text) matching the resolved catalog id, still refuses an unknown pair, and a failed attempt leaves existing tags untouched (validate-then-write preserved). `_lead_episode_in_scope()`'s own semantics proven through `get_institution_episode_roster_for_lead()` — the real, granted caller path, not the internal function directly: AND-across-dimensions (Lead A, funding=Tusla AND location=Dublin) both positive and negative cases; OR-within-a-dimension (Lead B, funding=Tusla OR HSE) picking up either value; two leads independently seeing the same client, no tie-break. The whole Stage 5 mechanism proven end to end through the rewritten functions: raising a request with a correctly FK-resolved `base_tags` snapshot, a lead approving a non-scoping change within scope, the SAME lead correctly refused approving a scoping change on the identical episode, the director approving that scoping change cleanly, and the staleness check refusing an approval whose snapshot no longer matches the current FK-resolved tags — request left genuinely pending, nothing silently merged. Fixture torn down, zero orphans.

  **Stage 7's own scoped work (the team fix, the leads-overseeing lookup, the outstanding-work buckets, caseload size per practitioner) is next — this was Step 0, the blocker it depended on, done first per instruction.**

- **PRD 5 STAGE 7 — SCOPE CONFIRMED BY DANIEL, RECON ONLY, NOT YET BUILT. 17 Sept 2026.** The clinical director's dashboard, last stage of PRD 5. Section 9 names four items; checked each against what actually exists in the schema before proposing scope, not assumed from the PRD's own list.

  **In scope for Stage 7:** the `get_passport_team()` fix (caller-side, `get_passport_clinicians()`, no migration — own entry above); the leads-overseeing-this-client reverse lookup (own entry above, new SQL, a reshape of `_lead_episode_in_scope()`'s own join); the outstanding-clinical-work buckets — pending tag change requests (`get_pending_tag_change_requests()`, 0220, already exists, needs only a dashboard-facing summary), episodes/clients with no active practitioner (new SQL, same "gap" RPC pattern `get_my_class_sna_gaps()`, 0165, already established on the school side — `episodes_of_care` LEFT JOIN `clinician_access` where no active institution-engaged match), pending clinic staff joins (new SQL, identical mechanism the school side already has), and FBAs still in draft/incomplete owned by this clinic's own practitioners (new SQL — `fba_reports.clinician_id` joined to `institution_staff`, confirmed FBAs work identically for a clinic-track practitioner post-Stage-6, nothing FBA-specific changed); and current caseload size per practitioner (`COUNT(*)` from `clinician_access`, institution-engaged, active, grouped by clinician — a real, useful number distinct from "capacity," which has no denominator to compare it against).

  **Parked, with the question recorded rather than a number invented — same posture as every other deliberately-deferred item in this file:**
  - **Capacity** — no capacity/target/max-caseload column exists anywhere (`clinicians`, `institution_staff`, `institutions` all checked). Inventing a number nobody set is the exact failure mode this file already has entries against.
  - **Funded placements** — `institution_tags.dimension` is freeform, clinic-defined text (PRD section 6's own words: "should not need us"); nothing designates which dimension, if any, means "funding," so a hardcoded lookup would work by coincidence for one clinic's own naming choice and silently return nothing for another's. The better answer, once the rename gap above is settled: not a hardcoded funding view at all, but grouping by whichever dimension the director picks from their own live catalog — works regardless of what they called it, sidesteps identification entirely.
  - **Overdue reviews** — `clinician_access.last_review_date` is queryable, but "overdue" has no threshold defined anywhere in this schema. Same class of decision this file already deferred once, deliberately, for `passport_section_e`'s own staleness flag: "held per Daniel's own instruction until built and seen." Not a fact until a threshold is actually set.
  - **The stagnation queue** — confirmed OUT, PRD 8's own territory. No cross-institution read authorization exists anywhere in this schema (`incidents` is scoped entirely to the institution that owns it, no grant mechanism lets a different institution read it even for a shared child) — the underlying data LINKAGE exists (`passport_institution_links` already supports a school and a clinic on the same passport simultaneously, deliberately, per the `enrolments` vs `episodes_of_care` scoping entry above), but the authorization to read across that seam does not, and building it is explicitly what PRD 8 is for.

  **Build the data, not the screen — matching every stage of this PRD so far, and PRD section 10's own explicit instruction.** Every stage through 6 shipped backend-only; the director's dashboard is one of three things section 10 names as deliberately deferred until "the foundation is used." Stage 7 builds the RPC layer for the confirmed-in-scope items above and stops there — the screen itself is its own later design pass, the same relationship PRD 2 (architecture) had to PRD 4 (design). Not started.

  **DONE, MIGRATION 0227 RUN, VERIFIED LIVE (22/22), CLOSED. 17 Sept 2026.** Built in the order Daniel set, four things.

  **1. The team fix — no migration, only the reasoning recorded so it survives.** A future clinic-client UI calls `get_passport_clinicians()`, never `get_passport_team()`. The reasoning is now written directly into `0227`'s own header, not left to float in prose elsewhere: a principal is not on their own school's team listing either, because institution-wide oversight and per-client team membership are already two different things in this schema — so nobody later "fixes" `get_passport_team()` to fold directors or leads into it.

  **2. `get_episode_overseeing_leads()` — the reverse lookup, built as a genuinely SEPARATE implementation of Stage 4's own AND-across-dimensions/OR-within-a-dimension rule, not a loop calling `_lead_episode_in_scope()` per lead.** The point of a second implementation is to have something to check the first one against — reusing the same function would have proven nothing. `GROUP BY`/`HAVING` instead of the original's two-CTE-and-compare-counts shape, but the same underlying arithmetic: a lead is in scope exactly when their own matched-dimension count equals their own total-dimension count.

  **3. Three genuinely new outstanding-work buckets** (episodes with no active institution-engaged practitioner, pending clinic staff joins, FBAs in draft owned by this clinic's own practitioners) **plus one reused, not duplicated** (`get_pending_tag_change_requests()`, 0220, already correctly institution-scoped — writing a second copy of a working RPC would have been exactly the "hand-rolled six access checks" mistake this file already has an entry against). All three new ones share the same director-only caller check, independently re-derived in each function rather than factored into a shared helper — matching the shape every other director-facing RPC in this schema already uses.

  **4. `get_institution_caseload_sizes()` — a count, not a capacity, deliberately.** No denominator exists anywhere in this schema and none was invented.

  **VERIFICATION — real fixture (`ZZPRD5S7DASHA`: a director, three leads with three DIFFERENT scope shapes, a real approved practitioner, a real pending one, two clients — one assigned and tagged, one deliberately unassigned; `ZZPRD5S7DASHB`: a second, genuinely separate clinic with its own director), 22/22, real signed-in sessions throughout.**

  **The "prove they agree" requirement, satisfied directly, not asserted.** Three leads, three genuinely different scope shapes tested against the same tagged episode: Lead A1 (AND-across-dimensions, funding=Tusla AND location=Dublin, both present — in scope), Lead A2 (OR-within-a-dimension, funding=Tusla OR HSE, Tusla present — in scope), Lead A3 (scoped to location=Carlow, the episode is Dublin — a genuine negative, correctly out of scope). For each, `get_episode_overseeing_leads()`'s own answer was checked against `_lead_episode_in_scope()`'s answer obtained via the REAL caller path (`get_institution_episode_roster_for_lead()`, that lead's own signed-in session) — not the internal function called directly, and not inferred from one case. All three agreed.

  **The buckets, proven both full and correctly empty in the same pass, not just "returns something":** the no-active-practitioner bucket showed the unassigned client and correctly excluded the assigned one; pending-staff-joins showed the real unapproved practitioner and correctly excluded the approved one; draft-FBAs showed the real draft and correctly excluded a second, COMPLETED FBA on the other client; caseload size read exactly 1, not 2, proving the deliberately-unassigned client was genuinely excluded rather than the count being coincidentally right.

  **THE ONE THAT MATTERS MOST, proven per RPC, not once and inferred: every one of the five new functions plus the reused `get_pending_tag_change_requests()` was called by a real second director's own session, at a real second clinic, against the FIRST clinic's own institution/episode id.** The per-episode lookup (`get_episode_overseeing_leads`) correctly raised an exception rather than returning another clinic's leads — the right refusal shape for a single-target authorization check. The four institution-scoped list RPCs all returned genuinely empty, not an error — the right shape for a list RPC authorized via `exists()` in its own `where` clause. A control eliminated the one remaining doubt: the SAME director's OWN clinic (genuinely empty) also returned nothing from the no-active-practitioner bucket, confirming the cross-clinic empty results were a real scoping refusal and not an artefact of Clinic B happening to have no data either way.

  Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution codes first.

  **PRD 5 closes here — all seven stages done.** The director's own dashboard SCREEN (section 10's own third deferred item, alongside tag configuration and the change-request queue) is a future design pass, not scoped or started as part of this build.

  **THE SCREEN ITSELF — BUILT, DURING PRD 9 STAGE 1, MIGRATION 0257 RUN. CLOSED. 20 Sept 2026.** Picked up as a side effect of PRD 9's own Step 0 recon needing a real home for `set_clinic_hours`/`set_booking_buffer_minutes`/`set_booking_window_days` — rather than park a third unrelated UI gap, the RPC layer this stage already shipped (0227) finally got its screen. `ClinicDirectorDashboard.tsx`, rendered on `/principal/dashboard` when `institutionType === 'clinic'` (a school principal's own dashboard is completely untouched — a separate component, not a conditional branch inside it). Reuses the principal's own work-queue pattern verbatim (`WorkQueueRow`, the entity/exception/context/action shape, the single "nothing needs your attention" empty state) rather than inventing a second dashboard idiom.

  **Why this needed building at all, stated in the component's own header and worth repeating here**: the school dashboard sitting beside it looks unfinished for a clinic only by coincidence — every one of its own buckets is incident/class-derived, and a clinic can never produce either. Left unbuilt, a clinical director's dashboard would have read "All clear." permanently, not because nothing needed attention but because nothing it looked at could ever be true for a clinic — a dashboard lying to someone, not an empty one.

  **Three standing constraints, Daniel's own, not negotiable, recorded so nobody "improves" them later without asking**: nothing comparative between practitioners (caseload size is a reference-only count, alphabetical, never sorted by size — `get_institution_caseload_sizes()`'s own 0257 comment explains why descending-by-size was wrong the moment it became a list); organised by CLIENT and by OUTSTANDING WORK, never by staff member (every bucket's own Entity column is the child, clinician name is attribution text only, never a groupable/sortable axis); capacity, funded placements, and overdue reviews all stay out — no denominator, no funding dimension, no threshold exists anywhere in this schema for any of the three, and none are invented here. `get_my_bookings_needing_attention()` (PRD 9) is deliberately never surfaced here either — it's clinician-facing by design ("their calendar, their session, only they can tell"), and pulling it onto a director's dashboard would contradict that decision, not extend it.

  Migration 0257 itself: `get_institution_caseload_sizes()` reordered alphabetically (same signature, bare `CREATE OR REPLACE`) plus three genuinely new buckets in the same shape Stage 7's own five functions already established (director-only caller check, independently re-derived per function). Room deliberately left for PRD 8's own stagnation queue — this page's layout (one outstanding-work section, then a separate reference section) assumes nothing about that queue's shape, so adding it later is a new section, not a rework.

- **PRD 5 STAGE 6 — CLINICIAN VERIFICATION + CASELOAD ASSIGNMENT. DONE, MIGRATIONS RUN, VERIFIED LIVE (TARGETED FIXTURE 46/46, FULL ADVERSARIAL SUITE 1157/1157), CLOSED. 17 Sept 2026.** The build against the "structurally inert role" finding immediately below (own dedicated entry, not repeated here) and the "does a clinic practitioner need both rows" recon question that opened the stage. `0221`–`0224`: `verification_route` (`'behaviour_hive' | 'organisation'`), six raw `verification_status = 'verified'` checks consolidated onto `is_verified_clinician()`, `approve_staff_join()`'s own new branch (a director-approved clinic practitioner's approval IS their verification — no separate credential review, matching class_teacher/SNA's own zero-extra-verification precedent), `select_clinician_specialty()`'s reset made route-aware (an organisation-verified practitioner correcting their own specialty must never silently un-verify themselves), and `bulk_grant_clinician_access()`'s new roster path (`p_roster_user_id`, clinic-only, resolves a practitioner directly via `institution_staff` — no `clinician_code` involved, since a director-approved practitioner never has one).

  **A real regression, found by the verification fixture itself, not a review pass: `0221` rewrote `approve_clinician()` from migration `0029`'s own original text without checking for `0030`, which already existed and fixed the exact function being touched** — `0029`'s `returns table (clinician_code text)` makes PL/pgSQL treat `clinician_code` as an implicit variable in the function's own namespace, colliding with the unqualified `where clinician_code = v_code` in the code-generation loop; `0030` renamed the output column to `code` and table-qualified the reference. `0221` silently reverted both, so `approve_clinician()` failed on the very first genuinely-new clinician it was asked to approve — precisely this file's own "read the live definition, not the first one you find" mistake, made in the one function that already had a dedicated fix for it. Fixed in `0225`, restoring `0030`'s shape and keeping `0221`'s own genuine addition (`verification_route`).

  **A permanent check added for exactly this failure mode, per Daniel's own instruction: "it was broken and a fixture caught it, not a check."** `approve_clinician()` has no client caller and no RLS to exercise it — nothing else in the adversarial suite ever called it, which is precisely why the regression sat live until a one-off fixture happened to hit it. `CHECK SSS` (11 assertions): success on a genuinely fresh clinician (forces the code-generation loop — the exact regression), the `code` return-shape regression anchor, persisted `verification_route`, refusing an already-verified or unknown clinician, and the existing-code-reuse branch combined with `0223`'s own route-aware pending reset. Run standalone (11/11) and as part of the full suite.

  **VERIFICATION — targeted fixture (`ZZPRD5S6CLINIC`: director + a REAL self-linked-then-approved practitioner + a second practitioner deliberately left unapproved as a regression control; `ZZPRD5S6SCHOOL`: principal; an independent clinician; a parent), 46/46, real signed-in sessions throughout.** The full join → approve → use sequence proven end to end with the real practitioner: `get_clinician_passports()` returns their caseload, they read the client's `passports` row and `passport_section_b`, they log and read back a real ABC entry, they appear on `get_passport_clinicians()` and `get_institution_clinicians()` (`covered_child_count` correct). `verification_route` proven on all three paths (organisation, behaviour_hive-fresh, behaviour_hive-backfilled). The specialty reset proven both directions. Roster-path boundaries (zero/two params, wrong institution type, non-clinician target, unapproved target) all refuse correctly. Existing code-based paths (parent connect, school bulk-assign) unaffected. All 7 consolidated functions confirmed behaviourally identical, including correctly EXCLUDING the never-approved practitioner from both listing RPCs. `get_passport_team()` tested directly and found empty, as expected — see the dedicated Stage 7 blocker entry immediately above, recorded rather than folded into this fix.

  **Full adversarial suite: 1157/1157, 69 distinct blocks (cross-checked against the file's own header list, zero missing), zero `SCRIPT ERROR`, rate limit raised and reverted per the standing gate rule.** Fixture torn down, zero orphans, checked the suite for the fixture's own institution codes first per standing rule.

  **The deployed-SHA caveat, stated plainly rather than left implied:** none of the three commits this verification pass produced (`2152081` the `approve_clinician()` fix, `a2dc218` the new check, `844d76f` this file) touch client-served code — the only behaviour-relevant change (`0225`) was applied directly against Supabase, independent of any Vercel deploy. Confirmed by Daniel to block nothing.

- **PRD 5 STAGE 2 SHIPPED A ROLE THAT IS STRUCTURALLY INERT — NOT "CASELOAD ASSIGNMENT IS BLOCKED," THE ROLE DOES NOT WORK AT ALL. FOUND DURING STAGE 6 RECON, FIXED IN STAGE 6. 17 Sept 2026.** A clinic practitioner (Stage 2, `institution_staff` role `clinician`, self-linked by organisation code, director-approved) could join and be approved and then reach NOTHING: not their own caseload, not a passport, not an ABC log, not the activity feed — and they were invisible to their own director and to parents, appearing in no roster or team listing anywhere. Traced exhaustively, not sampled: `is_verified_clinician()`/`verification_status = 'verified'` gates the entire clinician track — `clinician_access`'s own SELECT policy (a practitioner couldn't even see their own grant), ABC log read/write, activity log read/write, `passports` and `passport_section_b/c/d` SELECT, and critically `get_clinician_passports()` — the practitioner's OWN caseload list. A Stage-2 practitioner has no `clinicians` row at all, so every one of these evaluates false, silently, for every real thing the role exists to do.

  **The same gap as the `clinical_lead_scope` RLS finding, one stage earlier, in a different shape: Stage 2's own verification proved the mechanism ABOVE the thing — the join, the approval, the role constraint — and never asked whether the role could then do anything with what it had just been given.** Real accounts joined, were really approved, held a real `institution_staff` row with the right role — and none of that was ever exercised against a single clinician-track screen or RPC to confirm the role actually functions. Both gaps share the identical root cause named in this file's own "prove the policy with a real session doing the real operation" entry — verification exercised what was BUILT that stage, never what the built thing was supposed to enable.

  **The count Daniel asked for, before any decision about consolidating**: of the clinician-track access gates, **8 were raw `verification_status = 'verified'` checks, hand-rolled instead of calling `is_verified_clinician()`, across 7 distinct live functions** — `lookup_clinician_by_code()`, `connect_clinician()`, `grant_clinician_access()`, `get_passport_team()`, `get_passport_clinicians()`, `get_institution_clinicians()`, and `bulk_grant_clinician_access()` (two, one per resolution path). Found by tracing every historical occurrence to its LIVE (highest-numbered, non-superseded) version — several earlier raw checks in now-replaced functions were correctly excluded as dead code, not counted. The much larger remaining surface (ABC logs, activity log, FBA workspace, calm cards, messaging, passport sections — dozens of policies and functions) already calls the helper correctly. Same shape as the six hand-rolled access-check copies this file already has an entry for; fixed in `0221` regardless of anything else in this stage, per Daniel's own instruction.

  **THE FIX, `0222`: a clinic practitioner's own director-approval IS their verification** — the same relationship a principal's approval already has to a class teacher's standing, no separate credential review, because there is no credential to review. `0029`'s own header states plainly why the gate exists: closing a real exploit where an INDEPENDENT practitioner, with nobody else vouching for them, could self-declare credentials. A clinic practitioner has a director, and the clinic itself already passed Behaviour Hive's own manual institution-verification gate (this file's own "institution creation being manual is deliberate" entry) — forcing them through a credential check nobody performs would be theatre, confirmed by tracing the actual reason the gate was built, not assumed from its name.

  **`verification_route` (`'behaviour_hive'` | `'organisation'`), not a second overloaded meaning for the same string.** Reusing `'verified'` was the right call — it keeps all fourteen-plus existing gated call sites working with zero changes, which is what actually matters — but a clinical record system where a practitioner's own standing may genuinely be asked about should be able to answer "verified how" directly from the data, not by inferring it from whether an `institution_staff` row happens to exist. Nullable — a pending or rejected clinician has no route yet. Backfilled to `'behaviour_hive'` for every pre-existing verified row, as a fact: no other path has ever existed until `0222`. Write-site commented, per the `approved_by_parent` precedent this file already documents.

  **`specialty`'s own `'unspecified'` placeholder, added deliberately, not defaulted to a real value.** The column is `NOT NULL` with a six-value CHECK, and the auto-created `clinicians` row has to satisfy that before the practitioner has ever been asked their own specialty. Defaulting to `'behavioural_psychologist'` (the one specialty the independent path actually supports) would have been a false claim about a real qualification — the identical "a column name is a claim" failure this file already documents for `approved_by_parent`. `'unspecified'` says plainly that nothing has been asserted; `select_clinician_specialty()` (already built, unchanged in shape) is how a practitioner corrects it.

  **The interaction found BEFORE it could bite, not after — `select_clinician_specialty()`'s own reset-to-`pending`-on-change logic (`0029`) is correct for the independent path and wrong for this one.** A specialty change legitimately means "re-review this new claim" when Behaviour Hive is the reviewer; it means nothing of the kind when a director's approval is the verification. Left alone, the first clinic practitioner who corrected a typo in their own specialty would have silently un-verified themselves — presenting as "the app randomly stopped working for her," with nothing in the UI to explain why. Fixed in `0223`: the reset only fires when `verification_route` is anything other than `'organisation'`.

  **`0224`: the actual caseload-assignment mechanism, not just the verification fix.** PRD section 4 says reuse `clinician_access`/`bulk_grant_clinician_access()` — but that function's own lookup (a clinician code, or an existing engagement) was built for an EXTERNAL practitioner a school doesn't otherwise employ, and a clinic director assigning their own roster practitioner has neither. Added a third resolution path, `p_roster_user_id` — clinic-only, resolves directly via `institution_staff` (role `clinician`, current standing, at this same institution), the same way a principal already picks a class teacher or SNA from their own roster rather than by code. Signature grew, so `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS` first, per this file's own standing rule. No new roster-listing RPC needed — `get_institution_staff_roster()` (`0097`) already lists every `institution_staff` row with role and name; filtered client-side to `role = 'clinician'`, it already serves this.

  **Migrations `0221`–`0224` written, not yet run. Recorded before building, per Daniel's own instruction — this entry documents the finding and the fix design; live verification with a real fixture (a fresh clinic practitioner reaching their own caseload, a passport, an ABC log, and appearing in their director's own roster — the exact things Stage 2 never actually tested) is still owed once the migrations run.**

- **PRD 5 STAGE 5 — CHANGE REQUESTS. DONE, MIGRATIONS RUN, VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE (47/47), CLOSED. 17 Sept 2026.** An admin sets tags at onboarding; every later change is a request a director approves, or a lead approves within their own scope for a non-scoping change. Recon reported and confirmed by Daniel in full before any code was written (including the dimension-level scoping entry immediately above); this entry is the build against those confirmed answers.

  **`0218`: the request table, and `_replace_episode_tags()` extracted from `set_episode_tags()` (0214) before a second write path could hand-roll the identical validate-and-write logic a different way — this build's own "six hand-rolled copies" lesson applied before the copies exist, not after.** `tag_change_requests`' closest existing shape is `passport_completion_requests`/`fba_instrument_requests` (a standalone "someone is asking something of someone else" table with its own lifecycle), not `institution_staff`'s own join-approval columns, which are about the requester's own membership, a different kind of thing. `proposed_tags` is a full replacement set, matching `set_episode_tags()`'s own replace-not-merge semantics exactly — a request models the same operation a director's direct write already does. `base_tags` snapshots the episode's own tags AS THEY WERE at raise time, for the race named below. No client-facing write policy at all — raise/approve/decline are the only paths, matching `episodes_of_care`'s own established convention.

  **THE RACE DANIEL NAMED, NOT COVERED BY THE ORIGINAL RECON: what if the episode's tags change between raise and approval?** A director retagging a client and an admin's pending request are both ordinary, not hypothetical — approving a request whose `proposed_tags` were computed before that retag would silently overwrite the director's own change with a set computed against a moment that no longer exists. Fixed by comparing `base_tags` to the CURRENT real `episode_tags`, fresh, inside `approve_tag_change_request()` itself (`0220`) — if they still match, nothing has moved underneath the request and it applies; if they don't, the approval refuses outright and the request stays pending, surfaced rather than merged or silently overwritten. Same compare-and-swap idiom this schema already uses everywhere else a write can race another write. Deliberately not an override an approver can force through after seeing the drift — the only way past a conflict is a fresh request raised against current reality, which gets its own fresh, correct snapshot. Considered and rejected as insufficient on their own: a blind last-write-wins apply (silently loses whichever write happened first), and a "one pending request per episode" constraint (would stop two requests conflicting with each other, but not the more common case Daniel actually named — a *direct* write racing a *pending* request, which never touches the requests table at all).

  **`0219`: raising, and the scoping-touch check.** Any active clinic staff member may raise a request — Daniel's own instruction: raising changes nothing, so the boundary belongs entirely on approval, and a narrower "who's even allowed to ask" rule buys no real safety for real complexity. `_tag_change_touches_scoping_dimension()` computes the symmetric difference between `base_tags` and `proposed_tags` — only dimensions that actually CHANGE, not every dimension either full set happens to mention (an artefact of full-replacement semantics: restating an unchanged scoping-dimension value alongside a genuine change elsewhere must not spuriously require director approval for a dimension nothing touched). Refused on an already-ended episode — PRD section 8's "the old one stays, tagged as it was" leaves nothing to request a change to.

  **`0220`: deciding.** `approve_tag_change_request()` does NOT call `set_episode_tags()` as the approver's own session — a lead approving a request has no authority to call that function at all, since its own gate is director-always/admin-once-at-onboarding, unrelated to request approval. It calls `_replace_episode_tags()` directly, under its OWN authorization: director always; a `clinical_lead` only when the request does not touch a scoping dimension, `lead_can_approve_non_scoping_tag_changes` is on, AND the specific episode is within that lead's own current scope — both halves required. `decline_tag_change_request()` shares the identical gate, matching `approve_staff_join()`/`reject_staff_join()`'s own symmetric shape; no staleness check needed there, since nothing writes to `episode_tags` either way. `get_pending_tag_change_requests()` is infrastructure only, mirroring `get_institution_episode_roster()`'s own shape — PRD section 10 names the actual queue screen as one of three things (alongside the director's dashboard and tag configuration's own UI) that get designed later, once the foundation is used; every prior stage in this build has been backend-only, and this one matches.

  **VERIFICATION — real fixture (`ZZPRD5S5CLINIC`: director, one lead scoped to `funding=Tusla` only, one admin, one practitioner), 47/47, real signed-in sessions throughout, service-role reads to confirm persisted state.**

  **The race, proven in three parts, not one.** Raised a request, then had the director directly retag the SAME episode while it sat pending, then attempted approval — refused, drift surfaced, `episode_tags` left exactly as the director's own direct write set it (confirmed via `created_by` on the resulting rows: the director's own id, not the requester's). **Confirmed there is genuinely no override**: retried the identical approval a second time against the same stale snapshot — refused again, identically; the function takes no "apply anyway" parameter, and the request stayed pending both times. Then raised a fresh request against the now-current tags, with nothing intervening, and approved it cleanly — proving the staleness check itself isn't just always failing, only genuinely stale ones are.

  **The subtle case, the one most likely to be wrong, proven directly**: a request restating an unchanged scoping value (`funding=Tusla`, identical to what was already there) alongside a genuine non-scoping change (a real location move) — the lead approved it successfully, and a service-role read confirmed `funding` was untouched while `location` changed. The symmetric-difference logic correctly excludes a dimension nothing actually changed, even when that dimension is the scoping one and appears in both the old and new sets.

  **Scoping evaluated live, not cached from raise time, proven by actually moving it between the two moments**: raised a request touching `service` while `service` was still non-scoping, then had the director scope the same lead onto `service` too, then attempted approval as that lead — now refused, on the identical request, because the ONLY thing that changed between raise and approval was the clinic-wide fact of whether `service` counts as scoping. The director approved it without issue regardless.

  **Point 5's own guarantee, confirmed directly**: the lead was refused approving a `funding` change (the dimension they're scoped on) even though the specific episode was genuinely within their own scope — the exact case the whole dimension-level design exists to close, not inferred from the other tests but proven with its own dedicated request.

  **The rest**: toggle off refused, toggle on succeeds on the identical request; an out-of-scope episode refused for the lead even for a non-scoping change, approved cleanly by the director; a practitioner raised a request for a client with zero `clinician_access` rows connecting them at all — raising confirmed free of any caseload restriction; decline requires a real reason (empty refused), shares the identical authorization gate as approve, and leaves `episode_tags` untouched; deciding an already-decided request a second time refused; raising against an already-ended episode refused; raising with an unknown tag value refused. `get_pending_tag_change_requests()` correctly listed only the one request genuinely still stuck pending at the end (the original raced one, deliberately never re-decided, distinct from its own later clean replacement).

  Fixture torn down (`scripts/dev/zz-prd5-stage5-verify-teardown.mjs`), zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution code first.

  **Stage 5 closes here.** Stage 6 (caseload assignment, reusing the bulk-assign mechanism) is next per the PRD's own sequencing — but see the standing "STAGE 6 BLOCKER" entry above (`bulk_grant_clinician_access()` resolves a clinician via the `clinicians` table, the independent-practitioner path this session closed off at signup; a Stage-2-style practitioner may have no `clinicians` row at all) — that question needs answering before Stage 6 can actually start, not rediscovering from scratch.

- **PRD 5 STAGE 4 — TAGS. DONE, MIGRATIONS RUN, VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE (37/37), CLOSED. 17 Sept 2026.** Dimensions, values, episode tagging, and the thing that makes `clinical_lead` mean something — scope finally has real values to match against. Two authority rules settled by Daniel before any code was written (AND across a lead's own distinct scope dimensions, OR within one; two leads matching the same client both get authority, no tie-break), built in `0213`–`0216`, then a real, previously-undiscovered `clinical_lead_scope` RLS bug found live during verification and fixed in `0217` (its own dedicated CLAUDE.md entries, above: "A POLICY THAT LOOKS UP SOMEONE ELSE…" and the verification-lesson entry beside it — not repeated here).

  **`0213`/`0214`: the tag data model.** `institution_tags` (the catalog — director-writable, per clinic, matching `institution_vocabulary_overrides`'s posture rather than `discharge_reasons`'s Behaviour-Hive-controlled one, since PRD section 6 is explicit that clinics configure their own dimensions AND values) and `episode_tags` (the attachment — multi-value by construction, same `(dimension, value)` shape as `clinical_lead_scope` on purpose, so scope-matching is set intersection, not translation). `set_episode_tags()` captures both of section 7's write rules in one RPC: director always, admin only while the episode is genuinely untagged — the "at onboarding" window closes itself the moment it's used once, no separate state tracked. Deliberately excluded: any "is this dimension scoping" flag — that's change-request approval machinery, Stage 5, not decided yet.

  **`0215`/`0216`: scope made real.** `clinical_lead_scope`'s write policy validates every pair against the catalog (closing `0207`'s own left-open seam). `_lead_episode_in_scope()` implements both authority rules: count of the lead's own distinct scope dimensions vs. count of dimensions where the episode's tags intersect — equal and non-zero is in scope, which gives AND-across-dimensions and OR-within-one for free from one piece of arithmetic, and two leads never interact since the function only ever answers "is THIS caller in scope," called once per caller. Wired into `end_clinic_episode()` (the deferred branch Stage 3 left out) and two new read RPCs (`get_child_passport_profile_for_lead`, `get_institution_episode_roster_for_lead`) mirroring the existing principal equivalents (`0160`, `0211`) rather than widening `has_child_access()` — asked explicitly by Daniel, answered in the migration's own header: that chokepoint is `has_class_teacher_access() OR has_sna_access()`, entirely school-side, backing 45 references including write policies, and `0160` already drew this exact line for principal's own read access for the identical reason. Both new RPCs scope to ACTIVE episodes only — a lead's scope is present-tense oversight, not a permanent grant that outlives the client's relationship with the clinic.

  **VERIFICATION — real fixture (`ZZPRD5S4CLINIC`: director, three leads, admin, one practitioner), 37/37, real signed-in sessions throughout, service-role reads to confirm persisted state.** Both halves of the `0217` fix proven directly with real sessions, not inferred: a director's real `INSERT` scoping a DIFFERENT lead (the case that never worked) succeeded; a different staff member (lead B) reading lead A's own scope rows (the inverted read-policy half) succeeded; an invalid catalog pair and a non-director's own scope-write attempt were both refused. `AND across dimensions`: lead A (funding=Tusla AND location=Dublin) saw only the one client matching both, correctly missing clients matching just one. `OR within one dimension`: lead B (funding=Tusla OR funding=HSE, two rows) saw every client matching either value, including one whose tag was only set via a later director retag mid-test — confirmed as the correct, intended match rather than assumed. `Two leads, one client`: one client genuinely visible to both leads A and B at once, no tie-break needed or built. Default-deny confirmed for a lead with zero scope rows. The discharge branch: toggle off refused, wrong lead (out of scope) refused, right lead (in scope, toggle on) succeeded and recorded as `ended_by` the lead themselves; post-discharge the same lead's own roster and read access both dropped the client immediately — present-tense, not sticky. Light regression on the director-always and practitioner-own-caseload branches confirmed unaffected by the `end_clinic_episode()` rewrite. Fixture torn down (`scripts/dev/zz-prd5-stage4-verify-teardown.mjs`), zero orphans, checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's own institution code first.

  **Stage 4 closes here.** Stage 5 (change requests — the queue, approval rules) is next, per the PRD's own sequencing; not started.

- **PRD 5 STAGE 3 — EPISODES OF CARE. DONE, MIGRATIONS RUN, VERIFIED LIVE WITH A REAL FIXTURE. CLOSED. 17 Sept 2026.** Onboarding, discharge, the cascade — recon reported and confirmed by Daniel in full before any code was written; this entry is the build against those confirmed answers.

  **Three migrations.** `0209`: `episodes_of_care` (the table above) and `discharge_reasons` — a vocabulary table, not a CHECK enumeration, deliberately the opposite shape from `enrolments.end_reason`'s literal 3-value CHECK.
