Reason, beyond the `cpi_reason_types` parallel: if every clinic defines its own outcome categories, cross-clinic reporting becomes impossible, and Tusla/HSE returns are exactly what this data eventually feeds — so the vocabulary is Behaviour-Hive-controlled (no client write policy at all, matching `cpi_reason_types`/CLAUDE.md's own closed "CPI VOCABULARY EDITING" decision, not the narrower `incident_locations` exception), not clinic-editable. Seeded with six values — **not clinically authored, flagged for Catherine's review, not a settled vocabulary**: Goals met, Transferred to another provider, Client disengaged, Relocated, No longer eligible, Transitioned to adult services. The last two exist specifically because Daniel named the gap in the first draft (a client who simply disengages, and one who moves to adult services) — not in the original five.

  `0210`: `onboard_clinic_client()` (brand-new client — passport + `passport_institution_links` + episode, one transaction, mirroring `create_school_passport()`/`enrol_child()` exactly) and `reopen_clinic_episode()` (an existing, already-linked client returning — the clinic-side build of the case the school side left parked as "transfer-in," built ONLY for a passport already linked to THIS institution; a passport that exists but was never linked here — genuinely transferring in from elsewhere — stays exactly as out of scope as it always was, that's PRD 8's territory). Both use `institution_staff_has_current_standing()` for the caller check, not hand-written `deactivated_at`/`approved_at` conditions — a deliberate departure from `create_school_passport()`'s own literal text (written before that helper existed), per this file's own standing rule for new code. Authorization: director and admin always, practitioner only when `practitioner_can_onboard` is on (`0207`'s own toggle); `clinical_lead` is not an onboarder — PRD section 5 never names onboarding among a lead's capabilities. `institutions.type = 'clinic'` is checked explicitly in every new function here — `principal`/`clinician` exist at either institution type, so without it a school principal could call a clinic-shaped function against their own school.

  `0211`: `end_clinic_episode()` and the cascade. **The cascade closes caseload only** — `_close_caseload_for_episode_end()` is the SAME clause `_close_child_access_for_enrolment_end()` already runs (0121, extended 0123) for a school ending a school-engaged clinician's access, extracted as its own small helper since a clinic has no `class_children`/`child_assignments`/`passport_access` clauses to sit alongside. Confirmed NOT to touch: `passport_institution_links` (a discharge ends access, not the record the organisation authored — exactly 0121's own reasoning for the school side), `clinical_lead_scope`, the toggles, or any future tag on the episode row (PRD section 8: "the old one stays, tagged as it was"). **Who can discharge, AS OF STAGE 3 ITSELF: director always, practitioner only for their own caseload when `practitioner_can_discharge_own_clients` is on. `clinical_lead` was deliberately NOT a branch here yet, on Daniel's own instruction** — `lead_can_discharge_within_scope` exists (`0207`) but "within scope" means matching tag values on the episode, and every episode Stage 3 creates is untagged (tags are Stage 4). A toggle-plus-scope check that can only ever return false is worse than no check at all. **CORRECTED, PRD 5 Stage 4 recon (Sept 2026): the real branch landed one migration later, in the very same stage this entry deferred it to** — `0216` (`supabase/migrations/0216_clinical_lead_scope_grants_authority.sql`) adds a third authorization branch to `end_clinic_episode()`, `v_caller_role = 'clinical_lead' and lead_can_discharge_within_scope and _lead_episode_in_scope(...)`, exactly the shape this entry's own "once scope has something to match against" anticipated. Confirmed still live and unaffected by every later migration through PRD 10 Stage 5. This paragraph was found stale — still describing the toggle as unbacked — during PRD 10 Stage 5's own recon, a full migration set after the fix landed; left here as the historical record of the Stage 3 decision, with this correction attached rather than silently rewritten, so a future reader sees both the original reasoning and the point it stopped applying. Also added: `get_institution_episode_roster()`, a minimal, role-agnostic list of active episodes — not a "surface" in the sense PRD section 10 defers (the director's dashboard), just the infrastructure `end_clinic_episode()` needs to have an `episode_id` to call with at all, mirroring `get_institution_child_roster()`'s own shape exactly.

  **Untagged is not incomplete.** An episode created in Stage 3 has no tags and needs none to be a valid, complete episode — the same relationship `institutionType.ts` had to Stage 1, or `clinical_lead_scope` had to Stage 2: infrastructure built ahead of the stage that gives it its full meaning, correct and inert in between.

  **VERIFICATION — migrations 0209/0210/0211 run by Daniel, then a real fixture (clinic `ZZPRD5S3CLINIC`: director + three practitioners; school `ZZPRD5S3SCHOOL`: one principal), 26/26 assertions, real signed-in sessions for every RPC call, service-role reads only to confirm persisted state, never inferred from a return value.**

  **`get_child_passport_profile_for_principal()` (0160) confirmed working for a clinic director, unmodified, by testing it rather than reading it, per Daniel's own explicit instruction.** A real director session read a real client's Section A/B/C/D content through the exact RPC written for PRD 1's school principal, zero changes. **Recorded here as INTENDED BEHAVIOUR: this RPC's own auth check is `role = 'principal'` + `institution_staff` + `passport_institution_links` — no institution-type branch, and none is needed. The moment `onboard_clinic_client()` creates a `passport_institution_links` row, a clinical director inherits read access through a function nobody touched.** A negative control in the same pass confirmed the gate is real, not coincidental: a practitioner (`role = 'clinician'`) at the SAME clinic, same client, got zero rows back from this RPC — it is genuinely `principal`-gated, not "any clinic staff." Anyone touching this function in future should know a clinic depends on its current shape exactly as written.

  **The cascade, a real two-practitioner caseload, discharged by the director**: both `clinician_access` rows closed (`is_active = false`, `revoked_at`/`revoked_by` set, reason recorded); `passport_institution_links` confirmed untouched; the episode row intact with its own `end_reason`/`ended_by`; a second client at the same clinic, same practitioner, confirmed entirely unaffected — proving the WHERE clause is scoped to the discharged passport, not the clinic or the practitioner.

  **The constraint that matters most, proven directly**: a passport created via the real `create_school_passport()` (active `enrolments` row) then given a clinic-side `passport_institution_links` + `episodes_of_care` row — both inserts succeeded with no conflict, and both an active `enrolments` row and an active `episodes_of_care` row were confirmed to exist for the same passport at the same time. Worth stating plainly rather than manufacturing suspense: there was never going to BE a conflict, because they're separate tables — that IS the fix this stage's own uniqueness finding produced. No RPC exists yet to attach an already-existing passport to a new organisation (that's PRD 8's cross-org territory, deliberately not built here), so the clinic-side inserts were made directly, mirroring exactly what `onboard_clinic_client()` does internally for a brand-new passport — a direct proof of the schema's own capability, not a claim that a cross-org user journey exists.

  **Reopening**: discharge, then `reopen_clinic_episode()` on the same client — confirmed a genuinely new episode id (not the old row reactivated), the old episode intact with its own end reason, the new one active, and exactly ONE `passport_institution_links` row throughout — not duplicated on reopen.

  **Who can discharge, all four cases, real sessions**: a practitioner on their own caseload with the toggle OFF — refused. Toggle ON, a DIFFERENT practitioner not on that caseload — refused. Toggle ON, the right practitioner, their own client — allowed, and the episode recorded them (not the director) as `ended_by`. The episode was confirmed still active between the two refusals, proving neither refusal silently half-succeeded.

  **School side**: a real `create_school_passport()` + `end_enrolment()` round trip, unmodified by anything in this stage, behaved exactly as it always has.

  **Fixture torn down** (`scripts/dev/zz-prd5-stage3-verify-teardown.mjs`), zero orphans confirmed — checked the adversarial suite for the fixture's institution codes/email prefix first, per this file's own standing rule, before deleting.

  **Full adversarial suite NOT run in this pass.** This session has no Supabase Management API credential (`.env.local` carries only the project's own anon and service-role keys) — raising and reverting the Auth rate limit is a Dashboard-only action only Daniel can perform, the same constraint this file already documents elsewhere. The six targeted checks above used five real accounts, safely under the 30-per-5-minute limit with no change needed. Per this file's own gate rule, this stage touches the database (three new migrations, four new functions) and so still owes a full run — pending Daniel raising the rate limit.

- **STAGE 6 BLOCKER, FOUND DURING STAGE 3 RECON, NOT SOLVED — RECORDED NOW SO IT ISN'T REDISCOVERED FROM SCRATCH.** `bulk_grant_clinician_access()` (`0151`, "the bulk-assign screen" PRD 5 section 4 points at for caseload assignment) resolves which clinician to a `clinicians` table row — by `clinician_code`, or by already being `engaged_by = 'institution'` at the calling institution — the INDEPENDENT-practitioner path this session just closed off at signup (see the "no independent clinician" correction above). A Stage 2 clinic practitioner has an `institution_staff` row (role `clinician`, self-linked by organisation code) and may have NO `clinicians` row at all — nothing in Stage 2 created one, and the route that used to create one for a self-service clinician (the specialty/credential-verification flow) is exactly the path PRD 5's practitioners don't take. Before Stage 6 (caseload assignment) can actually work for a Stage-2-style practitioner, someone has to answer: does a clinic practitioner need BOTH rows — `institution_staff` for membership, `clinicians` for specialty/code, kept in sync — or does `bulk_grant_clinician_access()`'s own lookup need a second path that resolves a practitioner via `institution_staff` (role `clinician` at this same clinic) the way a school assigns its own roster, never touching `clinicians` at all? Leaning toward "both rows, deliberately" is plausible (`institution_staff` = membership, `clinicians` = specialty/code, matching Stage 2's own membership/caseload separation) but that reopens a question already closed: a practitioner joining by organisation code would then need to ACQUIRE a `clinicians` row somehow, through a route that isn't the specialty-picker (that's the independent path) and isn't a Behaviour-Hive manual step either (unlike the institution-employed-clinician-verification gap, already parked, this would block EVERY practitioner, not one). Not solved here, deliberately, per Daniel's own instruction — record the shape of the question, not a guess at the answer.

- **PRD 5 STAGE 2 — ROLES AND TOGGLES. DONE, DEPLOYED, MIGRATIONS RUN AND VERIFIED, CONSENT SCREENS WIRED IN AND VERIFIED LIVE. CLOSED. 17 Sept 2026.** Four roles: clinical director (`principal`, reused), practitioner (`clinician`, reused), `clinical_lead` and `clinic_admin` (new values). Step 0 recon (77 functions, 98 comparison lines, 8 multi-value enumerations, 5 other CHECK constraints — recorded under the Stage 1 entry below) found the shape of the work; this entry is the build.

  **THE PREMISE, AND WHY IT MATTERED ENOUGH TO STOP AND RESOLVE FIRST.** The recon's own opening assumption — "practitioners never touch `institution_staff.role`, unchanged by this PRD" — was wrong, caught by Daniel directly rather than in review after the fact. PRD 5 section 4 says "reuse `institution_staff` entirely" for clinic staff, and section 3 describes practitioners "joining by organisation code, approved by the director" — exactly what `institution_staff` already does for `class_teacher`/`sna`. The recon's own finding 4 (practitioners invisible to staff-to-staff messaging) was misdiagnosed as a messaging-function problem; it was the premise. **Resolved: a clinic practitioner IS an `institution_staff` row with `role = 'clinician'`.** Membership (`institution_staff` — joining, approval, standing, deactivation, handover) and caseload (`clinician_access`, `engaged_by = 'institution'`, migration 0123, unchanged) are separate, already-answered questions — reuse, not a new mechanism, on either count.

  **THE DEPARTURE GAP — A LIVE GAP FROM THE MOMENT PRACTITIONERS BECAME INSTITUTION_STAFF, NOT A THEORETICAL ONE.** `_close_child_access_for_departure()` (called by both `deactivate_institution_staff()` and `hand_over_principal()`'s own "leaving" path) closes `passport_access`, `class_teachers`, and `child_assignments` on a staff member's departure — all school-specific mechanisms. It never touched `clinician_access` at all, because no clinician had ever gone through this path before school-engaged clinicians were never `institution_staff`. The instant migration `0203` made `clinician` a legal `institution_staff.role` value, this became real: deactivating a clinic practitioner would leave their entire caseload accessible indefinitely, invisible only because nothing had exercised the path yet. Checked directly against migration `0123`'s own explicit comment ("`deactivate_institution_staff()` and `hand_over_principal()` are NOT touched... the engagement belongs to the institution, not the staff member who happened to click grant") before touching anything — that passage is about a GRANTOR's departure never affecting a GRANTEE's continued access ("the same way a class survives its teacher leaving"); this is the GRANTEE's own departure ending their own access, the identical shape `passport_access` already gets a few lines above in the same function. Not a contradiction of 0123's own reasoning — a different question it never addressed. Fixed in `0206`.

  **Migrations 0203–0207, written, committed, PUSHED, NOT YET RUN** (same Dashboard-SQL-editor step as 0200–0202; this session has no direct DB connection). Pushed as two separate commits deliberately, per the ordering lesson recorded under Stage 1's own entry below: all five migrations in one commit (`1a9e98a`) with zero dependent client code; a second, later commit (`295e1ca`) extends `vocabulary.ts` with the two new roles' PRD-decided default labels (`clinical_lead` → "Clinical Lead", `clinic_admin` → "Admin") — a pure constant with no query and no dependency on whether the migrations have run, since nothing can produce a `clinical_lead`/`clinic_admin` row until they do.

  **What the five migrations do, resolving Step 0's own enumeration answers:**
  - `institution_staff_role_check` widened: `clinician`, `clinical_lead`, `clinic_admin`.
  - The self-link INSERT policy — Stage 1's own recon named this a real security finding, not just a widening — made institution-TYPE-aware: a school accepts exactly its own four historical values, a clinic accepts `principal` plus the three clinic-only values, neither accepts the other's exclusive set. A flat wider list would have let someone self-link as `clinician` at a SCHOOL, bypassing that school's own real, verification-gated clinician mechanism entirely.
  - `hand_over_principal()` / `principal_handovers.staying_role` — same type-aware treatment. The clinic's own valid staying-role set turned out to be THREE-wide (`clinician`, `clinical_lead`, `clinic_admin`), not two like the school's, since a departing director going back to clinical practice is a real case the school parallel never had to consider.
  - `get_institution_staff_candidates()` / `send_message()`'s staff-to-staff branch widened — flat, no type-branch needed here, since a clinic-only role value can only ever exist at a clinic institution once the self-link policy above gates creation correctly.
  - `messages_sender_role_check` / `message_recipients_recipient_role_check` widened for `clinical_lead`/`clinic_admin` (`clinician` already present from the existing school-engaged-clinician messaging).
  - `_close_child_access_for_departure()` — the departure-gap fix above.
  - `clinical_lead_scope` (new table) — stored now, not waiting for tags (Stage 4): every one of a lead's own described capabilities is explicitly scope-gated, so without somewhere to store scope, Stage 2 couldn't build the code paths for any of them. `dimension`/`value` are plain text, no FK into a tags table that doesn't exist yet — the same hook-point pattern `institutionType.ts` already used successfully in Stage 1. Zero scope rows = zero elevated authority (default-deny), the honest state until a director populates it. Write policy is director-only, direct action — a lead's own scope is an authority grant, not routed through the change-request queue that governs an episode's tags (PRD section 7).
  - Five named toggles, director-only, as plain boolean columns on `institutions` (matching the `temporary_access_cutoff_time` precedent already live there) rather than a generic table — tags are clinic-defined and open-ended; toggles are a small, fixed, Behaviour-Hive-defined set a clinic only ever sets.

  **Messaging categories — decided, not yet built: translate the labels, don't add a second category set.** "Cover / Rota" and "General" already read fine for a clinic unchanged; "Class / Roster" becomes "Caseload" for a clinic. The STORED category value/row stays exactly as-is (one mechanism, not two); the DISPLAYED label goes through the same translation function role labels already use. Cheaper than a parallel clinic-specific category set, same reasoning as the role-label consolidation in Stage 1.

  **Consent — four screens touched, not two, and the wider net was the right catch.** Drafting the two genuinely new screens (`clinical_lead`, `clinic_admin`) surfaced that the two EXISTING screens reused by role value (`ClinicianAgreementScreen`, `PrincipalAgreementScreen`) also needed a clinic-type variant — most urgently `PrincipalAgreementScreen`, whose own emphasis line ("Countersigning an incident closes it permanently") centres on a concept with no clinic equivalent at all; a real clinical director's very first consent screen would have opened on it. Daniel's own framing: finding this now, rather than a real director hitting it first, was "the right catch." Deliberately did NOT invent a dramatic "this closes permanently" moment for the director's own screen to mirror the school version's shape — manufacturing a parallel where none is decided would have been worse than leaving it absent. Two correctness passes on the two new screens: `ClinicalLeadAgreementScreen`'s draft mixed "children" and "clients" in one screen (fixed to `clients` throughout) and conflated caseload with scope — a lead's caseload is their own practitioner-level relationship to specific clients; their SCOPE is separate authority that can reach clients never on their personal caseload at all, and the copy now says both, distinctly. `ClinicAdminAgreementScreen`'s lede used an em dash, against this codebase's own established house style (the five already-shipped screens consistently use a plain hyphen construction, never an em dash) — fixed, plus one the same pass caught in the body that Daniel hadn't named directly. Also fixed, per Daniel's own exact replacement text: `ClinicianAgreementScreen`'s "School staff never see them" line broke the onboarding restructure's own neutrality rule (a clinic must never be able to infer that schools use this product) while also being factually wrong for a clinic (the clinical director CAN read session notes, and by extension home logs, per PRD's own decided clinical-oversight model) — replaced with wording that states what DOES cross without naming who's on the other side. `consents_role_check` stays untouched until the corrected copy is approved and the screens are wired in, per this file's own now-standing "don't widen a constraint the copy it depends on isn't ready for" rule from Stage 1.

  **`consents_role_check` widened (migration `0208`, run and confirmed alone, per the standing ordering rule), then the four screens wired into `src/app/consent/page.tsx`** — `ConsentRole` widened, `institutionType` resolved from the user's own `institution_staff` row (the record, matching Stage 1's `RoleLabel` rule, not the viewer), switch cases added for `clinical_lead`/`clinic_admin`. Fixed a real bug while wiring: `hasJoined()` for `role = 'clinician'` now checks BOTH `institution_staff` (Stage 2's clinic path) and `clinicians.specialty` (the original independent/manually-verified path) — checking only one would wrongly say "not joined" for whichever path a given clinician didn't take. Found and removed a genuine copy redundancy in `ClinicianAgreementScreen.tsx` while wiring Daniel's approved home-logs text (the old "publish guidance, not raw account" sentence duplicated what the new approved text already said).

  **A SECOND, INDEPENDENT BUG FOUND DURING VERIFICATION, UNRELATED TO ANYTHING WRITTEN THIS STAGE — AND IT BROKE THE CONSENT SCREENS FOR EXACTLY THE TWO NEW ROLES.** `src/app/login/LoginContent.tsx`'s own sign-in handler had always called `router.push(getPostAuthRedirect(role))` directly — a FOURTH hand-copied "role → destination" path, never migrated to `resolveOnboardingDestination()` when the onboarding restructure built that shared resolver (that restructure's own account named three copies, not four — this one predates it and was missed). `getPostAuthRedirect()` skips the joined/consented checks entirely; for every role it already has a case for, that was masked because the destination dashboard's own `useRequireRole` independently re-checks and corrects course. `clinical_lead`/`clinic_admin` have no dashboard built yet (Stage 2 is roles/toggles only), so both fell straight through `getPostAuthRedirect()`'s default case to `/role-select` — a genuinely joined, unconsented clinical lead signing in landed on the top-level code-entry screen with nothing to self-correct, a dead end. Confirmed live: the clinical lead's first sign-in this session hit exactly this before the fix, and correctly reached `/consent` after it. Fixed by having `LoginContent.tsx` call `resolveOnboardingDestination()` like every other entry point — closes the gap for all roles, not just the two new ones. Client-only change, no migration involved; committed and pushed alone (`26a12ca`), confirmed deployed and re-verified live before continuing. Post-consent, `clinical_lead`/`clinic_admin` currently land on `/role-select` (`getPostAuthRedirect()`'s own default case) since neither has a dashboard yet — the honest, expected state for a role Stage 2 deliberately didn't build a destination for, not a further bug.

  **VERIFICATION — deployed app, real fresh sign-ins throughout, fixture `ZZPRD5CONSENT` (clinic: director/lead/practitioner/admin) + `ZZPRD5CONSENTSCHOOL` (school: principal/teacher/sna/parent, plus a separate already-consented teacher — a second principal would have hit `institution_staff_one_principal_per_institution`, so the regression account is a `class_teacher`; the role itself doesn't matter for what that case tests).** All four clinic roles reached their own correct screen with the exact approved copy and recorded the correct `role` value in `consents` on accept (service-role read-back after each: `principal`, `clinical_lead`, `clinician`, `clinic_admin`). The clinician's home-logs line rendered exactly as Daniel's approved text, on the clinic branch, alongside the clinic-correct footer ("speak to your clinical director") and retention clause ("the clinic's own retention policy") — both institutionType-conditional lines confirmed correct in the same screen. All four school roles confirmed unchanged: principal got the school branch of the shared screen (countersigning language intact), teacher/SNA/parent screens untouched (no institutionType prop on any of the three, as designed). The already-consented teacher signed in and landed straight on `/teacher/dashboard`, no re-prompt, confirming a version bump was correctly not needed. Fixture torn down (`scripts/dev/zz-prd5-stage2-consent-verify-teardown.mjs`), zero orphans.

  **Full adversarial suite NOT re-run for this verification pass — stated plainly rather than left implied.** The one code change this pass made (`LoginContent.tsx`) is client-side redirect logic only, calling no RLS/RPC path the adversarial suite could exercise differently — nothing at the database level changed. Running the suite's ~1000+ checks would need the Auth rate limit raised from its current, deliberately-reverted 30-per-5-minutes (see this file's own entry on that), which wasn't warranted for a change with no database surface. The relevant, targeted checks — Stage 1/Stage 2's own adversarial proofs (type-aware self-link, handover role sets, the practitioner-departure caseload closure, override resolution) — were already run and confirmed earlier this stage and are unaffected by anything in this pass.

  **Stage 2 closes here.** Next: PRD 5 Stage 3, episodes of care (onboarding, discharge, the cascade) — previewed by Daniel as mirroring `end_enrolment()`, with the practitioner-departure cleanup built this stage giving the discharge cascade's client-side half a clear shape to follow. Not started.

- **PRD 5 STAGE 1 — INSTITUTION TYPE, VOCABULARY, THE TRANSLATION LAYER. DONE, DEPLOYED, MIGRATIONS RUN AND ADVERSARIALLY VERIFIED. 17 Sept 2026.** The foundation the other three clinic PRDs sit on (PRD 5 section 2's own framing: "one system, two kinds of organisation, not a fork"). Stage 1 scope only, per the PRD's own sequencing (section 11): organisation type, type-driven vocabulary with a per-organisation override, and the translation layer. No new roles yet — `clinical_lead`/`clinic_admin` are Stage 2.

  **STEP 0 RECON, done first as instructed** — five parallel agents covering: (1) `institutions` schema and every `institutionType.ts` call site, (2) every hand-rolled role-label display site in the UI, (3) every print/export route's label handling, (4) every "school"/"pupil" copy site plus existing per-institution-config precedent, (5) the RLS/RPC blast radius of a new `institutions.type` column. Reported in full, Daniel gave eight numbered answers, this entry records all eight plus what got built from them.

  **THE ROLE-CONSTRAINT AUDIT — the load-bearing finding for Stage 2, recorded here explicitly so that stage starts from it instead of rediscovering it.** Current live `institution_staff_role_check` (migration `0068`, untouched since): `role in ('class_teacher', 'institution_admin', 'sna', 'principal')`. `institution_staff_has_current_standing()` is confirmed fully role-agnostic — zero changes needed there ever, whatever roles get added. But **~77 currently-live functions/policies hardcode a specific role value (98 individual comparison lines)**. Two shapes, different cost:
  - **8 multi-value enumerations** — these need an actual DECISION per role added, not just an audit, since they gate WHO MAY DO something: `can_own_incident()`, `raise_support_alert()`, `hand_over_principal()`, `grant_passport_access()`, `create_incident_stamp()`, `send_message()`, the `institution_staff` self-link INSERT policy itself (`0068`, the gate on who may self-link as which role at all), `get_institution_staff_candidates()`.
  - **~65 single-value `= 'principal'` checks** — each is a question of whether a `clinical_lead` should inherit any slice of a principal's authority, answerable one at a time, not in bulk.
  - **5 more CHECK constraints on OTHER tables independently duplicate a slice of the same role vocabulary**, unrelated to `institution_staff_role_check` and each needing its own separate widening decision later: `messages_sender_role_check`/`message_recipients_recipient_role_check` (`0168`), `abc_logs_logged_by_role_check` (`0065`, no `principal` — never widened), `consents_role_check` (`0181`), `principal_handovers.staying_role` (`0102`).
  - No generated Supabase TypeScript types file exists anywhere in this repo (confirmed independently twice, by two separate recon agents) — nothing regenerates when a CHECK constraint changes; every hand-maintained TS union (`SchoolStaffRoleSelectContent.tsx`'s `StaffRole`, `consent/page.tsx`'s `ConsentRole`, `roleConfig.ts`'s `ABCLoggerRole`, `/api/set-role`'s `SELF_SERVICE_ROLES`, several `useRequireRole([...])` call sites) is a separate, manual place Stage 2 will need to touch by hand.

  **Daniel's eight answers, and what each one built:**

  1. **`institutionType.ts` becomes option (a), pass the value in** — cheap, no redundant round-trip, composes with the translation function which needs `type` as an input anyway. `getInstitutionType(institution: { type })` is now a thin pass-through, not a stub. `role-select/page.tsx` and `teacher/join-institution/page.tsx`'s own institution-code lookups both now select `type` alongside `id, status`; `role-select/page.tsx` forwards it to `school-staff/page.tsx` via `?institutionType=`, validated (`isInstitutionType()`) before use — an invalid/missing value bounces back to `/role-select` the same way a missing `institutionId` already did.

  2. **The `ChildDetail.tsx` bug — fixed as a real defect closed, not just a risk noted.** Its own `ROLE_LABEL` map was missing `principal`/`institution_admin` entirely; an activity row attributed to a principal rendered the raw string `"principal"` to a user, live, on screen, before this fix. Closed by the same consolidation that fixed the "Class Teacher" vs "Teacher" drift across the other maps — both were the identical failure mode, one just more visible than the other.

  3. **The `(principal)` leak — fixed on both surfaces it lived on, not just the flagged print route.** `teacher/incidents/[incidentId]/print/page.tsx:599` (the originally flagged risk) and `CountersignCard.tsx` (the identical bug, same raw `countersigned_role_at_time` column, on-screen — would have been left behind if only print had been fixed) both now resolve the incident's own `institution_id` (a direct query — `incidents` has its own `can_view_incident()`-gated SELECT policy, no RPC change needed) and render through `<RoleLabel>`.

  4. **The SQL-side maps — `get_principal_activity_feed()` (migrations `0171`, `0192`) now returns the raw role, client formats it.** Migration `0202`: DROP+CREATE (the function's `RETURNS TABLE` shape widened, same rule this file already documents), the "staff joined" branch's `event_description` no longer bakes in `case s.role when 'class_teacher' then 'Class Teacher' ...` — it returns `"$name joined"` plus a new `actor_role` column, formatted client-side via a new shared `src/lib/principalActivityFeed.ts` (used by both `PrincipalActivityCard.tsx` and `principal/activity/page.tsx`, which previously hand-copied the child-name-prefix logic too).

  5. **The component, the function, and deleting the maps — all three, as instructed.** `src/lib/vocabulary.ts` (`getRoleLabel(role, institutionType, overrides?)`, pure) and `src/components/ui/RoleLabel.tsx` (its JSX counterpart) are now the only place a role's display label is decided. **Every one of the nine hand-rolled TypeScript `Record<role, label>` copies this stage's own recon found is deleted, not left importable alongside the shared one**: `types/messages.ts`, `ReviewStaffJoinSheet.tsx`, `StaffList.tsx`, `StaffDetail.tsx`, `ChildDetail.tsx`, `roleConfig.ts`'s `ABC_ROLE_DISPLAY_LABEL`, `YourTeamCard.tsx`, `fba/types.ts`'s `RECIPIENT_ROLE_LABELS`. (`YourTeamCard.tsx`'s own "Teacher" instead of "Class Teacher" was resolved to the majority/canonical school default as part of the consolidation — a visible wording change for parents, deliberate, not a side effect.)

     **The institution-context question, answered directly, not glossed over:** `institutionType` on `RoleLabel` is a required prop, never read from implicit/session context — a principal has one obvious institution, a clinician with several engagements or a parent does not, so there is no safe "current institution" to assume from the viewer's own identity. Every real call site resolves it from the RECORD being displayed (which institution owns this incident/staff row/passport), never from who's looking at it. Two small hooks do the resolving: `useInstitutionType(institutionId)` (institutions' own SELECT policy is `using (true)`, no RLS trap) for anything that already has an `institutionId` in hand (the Directory subtree — `StaffList`/`StaffDetail`/`ChildDetail` already had it threaded as a prop from `principal/directory/page.tsx`, an existing pattern, not a new one), and `usePassportInstitutionVocabulary(passportId)` — a new SECURITY DEFINER RPC, `get_passport_institution_vocabulary()` (migration `0201`), mirroring `get_approved_institution_phone()`'s (`0182`) exact auth/resolution shape, needed because `enrolments`' own SELECT policy is staff-only and a parent/clinician session reading it directly would get nothing back, RLS-silent — the identical gotcha CLAUDE.md already documents, just for a second table now. A third small hook, `useMessageInstitutionVocabulary()`, picks whichever of the two a given message thread needs (`ThreadMessage` already carries exactly one of `institutionId`/`passportId`, migration `0168`'s own design).

     **One map deliberately left alone, not silently skipped:** `QuestionnaireFlow.tsx`'s `TRACK_ACTIVITY_LABEL` — a write-time audit string (`activity_log.event_description`, never confirmed to surface on any real screen), keyed on `"teacher"` not `"class_teacher"` (a distinct `track` vocabulary, not a direct `getRoleLabel()` input), and resolving its own institution would mean querying `fba_reports` by `fbaId` AS THE RECIPIENT'S OWN SESSION (parent/teacher/sna filling out a questionnaire, not the clinician who owns the FBA) — `fba_reports`' SELECT policy for a recipient was not confirmed, and guessing wrong here risks the exact RLS-silent-empty-result gotcha this file already warns about, for a string with no confirmed reader. Documented in place, not fixed; revisit if it's ever found to actually surface.

  6. **The override — `institution_vocabulary_overrides` table and its READ path, built and live in Stage 1; writes are raw SQL only, no UI, exactly as decided.** Follows `0068`'s own seven-times-proven pattern (a small table scoped by `institution_id`) with one deliberate difference from that precedent: `institution_id` here is NOT nullable — there's no "global default" row needed, since the global default is small, fixed, and already owned by `vocabulary.ts`'s own code, not a data-driven list an institution needs to see and extend. `(institution_id, key)` unique, `key` namespaced per role (`role_principal`, `role_clinician`, …) so a future non-role override can share the table without colliding. Read policy matches `institutions`' own `using (true)`; write policy is director/principal-only, matching `institutions`' own update policy shape — real and correct now, not a placeholder, since Daniel's own reasoning was that the write path needed proving too, just not a settings screen yet.

  7. **"Child" is separate work, recorded as its own item so it isn't mistaken for small.** 580 raw hits of "child" as the domain's primary noun across `src/` — not attempted, not folded into this pass. Also NOT attempted in this pass, and not explicitly asked for: the ~350-hit "school"/"your principal" institution-noun copy sweep across roughly 90 files (onboarding screens, consent screens, dashboards, empty states, the privacy policy) that Stage 1's own recon inventoried. Role labels were the concrete, named defect; the noun sweep is a real, much larger follow-up in the same shape, whenever it's picked up.

  8. **The `institutionType.ts` stale citation — corrected, not just removed.** Its old comment cited "CLAUDE.md's own account of why this PRD does not add a type column" — that passage never existed. The corrected comment points at this entry instead, and says plainly what changed: `institutions.type` is real now (migration `0200`), the function reads it via option (a).

  **THE ORDERING BUG: client code that queries a column before its own migration ran, live, for a real window — the second time ordering has bitten this month.** This session had no Supabase Dashboard session and no direct Postgres connection, so — unlike most of this build's earlier migrations — `0200`/`0201`/`0202` couldn't be applied from here. The mistake wasn't that gap by itself; it's that the migration files and the CLIENT CODE that depends on them (`role-select/page.tsx`'s own `.select("id, status, type")`, among others) were written, committed, and PUSHED to `main` together, in one commit, before Daniel had run anything. Vercel auto-deploys `main` — the push alone put a `select("...type")` query live against a column that didn't exist yet, breaking `/role-select`'s institution-code lookup, the exact entry point the onboarding restructure had just fixed, for the window between that push and Daniel running the three migrations by hand from the SQL pasted directly into chat.

  **What actually stops this, stated plainly rather than left as "be more careful": commit and push SQL migrations in their own commit, FIRST, and confirm they're live before the dependent client code is ever pushed — never in the same commit, regardless of how mechanically connected the two changes feel while writing them.** When this session cannot run migrations itself (no Dashboard session, no DB connection — true here, and worth checking for explicitly before assuming otherwise), that means: write the migration, hand it to Daniel to run, WAIT for confirmation, and only then write and push the client code that queries the new column/RPC — never stage both and let a `git push` be the moment a live break gets discovered. The three migrations here were correct on the first try; the harm was entirely sequencing, not content. Confirmed run and adversarially verified same day (below) — but the fix for next time is the commit boundary, not faster turnaround after the fact.

  **Verification, deployed app, real sessions throughout** — a two-institution fixture (`ZZPRD5A`/`ZZPRD5B`), a principal + teacher + SNA at A, a second principal at B, a parent owning A's test child (zero institution affiliation of their own), and a wholly unrelated outsider account. In order:
  1. **`/role-select`'s code lookup — checked FIRST, as instructed, before anything else**: signed in fresh, entered institution `TEST11` (the one real verified institution in production), landed correctly on the school-staff role picker. Confirmed the entry point the ordering bug had broken now works.
  2. **The override read path, end-to-end**: wrote `institution_vocabulary_overrides` (`ZZPRD5A`, `role_principal` → `"Head of Service"`) via raw SQL, exactly as decided (writes are hand-run, no UI yet). Confirmed live on the deployed app: `CountersignCard` and the incident print route both read `"Countersigned by ... (Head of Service)"` — no more raw `"(principal)"`. `StaffList` independently showed `"(you) Head of Service"` for the same principal. Institution `ZZPRD5B`'s own overrides queried empty as its own principal — the override never leaked to a second institution.
  3. **`RoleLabel` resolving from the record, not the viewer — proven with a real case where they differ**, via `get_passport_institution_vocabulary()` called by four different real signed-in sessions against the same passport: the PARENT (zero institution of her own) correctly got institution A's real override (`Head of Service`) — proof the resolution follows the record, not an absent viewer identity; the OUTSIDER (zero access) and PRINCIPAL B (a real principal, but of the WRONG institution) both got the generic safe default, never A's real data — proof the RPC's auth gate refuses correctly rather than merely "resolving to something."
  4. **The two bug fixes, both confirmed live**: `ChildDetail.tsx`'s access list — granted the fixture's SNA direct access via the real "+ Grant Access" UI flow, confirmed it renders `"SNA"` (not a raw value) via `<RoleLabel>`; combined with (2) and (3) above already proving `principal`'s own translation, this confirms the whole role set the old map was missing. The `(principal)` leak — confirmed gone identically on both surfaces named: `CountersignCard` and `teacher/incidents/[incidentId]/print`.
  5. **Nothing school-side broke**: principal (dashboard, Directory, incident detail, print — all clean, console error-free), class teacher (dashboard clean), SNA (own passports dashboard clean, correctly showing the just-granted child), and parent (consent flow, dashboard, incident view — all clean) each signed in fresh and used their own real screens without incident. One incident export (the print route above) checked as part of the leak-fix confirmation.

  Fixture torn down (`scripts/dev/zz-prd5-stage1-verify-teardown.mjs`), zero orphans confirmed.

- **ONBOARDING RESTRUCTURE — CODE-FIRST, CONSENT-AFTER-JOINING — DONE, DEPLOYED, VERIFIED WITH GENUINELY FRESH ACCOUNTS FOR ALL FIVE ROLES. 16 Sept 2026.** Daniel's own brief, four parts: (1) institution code entered FIRST, before any role is picked — role options and copy now follow from the resolved institution, removing the old mismatch where someone chose "school staff" then typed a clinic's code (moot today, since no clinic-typed institution can exist yet, but the ordering itself was the point); (2) a second, explicit "I don't have a code, or I'm a parent" route, worded with deliberately NEUTRAL language — no "school", no "clinic" — so a school customer can't infer clinics use this product and vice versa, a commercial requirement, not a UX preference; (3) **consent moved from before joining to after it** — four of five consent screens say "your school"/"your principal", which used to be a presumption made before the person had joined anything, now a fact; (4) a clinic-onboarding placeholder, deliberately inert, greppable, for PRD 5.

  **Item 3 was flagged by Daniel as the risky one and got dedicated recon before any code moved** — RECON FIRST, explicitly, because "onboarding is the one flow where a bug is invisible until someone new signs up... every existing account is already past this point, so testing with accounts we have proves nothing" (his own words, echoing the 0117 incident above almost exactly). Recon found: the only enforcement that consent gates access is 100% CLIENT-SIDE — nothing in RLS or any RPC constrains write order between `institution_staff` and `consents` — and it was duplicated three times (`src/app/page.tsx`, `src/lib/supabase/proxy.ts`'s own comment admitted as much, and `src/hooks/useRequireRole.ts`), with **zero existing adversarial coverage of the ordering itself**.

  **The clinician design ambiguity, resolved by Daniel directly**: a clinician never had an institution code to enter — picking a specialty, then separately submitting PSI/CORU/BACB credentials, was always the real flow, no institution involved. Asked where clinicians fit in the new top-level fork; his answer, verbatim: *"even if i was a clinician, they would have a code, either for a school or a clinic."* This shaped the final design directly: the top-level code screen is universal (anyone with a code, including a future institution-employed clinician, uses it); the role picker after a resolved institution shows a fourth "Clinician" tile leading to an honest "not built yet" placeholder (institution-employed clinician self-service was never built — see the parked entry below); the REAL, working independent-clinician path (pick a specialty, submit credentials, no institution) stays exactly as it was, reachable only from the neutral "I don't have a code" route — not regressed, not gated behind a code it never had.

  **Consent's own new precondition, `hasJoined()`** (`src/lib/hasJoined.ts`, new): staff (`class_teacher`/`sna`/`principal`) — a non-deactivated `institution_staff` row exists (pending counts as joined; approval is a separate, later gate — confirmed live, see below); clinician — `clinicians.specialty` is set; parent — always true, no precondition, unchanged from before this restructure. Three previously-hand-copied "role → consent? → dashboard" implementations collapsed into one new `src/lib/resolveOnboardingDestination.ts`, called by `page.tsx` and `proxy.ts`; `useRequireRole.ts` keeps its own shape (it redirects mid-render) but gained an opt-out option for the one call site that must be reachable BEFORE consent.

  **The opt-out was deliberately named for how it reads, not just what it does — Daniel's own instruction mid-build**: *"requireConsent: false is reasonably explicit — consider whether something like allowBeforeConsent reads more like a deliberate exception."* Renamed accordingly. **The complete list of call sites carrying `allowBeforeConsent: true`, so this list lives somewhere other than a diff: exactly one — `src/app/clinician/specialty/page.tsx`.** Every other `useRequireRole` call site in the app (every dashboard, every deeper page, `/teacher/join-institution`, `/clinician/verify`, `/passport/welcome`, `/passport/claim`) is untouched and keeps the full consent gate.

  **CORRECTED, SAME DAY: THERE IS NO INDEPENDENT CLINICIAN — a model correction, not a bug fix.** The paragraph above shipped a self-service "I'm a clinician, not tied to an organisation" tile on the neutral no-code route, reasoned from the fact that the specialty-then-verify path has never itself required an institution. Daniel's own correction: every clinician belongs to an organisation regardless — a school's in-house BCBA and a clinic's practitioners both join by their organisation's code (the ordinary top-level `/role-select` entry every other staff role already uses); a clinical director gets a code from Behaviour Hive directly when their clinic buys the software, the same manual gate an institution's first principal already goes through. A self-service route with no organisation at all "contradicts the model and becomes something we have to police later" (his own words). The tile is removed — `/role-select/no-code` is now PARENT ONLY, copy unchanged and still neutral (never "school", never "clinic"). `"clinician"` was also dropped from `/api/set-role`'s own self-service allow-list, since no client-side call site sends it any more and leaving it reachable would have been exactly the same kind of dangling self-service capability the correction was about.

  **What this did NOT cost, because he asked directly**: the specialty-then-verify path at `/clinician/specialty` — the only real clinician onboarding that exists today, and what the trial school's own psychologist uses, approved manually via `approve_clinician()` — is untouched. Nothing about it was deleted; it simply stopped being reachable by tapping a tile, since reaching it requires `app_metadata.role` already set to `"clinician"`, which nothing in the app's own UI sets any more. **Getting a specific, vetted person into that state is now a manual, one-off Behaviour Hive operation**, `scripts/admin/set-clinician-role.mjs` (new) — looks a real, already-registered account up by email, refuses to touch one that already has a different role set, and sets `app_metadata.role = "clinician"` directly via the admin API, exactly the same shape as the manual institution-creation gate already documented above. Once set, the person's **very next sign-in** lands them on `/clinician/specialty` automatically — `resolveOnboardingDestination()`/`useRequireRole` see role `"clinician"` with no specialty chosen yet and route there on their own, the identical gating every other role already goes through. No new UI page, no tile, nothing else to build — confirmed live on the deployed app with a fresh test account: role set via the script while signed out, then a genuine fresh sign-in landed directly on the specialty picker with zero detour. This is exactly how the trial psychologist reaches it in under two weeks: she registers and verifies her email as anyone would, Behaviour Hive runs the one script against her email, and her next login does the rest.

  **Also decided, same conversation, both on Daniel's own instruction**: no institution snapshot on the `consents` row — *"You were right to flag rather than add it. The consent text says 'your school', not a named institution, so the record would capture something the person was never shown. Revisit only if consent copy ever becomes institution-specific"* — `consents` still only snapshots role, as before this change. And `/consent`'s own resume logic for an already-consented user changed from the old `getPostConsentDestination()` (which routed PAST consent to code-entry — backwards now that code-entry precedes consent, so it was removed) to `getPostAuthRedirect(role)` directly.

  **Verification, genuinely fresh accounts for all five roles, deployed app, per Daniel's explicit "not negotiable" instruction** — fixture: institution `ZZONBOARDVERIFY`, five role-less `zzonboard.*@thebehaviourhive.com` accounts (email-confirmed, no `app_metadata.role` set, mimicking the exact state a real fresh signup is in post-verification). All five confirmed end-to-end: principal (code → role picker → auto-approved as first principal → consent → `/principal/dashboard`), class teacher and SNA (same code, both correctly landed on the EXISTING pending-approval screen at `/teacher/join-institution` — migration 0100's own approval gate, unrelated to this restructure, confirming `hasJoined()` correctly treats "submitted a request" as joined, not "approved"), independent clinician ("I don't have a code" → "I'm a clinician" → specialty → consent → `/clinician/dashboard`), parent ("I don't have a code" → "I'm a parent" → consent → `/parent-dashboard`). `/passport/welcome` → `/passport/claim` confirmed unaffected for the fresh parent account. `/teacher/join-institution` confirmed unaffected for an already-active staff member (the principal account correctly bounced straight back to its own dashboard, proving the `friendlyJoinError()` extraction was a pure refactor, not a behaviour change).

  **The two edge cases a clean run would never exercise, both named explicitly by Daniel and both done deliberately rather than left to chance**: *"the interrupted principal: joined, tab closed before consenting, next login must land on /consent"* — verified by exploiting the real live "joined, not consented" window on the fresh principal account, hitting all three of the historically-duplicated gates in one pass: `/login` (proxy fast-path), `/` (`page.tsx`), and `/principal/dashboard` directly (`useRequireRole`) — all three correctly redirected to `/consent`, none let the joined-but-unconsented principal through. *"an already-consented account signing in with no detour"* — verified with a genuine full logout (via the real UI confirmation modal) followed by a fresh sign-in through the real login form, not just a re-navigation on a still-live session — landed straight on `/principal/dashboard`, no detour through `/consent` or `/role-select`.

  **Adversarial coverage, CHECK RRR** (own institution + fresh users, own teardown): replicates `hasConsented()`'s own query shape exactly, run as each user's own signed-in session (not the service-role client) — per this file's own "the suite tests the database, not the journey" rule, since the enforcement being tested is entirely client-side. Proves the "joined, not consented" window is real and correctly reported (a user with a real `institution_staff` row and no `consents` row queries as not-consented), a positive control (role + `institution_staff` + a current-version `consents` row queries as consented), and a negative baseline (role set, never joined at all, no `institution_staff` row) is distinguishable from the "joined, not consented" case.

  Fixture torn down (`scripts/dev/zz-onboard-verify-teardown-2026-09-16.mjs`), zero orphans confirmed. Typecheck/lint/build clean throughout.

- **THE SHARED INCIDENT/COUNTERSIGN PAGE HAS NEVER HAD A DESKTOP PASS — OWED, NOT SIZED SMALL. 16 Sept 2026.** Found during the principal desktop-layout audit: `/teacher/incidents/[incidentId]` — the ONLY route that renders an incident's full detail, including countersigning, for every track (principal, teacher, clinician, SNA all land here, there is no separate principal-specific incident-detail view) — has no sidebar and no width treatment of any kind at 1280px. Measured: 672px of content, centred, with roughly 304px of dead space on each side, identical at every viewport width from phone to desktop. This is not a stretched-element bug (the audit's own remit) — it is a page that has simply never received the kind of desktop pass `principal/layout.tsx` and `clinician/layout.tsx` each got as their own dedicated stage. Flagged rather than fixed in passing, on Daniel's own instruction: giving one shared, cross-track page a first desktop layout is the same scale of work the clinician desktop pass was (sidebar, nav tabs, content max-width, verified not to regress the other tracks that also render through it) — not a className fix borrowed from a different task.

  **Worth stating plainly, not just the measurement**: this is the screen a principal is on when they countersign a restraint record — arguably the single most consequential screen in the product — and it is currently the least finished-looking one at desktop width. Recorded here so its cost is legible before it gets picked up, not discovered again from scratch.

- **DIRECTORY CHILD-CARD COMPLETION BADGES — COLOUR-CODED, DONE, DEPLOYED, VERIFIED BOTH WIDTHS. 16 Sept 2026.** Stage 7 item 1 shipped all three completion badges (Passport/Clinician/Parent) in one colour, Prussian Blue — legible, but indistinguishable from each other at a glance, which defeated part of the point of an at-a-glance row. Daniel's own spec: Passport = Golden Brown (`#D78825`), Clinician stays Prussian Blue, Parent = Pastel Blue (`#BAD9EB`).

  **Checked, not assumed, per his own explicit instruction: Pastel Blue against a white card is barely legible when filled the same way the other two are** (a white icon on the original design). Computed: white icon on `#BAD9EB` is ~1.5:1 contrast — nowhere near the 3:1 WCAG minimum for a graphical/UI element, and the disc's own edge against a white card computes similarly low since the two colours are close in both luminance and saturation (unlike Golden Brown or Prussian Blue, which are nowhere near white in either). Fixed with the two things his own brief named as the likely fix: a solid 2px Prussian Blue border (Passport/Clinician's complete state stays borderless) and a Prussian Blue icon instead of white (computed contrast ~6:1 against the same fill). Confirmed live on the deployed app, both widths, via computed styles read directly off the real DOM (not just a visual read) — `border-2 border-brand-prussian-blue bg-brand-pastel-blue text-brand-prussian-blue`, exactly as designed, rendering distinctly filled rather than washed-out at both 375px and desktop.

  **The colourblind-safe shape signal (filled solid disc vs. 1px dashed hollow ring) was never carried by hue and needed no change** — confirmed it still holds with three fill colours instead of one, same live-DOM check: every incomplete badge is `rgba(0,0,0,0)` fill with a `1px dashed` border regardless of which badge it is, and every complete badge is a solid fill (Parent's own carrying a 2px SOLID border, the other two borderless) — dashed-vs-solid and hollow-vs-filled are the actual signal, and neither depends on being able to tell gold from blue. The legend row's own swatches now share each badge's exact `completeClassName` (one array of definitions, not a separately-hand-copied string) so the legend and the cards can never drift into showing different colours for the same thing.

- **THE FBA COMPLETED-LOCK REGRESSION — FOUND, FIXED, MIGRATION RUN AND VERIFIED, DONE, DEPLOYED. 16 Sept 2026.** Daniel reported live: the AFLS section of a finalised, locked FBA was still editable in the clinician's own view of a completed FBA — an immutable clinical document changing after it was signed and exported. Investigated before any fix, per his own instruction.

  **Answer to the gating question: DB-enforced for the generic path, not UI-only.** `fba_reports.status <> 'completed'` is the FBA's own completed-lock, enforced via that table's own RLS UPDATE policy, live since migration 0040, never altered since. AFLS specifically escaped it — not the whole document.

  **This is a regression, not a gap, and the framing matters, per Daniel's own correction**: the lock existed on `fba_afls_data` (the table AFLS data lived in originally) from the start. Migration 0060 (the "AFLS rebuild") replaced that table with `afls_assessments` and its own header comment claims "same gate `fba_afls_data` always used" — true for the parent-read policy, **false for all three clinician write policies** (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE). The completed-FBA lock was silently dropped, not merely never built, and 0060 explicitly (wrongly) reasoned it through: two separate comments in that migration justify the omission by analogy to `fba_calm_cards` — "companion layer, exempt from the lock." AFLS is scored clinical assessment data feeding the functional analysis itself; Calm Cards only reference an already-locked conclusion via `strategy_ref`, never constitute it. The analogy was wrong, and it was wrong on both the DB migration and, independently, on **two separate client-side files** (`FbaSectionEditor.tsx`, `ClinicalFileFbaSections.tsx`) that each carried their own comment citing the identical wrong reasoning — confirming Daniel's own instinct ("AFLS is the one someone noticed; it may not be the only one") that a full sweep, not a single-file fix, was needed.

  **Confirmed already exploited, not theoretical**: queried production directly. Exactly one completed FBA exists. Its `completed_at` is `2026-09-15T15:03:50Z`; its `afls_assessments` row's `updated_at` is `2026-09-16T09:14:48Z` — roughly 18 hours after the FBA was finalised. A real post-lock write on the only real (if lorem-ipsum test content) clinical document currently in the system. The FBA's published strategies (`passport_clinical_content`, all `created_at: 2026-09-15T15:10:34Z`) were extracted ~7 minutes after `completed_at`, via the old pre-Stage-7-item-6 two-step parent-approval flow, and so reflect the AFLS state as it stood BEFORE the later edit, not after — the underlying assessment record has since diverged from what was on file at finalise time, though what was actually published is unaffected. Left alone by the fix — repairing the gate, not rewriting history.

  **A second, independently-discovered gap in `fba_instrument_requests`, found auditing "does anything else sit outside the generic path the same way"**: the clinician's own INSERT policy had no completed-lock at all (a fresh questionnaire could be sent on an already-finalised FBA), and the recipient's own UPDATE policy checked only the REQUEST's status, never the parent FBA's — "a parent or teacher submitting into an FBA that was finalised last week is the same problem by a different route."

  **The fix, migration `0199_restore_fba_completed_lock.sql`, written, NOT yet run**: (1) restores the completed-lock on `afls_assessments`' INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies, matching `fba_reports`' own live shape; (2) adds the completed-lock to `fba_instrument_requests`' clinician INSERT policy, and rewrites the recipient UPDATE policy to check the FBA's own status, not just the request's; (3) adds `'cancelled'` to `fba_instrument_requests.status`'s CHECK constraint (dynamic `pg_constraint` lookup, matching 0029's own established pattern) and folds "cancel any outstanding (sent/in_progress) request" into `finalize_fba_report()`'s existing atomic transaction — so a request in flight doesn't silently become unanswerable with no explanation the moment its FBA is finalised; (4) widens `get_my_instrument_requests()` to also surface a recipient's own cancelled requests, so a cancelled item explains itself rather than vanishing without trace; (5) explicitly, deliberately leaves `fba_calm_cards` untouched, restating 0053's own reasoning directly in the new migration's header comment, so the next person who sees `afls_assessments` locked and `fba_calm_cards` not doesn't "fix" it the wrong way.

  **Client-side, matching the DB fix**: `FbaSectionEditor.tsx` and `ClinicalFileFbaSections.tsx` both now render AFLS as `AflsResultsGrid` (the existing colour-coded read-only table already used by the parent's own FBA reader/PDF export) once the FBA is completed, never the editable `AflsSection` — reusing the export's own presentation rather than building a third version, per Daniel's own instruction. `readOnly` in `FbaSectionEditor.tsx` now applies uniformly across every section kind (the AFLS carve-out is gone). A new risk this restoration created, found and fixed proactively: restoring a real USING-clause gate on the recipient's own UPDATE policy means a stale/late write can now silently no-op per this file's own documented "RLS on UPDATE silently filters" gotcha — `QuestionnaireFlow.tsx`'s `persist()` now chains `.select("id")` and treats an empty return as a definitive, explained failure ("this request is no longer open"), rather than trusting `error === null`. `QuestionnairePromptCard.tsx` and `InstrumentRequestChip.tsx` both gained a distinct, muted "Cancelled" treatment (no reminder button, no urgent styling) so a cancelled request reads as explained, not as a bug.

  **Adversarial coverage added — CHECK QQQ**, covering the whole shape in one place per Daniel's own instruction ("every FBA-related table, a completed FBA, and a clinician who would otherwise be authorised, refused on each"): `afls_assessments` INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE refused post-completion; `fba_instrument_requests` clinician INSERT and recipient UPDATE (on an auto-cancelled request) both refused; `fba_reports`' own original 0040 lock re-proven in the same place as a regression anchor; `get_my_instrument_requests()` confirmed to still surface a cancelled request rather than dropping it; and `fba_calm_cards` confirmed to STAY writable post-completion — the deliberate exception, asserted positively in the same check as the refusals so it can never be mistaken for something the next person should "fix." **Run against the live database with the fix NOT yet applied, deliberately, to prove the check actually catches the regression it's named for**: 3/13 passed — every refusal assertion failed exactly as the live bug predicts (AFLS `assessor_name` genuinely got overwritten to "Tampered post-lock", the row genuinely got deleted, the instrument request genuinely got updated with `{"hacked": true}`), and the two genuine positive controls plus the Calm Cards exemption passed. This is the check that would have caught 0060 dropping the gate, and it is what stops the next rebuild doing it again.

  **Migration 0199's own first run failed, caught before anything shipped, and is worth recording as a small instance of the same discipline this entry is about.** `get_my_instrument_requests()`'s replacement was re-derived from migration 0041 instead of the actual live definition — 0048 had already added an `instruction` column, still read today by `QuestionnairePromptCard.tsx`/`MyInstrumentRequest`, that the first draft silently dropped from the return shape. Postgres refused the `CREATE OR REPLACE` outright (`42P13`, "cannot change return type of existing function... Row type defined by OUT parameters is different") rather than letting it through. Fixed by re-grepping every migration that had ever touched the function (0041, 0048, 0141) before rewriting it — exactly this file's own "READ THE LIVE DEFINITION, NOT THE FIRST ONE YOU FOUND" gotcha, just caught by the database itself this time instead of a human.

  **Confirmed run and verified, 16 Sept 2026**: CHECK QQQ re-run against the live, fixed schema — 13/13 passed. Fixture torn down, zero orphans confirmed both times (once against the unfixed schema, proving the check catches the regression; once against the fixed one, proving the fix holds).

- **FBA STRATEGIES NOW PUBLISH ON FINALISATION — A GOVERNANCE CHANGE, NOT A CONVENIENCE FIX. DONE, DEPLOYED, Stage 7 item 6, 16 Sept 2026.**

  **What changed, mechanically:** an FBA's triggers/setting events/strategies used to reach the school and home passport only after TWO separate steps — the clinician finalised the report, and separately, the parent opened the FBA reader, read it, and pressed "Approve & Publish" (`ApprovalBanner.tsx`, `approve_fba_strategies()`, gated on `owns_passport()`). That second step is gone. `finalize_fba_report()` (migration 0197) is now one atomic RPC, gated on the FBA's own clinician (`fr.clinician_id = auth.uid()`), that sets the report to `completed` AND extracts its content into `passport_clinical_content` in the same transaction — replacing both the old plain client-side status update and the separate parent-triggered publish. `approve_fba_strategies()` itself is DROPPED, not left dormant — a parent-callable RPC that publishes clinical content into a child's passport, still reachable after the consent step it implemented no longer exists, is exactly the kind of stale entry point this file's own gotchas warn about elsewhere (dead policies, unwired escape hatches) — the difference here is it was never dead, it was actively wired to a banner, so leaving it live would have been worse than unused, it would have been a second, inconsistent path to the same outcome. `ApprovalBanner.tsx` and its one render site (`passport/fba/[fbaId]/page.tsx`) are deleted, not just unwired — a parent consenting to something that no longer requires consent is worse than no banner at all.

  **Why, in Daniel's own words, recorded so a future reader does not put this back:** an FBA is a clinical assessment. A parent cannot amend a clinician's professional judgement. The old flow treated a completed, signed-off clinical document as provisional until a parent separately signed off on it — as though the family's agreement was what made a clinician's own findings valid. It isn't. The clinician's finalisation IS the professional act; publishing is a direct consequence of that act, not a separate permission granted by someone without the clinical authority to have withheld it in the first place. This is not "approval removed" as a simplification or a UX convenience — it's a correction of what the approval step was ever supposed to represent. Compare CLAUDE.md's own "HOME LOGS REACH THE CLASSROOM BY DESIGN, VIA THE CLINICIAN" entry: the clinician's judgement about what's clinically relevant to share has always been the actual gate in this product (deciding strategy_home vs strategy_school vs strategy_shared) — this change removes a second, redundant, non-clinical gate sitting on top of that one, not the gate itself.

  **What did NOT change:** the clinician's own judgement about WHAT to share is still the real gate — `strategy_home` vs `strategy_school` vs `strategy_shared` still comes from the clinician's own section authoring, untouched by this migration. A parent still reads the full FBA report whenever they choose to — nothing about read access changed. What changed is narrowly that publishing to the passport is no longer conditional on a parent separately re-approving content their child's own clinician already finalised.

- **THE PRINCIPAL DASHBOARD GOES STICKY — OPEN, INTERMITTENT, UNREPRODUCED. 15 Sept 2026.** Reported live during the QA run-through: after a few actions the dashboard stops responding and needs a browser refresh, no clear trigger, happened after several clicks in Chrome. Investigated, not fixed — could not reproduce it, and per Daniel's own instruction this stays recorded as open rather than closed against a guess.

  **What the investigation found and ruled out.** The principal dashboard's own `load()` (one `useEffect`, nine RPCs in one `Promise.all`) has no obvious re-entrancy trigger from any button on the page. `BottomSheet`'s body-scroll-lock effect is correctly scoped and cleans up. `useMessagesAwaitingActionCount`/`useHasUnreadMessages` are one-shot fetches on mount, no polling, no listeners. Every deliberately-swallowed error found (`resolve_lapsed_incident_ownership`'s best-effort catch, the support-alert poll's own "fail quiet") is intentional and documented — swallowing means stale state, not a hang. No growing/leaking interval, no unresolved promise blocking render, and no unguarded infinite loop was found in any code path a principal dashboard session actually exercises.

  **One confirmed, real, now-FIXED issue found along the way, but explicitly NOT claimed as the cause**: `PrincipalSidebar` (mounted once, persistent) and every page's own `PrincipalBottomNav` (remounted per navigation) were each independently running the support-alert 5-second poll — CSS-hidden is not unmounted, so both ran simultaneously the whole time a principal was on any page. Fixed same day (`PrincipalSupportAlertProvider`, one poll shared via context) regardless of whether it's connected to this freeze, because it was real and wasteful either way. **Assessed honestly, not just fixed and assumed closed**: this is a steady, constant-rate 2x background overhead present from the moment of sign-in — it doesn't grow or compound with clicks, which is the specific shape Daniel described ("after a few actions," "after several clicks"). A flat background rate doesn't naturally explain a threshold effect tied to user actions. It's a candidate worth having eliminated, not a confirmed explanation — if the freeze recurs after this fix, that fix is ruled out as the cause, not vindicated as unnecessary.

  **What would more plausibly match "gets worse after several clicks"**: something that accumulates PER ACTION rather than running at a constant rate — e.g., the dashboard's own `load()` being re-invoked by some action without cancelling or deduplicating the previous in-flight call, so repeated clicks pile up concurrent nine-RPC batches rather than replacing the prior one. This was not confirmed either — no call site was found that re-triggers `load()` outside its own mount effect — but it fits the reported shape better than the poll did, and is where a future investigation should look first if this recurs.

  **Capture steps for next time, agreed with Daniel**: Chrome Task Manager (Shift+Esc) opened before it happens, watching whether that tab's memory keeps climbing (confirms a leak vs. a single blocking call). If it goes sticky: Network tab first — requests still completing (points at a stuck poll/request pile-up) vs. requests fine but nothing updates (points at the JS thread itself being blocked). A Performance recording spanning the ~30 seconds before it seizes, if triggerable deliberately, would be the single most useful capture.

- **THE `AbcLogReference` "View log" GAP FOR PRINCIPAL — OPEN, NEEDS DANIEL'S CALL.** Found alongside item 5b's `buildStrategyHref` fix (same file family, same missing-branch shape): `AbcLogReference.tsx`'s `buildHref()` has explicit branches for parent and class_teacher only; principal falls through to the clinician route (`/clinician/passport/...`), which `useRequireRole("clinician")` then refuses. Not fixed alongside 5b because, unlike the strategies case, there's no clean answer: principal's own `ChildDetail.tsx` has a tab literally named `"incidents"`, but it renders FORMAL incident records (`institutionIncidents`), not ABC logs — the opposite of SNA's own tab naming, where `"incidents"` = ABC Logs and `"incidentLog"` = formal Incidents. There is no ABC-log-specific tab in principal's `TabKey` union at all (`"enrolment" | "access" | "clinical" | "passport" | "incidents"`) to point at. Needs Daniel's own call on which existing tab (or a new one) an ABC log should surface under for a principal, rather than guessed at — a wrong destination would be worse than the current fallthrough.

- **`purge_stale_app_events()` SCHEDULING — CLOSED. pg_cron IS the live mechanism; the Vercel Cron alternative stays deliberately inactive. 20 Sept 2026.** Never independently confirmed from this environment (the `cron.*` catalog stays unreachable through PostgREST — tried again directly while wiring PRD 9's own two new cron routes, refused with "Invalid schema: cron", same as before) — closed instead on Daniel's own account, given while confirming which of the three `/api/cron/*` routes still needed wiring into `vercel.json`: *"two are inert"*, meaning `purge-app-events` is not one of them. `purge-app-events` therefore stays OUT of `vercel.json` — wiring it in alongside a working pg_cron job would be exactly the "two active schedulers doing the same job" confusion the paragraph below already warns against, not a safety net.
- **`purge_stale_app_events()` SCHEDULING — MIGRATION WRITTEN, RUN PENDING (Daniel), 15 Sept 2026.** Daniel's own instruction: 90 days, long enough to cover the trial and produce a report, short enough that operational telemetry doesn't accumulate indefinitely. Migration 0187 attempts `create extension if not exists pg_cron` then schedules a daily job (`0 3 * * *`, off-peak) calling `purge_stale_app_events(now() - interval '90 days')` — a ROLLING window, not a one-time cutoff: every night's run deletes whatever has crossed 90 days old as of that run, so the table's oldest row never gets much older than 90 days for as long as the schedule stays active. Idempotent (unschedules any existing job of the same name first), so re-running it later to change the schedule is safe.

  **Confirmed by reading the LIVE function body (0174), not assumed: this deletes `app_events` rows ONLY.** The entire function is one statement — `delete from public.app_events where created_at < p_before`. It has no relationship whatsoever to any other table's own timing columns (`incidents.recorded_at`, any `updated_at`, a passport section's own "Updated X ago") — those belong to the records they sit on, and there is no code path in this function that could reach them.

  **pg_cron availability could not be confirmed from this environment** — no direct Postgres connection is available here (only the PostgREST/Auth-scoped anon and service-role keys), and pg_cron's own `cron.*` catalog isn't exposed through PostgREST, so there is no way to check independently of either running the migration or Daniel checking the Supabase dashboard directly. This was an open question as far back as 0174's own comment ("this project's pg_cron availability hasn't been checked") — 0187 is the actual check: it fails loudly and specifically if this Supabase plan doesn't support the extension, rather than silently doing nothing.

  **The alternative, prepared and checked in, but deliberately NOT activated**: `src/app/api/cron/purge-app-events/route.ts`, a Next.js Route Handler using the existing `createAdminClient()` pattern (`src/lib/supabase/admin.ts`, the same service-role helper `/api/set-role` already uses) to call the identical RPC. Authenticated via `CRON_SECRET` — Vercel signs every cron-triggered request with `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}` automatically once that env var exists in the project. NOT wired into `vercel.json` (no `vercel.json` exists yet either) — activating it means adding one `crons` entry and setting the env var, nothing else. Deliberately inert until pg_cron is confirmed unavailable: running both would be harmless in outcome (a second purge of an already-emptied window deletes zero rows) but genuinely confusing for whoever next audits retention and finds two mechanisms both claiming to own "this runs daily" — exactly one should ever be the live source of truth.

  **Next step**: Daniel runs 0187 in the Supabase Dashboard SQL editor. If it succeeds, pg_cron is confirmed available and this entry closes as DONE. If it errors, the Vercel Cron alternative gets activated instead (the two-step change above) and this entry updates to record that path instead.

- **CLINICIAN DESKTOP PASS — STAGE 1 AND STAGE 2 DONE, BOTH DEPLOYED. RECORDED HERE: THE FBA "NEXT SECTION" WORKFLOW GAP, A SEPARATE ITEM, NOT A LAYOUT QUESTION.** Found during Stage 1's own recon, 15 Sept 2026: `fba/[fbaId]/section/[sectionId]/page.tsx` has no "next section" control at all — only a back-chevron to the 14-section index (`FbaSectionShell.tsx`'s own `onBack`). Finishing a section means returning to a list of fourteen cards and picking the next one by hand, every time, for all fourteen sections. This is a workflow problem independent of screen width — narrower than the desktop pass itself, and deliberately NOT folded into Stage 1's layout-only scope (a "section rail" that lets a clinician switch sections without returning to the index was flagged during recon and explicitly kept OUT of Stage 1 for the same reason: switching sections in place is a navigation/behaviour change, not a proportions one). Daniel's own call: record it as its own item, pick it up after the desktop pass, not before.

  **BUILT, DONE, DEPLOYED, 15 Sept 2026, immediately after the desktop pass.** Recon first (breadth vs sequence: the section-rail flagged above is a DIFFERENT answer, not this one — a rail is about jumping anywhere at lg+; Next/Previous is about finishing a section and moving on, width-independent, most valuable where a rail could never exist — see below). Then built: paired Previous/Next buttons in `FbaSectionShell` itself (one shared change point, all fourteen sections), explicit press only — no auto-advance, no timeout, nothing that moves a clinician who didn't press something (autosave-on-blur already makes leaving a field meaningful; auto-advancing on top of that would make tabbing out of the last field silently advance the task). The 14-section index is untouched, still the primary way in and still the only overview of which sections need work.

  **The pending-save problem, found during recon and fixed as part of this build**: navigating away never used to wait for or force a pending save — survivable with the old back-chevron (leaving meant the clinician had left the task), worse with Next (the whole point is they're still moving fast through fourteen sections, and a dropped save reads as a lost answer they believe they wrote). Both Next AND Previous AND the back-chevron now share one `flushAndAdvance()`: if `isDirty`, calls `triggerSave` (now returning `"saved" | "cancelled" | "error"` from `useFbaReport.ts`'s `saveContent`, a small additive change — every existing fire-and-forget caller is unaffected) and awaits it, looping if a newer edit landed mid-flush (checked via `changeVersionRef`/`versionAtSaveStartRef`, refs only, never re-reading `isDirty` state after an `await` — a stale closure there would silently under- or over-flush). On anything other than `"saved"` (cancelled via the existing Cancel control, or a real error), it stays put rather than navigating anyway — navigating on a cancelled/failed save would be the exact bug being fixed, just moved one step later.
