The existing `SavedStateIndicator` in the header is the only UI shown while this happens — no new indicator built, matching Daniel's own instinct exactly. All three (Back/Previous/Next) disable while a flush is in flight, so a second click can't race the first's outcome. **The back-chevron getting the same treatment was Daniel's own instinct, confirmed correct** — it was a pre-existing gap since Stage 1's own recon, only surfaced because Next made losing an in-flow edit a live concern worth naming.

  **AFLS (section 11), confirmed working in both directions, not by accommodating it in the generic path.** AFLS manages its own save queue entirely inside `AflsSection.tsx` (`saveQueueRef`, a promise chain per open assessment, its own inline status indicator) — nothing surfaced up to the section page at all, by design (see that component's own header comment). `flushAndAdvance` short-circuits straight to navigation whenever `section.kind === "afls"`, since there is no shared `content` to flush through the generic path — confirmed live, arriving at section 11 from section 10 via Next and leaving to section 12 via Next both worked cleanly, no crash, no visible special case, Previous/Next present and correct throughout. A real, separate gap was found while checking this — not fixed at the time, recorded below as its own item — rather than making the generic path accommodate it.

  **AFLS SAVE RESILIENCE — FOUND, CONFIRMED URGENT, FIXED, DEPLOYED, 15 Sept 2026. Done BEFORE `purge_stale_app_events()` scheduling and the section rail, on Daniel's own instruction, as the only one of the three that risked losing someone's work.**

  **What happened before this fix, confirmed by reading the code, not assumed**: not silent in the strictest sense — a failed save DID flip a local `status` to `"error"`, rendered as red "Couldn't save" text next to "Assessment details". But three things made that display practically unreliable as a safety net for how this section is actually used (rapid taps across 225 tasks, not lingering on one field): no automatic retry ever happened — a failed tap just sat there until the clinician manually redid it, with nothing telling them to; the very next tap anywhere else overwrote that same `status` with `"saving"` before most clinicians would ever notice the failure; and the moment the clinician left that assessment (back to the list, or to another FBA section entirely — which Next/Previous now makes easy), the state was discarded with nothing else anywhere (no header indicator, no persistent flag) to surface that it happened. **Verdict: not literally silent, but unreliable enough in real use to be urgent** — exactly the read that decided the ordering of this work.

  **Fixed by reusing the generic content_data path's own machinery, not writing a second version**, per instruction: `useAflsAssessmentsForFba.ts`'s `updateAssessment()` now goes through `insertWithOfflineRetry` (the same function `useFbaReport.ts`'s `saveContent` already uses), with the row `insertWithOfflineRetry`'s own `attempt` callback would otherwise discard (it only inspects `error`) captured via closure. A new `SaveCancelledError` mirrors the generic path's own cancelled/error distinction. `AflsSection.tsx`'s `queueSave` now threads `onStatusChange`/`signal` through and its own promise chain (`saveQueueRef`) resolves to a real outcome (`"saved" | "cancelled" | "error"`, never rejects) instead of `Promise<void>` — the same "return the outcome directly, don't infer it from state after an await" discipline the generic path's own `flushAndAdvance` was built on. The inline indicator (`InlineSaveStatus`) gained the same vocabulary as the header's own `SavedStateIndicator`: "Offline · Retry" plus a Cancel button while waiting for connectivity, "Couldn't save · Retry" on a real error — manual Retry re-sends the FULL current draft (every field, not just whichever one failed), same posture as the generic path's own always-whole-blob flush.

  **What genuinely could NOT be shared, and the closest equivalent built instead**: AFLS's own coalescing strategy (a promise-chain queue that re-reads the latest draft at run time, so a rapid burst of taps merges into one PATCH) is a DIFFERENT, already-correct answer to the same race the generic path solves by aborting-and-restarting — reusing `insertWithOfflineRetry`'s retry-loop didn't require touching this, and it was deliberately left alone rather than replaced. What the generic path's own `abortRef`-per-edit couldn't be reused for is per-tap superseding (AFLS doesn't need it, the queue already handles that) — a NEW `saveAbortRef` was added instead, scoped only to "whichever save is the currently-EXECUTING head of the queue", used solely by the manual Cancel button, and deliberately never auto-aborted by a later tap (that would have broken the existing coalescing behaviour, a real behaviour change this fix was not asked to make).

  **The navigation-safety gap named in the entry above is also closed**: `AflsSection` now exposes exactly one imperative method (`flushPendingSave(): Promise<boolean>`, via `forwardRef`/`useImperativeHandle`) that awaits its own current queue tail and reports whether it actually saved. The routing page's `flushAndAdvance` calls this instead of short-circuiting for `section.kind === "afls"` — Back/Previous/Next now all wait for AFLS's own pending save too, exactly the same "stay put unless truly saved" rule already applied to the other thirteen sections, with the coupling kept to one boolean method rather than reaching into AFLS's own tokens/queue/abort internals from the generic path.

  **Verified live on local-dev**, genuinely offline, not simulated by inspection: spoofed `navigator.onLine = false` and blocked the specific `afls_assessments` PATCH request via a `fetch` override, then tapped a score. Confirmed: the inline indicator showed "Offline · Retry" with Cancel; the score was NOT in the database while blocked (checked directly); clicking Next while blocked left the Next button disabled and did NOT navigate; clicking the AFLS Cancel button un-blocked Next (re-enabled) but Next STILL correctly refused to navigate (outcome was `"cancelled"`, not `"saved"`) — confirmed still on section 11; restoring real connectivity and tapping a fresh score saved successfully (confirmed in the database, alongside the earlier field — AFLS always sends the whole scores object, so the cancelled tap's own value rode along on the next successful save, though the SAFETY check is what actually prevented navigating away in the gap between); Next then advanced normally to section 12. Fixture torn down, zero orphans.

  **A narrower residual risk, named rather than fixed**: `AflsSection`'s `openEntryFor` resets `saveQueueRef` to a fresh chain every time a DIFFERENT assessment is opened. If a save for assessment A is still genuinely in flight (not yet settled) when the clinician switches to assessment B, that A-chain is orphaned — not cancelled, the underlying request still completes on its own, but `flushPendingSave` from that point on only awaits B's queue, not A's. Narrower than the fix above (requires switching assessments within the same in-flight window, not just poor connectivity generally) and not what this pass was asked to close — named here so it isn't re-discovered as a surprise later.

  **The section-rail — BUILT, DONE, DEPLOYED, 15 Sept 2026, after AFLS save resilience, per Daniel's own ordering.** Breadth (jump to any of fourteen sections at once) alongside Previous/Next's own sequence, not an alternative to it, confirmed and now shipped as designed. All fourteen sections as a persistent left list at lg+ (`fba/[fbaId]/page.tsx`'s own index page, `lg:flex` with a fixed `lg:w-72` rail column, not the 4/12 grid the caseload/Directory split views use — a section's own content is a number plus a title, narrow and short, and a grid fraction would either crowd long titles or waste width), section content on the right, switching in place — same `matchMedia("(min-width: 1024px)")` + `preventDefault()` interception on the rail cards' own `<Link>` as every other split view this pass built. Below lg: unchanged, confirmed live — the plain card list, real navigation to the standalone routed section page, same as it always was.

  **The section editor was extracted into `FbaSectionEditor.tsx`**, same move as `ClinicalFileDetail.tsx`'s own extraction (Stage 2) — the routed page (`section/[sectionId]/page.tsx`) is now a thin shell supplying real `router.push` navigation; the workspace index's own right pane renders the exact same component with navigation callbacks that just update local state instead. The one genuine design decision this needed, that `ClinicalFileDetail` never had to answer: Previous/Next/Back can't hard-code `router.push` anymore, because inside the rail-hosted pane that would navigate AWAY from the split view entirely, defeating the reason it exists — both callbacks (`onNavigateBack`/`onNavigateSection`) are now injected, so the identical component behaves correctly in both contexts without knowing which one it's in. Deliberately no `key` on either call site: `content` is the WHOLE FBA's `content_data` blob, not a per-section slice, so switching which section is being viewed never needs a reseed, and `isDirty`/`changeVersionRef` are already guaranteed caught-up by construction (flushAndAdvance refuses to switch sections at all otherwise) — same "no key, the fetch effect itself handles an identity-prop change" pattern `ChildDetail.tsx` already established for principal's own split view.

  **Rail clicks get the identical flush-before-navigate treatment Previous/Next/Back already have, including AFLS**, confirmed rather than assumed: `FbaSectionEditor` exposes `flushPendingSave(): Promise<boolean>` via `forwardRef`/`useImperativeHandle` (same shape as `AflsSectionHandle`, one level up) — the workspace index's own `handleRailSelect` awaits it before ever calling `setSelectedSectionSlug`, reusing the SAME internal logic Previous/Next/Back call directly, including the AFLS branch that waits on `AflsSection`'s own separate save queue via its own handle.

  **Verified live on local-dev**, mirroring the AFLS save-resilience pass's own rigor rather than a lighter check because this was "just a rail": selecting a rail item switches the pane in place with no URL change; an edited-but-unflushed field survived a rail click and was confirmed in the database afterward; Previous/Next clicked INSIDE the pane correctly moves the rail's own highlighted selection too (not just the pane's content); genuinely offline (spoofed `navigator.onLine` + blocked the real AFLS PATCH, not simulated) confirmed the rail refuses to switch away from AFLS while a save is stuck waiting for connectivity, and continues refusing immediately after a manual Cancel (the tap was never actually saved, so staying put is correct, not a bug) until the SAME field was successfully re-saved, at which point the rail switched normally. Below lg confirmed unchanged: plain list, real navigation to the standalone route. Fixture torn down, zero orphans.

  **STAGE 2 — the caseload split view — DONE, verified on local-dev, deployed.** Same `ClassesList`/`ChildrenList` interception pattern as PRD 4's Directory, applied without inventing anything new: `clinician/passports/page.tsx` gained the same 4/8-of-12 grid, `matchMedia("(min-width: 1024px)")` + `preventDefault()` on the caseload cards' own `<Link>`. Below lg, unchanged — a real navigation to `/clinician/passport/[id]`, confirmed live (browser back button correctly returns to the list). The 569-line clinical file was extracted into `ClinicalFileDetail.tsx` (mirrors `ChildDetail.tsx`'s own extraction from `principal/passports/[passportId]/page.tsx` exactly), reused both by the real route (now a thin shell: back-chevron + title only) and inline in the split pane's right column. The eleven-tab strip becomes a vertical list at lg+ inside the pane (all eleven visible, no scrolling) via one shared button map with responsive classes — below lg it's the identical horizontal scroller, untouched.

  **Two things the extraction surfaced, both flagged rather than silently decided:**
  1. The original page's header carried real functionality beyond a title — the Calm-button status pill and "Connected by X / End your involvement". Left in a thin route shell, both would have vanished from the split-pane's right column ("End your involvement" specifically would have become unreachable purely by being on a wide screen). Moved into `ClinicalFileDetail`'s own body instead — not a behaviour change, same content and action, only where it renders moved, which the extraction itself required.
  2. The floating "+ Log ABC Incident" bar is `fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0`, which ignores whatever contains it — inside the split pane it would have spanned the full page width, under the sidebar too. Same problem `AppBottomNav` already had, solved the same way: `lg:hidden`, not redesigned into something pane-scoped. **Decided, 15 Sept 2026: stays hidden at lg+, deliberately.** A fixed bar spanning under the sidebar is a worse outcome than its absence, and "Add Log" on the dashboard reaches the same ABC logger for any case. But this IS a real loss, not a clean resolution — record it as one: a clinician working the desktop split view loses the one-tap shortcut straight from the case they're already looking at, and has to leave the clinical file to log against it. If a clinician mentions missing this, that's why — not a bug to re-diagnose, a known, accepted trade-off from this stage.

  **A real bug found and fixed, not part of the brief but blocking it:** `min-width: auto` is the default on a flex item, and `src/app/clinician/layout.tsx`'s content div is a flex item of the layout's own row (`flex min-h-full flex-1`) — without an explicit `min-w-0`, it refuses to shrink below its own content's intrinsic width. Any `overflow-x-auto` scroller inside sets that intrinsic width to its own full unscrolled size instead of containing its own overflow. Confirmed live: the plain clinician dashboard was ALREADY scrolling horizontally at 375px (796px of real content) before this fix — a Stage 1 gap that went unnoticed because nothing in Stage 1's own verification checked `document.body.scrollWidth` against the viewport, only nav visibility. Stage 2's eleven-item tab strip made it severe (1128px), which is what surfaced it. Fixed with `min-w-0` on `clinician/layout.tsx`'s content div. **`principal/layout.tsx` had the identical missing `min-w-0`, copied verbatim as the Stage 1 precedent — FIXED, same day, 15 Sept 2026.** Confirmed dormant there at the time (no principal page then had wide enough `overflow-x-auto` content to trigger visible overflow) — fixed pre-emptively rather than left as a documented landmine, on Daniel's own instruction: dormant means it fires the first time anyone puts a wide child on a principal screen, which is exactly what remaining PRD 4 work would do, and flagging a known bug in a file this team controls is a worse outcome than the one-line fix. Verified on local-dev: dashboard and Directory both clean at 375px and 1280px, sidebar and split-view unaffected.

  **Verified on local-dev** (not yet checked against a fresh deploy at the time of this entry) with a real two-child caseload, both connected via the real `connect_clinician()` RPC: split pane renders both cases, selecting one fills the right pane without navigating, tab switching works inside the pane, switching between the two selected children refetches data but doesn't reset `activeTab` (matching `ChildDetail`'s own accepted, un-fixed precedent, not a new gap). `?tab=effectiveness` deep link still pre-selects the right tab on the standalone route. Browser back button after a real mobile navigation returns to the list. Same four-other-tracks-unaffected check as Stage 1, repeated (teacher/parent/sna/principal all clean at 375px and 1280px, no overflow, principal's own sidebar/nav intact) specifically because the layout file changed again for the `min-w-0` fix.

  **Stage 1 itself — DONE, verified on local-dev, not yet deployed.** Same precedent as PRD 4's principal track, applied without inventing anything new: `ClinicianSidebar.tsx` (mirrors `PrincipalSidebar.tsx`, `hidden lg:flex lg:w-64`, no Support Button slot — the clinician track never had one), `clinicianNavTabs.ts` (mirrors `principalNavTabs.ts`, the same four destinations `ClinicianBottomNav` already had — Dashboard/Passports/Messages/More — lifted out, not added to), `src/app/clinician/layout.tsx` (mirrors `principal/layout.tsx` exactly, `lg:pl-64` content shift), `ClinicianBottomNav` wrapped in `lg:hidden` (same reasoning as `PrincipalBottomNav`'s own wrap — `AppBottomNav` itself untouched, still shared and unmodified across every track). A max-width pass (`lg:max-w-[66.6667%]`, School's own precedent) on dashboard, activity, insights, messages, log, the FBA list, the FBA section index, and `FbaSectionShell` — the last one is the single change point for all fourteen FBA sections' own forms, the "long forms built for a phone" problem the recon named as the actual readability issue. Deliberately NOT touched: `passport/[passportId]` (569 lines, 11-tab horizontal scroller) and the caseload list (`passports/page.tsx`) — both explicitly Stage 2 (the split-view work), confirmed still rendering full-width and unchanged in this pass. Verified: full typecheck/lint/build clean; a cross-track fixture (5 accounts, one per role) confirmed teacher/parent/sna show no sidebar and an unaffected bottom nav at both 375px and 1280px; principal's own PRD 4 sidebar/bottom-nav behaviour is unaffected (5 items, `lg:hidden` wrap intact); clinician shows no sidebar + visible bottom nav at 375px, and the sidebar (4 items, 256px, `lg:pl-64` content shift) + hidden bottom nav + `682.66px` (66.6667% of the 1024px remaining width) capped content at 1280px, confirmed on the dashboard, FBA list, FBA index, and a section editor specifically. Fixture torn down, zero orphans. **Run against local-dev, not the deployed app — this has not shipped yet.** Stage 2 (the passport list/detail split view, plus the tab-strip-to-vertical-list decision inside the pane) is next.

- **CONSENT/AGREEMENT SCREENS — REBUILT, DONE, DEPLOYED (`bdff8ef`).** The old screen was wrong twice over: false content (told parents "you control who sees it" when the school owns its own record) and false form (presented staff AGREEMENT, which they cannot decline and keep working, as CONSENT, a real choice). Replaced with five distinct components (`src/components/consent/`) — parent gets one genuine consent tick, staff/clinicians each get three separate required agreement ticks (use, confidentiality, account security) — no shared component takes a role prop and swaps copy. `consents` table gained a `role` column (migration 0181, backfilled exact from real `app_metadata` at the time), and `hasConsented()` now checks `consent_version >= current`, not "any row exists," so a version bump (1→2, for the copy rewrite) genuinely re-prompts existing consenters rather than grandfathering them into copy they never saw. Design went through two corrections after the initial build read as "too similar to the rest of the app" then "a wireframe" — final version is medtech-sharp (Inter, `#1A1A1A`/`#5A5A5A` type scale, no card, pure white background, hairline Pastel Blue rules, a content-width pill button that recedes when disabled) with Golden Brown reserved for each staff screen's one emphasised sentence. See the "deliberate break in visual continuity" entry above for why this doesn't read as broken.

- **TEST ACCOUNT CLEANUP — DONE, 10 Sept 2026.** The 16 leftover accounts found the same day (`p0150b.*`, `p0151b.*`, `p0152.*`, `mcclasscheck.*`, `navsupport.*`, `pincident.*`, `zzbulk.*`) are removed — `scripts/dev/teardown-2026-09-10-leftover-test-accounts.mjs`. Production is down to exactly the 3 real accounts (`daniel@dunvaris.co.uk`, `daniel@thebehaviourhive.com`, `dcasey28@outlook.com`), confirmed by a fresh `listUsers()` after the run. Zero orphans confirmed by direct query across passports, passport_guardians, passport_institution_links, incidents (all three FK roles), messages, message_recipients, consents, app_events, institution_staff, passport_access. **How they survived, since several were reported torn down at the time:** `scripts/dev/teardown.mjs`'s own user mode refuses any email that doesn't contain the literal string `"zzfixture"` — none of these six prefixes match it, so any real attempt to tear them down with the one general-purpose teardown tool in this repo would have been refused outright, loudly. No setup or teardown script for any of these six naming conventions exists anywhere in git history or in the untracked working tree today (checked both) — whatever created them was never committed and is gone without a trace, so there's no way to confirm whether a teardown was attempted and silently failed, or never run at all despite being reported. The concrete, checkable fact: nothing in the repo could have removed them before this pass. All 16 had zero institution_staff rows already (their institutions' own teardown had happened), zero incidents in any role, zero messages — genuinely isolated leftovers, not a sign of a deeper cleanup failure. One unrelated thing noticed in passing, not acted on: a second orphaned institution, `ZZCHECKSIGNOFF103`, exists with no live staff — unconnected to any of these 16 accounts, not investigated further here.

- **MEDICAL AND INTIMATE CARE NEEDS DO NOT EXIST IN THE PASSPORT — SAFEGUARDING GAP, GOING TO CATHERINE.** Found 7 September 2026, investigating why a supply-teacher passport-review prompt could only ever cover three of four categories it was meant to. Confirmed by a full search of every migration and every passport section's own client code: neither a medical-needs field nor an intimate-care-needs field exists anywhere in the schema, under any name. `passport_section_b` has triggers (`hard_triggers`), `passport_section_c` has communication needs (`communication_methods`, `phrases_to_avoid`), `passport_section_d` has calming/distress-response content (`during_distress`, `after_distress`) — all three real, all three confirmed reachable through SNA-tier access (traced end to end: `has_sna_access()`'s live temporary-grant branch → `has_child_access()` → each section's own SELECT policy). Medical and intimate care needs are simply not there. This is not a newly-discovered "nice to have" — both have been referenced repeatedly, across at least three separate briefs, as part of what "roster-tier support information" already means; none of those briefs surfaced that the fields themselves don't exist. **A special school where a covering supply teacher cannot learn a child's medical or intimate-care needs from this product is a safeguarding gap, not a missing feature.** Not a design decision to make here — Catherine needs to see this before any further product/clinical planning assumes these fields are real. The supply-teacher passport-review prompt (below) was shipped scoped to the three real sections, saying so plainly in its own copy, rather than held pending these two.

  **BUILT, DONE, DEPLOYED (`b1751a4`), 10 Sept 2026.** New `passport_section_e` table (migration 0184), RLS copied verbatim from Section B/C/D's live post-Step-1b shape (parent SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE via `owns_passport()`, staff SELECT via `has_child_access()` — so roster-tier and covering/temporary SNA access reach it exactly like B/C/D — clinician SELECT via `is_verified_clinician()` + active `clinician_access`). Five discrete fields: allergies, medical conditions relevant to daily care, medications (incl. anything given during the school day), emergency protocol, intimate care needs (its own field, not folded into medical). Single-page form at `/passport/section-e` (not a wizard, unlike Section D) via `usePassportSectionE.ts`. Wired into both the SNA and teacher passport pages as a new "Medical & Care" tab, and into `SECTION_PAGE_COUNTS`. The existing passport-completion-request system (`passport_completion_requests`, previously hardcoded to Section A only) was generalized with a `target_section` column (migration 0185, DROP+CREATE on all four affected RPCs, old unique constraint dynamically located and dropped rather than assumed-named) so a teacher/SNA can request Section E specifically, not just Section A — `PassportCompletionSection`/`PassportCompletionPromptCard` both branch on `targetSection`. `formatRelativeDate()` (`src/lib/relativeDate.ts`) shows a plain "Updated X ago" on each field group — deliberately no threshold-based staleness flag, held per Daniel's own instruction until built and seen. `SupplyTeacherPassportReviewCard`'s copy updated from three-of-four to all-four-sections coverage, closing the gap the section above first surfaced. Live-verified on the deployed app (`https://behaviour-hive-app.vercel.app`) with a real 2-parent fixture: all 5 fields render correctly on the teacher passport page's Medical & Care tab. **One thing to record, not solve: a parent-authored field is NOT the same thing as a formal intimate care plan.** An intimate care plan in Irish special schools is normally a formal school document with agreed procedures, often signed by both parties — a structured agreement between school and family, not a field a parent fills in alone on their own schedule. This field must never be presented, described, or mistaken as that formal plan, and nobody building on top of this later should assume the app holds it. If a formal intimate care plan is ever wanted in-product, that's a separate, considered piece of work — a document with two-party agreement built into its own shape — not an extension of this field.

- **SUPPLY TEACHER PASSPORT-REVIEW PROMPT — DONE, SCOPED TO THREE OF FOUR SECTIONS.** Built 7 September 2026 per Daniel's own brief: appears on the covering person's `/sna/passports` dashboard the moment a live `temporary_access` grant exists, lists every child in the covered class, one tap each to `/sna/passport/[passportId]`, clears automatically once every child has been opened (or dismissed in one action — "I already know this class"), and disappears naturally at cut-off with the grant itself (no separate expiry logic needed — the RPCs re-derive the same live start/cutoff window `has_sna_access()` already uses, so an expired grant simply stops matching). "Reviewed" = opened, tracked per child (`passport_review_views`, scoped to the specific `temporary_access` row, not the person or the class in general — a new day is a new grant is a new "walking into the room" moment). Migration 0178. See the medical/intimate-care entry immediately above for the one thing this deliberately does NOT cover.

- **THE ATTESTATION SIGN-OFF RACE — FIXED.** Found live, real production data, 7 September 2026: a named colleague could be recorded as never-attested when they were never actually given the chance — an owning teacher could request attestations and sign off minutes later, and nothing distinguished "nobody was asked" from "asked, then the window closed" (the request became simultaneously unfulfillable — `attest_to_incident()` already refuses post-signoff — and invisible, filtered out of both `teacher/dashboard` and the dedicated attestations page by the same `is_closed` check). `incident_signoff_issues()` still deliberately never blocks sign-off on a merely-never-attested colleague — right for a record where nobody was asked at all. Fixed narrower than that: `sign_off_incident()` (migration 0176) now soft-blocks — refuses by default, proceeds only on an explicit second confirmation (`p_proceed_without_attestations`) — whenever attestations were genuinely requested and someone named hasn't responded, and records that it happened (`incidents.signed_off_with_outstanding_attestations`). Surfaced three places: `SignOffCard`'s own explicit checkbox at the moment of the decision, `CountersignCard`'s own non-muted callout for the countersigning principal, and a new "Routine" dashboard bucket (`get_institution_incidents_signed_off_with_outstanding_attestations`) so it's discoverable without opening the incident. The named person's own surface (`teacher/incidents/attestations`) now says "this was signed off before you responded" instead of the same generic "preserved, read-only" every closed row got before.

- **PARENT CALL — THE EXPLICIT NO — FIXED.** Found alongside the attestation race, same real incident: a teacher's own "No" to "parent call required?" was silently overwritten by an auto-raise trigger (`flag_parent_call_for_injury()`/`flag_parent_call_for_restrictive_practice()`) the moment an injury or restrictive practice was logged in the same session — the schema had no way to represent "explicitly answered No" as distinct from "never answered", both were the same `false`. Worse: the client UI never actually offered an explicit "No" action at all before this fix — `false` was simply the untouched default, displayed as a "No" pill but never affirmatively chosen. Fixed with `incident_children.parent_call_answered_at` (migration 0177), set only by the teacher's own explicit write (`set_parent_call_answer()`, now genuinely reachable in both directions from the incident detail page), checked by both auto-raise triggers before they touch the column again. When a real injury/restraint exists on an incident where the teacher explicitly answered No, a new institution-wide bucket (`get_institution_restraints_with_declined_parent_call`) surfaces it to the principal — worded as a fact to review ("answered No to a parent call"), never an instruction to call (they already decided not to).

- **INCIDENT LOG PHASE 7 — DONE.** Deferred since PRD 1's foundational changes to the module were still in flight; picked back up once that foundation had settled. Three asks, all delivered:
  1. **The two guard triggers with zero adversarial coverage anywhere in the suite**, found by grepping every incident-module RPC/trigger name against every existing check rather than assuming the 1048-check suite already covered everything: `guard_incident_injuries_named_party()` (an injury naming a child/staff member not already on the incident) and `guard_restrictive_practice_staff_real_account()` (linking a free-text-only staff entry, or one named on a different incident, to a restrictive-practice record). Both were reachable, untested, on a legal record. Closed with CHECK JJJ (6 assertions, each guard's own refusal plus a regression control proving the genuinely-valid case still works).
  2. **The continuous lifecycle run** — CHECK III, 23 assertions. Every prior check (over a thousand of them) builds its own fixture and proves one guarantee in isolation; nothing had ever walked a single incident through every stage in one continuous sequence. This does: `create_incident_stamp()` (two children, deliberately, so two-child scoping survives the whole run) → content written separately for both children → `attestations_requested` toggled (stage two) → attest → withdraw (proven to genuinely block `sign_off_incident()` on the very next call, not just flip a label) → re-attest → a debrief completed → `sign_off_incident()` (the RPC) → an immutability tamper attempt by the same owning teacher who just signed (confirmed not persisted) → `countersign_incident()` (the RPC) → `get_incident_export()`, checked specifically for the two-child content split (each child's own distress/remained-on-site fields, the restrictive practice attributed to child A, the injury attributed to child B) — the one thing no prior check had proven, since CHECK U's own export coverage was one child only and CHECK B's own two-child incident's sign-off was a raw update that was never fed to export. **Result: the continuous run found nothing the per-stage checks had missed.** Every stage composed correctly with every other stage on first attempt — a real, positive result, not a gap in how thoroughly it was checked.
  3. Full regression: 1077/1077 passing (1048 prior + 29 new, zero regressions). SHA confirmed deployed.

- **SNA MESSAGING — SPLIT IN TWO, AND THIS ENTRY WAS STALE ABOUT HALF OF IT.** Staff-to-staff (institution-wide, non-child) messaging for SNAs is DONE — migration 0168 added `sna` to `message_categories.allowed_sender_roles` for all three staff categories (Cover/Rota, Class/Roster, General) and to `messages`' own role constraints; `src/app/sna/messages/page.tsx` is fully wired (`useStaffMessageThread`, `ComposeMessageSheet`, real `institutionId`) — confirmed both the SQL and the client live, 10 Sept 2026, not assumed from the migration alone. What's still genuinely unbuilt is narrower than this entry originally said: CHILD-scoped messaging (a specific passport, not the staff channel) has no SNA branch anywhere — `get_message_recipient_candidates()` has no `sna` case, and none of the `applies_to: 'child'` categories list `sna` in `allowed_sender_roles`. Still parked as a product decision about which categories (if any) an SNA may raise about a specific child, not a query change — the mechanism migration 0161 built for the principal case would extend cleanly the same way once that decision is made.

- **PRINCIPAL ACTIVITY FEED — PER-CHILD EVENTS — BUILT, DONE, 15 Sept 2026, REVERSING THE ENTRY BELOW. THE REASONING FOR BOTH SIDES IS KEPT HERE, NOT JUST THE OUTCOME.**

  **The original exclusion, kept for the record:** staff-level events were in (`get_principal_activity_feed()`, migration 0171: joins, leaves, rejections, handovers, temporary access grants); per-child events (ABC logs, incidents) were deliberately excluded. The live objection at the time: an institution-wide stream of individual per-child logging reads as watching staff work, which the safeguarding literature warns produces under-logging — the failure that matters most in a restraint-recording product.

  **Daniel's own reason for reversing it, Stage 4 item 3:** a principal needs to see which CHILDREN are having a hard time, so they can put support where it is needed — that is a principal's job, and it is the OPPOSITE concern from watching staff. The two readings were never actually in tension once named this precisely: "who is struggling" and "who is under-performing" are different questions asked of the identical data, and the exclusion had been guarding against the second without noticing a real, legitimate version of the first was the cost.

  **THE SAFEGUARD SURVIVES, IN A DIFFERENT FORM, NOT DROPPED:** no per-staff grouping, no ranking, no sorting by who logged what. `get_principal_activity_feed()` (migration 0192) adds two new institution-wide branches — incidents and ABC logs, every child at the principal's own school — and neither carries any staff-identifying data at all: incident rows use the exact generic text `get_teacher_activity_feed()` already uses ("An incident was recorded.", no author), and ABC log rows use `activity_log.event_description`'s own stored text, which is role-generic ("ABC incident logged by Teacher"/"...by SNA"), never a named individual (confirmed by reading `ABCLogger.tsx`'s own insert call directly). `passport_id`/`child_name` identify the CHILD; nothing in either branch can be grouped or sorted by staff identity because no staff identity is present in the row to begin with. This is the same distinction the "principal may see and sort by who logged an incident" entry above draws between identification (fine) and a staff performance metric — a league table, a score, comparison between staff (not fine, needs its own conversation) — this feed is squarely the identification case, about children, not staff.

  **The teacher-side companion bug, found in the same pass and fixed alongside it (migration 0193):** `get_teacher_activity_feed()`'s own `activity_log` branch had a SEPARATE restriction, unrelated to `has_child_access()` — `and (al.event_type <> 'abc_logged' or al.actor_id = auth.uid())` — meaning an `abc_logged` row only ever showed in a teacher's OWN activity feed if the teacher themselves was the one who logged it. A class teacher with full class-derived access to a child could not see an SNA's own ABC entry for that same child in their feed, even though the ABC Logs tab itself (`get_abc_logs()`) has always correctly shown every staff member's entries to anyone with `has_child_access()` — this self-authored restriction was unique to the feed, not the underlying data visibility. A straight bug, not a deliberate narrowing: nothing else in this schema treats "logged it yourself" as a precondition for seeing a colleague's entry about a child you both have access to. Removed in 0193 — the teacher feed's `abc_logged` branch is now gated purely on `has_child_access()`, same as every other event type in that branch.

- **PARENT-VERSION INCIDENT PDF (PER CHILD) — DONE, DEPLOYED (`b1751a4`), 10 Sept 2026.** A new route, `/incidents/[incidentId]/print-parent/[passportId]`, one export per child on a multi-child incident, reachable two ways with one authorization each: the parent's own on-screen incident page (`Export this record`) and the staff incident page (`Export for parent`, one link per child), both landing on the exact same route — same route, two entry points, per the brief, not a mode flag and not two functions. Redaction is NOT re-derived: `get_parent_incidents()` (migration 0183, DROP+CREATE) is the same function that already gates the on-screen parent view, extended in place (`owns_passport(p_passport_id) or can_view_incident(i.id)` — the second branch is what lets staff generate it) rather than a second definition of what a parent may see. Decisions from the brief, all shipped: the child's real name in the header; **restraint stated as a bare fact of what happened, not evidence to withhold** — "Physical intervention was used." plus `planning_status`/`ncse_report_complete` only, never hold type/position/level/who — and the on-screen parent page was changed to match (previously conditional-rendered the restrictive-practice block only when present; now always renders an explicit used/not-used statement, same as the PDF); body-map marks included for the child's own injuries only (`BodyMapPrintCard` reused, not duplicated, across the school PDF, this new parent PDF, and the on-screen page); amendments included, unattributed (no author name). **The cross-child amendment leak this flagged as a named, un-fixable risk was real, confirmed live, and has since been FIXED — see the dedicated entry below; do not read this paragraph as still describing current behaviour.** Live-verified on the deployed app with a genuine 2-child incident (restraint + injury + body mark on one child only): Parent A's PDF and on-screen page both show the full restraint statement and body map; Parent B's PDF correctly shows "No physical intervention was used." and no Injuries section.

- **THE AMENDMENT LEAK — FOUND, FIXED, LIVE-VERIFIED, 10 Sept 2026.** The risk the parent-PDF entry above flagged as "not fixable by a WHERE clause" turned out to be real and live: on the same 2-child fixture, Parent B's export and on-screen record showed the SAME amendment text as Parent A's, because `incident_amendments` has no per-child scoping at all (`incident_id` only). Considered three fixes: drop amendments from the parent version entirely (safe, loses a genuine correction the family should see); require the author to write child-agnostically (unenforceable — free text, nothing to check it against); make sharing an explicit per-amendment choice made by the author, who actually knows the incident and who else is on it. Built the third. `incident_amendments.is_parent_visible` (migration 0186), a boolean the author sets via a checkbox in `AddAmendmentSheet.tsx` ("Share this amendment with the family"), **default false**. `get_parent_incidents()` (same 0186) now only returns amendments where `is_parent_visible = true` — same reuse-not-re-derive posture as the rest of this feature; nothing about who may call the function changed, only what it returns. **Every amendment written before this migration defaults to NOT shared** — nobody was asked when they wrote it, so nothing already on the record retroactively reaches a parent who was never meant to see it; sharing an existing correction means writing a fresh amendment and actively checking the box, a deliberate re-decision rather than an inferred one. The multi-child warning banner (`isMultiChild`) now only shows once the author has actually checked the share box — precise to the moment it's relevant, rather than shown unconditionally on every multi-child amendment regardless of whether it will ever reach a parent. `get_incident_amendments()` (staff-side list, `IncidentAmendmentsSection.tsx`) also now returns and displays `is_parent_visible`, so a teacher/principal can see which amendments a parent will see without opening the export. Live-verified on the deployed app with a fresh 2-child fixture: added one unchecked ("staff-only") and one checked ("shared") amendment through the real UI as the principal — the multi-child warning correctly appeared only once the share box was checked, the staff-side list showed a "Shared with family" tag on only the shared one, and both `get_parent_incidents()` (real signed-in Parent A and Parent B sessions) and Parent A's own on-screen page and PDF returned only the shared amendment — the staff-only one never appeared anywhere a parent could reach. Fixture torn down, zero orphans confirmed.

- **REAL NAMES IN THE PRINCIPAL'S PDF EXPORT — CLOSED, DECIDED, DONE, 10 Sept 2026.** Was open, held pending a clinical/data-protection view: the principal's incidents TABLE showed real child names but the PDF export (`/principal/incidents/print`) still fell back to anonymous letter codes, on the reasoning that a printed list is a document that leaves the app in a way a scrolled screen doesn't. Closed by a bigger decision made in the same session: the parent-facing incident PDF (per child, see below) now carries a child's real name in its own header — a materially larger exposure than a principal, already authorized to see every child at their own school by name on the detail page, seeing those same names on a list they themselves requested. The print page now renders `child_names` (falling back to `child_indices` only as a guard, not a real path) — same field the on-screen table already used.

- **`get_passport_clinicians()`'S CALLERS — RE-CHECKED 10 Sept 2026, NOT A LIVE BUG. THIS ENTRY WAS WRONG.** Previously flagged as "four callers treat every row as currently connected — a revoked clinician still counts." Read the live function definition (0124, the current one, not assumed from an earlier version) before touching any client code: its own `where` clause has `ca.is_active = true` in it, and its `returns table` column list doesn't even include an `is_active` field — every row it can ever return is, by construction, currently active. A caller "not checking `is_active`" isn't a gap: there is no `is_active` to check, because the server-side filter already guarantees it on every row that arrives. Confirmed against all five real call sites (the four this entry named, plus `ChildDetail.tsx`'s own separate call), including that `ChildDetail.tsx` already does the historical case correctly through a DIFFERENT function (`get_passport_clinician_history()`, 0128), never through this one. No code changed — there was nothing to fix. Exactly the "read the live definition, not the first one you find" mistake, just this time in a written note instead of code: this entry itself was the stale artefact, carried forward and recited without re-verification until asked to close it.

- **TRIAL EVIDENCE — TIMESTAMP ANALYSIS.** Six of seven process-timing questions are answerable from existing columns. Using them for efficiency reporting is a SECONDARY PURPOSE under GDPR and needs Catherine plus a DPA reviewer before any report is produced. Not a build item.

- **TIME-ON-TASK INSTRUMENTATION — NOT BUILT.** Capturing screen-open or first-keystroke alongside submission would answer "how long does the 15-second stamp actually take", which nothing answers today. One extra parameter on existing RPCs, no new table. Blocked on the same review.

- **SESSION AND NAVIGATION TRACKING — NOT BUILT, AND MAY NOT BE WORTH IT.** Purpose-built monitoring of when and how long a named employee spends on a named record about a named child with a disability. Likely requires a formal DPIA. If Part 1's existing timestamps meet the trial's evidence needs, this should not be built at all.

- **EOD `submission_source` COLUMN — NOT ADDED.** Nothing records whether an EOD update came from the bulk flow or the standalone one, so bulk-vs-not comparison currently means inferring from clustering.

- **SUPABASE AUTH RATE LIMIT — BACK TO 30, PER DANIEL, 10 Sept 2026.** Was raised from the default 30 to 300 requests per 5 minutes per IP so the full adversarial suite could complete in one run (root cause of the suite dying at the same point on every attempt regardless of how long between retries, finally diagnosed 2026-08-30: ~90 real sign-ins plus ~122 `createUser` calls in one run against a 30-per-5-minute window — not a slow-clearing hourly bucket as first assumed). Daniel reports it's back to 30. Not independently verified here — this setting lives in the Supabase Auth dashboard, outside what the service-role/anon keys this environment has can read; taken on his own direct report, the same way any other account-level config change would be. If the adversarial suite needs a full run again, this will need raising again first, and reverting again after.

- **THE GATE RULE, SINCE THE 30-PER-5-MINUTE LIMIT MEANS A FULL ADVERSARIAL RUN NEEDS A SECURITY SETTING TEMPORARILY RAISED. Decided with Daniel, 17 Sept 2026, after PRD 5 Stage 2's login-redirect fix was shipped on typecheck/lint/build/live-verification alone, correctly.** Client-only changes that touch no RLS policy, no RPC, no trigger, and no query shape (a `.select()`/`.eq()`/filter change against a table under RLS, even with no migration attached — a new shape can trip a gap an old one never reached, same class of risk this file's own "embedded join across RLS" and "a narrower TypeScript interface" gotchas describe) ship on typecheck, lint, build, and live verification, with the reason stated in the report. Anything that touches the database — a migration, a new or altered RLS policy, an RPC, a trigger, a CHECK constraint, OR a client-side query shape change against an RLS-gated table — gets a full adversarial suite run, full stop, no case-by-case judgment call. For that class: raise the rate limit for the duration of the run, and put it back to 30 immediately after — never leave it raised past the run that needed it.

- **`sync_passport_guardian_from_user_id()` — REVERSED, 15 Sept 2026: PARENT-LED SELF-CREATION IS RETIRED.** This entry (31 Aug 2026) called the trigger permanent on the reasoning that a parent who finds the app before a school has enrolled their child should still be able to build a passport. That was true when written and is not the shape of the product now — school-led creation (PRD 3's own pivot) means every real family arrives via a code the school issues, not a blank form.

  **The stronger reason to retire it, not just the product-shape one**: every orphan-passport bug this build has hit traces back to that one path being live. The 134-row cleanup (nothing joins through an institution to find a passport with none), the CHECK NN/OO fixture leak (the same shape, in the adversarial suite itself), and a real, live instance during this very QA pass (a parent account created a brand-new passport by mistake instead of claiming the school's one, torn down by hand) — three separate, independently-discovered bugs, one root cause each time. Retiring the entry points removes a bug class, not just a screen.

  **What actually changed, 15 Sept 2026**: `/passport/welcome` no longer offers "Next" into the self-created wizard — it's now a thin redirect (existing passport → dashboard, no passport → `/passport/claim`, the prompt already built and tested for this, PRD 1 Stage 5 Step 3 Requirement 4). `/passport/claim`'s own "I don't have a code" link is now explanatory text ("ask your child's school"), not a route to anywhere, since there's no longer an alternative path to send it to. `/passport/section-a`'s own insert branch (creates a bare passport when none exists) is deliberately left as defensive dead code, not stripped — it's a small piece of a heavily shared save function, and no normal navigation path reaches it anymore now that nothing links to section-a without an existing passport. `getPassportResumeHref.ts` (the self-created wizard's own A→B→C→D resume-walk) is deleted outright, not left inert — both parent-dashboard and passport/dashboard's own `isSelfCreated` branch and the sectionB/C/D fetches that existed solely to feed it are gone; every guardian now takes the single claimed-guardian resume path (section A incomplete → section-a, else → dashboard).

  **Confirmed before any of this shipped, not assumed**: zero self-created passports exist in production (queried live, post-wipe, 15 Sept 2026) — a genuinely clean cutover, nothing pre-existing depended on the removed resume logic.

  The trigger itself (`sync_passport_guardian_from_user_id()`) is UNCHANGED and still fires on the rare row that reaches `passports.user_id` via the dead insert branch above — kept for the same reason that branch is kept, not because self-creation is still a real product path. 44 adversarial fixtures still insert `passports.user_id` directly and depend on the trigger for that reason alone; unaffected by this change.

- **STEP 1b — DONE.** `passport_section_b/c/d`'s stale `unique(user_id)` constraints, dropped in `0139`; `usePassportSection{B,C,D}.ts` branch insert-vs-update on `passport_id`, no `onConflict` left anywhere in the client.

- **STRUCTURAL-ONLY CHECKS, STAGE 1 — CONVERTED WITH STAGE 1C.** Four call sites/surfaces protected a state that was unreachable before `hand_over_principal()` existed — `can_countersign_incident()`'s principal branch, `incident_locations` add/edit principal branches, `mark_parent_called()`, and `/principal/dashboard`'s own redirect-away behavior for a deactivated principal (corrected from an earlier "pending/rejected overlay" description — that page never renders an overlay at all, pending or rejected; it redirects to `/teacher/join-institution`, which is the one place any such overlay lives). All four needed SOME real deactivated principal to exist, which a 'leaving' handover now produces (`deactivated_at` set on the predecessor's row, `role` column untouched, still `'principal'` — structurally identical to what `deactivate_institution_staff()` would have produced if it could ever target a principal). All four converted: the first three via CHECK X's own adversarial coverage (`can_countersign_incident()` called directly, both `incident_locations` policy branches — add and edit are separate policies, tested separately — and `mark_parent_called()` isolated from its creator/owner bypass by using an incident the deactivated principal didn't create); the fourth via live browser verification on the deployed app — a genuinely departed principal, signing in fresh, lands on the plain join form via `/principal/dashboard`'s existing redirect, not stuck, not shown a wrongful message.

- **TWO items are PERMANENTLY STRUCTURAL, not four as an earlier pass through this list claimed — corrected after re-checking, not assumed equivalent.**
  - `deactivate_institution_staff()`'s last-principal guard: its ALLOW branch requires two simultaneously active principals at one institution, which the one-principal index excludes by design and handover's atomicity preserves. Defense-in-depth against a future relaxation of that invariant, not owed a live test. (Considered and rejected: reusing this RPC as handover's own 'leaving' mechanism would make the guard reachable, but only by first loosening its separate self-deactivation check — not a trade worth making.)
  - `approve_staff_join()`/`reject_staff_join()`'s caller check together with `derive_staff_join_approval()`'s EXISTS clause — the claim that a pending or rejected principal doesn't count as active. Originally listed as converting with Stage 1c on the reasoning that handover would open "a genuine window where a second principal-role join could exist as pending or rejected." Checked empirically once `hand_over_principal()` actually existed, and that reasoning was wrong: handover is atomic specifically so the institution is never observably principal-less, even momentarily — the successor's new principal row is inserted, active, in the same transaction that closes the predecessor's, so `institution_staff_one_principal_per_institution` is satisfied by exactly one row continuously, before and after. A third party's ordinary principal-role join attempt immediately after a real handover still fails at the same unique index, confirmed live: `duplicate key value violates unique constraint`. This item needs the SAME two-simultaneous-principals state as the last-principal guard above, for the identical structural reason — it belongs on this list, not the conversion one.

- **SECOND-PRINCIPAL JOIN — DONE.** `src/app/teacher/join-institution/page.tsx`'s `friendlyJoinError()` now points at the real mechanism: "This school already has a principal. Ask them to hand over the role to you, or join as a class teacher instead." — no "contact support," deliberately (that's the abandoned-principal path below, not the ordinary one).

- **SNA-SUCCESSOR HANDOVER — CLOSED.** `hand_over_principal()`'s own SQL always treated `class_teacher` and `sna` successors identically — nothing role-specific in its guards — but only the `class_teacher` case had ever been clicked through live; deliberately deferred at the end of Stage 1b rather than reasoned about ("I'd rather it was clicked than reasoned about"). Closed via a manual browser pass on the deployed app: a real SNA successor, handed the principal role via a genuine `hand_over_principal()` call, signs in fresh and lands on `/principal/dashboard` as the new principal — not `/sna/passports`, not stuck, not shown a wrongful message.

- **CROSS-INSTITUTION TEMPORARY ACCESS — DEFERRED BY DECISION, CLOSED, 10 Sept 2026.** Staff belonging to multiple institutions at once is a real, eventual requirement — not a trial one. Decided: not solved now, not tracked as a gap to work around. Client-side routing (`useRequireRole`/`getPostAuthRedirect`) is single-role-per-account: `app_metadata.role` is one claim, read to decide which screens a person can even reach, entirely separate from the database-level access `grant_temporary_access()` (Stage 3) actually grants. A brand-new account (no prior claim) works — they self-select 'sna' at `/role-select` and reach it. An existing same-institution SNA colleague works — they already claim 'sna' and already reach `/sna/passports`. A person whose account already claims a DIFFERENT role — most plausibly a class teacher at one school covering as supply staff at another — has no reachable path to the covering assignment at all, even though the database would grant it correctly. `grant_temporary_access()` deliberately does not override someone's own claimed identity to work around this — that would break their own primary navigation elsewhere. A multi-role-per-account redesign is what actually solves this, and it's real, separate, sizeable work — for post-trial scope, when it comes up.

- **CLINICIAN DOUBLE-ENGAGEMENT — DECIDED, CLOSED, NOT AN ISSUE, 10 Sept 2026.** `clinician_access`'s own `unique(passport_id, clinician_id)` constraint means the same clinician cannot be both parent-engaged and institution-engaged for the same child — there is exactly one row per (child, clinician) pair, and `engaged_by` can only ever hold one value at a time. Previously tracked as a known limitation; decided it isn't one. The refusal is the correct behaviour, not a compromise standing in for a fix: both `grant_clinician_access()` and `connect_clinician()` (0123) check for the OTHER authority's engagement before ever reaching the table's own unique constraint, and refuse with a specific, explanatory message — a principal trying to engage a parent-already-engaged clinician is told plainly why, never shown a raw `duplicate key value violates unique constraint`. Nothing to build here.

- **PRINCIPAL HANDOVER WITH NO SUCCESSOR — DECIDED, CLOSED, NOT AN ISSUE, 10 Sept 2026.** Previously tracked as an abandoned case needing a fix (`institution_admin` onboarding, C-08). Decided: there will always be a successor, or a temporary one — a school does not simply go without a principal. Because this isn't a real case, `institution_admin` self-service onboarding is NOT needed for the trial on this basis either — see the entry immediately below, which already reached the same conclusion (deferred, not blocking) from a different angle (institution creation itself being manual and deliberate). Both reasons now point the same way. If a genuinely successor-less departure ever happens in practice, it stays a documented Behaviour Hive support operation performed with the service role, same as today.

- **INSTITUTION CREATION BEING MANUAL IS DELIBERATE, NOT A GAP — C-08 DOWNGRADED BACK TO DEFERRED.** No signup path — teacher, SNA, or principal — can create an `institutions` row; every join flow (`teacher/join-institution`) only ever looks up an existing `institution_code`. Checked directly (2026-09-01, prompted by the full production wipe) and initially mis-framed as a blocking gap before a trial school could onboard. It is not: Behaviour Hive reviews a school's credentials before approving them onto the platform, and the institution code is sent by email only after that approval — a school with a code can enrol children and hold restraint records, so verifying who they are first is a control being kept on purpose, not friction to remove. C-08 (`institution_admin` self-service onboarding) is NOT blocking and NOT "the last structural gap" — it's deferred, same as principal handover above, and the two are related: what's actually missing is TOOLING around the manual approval step (a request queue, an approve action, the code-email itself), not the manual review step. Fine at one school; awkward at ten. Worth building before the platform has ten schools waiting on Daniel personally to run a service-role insert for each one — not before this trial.

- **FIXED, 10 Sept 2026: `fetchApprovedInstitutionPhone()` could show the wrong school's emergency contact number.** Was `passport_id` + `approved_by_parent = true`, `.limit(1)`, no `institution_id` scoping — a child with approved links to two institutions got *an* institution's phone number, not a defined one, decided by Postgres row order. Fixed by resolving through the passport's CURRENT ACTIVE ENROLMENT instead of the approval flag — `enrolments_one_active_per_child` (migration 0121) is a real unique partial index, confirmed live while fixing this, not assumed: **the "Stage 6's one-active-enrolment constraint doesn't exist yet" half of this entry's own original claim was itself stale — that constraint has existed since 0121.** Moved from a raw client query to a new `get_approved_institution_phone()` SECURITY DEFINER RPC (migration 0182) for a second reason found in the same pass: `enrolments`' own SELECT policy is staff-only, so a parent or clinician calling this directly would have silently gotten zero rows back (the RLS-embedded-join gotcha, again) — the RPC's own broader check (owner, staff with child access, an engaged clinician, or a linked institution's principal) covers all three tracks that actually call this. Falls back to the old approved-link behaviour only when a passport has no enrolment row at all. No caller changed — same function signature, all seven call sites untouched.

- **CLOSED, PRD 3 STAGE 1 + STAGE 3: a claimed passport can now be both READ and COMPLETED by its guardian.** Was: `passports`' SELECT policy moved to `owns_passport()` (0117) while INSERT/UPDATE stayed `user_id`-keyed, so a claimed guardian could see their child's passport but not fill it in — `section-a` landing them on a blank "create new" form that would silently make an unrelated second passport. Stage 1 extended `section-a`/`b`/`c`/`d`'s own write paths to guardian-based auth (`useMyPassport()`, explicit insert-vs-update, never a `user_id`-keyed upsert). Stage 3 closed the remaining gap — the write path worked but nothing surfaced the door in: `parent-dashboard`'s own "Build your child's passport" card was still gated on self-created-only, its `resumeHref` defaulting to a dead end for anyone claimed. Fixed to show for a claimed guardian too, pointing straight at `/passport/section-a`, and to stop rendering the moment `section_a_complete` is true.

- **FIXED, STAGE 5 STEP 3: fourteen parent-facing screens used to resolve "my passport" via `.from("passports").eq("user_id", user.id).maybeSingle()`, and `.maybeSingle()` throws if more than one row matches.** A parent with two children's passports would have hit a hard error on every one of these screens, not silently seen one child — worse than the guardian-visibility gap Stage 5 Step 2 fixed alongside it. This had never been observable because `passports.user_id unique` (still live — a separate constraint from `passport_section_b/c/d`'s, which Step 1b closed; see the trigger entry above) has always made two rows structurally impossible — the bug was real and sitting underneath that constraint since before this stage existed, not introduced by it. `get_my_passports()` (Stage 5 Step 2) fixes this properly by returning a list instead of a single row; ten of the fourteen sites now go through it via the shared `useMyPassport` hook ([useMyPassport.ts](src/hooks/useMyPassport.ts)): `parent-dashboard/page.tsx`, `passport/dashboard/page.tsx`, `passport/progress/page.tsx`, `parent-dashboard/activity/page.tsx`, `parent-dashboard/incidents/page.tsx`, `messages/page.tsx`, `morning-checkin/page.tsx`, `more/page.tsx`, `CalmUnlockSheet.tsx`, `useParentCalmAccess.ts`. The remaining four — `passport/section-a/page.tsx` and `usePassportSectionB/C/D.ts` — are deliberately NOT migrated: they're one coherent unit, the self-created-passport wizard, and both their read AND their write are keyed on `user_id` (the section tables' own `onConflict: "user_id"` upsert at the time — since fixed, Step 1b, see above) — migrating only the read half would resolve a guardian's OWN claimed passport into a form whose write path would silently create an unrelated second passport row instead of editing the one just resolved. Left exactly as-is; the residual risk (a claimed-passport guardian navigating here directly by URL gets a blank "create new" form rather than an error) is real but safe — it can create an extra passport, never corrupt the claimed one — and is now genuinely contained by `passport/welcome/page.tsx`'s own redirect (added in the same pass): a parent who already has any passport is sent straight to `/passport/dashboard` on landing, never shown the create-new form at all. `passport/section-d/4/page.tsx`'s own passport UPDATE (`.eq("id", passportId)`) was already id-keyed, not part of this bug, no change needed. Also added this same pass, at `/passport/claim`: the claim screen itself (Requirement 4), and `/passport/welcome`'s "Already have a code from your child's school?" branch (Requirement 3) linking to it. `/principal/passports/[passportId]` (Requirement 2) now shows either the claim-code generation state or the guardian list, driven by `get_passport_claim_code_status()`/`get_passport_guardians_for_child()` (both 0114) — exactly the state `get_passport_guardians_for_child()`'s own migration comment named as missing ("the section vanishes is not a designed state"). One loose end from this: `revoke_passport_claim_code()` (0114/0115) still has no client caller anywhere — `get_passport_claim_code_status()` doesn't return the claim code's own `id`, only `code`/`expires_at`, so there's nothing for a standalone "Revoke" button to call. Not fixed here: the principal UI uses "Generate a new code" instead, which `generate_passport_claim_code()` already implements as an atomic revoke-then-reissue of the caller's own prior code — a real, working substitute for the common case, not a gap in what a principal can actually do, but `revoke_passport_claim_code()` itself is dead code today.

- **CLOSED, PRD 3 STAGE 5: two guardians of the same passport can no longer independently see "not checked in yet" and both submit as if the other didn't exist.** `morning_checkins`' SELECT policy widened (0144/0145) from `auth.uid() = user_id` to `owns_passport(passport_id)` — INSERT stays user_id-scoped (each guardian's own submission), no UPDATE policy, no unique constraint, deliberately: the fix is information, not prevention or editing. `get_todays_checkin()` (0144) resolves which row a guardian sees — their own if they submitted, otherwise the most recent from any guardian — and `parent-dashboard`'s `CheckInCard` renders three states on that: nobody yet, someone-else's (with "add your own"), or your own static confirmation. Teacher-side attribution added alongside it (0146, `get_todays_checkins_for_passports()`): the grid tile shows a count badge and sorts on worst-of-all-accounts RAG; the detail sheet shows every account in full, attributed by name, equal weight, no narrative copy about separated families. Adversarially covered (`CHECK AAA`, `CHECK BBB`) and live-verified on the deployed app with two real guardians and a real teacher.

  One thing worth recording from the verification pass itself, not a process rule — just an observation: the first version of this stage's report claimed the dashboard card "renders nothing" after 1pm, based on screenshots that in fact had the afternoon "Waiting for today's update from school" state (a separate branch of the same `CheckInCard`, [parent-dashboard/page.tsx:590](src/app/parent-dashboard/page.tsx:590), unrelated to Stage 5 and already guardian-aware since 0118) rendering in full on every one of them. It was described as unrelated dashboard content instead of read as the answer to a question not yet asked. Same shape as several real bugs already in this file: a partial view of the evidence, inferred into a conclusion, rather than read fully before concluding anything.

# Parked work

- **INSTITUTION-EMPLOYED CLINICIAN VERIFICATION — PARKED, POST-TRIAL. Manual path used for the trial instead, 16 Sept 2026.** The current verification flow (`clinician/verify/page.tsx`, `submit_clinician_verification()`) is built for an INDEPENDENT practitioner only — PSI membership number, CORU registration number, BACB certification number, and a government ID all required, reviewed by a human via `approve_clinician()`/`reject_clinician()` (service-role only, run in the Supabase Dashboard). Nothing in `clinicians`' own schema distinguishes an institution-employed clinician from an independent one — no `employer_institution_id`, no `verification_method`, one undifferentiated path.

  **Decided: one school-employed psychologist does not justify building a general institution-vouches mechanism under trial deadline pressure** — the same posture this product already takes with institution creation itself (deliberately manual, `institution_staff` gate is the point, not friction to remove — see the "institution creation being manual is deliberate" entry above). The trial school's own BCBA was onboarded via the existing `approve_clinician()` RPC directly (she signs up normally, selects "Behavioural Psychologist" as her specialty, and is verified without ever submitting the independent-credential form — `approve_clinician()` only requires `verification_status = 'pending'`, which merely selecting a specialty already produces; it does not require `submit_clinician_verification()` to have been called first). No schema change, no new migration, no new code — the existing manual-review mechanism already covered this case once the credential-form step was skipped.

  **The general mechanism, sized before any of it is built, so nobody mistakes it for small:** a real multi-part feature, not a quick addition —
  - **Schema**: a way to record that a clinician is institution-employed and by which institution (most naturally `employer_institution_id uuid references institutions(id)` on `clinicians`), plus a `verification_method` column (e.g. `'independent_credentials' | 'institution_vouched'`) so the two paths never collapse into one ambiguous state — the exact "a column name is a claim" risk this file's own gotchas already warn about elsewhere.
  - **Two new RPCs**: a submission path for the school-employed case (parallel to `submit_clinician_verification()`, without the PSI/CORU/BACB/government-ID requirements that don't apply), and a `vouch_for_clinician()`-style approval RPC modeled on `approve_staff_join()`'s own already-proven authorization shape (caller must be an active, approved principal at the institution the clinician claims employment at) — a real, reusable precedent for "an institution vouches for someone," but still new code here since `submit_clinician_verification()`/`approve_clinician()`/`reject_clinician()` are tightly scoped to the independent-credential shape today.
  - **Two client flows**: a clinician-side submission screen (pick employer institution by code, mirroring `join-institution`'s own pattern) plus a pending-state screen, and a principal-side approval screen (Directory's existing Clinicians segment is the natural home).

  Roughly half the hard design work (the vouching authorization model, the pending/verified/rejected state machine) is a direct adaptation of `approve_staff_join()`'s already-shipped pattern — but this is schema + two RPCs + two client flows, not a small addition. Revisit when a second institution-employed clinician needs onboarding, or when the manual path becomes the bottleneck it was deliberately allowed to be for one person.

- **CPI VOCABULARY EDITING — DECIDED, CLOSED, NO BUILD. STAYS READ-ONLY.** `cpi_reason_types`, `cpi_disengagement_types` and `cpi_result_types` remain select-only by design (`0068`), permanently, not pending Catherine's view. Decided 10 Sept 2026: a school on MAPA or Team Teach needing different terms is a vocabulary edit Behaviour Hive makes for them, not a control handed over — an editable list means staff could record a technique nobody was trained in, on a legal record. Schema-ready for a write policy if this is ever revisited, but revisiting it is a new decision to make deliberately, not a default to fall back into.

- **PARENT INVITATION BY EMAIL — PARKED.** Claim codes must currently be handed over in person, which fails once a school is inviting parents remotely. Recon done. Scope when picked up: an email carrying the school's name, an explanation and a LINK to the claim screen — NOT the code and NOT the child's name, so a mistyped address costs nothing and the credential stays on a separate channel. Principal-only, one action beside "Generate code". The show-the-code path stays untouched.

  Open questions: whether Resend already exists as Supabase's custom SMTP provider (dashboard config, invisible to a codebase grep) and whether its domain is already verified — which would remove both the deliverability warm-up and the new-processor question. Do NOT use `inviteUserByEmail()`: it routes through the Supabase Auth signup bucket, the same one currently inflated 30→300 and flagged as must-revert-before-scale.

  Auto-applying the code through signup is a SEPARATE, larger piece of work — confirmation is OTP-entry not a magic link, so there is no link-click for a query param to ride on, and chaining it would touch shared registration code every account type depends on.

- **PARENT-TRACK LEFTOVERS FROM THE PARENT-LED MODEL — CLOSED, PRD 3 STAGE 2.** PRD 1 changed who owns a child's file; the parent's own screens went unrevisited until Stage 2. `AddChildSheet` (teacher/SNA self-link by passport code, gated on `approved_by_parent`) is deleted, along with its three call sites (`teacher/dashboard`, `teacher/students`, `sna/passports`) — recon found no gap, `grant_passport_access()` already covers every case it served and more. `ShareBottomSheet`'s `handleApprove()` (parent-approves-a-school-by-code) is deleted along with its entry point; the outbound passport-code section and clinician-connect are unrelated and untouched. The Connected Schools list stays as read-only information, relabeled, with the `approved_by_parent = true` filter dropped (it was hiding links the flag no longer distinguishes). Deployed `b555d49`, live-verified.

  **CLOSED, 10 Sept 2026:** the two copy locations this entry named are fixed. `src/app/consent/page.tsx` is gone entirely — replaced by the consent/agreement screens rebuild (`src/components/consent/`, five distinct role screens, real signed-off copy, no lookup table). `PrivacyPolicyContent.tsx` no longer claims the data "belongs to you" or that school access can be "reviewed and revoked... from the passport's Manage Access section" (a section that, for school access, no longer exists) — it now says the school keeps its own record under its own obligations, and the parent controls their own contributions and their own clinician connections. A full sweep of the rest of `src/` for the same claim found nothing else.
